除了阿伯肖正在假扮的那种眼里只有自己的傻瓜，谁都不可能看不出这人那副神经紧绷到极点的可怜相。他浑身发颤，嗓音也全然失了控。阿伯肖手腕一挥，潇洒地签下名字，把笔递还回去。他朝门口走去时，屋里清晰地响起一片如释重负的叹息。

===Original===
On the threshold he turned and looked back.

“Poor young Petrie knows all about this, I suppose?” he inquired. “I trust he’s not very cut up? Poor lad.”

“Mr. Petrie has been informed, of course,” Dr. Whitby said stiffly. “He felt the shock—naturally—but like the rest of us I fancy he must have expected it for some time. He was only a relative by his aunt’s marriage, you know, and that took place after the war, I believe.”

===Chinese===
在门槛上，他回身望了一眼。

“可怜的年轻皮特里已经知道这一切了吧？”他问道，“但愿他没太伤心？可怜的小伙子。”

“皮特里先生当然已经获知了，”惠特比医生生硬地说，“他受了震动——这是自然——不过我想，他和我们大家一样，多半早料到有这一天了。您知道，他不过是凭姑母的婚事才与上校沾的亲，而那桩婚事，我记得还是战后的事。”

===Original===
“Still,” said Abbershaw, with a return of his old fussiness of manner, “very shocking and very distressing—very distressing. Good night, gentlemen.”

On the last words he went out and closed the door of the great sombre room behind him. Once in the corridor, his expression changed. The fussy, pompous personality that he had assumed dropped from him like a cloak, and he became at once alert and purposeful. There were many things that puzzled him, but of one thing he was perfectly certain. Colonel Gordon Coombe had not died of heart disease.

===Chinese===
“话虽如此，”阿伯肖说，又摆回他惯常那副啰里啰嗦的做派，“还是非常令人震惊、非常令人痛心——非常痛心。晚安，先生们。”

说完最后几个字，他便走了出去，把那间幽暗大房的门带上。一到走廊里，他的神情就变了。方才假扮的那副手忙脚乱、妄自尊大的性子，像件斗篷似的从他身上滑落，他立时变得警觉干练。还有许多事情令他困惑不解，但有一件事他有十足的把握：戈登·库姆上校不是死于心脏病。

## V The Mask / 第五章 面具

===Original===

Abbershaw made his way quietly down the corridor to Wyatt’s room. The young man had taken him into it himself earlier in the day, and he found it without difficulty.

There was no light in the crack of the door, and he hesitated for a moment before he knocked, as if undecided whether he would disturb its occupant or not, but at length he raised his hand and tapped on the door.

There was no reply, and after waiting a few minutes he knocked again. Still no one answered him, and obeying a sudden impulse, he lifted the latch and went in.

He was in a long, narrow room with a tall window in the wall immediately facing him, giving out on to a balcony. The place was in darkness save for the faint light of a newly risen moon, which streamed in through the window.

===Chinese===

阿伯肖悄没声儿地顺着走廊走向怀亚特的房间。白天那位年轻人曾亲自领他进去过，他没费什么周折便找到了门。

门缝里透不出一丝光亮。敲门前他踌躇了片刻，仿佛拿不定主意要不要惊扰房里的人，但末了还是抬起手，在门上轻轻叩了两下。

屋内无人应声。等了几分钟，他又敲了一回，依旧没有人答理他。他一时心血来潮，便掀开门闩走了进去。

这是一个狭长的房间，正对面的墙上开着一扇高窗，通向一座阳台。屋里一片漆黑，唯有新升的月亮透过窗子，泻进一地淡淡的光。

===Original===

He saw Wyatt at once. He was in his dressing-gown, standing in the window, his arms outstretched, his hands resting on either side of the frame.

Abbershaw spoke to him, and for a moment he did not move. Then he turned sharply, and for an instant the moonlight fell upon his face and the long slender lines of his sensitive hands. Then he turned round completely and came towards his friend.

But Abbershaw’s mood had changed: he was no longer so determined. He seemed to have changed his mind.

===Chinese===

他一眼便看见了怀亚特。他穿着晨衣，立在窗前，双臂张开，两手搭在窗框两侧。

阿伯肖唤了他一声，他却半晌一动不动。随后他猛地转过身，月光有一瞬落在他脸上，也照出了他那双敏感的手修长纤细的轮廓。接着他整个人转了过来，朝自己的朋友走去。

但阿伯肖的心思已经变了：他不再那样坚决，似乎是改了主意。

===Original===

“I’ve just heard,” he said, with real sympathy in his tone. “I’m awfully sorry. It was a bit of a shock, coming now, I suppose? Anything I can do, of course﻿ ﻿…”

Wyatt shook his head.

===Chinese===

“我刚听说了。”他说，语气里透着真切的同情，“我非常难过。这事偏偏赶在这时候，想必让人心里一震吧？当然，有什么我能效劳的，尽管开口……”

怀亚特摇了摇头。

===Original===

“Thanks,” he said, “but the old boy’s doctor had been expecting it for years. I believe all the necessary arrangements have been made for some time. It may knock the life out of the party pretty thoroughly, though, I’m afraid.”

“My dear man.” Abbershaw spoke hastily. “We’ll all sheer off first thing tomorrow morning, of course. Most people have got cars.”

===Chinese===

“谢谢你，”他说，“不过老先生的医生多年来就一直料着有这一天了。我想该办的各项事宜，早些时候都已安排妥当。只是恐怕这场聚会要被搅得兴致全无了。”

“亲爱的朋友，”阿伯肖急忙说道，“明儿一早我们自然全都撤走。大多数人都自己开车来的。”

===Original===

“Oh, don’t do that.” Wyatt spoke with sudden insistence. “I understand my uncle was very anxious that the party should go on,” he said. “Really, you’d be doing me a great service if you’d stay on till Monday and persuade the others to do the same. After all, it isn’t even as if it was his house. It’s mine, you know. It passed to me on Aunt’s death, but my uncle, her husband, was anxious to go on living here, so I rented it to him. I wish you’d stay. He would have liked it, and there’s no point in my staying down here alone. He was no blood relative of mine, and he had no kin as far as I know.” He paused, and added, as Abbershaw still looked dubious, “The funeral and cremation will take place in London. Gideon has arranged about that; he was his lawyer, you know, and a very close friend. Stay if you can, won’t you? Good night. Thanks for coming down.”

===Chinese===

“哦，可别这样。”怀亚特忽然执拗起来。“我知道，叔父生前非常希望聚会照常进行。”他说，“说实话，你们要是能待到星期一，再劝旁人也留下来，那真是帮了我大忙。毕竟，这宅子压根儿就算不得他的。它是我的，你知道。叔母去世后宅子便传到了我手里，可我叔父——她的丈夫——一心想继续住在这儿，我便把它租给了他。我希望你们留下。他若泉下有知也会高兴的，何况我一个人待在这乡下也没什么意思。他与我并无血亲关系，而且据我所知，他也没有什么亲属。”他顿了顿，见阿伯肖仍面有疑色，又补了一句：“葬礼和火化都在伦敦举行。吉迪恩已经安排好了；你知道，他本是叔父的律师，也是他极亲近的朋友。能留下就留下吧，好吗？晚安。谢谢你过来一趟。”

===Original===

Abbershaw went slowly back to his room, a slightly puzzled expression in his eyes. He had meant to tell Wyatt his discoveries, and even now he did not know quite why he had not done so. Instinct told him to be cautious. He felt convinced that there were more secrets in Black Dudley that night than the old house had ever known. Secrets that would be dangerous if they were too suddenly brought to light.

He found Prenderby sitting up for him, the ashtray at his side filled with cigarette-stubs.

===Chinese===

阿伯肖缓步走回自己的房间，眼里带着一丝困惑不解的神情。他本打算把自己的发现告诉怀亚特，可直到此刻，他也不大明白自己为何终究没有说出口。本能告诫他要谨慎行事。他确信，那天夜里布莱克·达德利所藏的秘密，比这座老宅有生以来所知道的还要多——这些秘密若被过于仓促地揭破，将是危险的。

他回到房里，发现普伦德比还在坐着等他，手边的烟灰缸里已盛满了烟头。

===Original===

“So you’ve turned up at last,” he said peevishly. “I wondered if they’d done a sensational disappearing act with you. This house is such a ghostly old show I’ve been positively sweltering with terror up here. Anything transpired?”

Abbershaw sat down by the fire before he spoke.

“I signed the certificate,” he said at last. “I was practically forced into it. They had the whole troupe there, old Uncle Tom Beethoven and all.”

===Chinese===

“你可算回来了。”他没好气地说，“我还当他们也给你变了一出轰动一时的失踪戏法呢。这宅子阴森得像个鬼地方，我在楼上吓得直冒冷汗。有什么新动静吗？”

阿伯肖先在炉火边坐下，这才开口。

“我在死亡证明上签了字。”他终于说道，“简直是被迫的。他们全班人马都在场，连那位‘老汤姆叔叔’贝多芬也没落下。”

===Original===

Prenderby leant forward, his pale face becoming suddenly keen again.

“They are up to something, aren’t they?” he said.

“Oh, undoubtedly.” Abbershaw spoke with authority. “I saw the corpse’s face. There was no heart trouble there. He was murdered﻿—stuck in the back, I should say.” He paused, and hesitated as if debating something in his mind.

Prenderby looked at him curiously. “Of course, I guessed as much,” he said, “but what’s the other discovery? What’s on your mind?”

===Chinese===

普伦德比倾身向前，苍白的脸上陡然又现出机敏的神色。

“他们在搞什么名堂，是不是？”他说。

“哦，那是肯定的。”阿伯肖的口气不容置疑，“我看过死者的脸。那上面可没有半点心脏病的迹象。他是被人谋杀的——要我说，是背后挨了一刀。”他顿住了，迟疑着，仿佛心里在权衡什么。

普伦德比好奇地望着他。“这个我当然猜到了几分，”他说，“可另一个发现是什么？你在琢磨什么呢？”

===Original===

Abbershaw looked up at him, and his round grey-blue eyes met the boy’s for an instant.

“A darned queer thing, Prenderby,” he said. “I don’t understand it at all. There’s more mystery here than you’d think. When I twitched back the sheet and looked at the dead man’s face it was darkish in that four-poster, but there was light enough for me to see one thing. Extreme loss of blood had flattened the flesh down over his bones till he looked dead﻿—very dead﻿—and that plate he wore over the top of his face had slipped out of place and I saw something most extraordinary.”

===Chinese===

阿伯肖抬眼看他，一双圆滚滚的灰蓝色眼睛与年轻人的目光相触了一瞬。

“一件邪门透顶的事，普伦德比。”他说，“我全然摸不着头脑。这里的蹊跷，比你想象的要多。我掀开床单去看死者的脸——那张四柱大床里光线昏暗，可那一星半点的光亮，已够我看清一件事了。极度的失血让他的皮肉塌陷下去、紧贴着骨头，他看上去死了——死得透透的——而他罩在脸上那片假面又滑了位，于是我看见了一件极其异样的事情。”

===Original===

Prenderby raised his eyes inquiringly. “Very foul?” he said.

“Not at all. That was the amazing part of it.”

Abbershaw leaned forward in his chair and his eyes were very grave and hard. “Prenderby, that man had no need to wear that plate. His face was as whole as yours or mine!”

===Chinese===

普伦德比探询地抬起眼来。“很骇人吗？”他说。

“一点也不。这正是最叫人吃惊的地方。”

阿伯肖在椅子里向前倾身，眼神异常凝重而冷峻。“普伦德比，那个人根本用不着戴那片假面。他的脸完好无缺，同你我的一样！”

===Original===

“Good God!” The boy sat up, the truth slowly dawning on him. “Then it was simply﻿—”

Abbershaw nodded.

“A mask,” he said.

===Chinese===

“天哪！”小伙子坐直了身子，真相在他心里缓缓亮开。“那么它根本就是——”

阿伯肖点了点头。

“是一副面具。”他说。

## VI Mr. Campion Brings the House Down / 第六章 坎皮恩先生把宅子闹翻了天

===Original===
Abbershaw sat up for some time, smoking, after Prenderby left him, and when at last he got into bed he did not sleep at once, but lay staring up into the darkness of the beamed ceiling﻿—thinking.

He had just fallen into a doze in which the events of the evening formed themselves into a fantastic nightmare, when a terrific thud above his head and a shower of plaster upon his face brought him hurriedly to his senses.

He sat up in bed, every nerve alert and tingling, waiting for the next development.
===Chinese===
普伦德比走后，阿伯肖又坐着抽了半晌的烟；待到终于上了床，他也没有立刻入睡，而是仰面躺着，凝望橡木梁天花板上方的一片黑暗——想着心事。

他刚刚朦胧睡去，晚间种种事端正在梦中凝成一场光怪陆离的噩梦，头顶上忽地一声巨响，劈头盖脸一阵灰泥碎屑洒落在他脸上，把他仓皇地唤回了神智。

他在床上坐起身来，每一根神经都警醒亢奋、微微震颤，静候事态的下一步发展。

===Original===
It came almost immediately.

From the floor directly above his head came a series of extraordinary sounds. It seemed as if heavy pieces of furniture were being hurled about by some infuriated giant, and between the crashes Abbershaw fancied he could discern the steady murmur of someone cursing in a deep, unending stream.

After a second or so of this he decided that it was time to get up and investigate, and slipping on his dressing-gown he dashed out into the corridor, where the grey light of morning was just beginning to pierce the gloom.
===Chinese===
事态几乎立刻就来了。

正头顶上的楼层里传来一连串异样的声响，仿佛有个暴怒的巨人正把一件件沉重的家具四处乱砸；而在声声巨响的间隙，阿伯肖觉得还能分辨出一股低沉的咒骂，绵绵不绝、滔滔不断。

这般闹了片刻，他断定该起身查探一番了，于是披上晨衣，一头冲进走廊——清晨灰白的微光正开始刺破那里的昏暗。

===Original===
Here the noise above was even more distinct. A tremendous upheaval seemed to be in progress.

Not only Abbershaw had been awakened by it; the whole house appeared to be stirring. He ran up the staircase in the direction from which the noise was coming to discover that an old-time architect had not built another room above the one in which he slept but a wide gallery from which a second staircase descended. Here he was confronted by an extraordinary scene.
===Chinese===
在这里，头顶上的喧闹听得愈发真切了。楼上似乎正进行着一场天翻地覆的大骚动。

被吵醒的不止阿伯肖一人；整座宅子似乎都在骚动。他循着声响传来的方向奔上楼梯，才发现当年的老建筑师在他卧室上方造的并不是另一间屋子，而是一道宽阔的长廊，长廊上又降下第二道楼梯。就在这里，一幅异乎寻常的景象撞入他的眼帘。

===Original===
The manservant he had noticed so particularly on the evening before was grappling with someone who was putting up a very stout resistance. The man was attacking his opponent with an amazing ferocity. Furniture was hurled in all directions, and as Abbershaw came up he caught a stream of oaths from the infuriated footman.

His first thought was that a burglar had been surprised red-handed, but as the two passed under a window in their violent passage round the place, the straggling light fell upon the face of the second combatant and Abbershaw started with surprise, for in that moment he had caught a glimpse of the vacant and peculiarly inoffensive features of Mr. Albert Campion.
===Chinese===
头天晚上他曾格外留意过的那个男仆，正跟一个抵抗得极其顽强的人扭作一团。那男人攻势之猛令人咋舌。家具被砸得四下横飞，阿伯肖赶到近前时，还听见那名怒不可遏的仆役口中骂声连成一串。

他的头一个念头是：窃贼被当场逮了个正着。可是当那两人绕着廊子激烈缠斗、从一扇窗下经过时，零零落落漏进来的微光照在了第二个斗士的脸上，阿伯肖惊得一震——就在那一瞬间，他瞥见了阿尔伯特·坎皮恩先生那张茫然失神、看上去格外无害的面孔。

===Original===
By this time there were many steps on the stairs, and the next moment half the house-party came crowding round behind Abbershaw; Chris Kennedy in a resplendent dressing-gown was well to the fore.

“Hullo! A scrap?” he said, with something very near satisfaction in his voice, and threw himself upon the two without further preliminaries.

As the confusion increased with this new development Abbershaw darted forward and, stooping suddenly, picked up something off the floor by the head of the second staircase. It was very swiftly done, and no one noticed the incident.
===Chinese===
这时楼梯上已是脚步杂沓，转眼间半数宾客便簇拥到了阿伯肖身后；克里斯·肯尼迪裹着一件光艳夺目的晨衣，一马当先冲在前头。

“哟！打架呢？”他发话道，嗓音里透着一股近乎称心的满足，便不再多作客套，径直朝那两个人扑了上去。

这一番新发展使乱局愈演愈烈，阿伯肖却趁乱箭步上前，猛然弯腰，从第二道楼梯梯头旁的地板上拾起了一样什么东西。整套动作干净利落之至，没有一个人察觉这一幕。

===Original===
Chris Kennedy’s weight and enthusiasm brought the fight to an abrupt finish.

Mr. Campion picked himself up from the corner where he had been last hurled. He was half strangled, but still laughing idiotically. Meanwhile, Chris Kennedy inspected the butler, whose stream of rhetoric had become much louder but less coherent.

“The fellow’s roaring tight,” he announced, upon closer inspection. “Absolutely fighting-canned, but it’s wearing off a bit now.”
===Chinese===
克里斯·肯尼迪的块头与热情，令这场打斗戛然而止。

坎皮恩先生从自己最后被摔进去的那个角落里爬了起来。他险些被勒得背过气去，却还在傻里傻气地笑个不停。与此同时，克里斯·肯尼迪察看起那位管家来——后者滔滔不绝的高谈阔论愈发响亮了，只是愈发前言不搭后语。

“这家伙醉得又嚷又闹，”他仔细端详过之后宣布道，“彻头彻尾醉到了见谁打谁的地步，不过这会儿酒劲稍稍过去了些。”

===Original===
He pushed the man away from him contemptuously, and the erstwhile warrior reeled against the stairhead and staggered off down out of sight.

“What’s happened? What’s the trouble?” Wyatt Petrie came hurrying up the passage, his voice anxious and slightly annoyed.

Everybody looked at Mr. Campion. He was leaning up against the balustrade, his fair hair hanging over his eyes, and for the first time it dawned upon Abbershaw that he was fully dressed, and not, as might have been expected, in the dinner-jacket he had worn on the previous evening.
===Chinese===
他轻蔑地把那人一把推开。方才那位勇士踉跄着撞上梯头，跌跌撞撞地下了楼，转眼不见踪影。

“出什么事了？怎么回事？”怀亚特·皮特里匆匆穿过走廊赶来，语气又焦急又微带愠意。

人人都望向坎皮恩先生。他斜倚在栏杆上，浅色的头发耷拉下来遮住了眼睛。阿伯肖这才头一回恍然察觉：他身上穿的是齐齐整整的全套衣服，而不是意想之中昨夜晚饭时那件晚宴上衣。

===Original===
His explanation was characteristic.

“Most extraordinary,” he said, in his slightly high-pitched voice. “The fellow set on me. Picked me up and started doing exercises with me as if I were a dumbbell. I thought it was one of you fellows joking at first, but when he began to jump on me it percolated through that I was being massacred. Butchered to make a butler’s beano, in fact.”

He paused and smiled fatuously.
===Chinese===
他的解释一如既往地本色。

“真是非同寻常之至，”他用他那略偏尖细的高音说道。“是那家伙扑上来袭击我的。把我拎起来，拿我当哑铃做起了体操。起先我还以为是你们哪位在开玩笑，可等他开始往我身上蹦跶，我才慢慢回过味来：我这是在惨遭屠戮了。说白了，就是宰了我好给管家凑一场狂欢酒宴。”

他顿了顿，傻乎乎地一笑。

===Original===
“I began to hit back then,” he continued. “The bird was tight, of course, but I’m glad you fellows turned up. I didn’t like the idea of him chipping bits off the ancestral home with me.”

“My dear fellow, I’m frightfully sorry this has happened. The man shall be discharged tomorrow. I’ll see to it.” Wyatt spoke with real concern, but Abbershaw was not nearly so easily satisfied.

“Where did he get at you?” he said, suddenly stepping forward. “Where were you?”
===Chinese===
“于是我便开始还手，”他接着说。“那家伙当然醉得可以，不过诸位及时赶到，我很高兴。一想到他要拿我去磕磕碰碰，把这座祖宅敲下一块块碎片来，我就不大乐意。”

“亲爱的伙计，发生这种事我真是抱歉极了。明天就把这人辞退。我一定办到。”怀亚特说得满含真切的关切，可阿伯肖远没有这么容易打发。

“他是在哪儿扑上你的？”他说着突然上前一步。“你当时在哪儿？”

===Original===
Mr. Campion met the question with charming ingenuousness.

“Just coming out of my room﻿—that’s the door, over there,” he said. “I opened it and walked out into a war.”

He was buttoning up his waistcoat, which had been ripped open in the fight, as he spoke.
===Chinese===
坎皮恩先生以一派迷人的坦率迎住了这个问题。

“正从我房间里出来——喏，就是那扇门，”他说。“我开了门，一头走进了战争。”

说话间，他正扣着马甲的纽扣——那件马甲在打斗中被人扯开了。

===Original===
Abbershaw glanced at the grandfather clock at the head of the staircase. It showed the hour at eight minutes past four. Mr. Campion followed the direction of his eyes.

“Yes,” he said foolishly, “I﻿—I always get up early.”

“Amazingly early,” said Abbershaw pointedly.
===Chinese===
阿伯肖瞥了一眼楼梯口的落地大摆钟。钟面指着四点过八分。坎皮恩先生顺着他的目光望了过去。

“是啊，”他傻里傻气地说，“我——我一向起得早。”

“早得惊人哪。”阿伯肖意有所指地说。

===Original===
“I was, this morning,” agreed Mr. Campion cheerfully, adding by way of explanation, “I’m one of those birds who can never sleep in a strange bed. And then, you know, I’m so afraid of ghosts. I didn’t see any, of course,” he went on hastily, “but I said to myself as I got into bed last night, ‘Albert, this place smells of ghosts,’ and somehow I couldn’t get that idea out of my head all night. So as soon as it began to get light I thought a walk was indicated, so I got up, dressed, and sallied forth into the fray.” He paused and yawned thoughtfully. “I do believe I shall go back to bed now,” he remarked as they all stared at him. “I don’t feel much like my walk now. In fact, I don’t feel much like anything. Bung-ho, everybody, Uncle Albert is now closing down until nine-thirty, when the breakfast programme will begin, I hope.” On the last word he waved his hand to them and disappeared into his own room, shutting the door firmly behind him.
===Chinese===
“今儿早上正是如此，”坎皮恩先生欣然附和，又补充说明道，“我是那种一到陌生床上就睡不着的家伙。再说呢，你们知道，我顶怕鬼了。当然，我一只鬼也没瞧见，”他慌忙接下去，“可昨晚上床的时候我对自己说：‘阿尔伯特，这地方一股子鬼气。’不知怎么的，这念头在我脑子里盘桓了一整夜也挥之不去。于是天刚透亮，我便断定对症的方子是散个步，就起了床，穿好衣裳，昂然出征，杀入战团。”他顿了顿，若有所思地打了个哈欠。“我看这会儿我还是回床上去吧，”众人都瞪着他瞧，他却这般说道。“如今我可不太想散步了。说实在的，我什么都不太想了。诸位，回见！阿尔伯特叔叔现在闭台停播，直到九点半——届时早餐节目有望开播。”话音落地，他朝众人挥了挥手，径自进了自己的房间，随手把门牢牢关上。

===Original===
As Abbershaw turned to go back to his bedroom he became aware of a slender figure in a dressing-gown at his side. It was Meggie. Seized by a sudden impulse, he spoke to her softly.

“Who brought Campion down?”

She looked at him in surprise.
===Chinese===
阿伯肖转身要回卧室时，察觉身旁立着一个裹着晨衣的纤细身影。是梅吉。一阵突来的冲动攫住了他，他放轻声音对她开口。

“是谁把坎皮恩带到这儿来的？”

她诧异地望着他。

===Original===
“Why, Anne,” she said. “I told you. They arrived together about the same time that I did. Why the interest? Anything I can do?”

Abbershaw hesitated.

“Well, yes,” he said at last. “She’s a friend of yours, isn’t she?”
===Chinese===
“咦，是安妮呀，”她说，“我不是跟你讲过了嘛。他们俩差不多跟我同时到的。怎么忽然关心起这个？有什么我能帮上忙的吗？”

阿伯肖迟疑了一下。

“这个……有的，”他终于开了口。“她是你的朋友，对吧？”

===Original===
Meggie nodded.

“Rather; I’ve known her for years.”

“Good,” said Abbershaw. “Look here, could you get her to come down into the garden? Meet me down there in half an hour in that shrubbery we found last night? There’s one or two things I want to ask her. Can you manage that for me?”
===Chinese===
梅吉点了点头。

“何止是朋友；我认识她好多年了。”

“好，”阿伯肖说。“是这样，你能设法请她到花园里来一趟吗？半小时后在昨晚我们待过的那片灌木丛里跟我会合？我有一两件事要问问她。你能替我办到吗？”

===Original===
“Of course.” She looked up at him and smiled; then she added, “Anything happened?”

Abbershaw looked at her, and noticed for the first time that there was a faintly scared expression in her narrow brown eyes, and a sudden desire to comfort her assailed him. Had he been a little less precise, a little less timid in these matters, he would probably have kissed her. As it was, he contented himself by patting her hand rather foolishly and murmuring. “Nothing to get excited about,” in a way which neither convinced her nor satisfied himself.

“In half an hour,” she murmured and disappeared like a fragile ghost down the corridor.
===Chinese===
“当然。”她抬眼望着他，笑了笑；随即又添了一句：“出什么事了吗？”

阿伯肖望着她，头一回注意到她那双狭长的棕色眼睛里隐隐含着一丝惊惧，一阵想要安慰她的冲动蓦然袭上他的心头。倘若他在这类事情上不那么一丝不苟、不那么缩手缩脚，他很可能会吻她。而眼下，他只是颇有些傻气地拍了拍她的手，含含糊糊地咕哝了一句“没什么好大惊小怪的”，那副样子既没能让她信服，也没能让他自己满意。

“半小时后见。”她低声应了一句，便像个纤弱的小幽灵似的，顺着走廊飘然隐去了。

## VII Five O’Clock in the Morning / 第七章 清晨五点

===Original===
George Abbershaw stood in front of the fireplace in his bedroom and looked down into the fast-greying embers amongst which some red sparks still glowed, and hesitated irresolutely. In ten minutes he was to meet Meggie and Anne Edgeware in the garden. He had until then to make up his mind.

He was not a man to do anything impulsively, and the problem which faced him now was an unusual one.

On the mantelpiece near his head lay a small leather wallet, the silk lining of which had been ripped open and something removed, leaving the whole limp and empty. Abbershaw looked down on a sheaf of paper which he held in one hand, and tapped it thoughtfully with the other.

If only, he reflected, he knew exactly what he was doing. The thought occurred to him, in parenthesis, that here arose the old vexed question as to whether it was permissible to destroy a work of art on any pretext whatsoever.

For five minutes he deliberated, and then, having made up his mind, he knelt down before the dying fire and fanned the embers into a flame, and after coolly preparing a small bonfire in the grate stood back to watch it burn.

===Chinese===
乔治·阿伯肖站在卧室的壁炉前，低头望着迅速灰暗下去的余烬——其间仍有几粒红火星在闪烁——迟疑不决。再过十分钟，他就要同梅吉和安妮·埃奇韦尔在花园里碰头。在那之前，他得拿定主意。

他不是个逢事冲动的人，而此刻摆在面前的难题又非同寻常。

他头边的壁炉架上摊着一只小皮夹，丝绸衬里已被撕开，里面的东西被人取走了，整只夹子软塌塌、空瘪瘪的。阿伯肖低头看着一手捏着的那沓纸，用另一只手若有所思地轻轻叩着它。

他心想，要是能确切知道自己这是在干什么就好了。他还顺带冒出一个念头：这里又牵出了那个聚讼纷纭的老问题——究竟可不可以随便什么借口，就毁掉一件艺术品。

他斟酌了五分钟，随后拿定主意，在奄奄一息的炉火前跪下，将余烬扇成火苗，又不动声色地在炉膛里架起小小一堆篝火，退后站着，看它烧起来。

===Original===
The destruction of the leather case was a problem which presented more difficulties. For a moment or two he was at a loss, but then taking it up he considered it carefully.

It was of a usual pattern, a strip of red leather folded over at either end to form two inner pockets. He took out his own case and compared the two. His own was new; an aunt had sent it to him for his birthday, and in an excess of kindliness had caused a small gold monogram stud to be made for it, a circular fretted affair which fastened through the leather with a small clip. This stud Abbershaw removed, and, gouging a hole in the red wallet, effected an exchange.

A liberal splodging with ink from his fountain pen completed the disguise, and, satisfied that no one at a first or second glance would recognize it, he ripped out the rest of the lining, trimmed the edges with a pair of nail scissors, and calmly transferred his papers, with the exception of a letter or two, to it, and tucked it in his pocket. His own wallet he put carefully into the inner pocket of his dinner-jacket, hanging up in the wardrobe.

Then, content with his arrangements, he went softly down the wide staircase and let himself out into the garden.

===Chinese===
销毁那只皮夹却是个更棘手的难题。他一时没了主意，过了片刻才把它拿起来，细细端详。

它是常见的样式：一条红皮革，两端各自折拢，形成两个内袋。他掏出自己那只皮夹，把两者比较了一番。他这只是新的，是位姑母送他的生日礼物；她好心得过了头，还特意叫人给配做了一枚小小的金质花押饰扣——圆形镂空的小玩意儿，靠一枚小别扣穿透皮革固定。阿伯肖把这枚饰扣取了下来，在那只红皮夹上凿出一个洞，完成了调包。

再用自来水笔的墨水饱饱地涂上几道，伪装便告完成。确信无论第一眼还是第二眼都不会有人认出它之后，他把剩下的衬里统统撕去，用指甲剪修齐边口，然后不动声色地把自己的纸件——除了一两封信——尽数转移进去，塞进衣袋。他自己那只皮夹则小心收进挂在衣柜里的晚礼服内袋。

一切安排停当，他心满意足，放轻脚步走下宽阔的楼梯，出了宅门，进到花园里。

===Original===
Meggie was waiting for him. He caught a glimpse of her red-gold hair against the dark green of the shrubbery. She was dressed in green, and despite his preoccupation with the affairs on hand, he noticed how very much it suited her.

“Anne is just coming,” she said, “I expect her any moment. I hope it’s something important you want to ask her. I don’t think she’ll relish getting up just to see the sun rise.”

Abbershaw looked dubious.

“I’m afraid that didn’t occur to me,” he said. “It is important, as it happens, although it may not sound so.”

The girl moved a step closer to him.

===Chinese===
梅吉在等他。他瞥见她那红金色的头发，映在灌木丛的墨绿底色上。她一身绿衣，尽管满心惦着眼前的事务，他还是留意到这颜色何等称她。

“安妮这就来，”她说，“我估摸着她随时到。我希望你要问她的当真是要紧事。要她大清早爬起来，就为看个日出，我看她未必乐意。”

阿伯肖面露迟疑。

“这一点我倒没想到。”他说，“碰巧确实是要紧事，尽管听上去未必像。”

姑娘朝他挨近一步。

===Original===
“I told *you*,” she said, looking up into his face. “Tell me. What are the developments?”

“I don’t know,” he said, “… yet. There’s only one thing I can tell you, and that will be common property by breakfast-time. Colonel Coombe is dead.”

The girl caught her breath sharply, and looked at him with fear in her brown eyes.

“You don’t mean he was﻿ ﻿… ?” She broke off, not using the word.

Abbershaw looked at her steadily.

“Dr. Whitby has pronounced it heart failure,” he said. The girl’s eyes widened, and her expression became puzzled.

“Then﻿—then the dagger﻿—?” she began.

===Chinese===
“话可是我先跟你说的。”她仰起脸望着他，“现在你说吧。事情有什么进展？”

“我还不知道，”他说，“……暂时还不知道。只有一件事我能告诉你，而且到早饭时分就要人尽皆知了。库姆上校死了。”

姑娘猛地倒吸一口气，棕色的眼睛里含着惧意，望着他。

“你该不是说他是……？”她话到一半收住了，没把那个字说出口。

阿伯肖定定地望着她。

“惠特比医生宣布的死因是心力衰竭。”他说。姑娘睁大了眼睛，神情变得困惑。

“那么——那么那把匕首——？”她开口道。

===Original===
“Ssh!” Abbershaw raised his hand warningly, for in the house a door had creaked, and now Anne Edgeware, a heavily embroidered Chinese dressing-gown over her frivolous pyjamas, crossed the grass towards them.

“Here I am,” she said. “I had to come like this. You don’t mind, do you? I really couldn’t bring myself to put on my clothes at the hour I usually take them off. What’s all the fun about?”

Abbershaw coughed: this kind of girl invariably embarrassed him.

“It’s awfully good of you to come down like this,” he said awkwardly. “And I’m afraid what I am going to say will sound both absurd and impertinent, but if you would just take it as a personal favour to me I would be eternally grateful.” He hesitated nervously, and then hurried on again. “I’m afraid I can’t offer you any explanation at the moment, but if you would just answer one or two questions and then forget I ever asked them, you would be rendering me a great service.”

The girl laughed.

“How thrilling!” she said. “It sounds just like a play! I’ve got just the right costume too, haven’t I? I feel I shall break out into song at any moment. What is it?”

Abbershaw was still ill at ease, and he spoke with unwonted timidity.

===Chinese===
“嘘！”阿伯肖抬手示警，因为宅子里一扇门刚吱呀响过，此时安妮·埃奇韦尔正穿过草地朝他们走来——花哨轻俏的睡衣外面，罩着一件绣得密密实实的中式晨衣。

“我来了。”她说，“我只得这副打扮来。你们不介意吧？要我在平常脱衣就寝的钟点重新把衣裳穿上，我实在狠不下这个心。到底有什么热闹事儿？”

阿伯肖咳了一声：这类姑娘回回都叫他发窘。

“你肯这样下楼来，真是太好了。”他局促地说，“恐怕我要讲的话听起来既荒唐又冒失，可要是你肯把它看作帮我个人的忙，我会感激不尽。”他紧张地顿了顿，随即又急急接下去：“眼下我恐怕无法向你作任何解释，但只要你肯回答一两个问题，过后忘掉我问过，就是帮了我一个大忙。”

姑娘笑出声来。

“太带劲了！”她说，“听着就跟一出戏似的！我这身行头也正合适，是不是？我觉得自己随时都会放声唱起来。到底什么事？”

阿伯肖仍不自在，说话带着少有的怯意。

===Original===
“That’s very good of you. As a matter of fact I wanted to ask you about Mr. Campion. I understood that he’s a friend of yours. Excuse me, but have you known him long?”

“Albert Campion?” said Anne blankly. “Oh, he’s not a friend of mine at all. I just gave him a lift down here in ‘Fido’﻿—that’s my car.”

Abbershaw looked puzzled.

“I’m sorry. I don’t quite understand,” he said. “Did you meet him at the station?”

“Oh no.” The girl was amused. “I brought him all the way down. You see,” she went on cheerfully, “I met him the night before we came down at the Goat on the Roof﻿—that’s the new nightclub in Jermyn Street, you know. I was with a party, and he sort of drifted into it. One of the lads knew him, I think. We were all talking, and quite suddenly it turned out that he was coming down here this weekend. He was fearfully upset, he said: he’d just run his bus into a lorry or something equally solid, so he couldn’t come down in it. So I offered him a lift﻿—naturally.”

===Chinese===
“你人真是太好了。其实，我想打听的是坎皮恩先生。我原以为他是你的朋友。恕我冒昧——你认识他很久了吗？”

“阿尔伯特·坎皮恩？”安妮一脸茫然地说，“哦，他压根儿不是我的朋友。我不过是让他在‘菲多’里搭了趟便车下来——那是我的车。”

阿伯肖露出困惑的神色。

“抱歉，我没太听明白。”他说，“你们是在车站碰的头？”

“哦，不。”姑娘觉得有趣，“是我一路把他捎下来的。是这样，”她兴冲冲地往下讲，“我们下来的前一晚，我在‘屋顶山羊’认识了他——就是杰明街新开的那家夜总会，你知道的。我当时同一伙人在一起，他就那么晃晃悠悠地凑了进来。我记得是哪个小伙子认识他。大家正聊着，突然发现他这个周末也要来这儿。他说他懊恼得要命：刚把自己的车撞上了一辆卡车还是什么同样结实的家伙，没法开着来了。于是我提出捎他一程——这还用说。”

===Original===
“Oh, er﻿—naturally,” said Abbershaw, who appeared to be still a little bewildered. “Wyatt invited him, of course.”

The girl in pyjamas looked at him, and a puzzled expression appeared on her doll-like face.

“Oh no,” she said. “I don’t think so﻿—in fact I’m sure he didn’t, because I introduced them myself. Not properly, you know,” she went on airily. “I just said, ‘Hullo, Wyatt, this thing is Albert Campion,’ and ‘Albert, this is the man of the house,’ but I could swear they didn’t know each other. I think he’s one of the Colonel’s pals﻿—how is the poor old boy, by the way?”

Neither Abbershaw nor Meggie spoke, but remained looking dubiously ahead of them, and Anne shivered.

“Here, I’m getting cold,” she said. “Is that all you wanted to know? Because if it is, I’ll get in, if you don’t mind. Sunrises and dabbling in the dew aren’t in my repertoire.”

She laughed as she spoke, and Abbershaw thanked her. “Not a word, mind,” he said hastily.

“Not a hint,” she promised lightly, and went fluttering off across the lawn, the Chinese robe huddled about her.

===Chinese===
“哦，呃——这还用说。”阿伯肖说，看样子仍有点发懵，“是怀亚特请的他，自然。”

睡衣姑娘望着他，那副娃娃似的脸上现出困惑的表情。

“哦，不对，”她说，“我不这么想——事实上我敢肯定不是，因为是我亲手给他们引见的。不算正儿八经的引见啦，你知道，”她轻飘飘地接着说，“我只说了句：‘喂，怀亚特，这个东西是阿尔伯特·坎皮恩。’又补了句：‘阿尔伯特，这位是这宅子的主人。’可我敢发誓，他们俩原先并不认识。我想他是上校的哪位朋友——对了，那可怜的老头儿近来可好？”

阿伯肖和梅吉都没作声，只顾满腹狐疑地望着前方，安妮打了个寒噤。

“喂，我要冻着了。”她说，“你们想知道的就这些？要是就这些，不介意的话我就进屋去了。看日出、蹚露水，可不在我保留节目之列。”

她边说边笑，阿伯肖向她道谢。“一个字也别露，记住。”他急急地说。

“半点口风也不透。”她轻快地应承，裹紧那件中式晨衣，翩然穿过草坪去了。

===Original===
As soon as she was out of earshot Meggie caught Abbershaw’s arm.

“George,” she said, “the Colonel didn’t invite Albert Campion here.”

He turned to her sharply.

“How do you know?” he demanded.

The girl spoke dryly.

“Because,” she said, “the Colonel himself pointed Campion out to me and asked who he was. Why, George,” she went on suddenly, as the idea occurred to her, “*nobody* asked him﻿—he hasn’t any business here at all!”

Abbershaw nodded.

“That’s just exactly what had occurred to me,” he said, and relapsed into silence.

===Chinese===
她一走到听不见的地方，梅吉便一把抓住阿伯肖的手臂。

“乔治，”她说，“上校并没有请阿尔伯特·坎皮恩到这儿来。”

他霍地转向她。

“你怎么知道？”他追问。

姑娘的语气干巴巴的。

“因为，”她说，“是上校他本人把坎皮恩指给我看、问我是谁来着。哎呀，乔治，”这个念头一冒出来，她便失声接了下去，“根本没有人请他——他到这里来，完全是没来由的！”

阿伯肖点了点头。

“我想到的正是这一点。”他说完，又陷入沉默。

===Original===
They walked slowly back to the house together, Meggie quiet and perturbed, her brown eyes narrowed and thoughtful; Abbershaw walking with his hands clasped behind his back, his head bowed.

He had had, he supposed, as much association with crime and criminals as any man of his age, but never, in any of his previous experiences of crime mysteries, had he been placed in a position which required of him both initiative and action. On other occasions an incident had been repeated to him and he had explained it, a problem had been put before him and he had solved it. Now, for the first time in his life he had to pick out his own questions and answer them himself. Every instinct in him told him to do something, but what exactly he ought to do he did not know.

They had almost reached the heavy iron-studded door which led into the hall, when a smothered exclamation from the girl made him stop suddenly and look up. The next instant he had stepped back into the shadow of some overgrown laurels by the house and drawn the girl back after him.

Out of the garage, silent as a cloud of smoke, had come the incredible old car which Abbershaw had noticed on the previous evening.

The manservant who had created the scene with Mr. Campion not an hour before was at the wheel, and Abbershaw noticed that for a man who had been murderously drunk so recently he was remarkably fresh and efficient.

The car drew up outside the main door of the mansion not ten paces from where they stood, hidden by the greenery. The man got out and opened the door of the car. For some minutes nothing happened, then Gideon appeared followed by Dawlish and Doctor Whitby, bearing between them a heavy burden.

===Chinese===
两人缓缓走回宅子，梅吉安静而心绪不宁，棕色的眼睛微微眯起、若有所思；阿伯肖双手交叠背在身后，垂着脑袋。

他自忖，以他这般年纪的人而论，他与罪行和罪犯打过的交道已算得上多了；可在他以往接触罪案疑云的经历中，从来没有哪一桩要求他又拿主意、又亲自行动。换作从前，总是一桩事情复述给他听、由他来解释，一道难题摆到他面前、由他来解答。而现在，平生头一遭，他得自己挑出问题、再自己作答。他浑身的直觉都在催他干点什么，可究竟该干什么，他自己也不知道。

眼看就要走到通往门厅的那扇包铁厚重的大门前，姑娘突然一声压低的惊呼，使他猛然驻足抬头。说时迟那时快，他已退进宅子旁一丛疯长的月桂树影里，顺势把姑娘也拽了回来。

那辆令人难以置信的老爷车从车库里滑了出来，像一缕青烟般悄然无声——正是阿伯肖前一晚注意到的那辆。

不到一个钟头前还同坎皮恩先生大闹一场的那名男仆正握着方向盘。阿伯肖注意到，对一个不久前还醉得那么凶的人来说，他此刻出奇地神清气爽、干净利落。

汽车停在宅子正门外，离两人藏身的绿荫不到十步。那男人下车，拉开车门。好几分钟毫无动静，随后吉迪恩现身了，道利什和惠特比医生跟在后面，两人抬着一件沉重的担子。

===Original===
They were all fully dressed, and appeared to be in a great hurry. So engrossed were they that not one of them so much as glanced in the direction of the laurel clump which hid the two onlookers. Whitby got into the back of the car and drew the blinds carefully over the windows, then Dawlish and Gideon lifted the long heavy bundle in after him and closed the door upon it.

The great car slid away down the drive, and the two men stepped back noiselessly into the house and disappeared.

The whole incident had taken perhaps three minutes, and it had been accomplished with perfect silence and precision.

Meggie looked up at Abbershaw fearfully.

“What was that?” she said.

The violence of his reply surprised her.

“Damn them!” he said explosively. “The only piece of real evidence there was against them. That was the body of Colonel Coombe.”

===Chinese===
三人都衣冠齐整，看样子十万火急。他们全神贯注，竟没有一个人朝遮着两个旁观者的那丛月桂哪怕瞟上一眼。惠特比钻进汽车后座，仔细拉严车窗上的帘子，随后道利什和吉迪恩把那件又长又沉的包裹抬了进去，随手带上了车门。

大车顺着车道悄然滑走，两个男人悄没声儿地退回宅内，没了踪影。

前后不过三分钟光景，全程完成得悄然无声、干净利落。

梅吉怯怯地仰望着阿伯肖。

“那是什么？”她说。

他答话时的火气让她吃了一惊。

“这帮该死的！”他勃然骂道，“那是唯一一件能指证他们的真凭实据。那是库姆上校的尸体。”

## VIII Open Warfare / 第八章 公开交战

===Original===
Breakfast that morning showed every promise of being a gloomy and uncomfortable meal.

Wyatt had discreetly announced his uncle’s death, and the news had circulated amongst the guests with inevitable speed.

The general opinion was that a tactful farewell and a speedy departure was the obvious procedure of the day. The story of the old man’s last wish had not tended greatly to alter anyone’s decision, as it was clear that no party was likely to be a success, or even bearable in such circumstances. The wishes of the dead seemed more kindly in intention than in fact.

Wyatt seemed very crestfallen, and a great deal of sympathy was felt for him; events could not well have turned out more unfortunately for him. He sat at the end of the table, a little paler than usual, but otherwise the same graceful, courteous scholar as ever. He wore the coloured tie of one of the more obscure Oxford clubs, and had not attempted to show any outward signs of mourning.
===Chinese===
那天早晨的早餐，眼看着就要成为一顿阴郁难挨的饭食。

怀亚特已经婉转地宣布了叔父的死讯，这消息果然以不可避免的速度在宾客之间传了开来。

大家的共同看法是：得体地道个别、尽快启程离开，显然是这一天该走的程序。关于老人临终心愿的那番话，也没能让谁多大程度地改变主意，因为情势明摆着，这种境况下无论什么聚会都不可能尽兴，甚至连勉强熬下去都难。死者的心愿，看来是心意可感、于事无补。

怀亚特显得十分垂头丧气，众人对他满怀同情；事情的走向，对他而言再倒霉不过了。他坐在餐桌末端，脸色比平日苍白几分，除此之外，依旧是那位风度优雅、温文有礼的学者。他系着牛津某个较为冷门的社团的彩色领带，并未刻意在外表上做出任何服丧的表示。

===Original===
Albert Campion, looking none the worse for his nocturnal adventure, sat next to Anne Edgeware. They were talking quietly together, and from the sullen look upon Chris Kennedy’s handsome face it was evident to anybody who cared to see that the irrepressible young lady was indulging in the harmless feminine sport of encouraging one admirer in order to infuriate and thereby gain the interest of another more valued suitor﻿—even though the occasion was so inauspicious. Mr. Campion was amazingly suited to his present role, and in low tones they planned their journey back to town together. Coming departures were indeed a subject for the general conversation of the rather dispirited assembly in the big sunlit hall.

Michael Prenderby was late for breakfast, and he came in, a trifle flushed and hurried, and took his place at the table between little Jeanne Dacre, his fiancée, and Martin Watt, the black-haired beaky youngster whom Meggie had described as “Just a stray young man.” He was, in point of fact, a chartered accountant in his father’s office, a pleasing youth with more brains than energy.

Neither Gideon nor Dawlish had appeared, nor had places been set for them, but the moment that Prenderby sat down and the number of the guests was completed, the door opened and the two men who most interested Abbershaw in the house that day walked into the room.
===Chinese===
阿尔伯特·坎皮恩看不出丝毫夜间历险的疲态，坐在安妮·埃奇韦尔身旁。两人正低声交谈；而克里斯·肯尼迪那张英俊面孔上罩着的一层阴沉，但凡肯留意的人都看得明白：这位安分不下来的年轻小姐正沉溺于一桩无害的女性消遣——抬举一位爱慕者，好激怒另一位她更为看重的追求者，从而赢回他的关注——尽管眼下这个场合如此不宜。坎皮恩先生对当下这个角色胜任得出奇，两人压低嗓音，商量着一同回城的行程。在洒满阳光的大厅里，这群意气消沉的聚会者闲谈的话题，倒也确实尽是即将成行的各奔归途。

迈克尔·普伦德比早餐来迟，他走进屋来，微微涨红着脸，带着几分匆促，在桌边落了座，一侧是他的未婚妻、小巧的让娜·戴克（Jeanne Dacre），另一侧是马丁·沃特（Martin Watt）——那个黑发、鹰钩鼻的年轻人，梅吉曾形容他“只是个东飘西荡、凑数的年轻人”。事实上，他是他父亲事务所里的一名特许会计师，一个讨人喜欢的青年，脑子有余而干劲不足。

吉迪恩和道利什都没有露面，桌上也没有为他们设座；可普伦德比刚一落座、宾客人数刚一凑齐，房门便开了，当天这座宅子里最令阿伯肖感兴趣的两个男人走了进来。

===Original===
Dawlish came first, and in the sunlight his face appeared more unprepossessing than it had seemed on the evening before. For the first time it became apparent what an enormous man he was.

He was fat to the point of grossness, but tall with it, and powerfully built. The shock of long grey hair, brushed straight back from the forehead, hung almost to his shoulders, and the eyes, which seemed to be the only live thing in his face, were bright now and peculiarly arresting.

Gideon, who came in behind him, looked small and insignificant by comparison. He was languid and sinuous as before, and he glanced over the group of young people round the table with a thoughtful, mildly appraising eye, as if he were estimating their combined weight﻿—or strength.
===Chinese===
道利什走在头里。在日光下，他的面容比前一晚显得更加面目可憎。众人也是头一回看清，他是何等魁伟的一条大汉。

他肥胖得近乎臃肿，却又身量高大、体格壮硕。一蓬长长的灰发从额前直直向后梳去，几乎垂到肩头；那双眼睛似乎是他脸上唯一有生气的东西，此刻正亮着，出奇地摄人。

跟在他身后进来的吉迪恩，两相对照之下便显得矮小而不起眼。他还是先前那副懒洋洋、软腻腻的模样，用一种若有所思、温和估量的目光扫视着围桌而坐的这群年轻人，仿佛在掂量他们加在一起的分量——或者说实力。

===Original===
Wyatt looked up as they came in and bade them a polite “Good morning.” To everyone’s surprise they ignored him.

Dawlish moved ponderously to the top of the table, where he stood looking round at the astonished faces, with no expression on his own.

“Let there be silence,” he said.
===Chinese===
两人进来时，怀亚特抬起头，客客气气地道了声“早安”。令所有人吃惊的是，他们竟全然置之不理。

道利什沉重迟缓地走到桌子上首站定，环视着一张张惊愕的面孔，自己脸上却毫无表情。

“肃静。”他说。

===Original===
The words were so utterly unexpected and out of keeping with the situation that it is probable that a certain amount of amusement would have greeted them had not the tone in his deep Teutonic voice been singularly menacing.

As it was, the silence was complete, and the German went on, his expression still unchanged so that it seemed that his voice came to them through a mask.

“Something has been lost,” he said, dividing the words up as he uttered them and giving equal emphasis to each. “It must be returned to me. There is no need to explain what it is. Whoever has stolen it will know of what I speak.”
===Chinese===
这话来得全然出人意料，与眼下的局面毫不相称；倘若不是他那深沉的日耳曼嗓音里透着一股异样的威胁，恐怕多半要招来一阵哄笑。

而此刻，满室鸦雀无声。那德国人继续说了下去，脸上的表情依旧纹丝不动，仿佛他的声音是隔着一副面具传到众人耳中的。

“有一件东西丢了。”他说，一字一顿，每个字都咬得同样用力。“它必须归还给我。没有必要解释那是什么。偷了它的人，自然知道我说的是什么。”

===Original===
At this colossal piece of impudence a sensation ran round the table, and Wyatt sprang to his feet. He was livid with anger, but he kept his voice under perfect control, and the polished intensity of his icy tone contrasted sharply with the other’s heavy rudeness.

“Mr. Dawlish,” he said, “I think your anxiety to recover your property has upset your sense of proportion. Perhaps you are aware that you are a guest in a house that is mine, and that the people that you have just insulted are my guests also. If you will come to me after breakfast﻿—before you go﻿—I will do all I can to institute a proper search for the thing you have mislaid.”

The German did not move. He stood at the head of the table and stared unblinkingly at the man before him.
===Chinese===
这份登峰造极的厚颜无礼令满桌哗然，怀亚特霍然起身。他气得脸色发青，声音却控制得纹丝不乱；那冷冰冰的腔调里打磨出的凌厉，与对方粗鲁的沉重恰成鲜明对照。

“道利什先生，”他说，“我想，您急于寻回财物的这份焦切，已经搅乱了您的分寸感。也许您知道，您是这座宅子里的客人，而这宅子是我的；您方才出言羞辱的那些人，也是我的客人。如果您愿意在早餐之后——在您离开之前——来找我，我将竭尽所能，为您遗失的东西安排一次像样的搜寻。”

德国人一动不动。他站在餐桌头上，一眨不眨地盯着面前的这个人。

===Original===
“Until it is returned to me nobody leaves this house,” he said, the same solid force behind his tone. Wyatt’s snub he did not appear to have heard. A faint wave of colour passed over the young man’s pale face, and he turned to the others, who were staring from one to the other in frank astonishment.

“I must apologize,” he said. “I ask you to forgive this extraordinary display. My uncle’s death appears to have turned this unfortunate man’s brain.”

Dawlish turned.
===Chinese===
“在它归还给我之前，谁也不许离开这座宅子。”他说道，语气背后是同样沉实的一股力道。怀亚特碰的这个钉子，他似乎压根儿没有听见。一丝淡淡的红晕掠过年轻人苍白的脸，他转向其余众人——他们正毫不掩饰地惊愕地面面相觑。

“我必须向大家道歉，”他说。“请各位原谅这番离奇的表演。看来是我叔父之死，把这个可怜人的脑子搅乱了。”

道利什转过身来。

===Original===
“That young man,” he said. “Let him sit down and be quiet.”

Gideon smiled at Wyatt, and the look on his grey decadent face was an insult in itself.

“My dear Mr. Petrie,” he said, and his peculiarly oily voice was suave and ingratiating, “I don’t think you quite realize the position you are in, you and your friends. Consider: this house is two miles from the public road. There is no telephone. We have two women servants and six men and a gatekeeper. All of these people are in Mr. Dawlish’s employ. Your cars have been drained of petrol. I am afraid you are entirely helpless.” He paused, and allowed his glance to take in the amazed expressions round the table.

“It would be better,” he continued, “to listen rationally, for I must warn you, my friend Mr. Dawlish is not a man who is accustomed to any opposition to his wishes.”
===Chinese===
“那个年轻人，”他说。“让他坐下，安静些。”

吉迪恩朝怀亚特笑了笑，他那张灰败颓唐的脸上的那副神情，本身就是一种侮辱。

“亲爱的皮特里先生，”他开了口，那口格外油滑的嗓音温文讨好，“我看，您还不甚明白您和您的朋友们眼下的处境。想一想吧：这座宅子离大路有两英里。没有电话。我们有两名女仆、六名男仆，外加一个看门人。这些人统统受雇于道利什先生。你们的汽车，汽油都已放空。恐怕诸位是全然无能为力了。”他顿了顿，任自己的目光扫过满桌惊愕的神情。

“诸位还是理性地听下去为好，”他接着说，“因为我必须提醒各位，我这位朋友道利什先生，可不是个惯于容忍有人违逆其意愿的人。”

===Original===
Wyatt remained on his feet; his face had grown slowly paler, and he was now rigid with barely controlled fury.

“Gentlemen, this farce has gone on long enough,” he said, in a voice which quivered in spite of himself. “If you will please go away we will get on with our breakfast.”

“Sit down!”
===Chinese===
怀亚特依然站着；他的脸渐渐愈发苍白，此刻已因勉强按捺的狂怒而浑身僵直。

“两位先生，这出闹剧闹得够久了，”他说，那嗓音不由自主地发着颤。“如果你们肯请便走开，我们还要继续用早餐。”

“坐下！”

===Original===
The words were uttered in a sudden titanic bellow, though but for the obvious fact that Gideon was incapable of producing so much noise there was nothing upon Benjamin Dawlish’s face to betray that it was he who had shouted.

Wyatt started; the limit of his patience had come. He opened his mouth to speak, to assert his authority. Then, quite suddenly, he dropped back into his chair, his eyes dilating with as much surprise as fear. He was looking into the black barrel of a revolver.

The German stood stolidly, absolutely immobile, the dangerous little weapon levelled in one ponderous hand. “Here,” he said in his unwieldy English, “there is one who has what I seek. To him I speak. When he returns to me what he has taken you shall all go free. Until then no one leaves this house﻿—no one at all.”
===Chinese===
这一声怒吼骤然而出，直如雷霆炸响；若不是明摆着吉迪恩发不出这般巨响，本杰明·道利什的脸上没有任何迹象表明，喊出这一声的正是他本人。

怀亚特一惊；他的耐性已到极限。他张开嘴正要说话，要申明自己的权威。随后，冷不防地，他跌坐回椅子里，双眼圆睁，惊惧交集。他正望进一只左轮手枪黑洞洞的枪管。

德国人木然而立，纹丝不动，那件危险的小武器端平在他一只沉重的大手里。“听着，”他用他那笨拙费力的英语说道，“这里有一个拿了我所寻之物的人。我是对他说话。等他把拿走的东西还给我，你们大家就都可以走了。在那之前，谁也不许离开这座宅子——一个都不许。”

===Original===
In the silence which followed this extraordinary announcement Jesse Gideon moved forward.

“If Mr. Dawlish were to receive his property immediately it would save us all a great deal of inconvenience,” he murmured.

For several seconds there was no movement in the room, and the singing of the birds in the greenery outside the windows became suddenly very noticeable.
===Chinese===
这番离奇宣告之后的沉寂里，杰西·吉迪恩向前挪了一步。

“倘若道利什先生能立即收回他的财物，我们大家都能省去许多不便。”他喃喃道。

有好几秒钟，屋里纹丝不动，窗外绿荫间的鸟啼莺啭骤然显得格外分明。

===Original===
Then Albert Campion coughed discreetly and handed something wrapped up in his table napkin to the girl who sat next him.

She passed it to her neighbour, and in utter stillness it went the whole length of the table until Gideon pounced on it avidly and set it before the German on the table. With a grunt of satisfaction the big man thrust the revolver into his coat pocket and threw aside the white napery. Then an exclamation of anger escaped him, and he drew back so that Mr. Campion’s offering lay exposed.

It was a breakfast egg, the very one, in fact, which the fatuous young man had been on the verge of broaching when the extraordinary interruptions had occurred.
===Chinese===
就在这时，阿尔伯特·坎皮恩审慎地咳了一声，把一样用餐巾裹着的东西递给了坐在他身旁的姑娘。

她又递给她的邻座；在一片全然的死寂中，那东西顺着整张餐桌一路传了下去，直到吉迪恩急切地一把攫住，将它摆到桌上德国人的面前。大汉满意地哼了一声，把左轮手枪塞进外套口袋，一把掀开那方白色餐巾。随即一声怒叱从他口中迸出，他向后一让，好让坎皮恩先生的这份“贡品”一览无余地摊露出来。

那是一枚早餐鸡蛋——事实上，恰恰是那接二连三的离奇变故打断之前，这个蠢头蠢脑的年轻人正要敲开的那一枚。

===Original===
The effect was instantaneous; the reaction from the silent tension of a moment before complete.

The entire table shook with laughter.

The German stood stiffly as before. There was still no expression of any sort upon his face, and his little eyes became dull and lifeless.
===Chinese===
效果立竿见影；片刻之前那无声的紧张，就此消解得干干净净。

满桌的人笑得直颤。

德国人像先前一样僵立着。他脸上依旧看不出任何表情，一双小眼睛变得黯淡呆滞。

===Original===
Gideon, on the other hand, betrayed his anger vividly. His eyes were narrowed with fury and his long thin lips were drawn back over his teeth like an angry dog’s. Gradually the laughter subsided. Benjamin Dawlish’s personality was one that could not be ignored for long. When at last there was perfect silence in the room he put his hand in his pocket and drew out his revolver again.

“You laugh,” he said heavily. “I do not laugh. And she, the little one,” he tossed the gun in his hand with incredible delicacy for one who looked so clumsy, “*she does not laugh either*.”

The last words were uttered with such amazing ferocity that his hearers started involuntarily, and for an instant there appeared upon the heavy face, which hitherto had seemed immovable, an expression of such animalic violence that not one at that table looked him in the eyes.
===Chinese===
吉迪恩则不然，他的怒火显露无遗。他双眼气得眯起，又长又薄的嘴唇朝后绷开、露出牙齿，活像一条发怒的狗。笑声渐渐平息下去——本杰明·道利什这个人的存在，是不容人长久无视的。待屋里终于重归完全的寂静，他把手伸进口袋，又掏出了左轮手枪。

“你们笑，”他沉甸甸地说。“我不笑。还有她，那个小的，”他把手枪在手中抛了抛，那手法之轻盈，与他那副笨相相比简直不可思议，“*她也不笑*。”

末了几个字出口时带着那般惊人的凶戾，听的人无不不由自主地一悚；刹那之间，那张此前仿佛纹丝不动的沉重面孔上，浮现出一种兽性般的暴戾，满桌竟无一人敢与他对视。

===Original===
A moment later his features had relapsed into their usual stolidity, and followed by Jesse Gideon he walked slowly from the room.

As the door closed behind them, the silence became painful, and at last a fitful, uneasy conversation broke out.

“What an unpleasant old bird!” said Prenderby, looking at Abbershaw. He spoke lightly, but there was a worried expression in his eyes; one hand rested over his fiancée’s, who sat very pale by his side apparently on the verge of tears. Even Anne Edgeware’s magnificent sangfroid seemed a little shaken, and Meggie, although the least alarmed of the three girls, looked very white.
===Chinese===
片刻之后，他的五官又恢复了一贯的木然；杰西·吉迪恩跟在他身后，他缓缓地走出了房间。

他们身后的房门刚一合上，沉默便变得令人难堪；好一阵之后，一阵断断续续、忐忑不安的交谈才算起来了。

“好一个讨厌的老怪物！”普伦德比望着阿伯肖说道。他话说得轻快，眼里却带着忧色；他一只手覆在未婚妻的手上——她坐在他身旁，脸色煞白，眼看就要落泪。连安妮·埃奇韦尔那份了不起的沉着冷静似乎也动摇了几分；三个姑娘里虽数梅吉最处变不惊，脸色也白得很。

===Original===
Wyatt was still angry. He gave up trying to apologize for the incident, however, and joined with the others in discussing it.

“He’s loony, of course,” said Martin Watt lazily. “Campion got his goat beautifully, I thought.”

“Still, even if he is potty, if what he says is true, things are going to be pretty sportive,” remarked Chris Kennedy cheerfully. “I fear I may be called upon to bash his head in.”
===Chinese===
怀亚特余怒未消。不过，他已不再为方才的事道歉，而是加入众人一起讨论起来。

“他当然是疯了，”马丁·沃特懒洋洋地说。“依我看，坎皮恩把他气得漂亮极了。”

“不过，就算他脑子有毛病，倘若他说的是真的，往后的事可就有趣了，”克里斯·肯尼迪兴冲冲地说，“我怕免不了要轮到我去敲碎他的脑袋。”

===Original===
Abbershaw rose to his feet.

“I don’t know what you think, Wyatt,” he said, “but it occurs to me that it might be an idea if we all went into the other room and talked this thing over. The servants won’t disturb us there. I don’t think there’s any real danger,” he went on reassuringly, “but perhaps we ought to find out if what Gideon says about the cars is true.”

Chris Kennedy got up eagerly.

“I’ll toddle down and discover, shall I?” he said. “Really﻿—I should like to,” he added, as Wyatt regarded him doubtfully, and he went off whistling.
===Chinese===
阿伯肖站起身来。

“我不知道你是怎么想的，怀亚特，”他说，“不过我想到一个主意：我们不妨全体到隔壁房间去，把这件事商量个明白。仆人们不会到那边打扰我们。我看并没有什么真正的危险，”他接着宽慰众人道，“不过，吉迪恩关于汽车说的那番话是真是假，我们或许该弄个清楚。”

克里斯·肯尼迪迫不及待地起了身。

“我溜达下去探个究竟，好不好？”他说。见怀亚特将信将疑地望着他，他又补了一句：“真的——我很乐意去。”说罢便吹着口哨走了。

===Original===
The party adjourned to the next room as Abbershaw had suggested. They still talked lightly, but there was a distinctly constrained atmosphere amongst them. Jeanne was frankly scared, Anne Edgeware out of her depth, and the rest apprehensive.

Abbershaw was the last to step into the enormous hall that was now a blaze of sunlight. It poured in through long diamond-paned windows, glinted on the polished floor, and shone softly on Tudor rose and linenfold. But it was not these which caught his eye and made him start back with a half-concealed exclamation.

Over the far fireplace, set in the circle of lance-heads, its clear blade dazzling in the sun and gleaming as brightly as if it had never left its plaque, sinister and beautiful, was the Black Dudley Dagger.
===Chinese===
众人依着阿伯肖的提议，移步到了隔壁房间。他们言谈依旧轻快，彼此之间却分明罩着一层拘束。让娜明摆着吓坏了，安妮·埃奇韦尔全然没了主意，其余的人也个个心怀忐忑。

阿伯肖最后一个踏进那座此刻已是一片灿烂阳光的宏大门厅。阳光从长长的菱格窗里倾泻而入，在光洁可鉴的地板上熠熠生辉，又柔柔地映亮都铎玫瑰雕饰与亚麻折纹壁板。然而，攫住他的目光、令他半掩着一声惊呼倒退半步的，却不是这些。

在离门最远的那座壁炉上方，长矛矛头围成的圆圈中央，那柄布莱克·达德利匕首安然在焉，明净的刀刃在太阳下炫目生辉，亮得仿佛从未离开过它的深红饰板——阴森，而美丽。

## IX Chris Kennedy Scores a Try Only / 第九章 克里斯·肯尼迪仅得一次达阵

===Original===
As soon as Abbershaw had recovered from his first surprise, he turned to Meggie. She was standing just beside him, the others having split up into little groups talking quietly together. “Did you come in here this morning,” he said, “after we came in from the garden?”

She nodded, and he saw that she was trembling slightly. “Yes,” she whispered, “and—and *it* was here then, hanging just where it is now. I—I couldn’t help coming in to see. Someone must have put it back—in the night.”

Her voice died away in a sob on the last word, and he laid a hand on her arm.

===Chinese===
阿伯肖从最初的惊愕中一缓过神来，便转向梅吉。她正站在他身旁，其余的人已经三三两两分成小堆，低声交谈着。“今天早上你进过这间屋子吗？”他说，“就是我们从花园里回来之后？”

她点了点头，他看出她正微微发抖。“进来过，”她低声道，“而且——而且它那会儿就在这儿，就挂在它如今挂的地方。我——我忍不住要进来看一眼。准是有人把它放了回去——在夜里。”

话到最后一个字，她的声音化作一声呜咽消散了，他伸手搭在她的臂上。

===Original===
“Scared?” he said.

She met his eyes bravely.

“I’m glad you’re here too,” she said simply.

===Chinese===
“害怕了？”他说。

她勇敢地迎住他的目光。

“有你也在这儿，我很高兴。”她坦然地说。

===Original===
A wave of pleasure swept over Abbershaw, and he coloured, but he did not speak. The gravity of the situation was by no means lost to him. He was the eldest of the party, and, moreover, he knew more about the events of the last twelve hours than probably anyone else in the room.

Something told him to keep quiet about his discoveries, however; he realized that they were up against dangerous men. Mr. Benjamin Dawlish, as he styled himself, was no ordinary individual, and, at the moment, he was angry.

The main idea now was to get away at all costs; Abbershaw was sure of it.

===Chinese===
一阵喜悦的暖流席卷过阿伯肖，他涨红了脸，却没有作声。他绝非没有掂出眼下处境的分量。他是这伙人里年纪最长的，况且，对于过去十二个小时里发生的种种，他所知道的恐怕比屋里任何人都要多。

然而，某种直觉叮嘱他，对自己发现的东西要守口如瓶；他意识到他们对抗的是一伙危险人物。那位自称本杰明·道利什先生的，绝非寻常之辈，而且此时此刻，他正在火头上。

眼下的头等大事，是不惜一切代价脱身；这一点阿伯肖确信无疑。

===Original===
He had not dreamed that the late Colonel’s extraordinary friends would dare to take this extreme course, but since they had done so, he was not fool enough to think that they would risk the possibility of being overpowered; their forces must be very strong.

Once out of the house he himself could get an immediate inquiry instituted by the highest authorities. If the police could be informed without their captors’ knowledge, so much the better, but the principal problem was escape, and that, in the present circumstances, did not appear to be any too simple.

There was, of course, one way of obtaining freedom; he felt the battered red wallet in his pocket now, but he was loth to take that path, for it meant the escape of what he felt certain was a leader of one of the most skilful criminal organizations in the world. So far he had been working in the dark, and if he gave in now, that darkness would never be lightened. It would mean complete surrender. The mystery would remain a mystery.

===Chinese===
他做梦也没想到，已故上校那批非同寻常的朋友竟敢使出这一手极端的招数；可他们既然干了，他也绝不傻到以为他们会甘冒被人制伏的风险——他们的人手必定十分充足。

一旦出了这座宅子，他本人便能促成最高当局立即立案调查。倘若能在扣押他们的这伙人毫不知情的情形下通知警方，那自然再好不过；可首要的问题仍是逃脱，而在眼下这种局面下，这似乎绝不是一件轻而易举的事。

当然，获取自由的路子倒是有一条；此刻他就隔着衣袋摸得到那只磨损破旧的红皮夹，可他不情愿走这条路，因为那意味着放走一个人——他确信此人乃是世界上最老练的犯罪组织之一的头目。迄今为止，他一直在黑暗中摸索，倘若此刻服软，那片黑暗便永无照亮之日。那将意味着彻底投降。谜团将永远是谜团。

===Original===
He glanced down at Meggie.

“We’ll lick ’em yet,” he said.

She laughed at him.

“Or die in the attempt.”

Abbershaw appeared vastly relieved.

“That’s how I feel,” he said.

===Chinese===
他低头瞥了一眼梅吉。

“咱们迟早能收拾了他们。”他说。

她冲他笑了。

“要不然，就死在这上头。”

阿伯肖显出一副大大宽慰的样子。

“我要的就是这股劲儿。”他说。

===Original===
It was at this moment that Mr. Campion made the entire party one group again by a single fatuous remark.

“Of course,” he said affably, “I suppose nobody has pinched anything.”

“I’ve got two bits of soap in my room,” murmured Prenderby, “but I shouldn’t think that’s what the old bird’s after by the look of him. And look here, Wyatt,” he added suddenly, “there’s something damned queer about something else! I suppose you know—”

===Chinese===
正是在这个当口，坎皮恩先生凭一句蠢话，让全体宾客重又聚成了一堆。

“当然啦，”他一团和气地说，“我猜总不至于有人顺手牵羊，拿走了什么东西吧。”

“我房间里倒是有两块肥皂，”普伦德比咕哝道，“可照那位老家伙的模样看，他要找的八成不是这个。还有，我说，怀亚特，”他忽然加了一句，“另外有桩事儿邪门得很！我想你是知道的——”

===Original===
Abbershaw interposed hastily.

“The whole thing is a bit queer, Michael,” he said, fixing the boy with his eyes. Prenderby took the hint, and was silent, but Wyatt turned to him.

“I’m beyond apologizing,” he said. “The whole business is quite out of my experience. My uncle asked me to bring a party down for this weekend. He had often done so before. I have met Gideon here before, but never exchanged more than half a dozen words with him. As for that Hun, Dawlish, he’s a complete stranger.”

===Chinese===
阿伯肖急忙插了进来。

“整件事都有点蹊跷，迈克尔。”他说，目光定定地盯住这小伙子。普伦德比会了意，不再作声，怀亚特却朝他转过身来。

“如今道歉也无济于事了，”他说。“这整桩事全然超出了我的阅历。是我叔父要我邀一伙人来度这个周末的。他以前也常这么办。吉迪恩我从前在这宅子里见过，可同他统共没说上六句话。至于那个德国佬道利什，完完全全是个陌生人。”

===Original===
Prenderby, to whom the words had sounded like a reproach, coloured, and what might have been an uncomfortable pause was covered by the sudden return of Chris Kennedy. He was in high good humour. His handsome young face was flushed with excitement, and the others could not banish the suspicion that he was enjoying the situation thoroughly.

“They *have*, the blighters!” he said, bursting into the group. “Not a drain of juice in any of the buses. Otherwise they’re all right, though. ‘Exhibit A’ has vanished, by the way—crumbled into dust, I should think—but apart from that they’re all there.”

“Meet anyone?” said Martin.

===Chinese===
这话听在普伦德比耳中像是责备，他红了脸；而这段本可能尴尬的冷场，被克里斯·肯尼迪的突然归来遮掩了过去。他情绪高涨得很。那张英俊的年轻面孔兴奋得通红，众人怎么也挥不去一个疑心：他实在是把这场面从头到尾享受了个透。

“他们还真拿了，这帮混蛋！”他一头闯进人堆里说道。“那几辆车，一滴油都不剩。不过其他倒都完好。对了，‘证物甲’不见了——照我看，八成是化成灰了——可除了它，其余的全在。”

“碰见什么人了吗？”马丁问。

===Original===
“Not a soul,” said Kennedy cheerfully, “and little Christopher Robin has an idea. If I asked you for a drink, Petrie, would you give me ginger-beer?” There was an air of suppressed jubilation in his tone as he spoke.

“My dear fellow …” Wyatt started forward. “I think you’ll find all you want here,” he said, and led the way to a cupboard set in the panelling of the fireplace. Kennedy stuck his head in it, and came out flushed and triumphant. “Two Scotch and a ‘Three Star’ Brandy,” he said, tucking the bottles under his arm. “It’s blasphemy, but there’s no other way. Get to the window, chicks, and Uncle Christopher will now produce the rabbit.”

“What are you going to do with that stuff?” said Watt, who was not an admirer of the athletic type. “Fill yourself up with it and run amok?”

===Chinese===
“一个鬼影也没碰见，”肯尼迪兴冲冲地说，“小克里斯托弗·罗宾有个主意。皮特里，要是我管你讨杯喝的，你能给我姜汁汽水吗？”说话时，他的语气里透着一股强自按捺的欢腾。

“我亲爱的伙计……”怀亚特抢步上前。“我想你要的东西这儿都能找到，”他说着，领大家走到壁炉护壁板里嵌着的一只橱柜前。肯尼迪把脑袋探了进去，出来时满面通红，得意洋洋。“两瓶苏格兰威士忌，一瓶‘三星’白兰地，”他说着把酒瓶往腋下一夹。“这是亵渎，可没别的法子。都到窗口去，小家伙们，克里斯托弗叔叔现在要把兔子变出来了。”

“你拿这些东西干什么？”沃特说道——他向来不欣赏运动健将那一型。“灌进自己肚子里，然后撒起酒疯来？”

===Original===
Kennedy grinned at him over his shoulder; he was already half out of the room.

“No fear!” he said, pausing with his hand on the door-handle. “But the Salmson is. Watch the garage. Keep your eyes upon the performance, ladies and gentlemen. This trick cannot be repeated.”

The somewhat bewildered little group regarded him doubtfully.

===Chinese===
肯尼迪回头冲他咧嘴一笑；他人已经半个身子出了房门。

“才不呢！”他说，手搭在门把上停住脚。“要灌的是萨尔姆森。盯住车库。诸位女士先生，请把眼睛盯牢这场演出。本戏法概不重演。”

这群多少被弄糊涂了的人疑疑惑惑地望着他。

===Original===
“I’m afraid I don’t follow you even now,” said Martin, still coldly. “I’m probably infernally thick, but I don’t get your drift.”

Michael Prenderby suddenly lifted his head.

“Good Lord!” he said. “I do believe you might do it. What a stunt!”

“That’s what I thought,” said Kennedy.

He went out, and they heard him racing down the corridor.

===Chinese===
“恐怕我到现在也没听明白，”马丁依旧冷冷地说，“八成是我蠢得没救，可我实在没摸着你的用意。”

迈克尔·普伦德比猛地抬起了头。

“天哪！”他说。“我真信你干得成了。多妙的一手！”

“我也是这么想的。”肯尼迪说。

他出了门，众人听见他一路飞奔跑过走廊。

===Original===
Abbershaw turned to Michael.

“What’s the idea?” he said.

Prenderby grinned.

“He’s going to use the booze as juice,” he said. “Rather an idea, don’t you think? A car like that ought to run on pure spirit, I suppose. Let’s watch him.”

He led the way to the windows and the others followed him. By craning their necks they could just see the doors of the barn, both of which stood open.

===Chinese===
阿伯肖转向迈克尔。

“他打的什么主意？”他说。

普伦德比咧嘴一笑。

“他打算拿酒当油使，”他说，“挺妙的主意，是不是？那样的车，我想烧得动纯酒精。走，咱们瞧瞧去。”

他领头走向窗口，其余人跟了上去。伸长脖颈，他们刚好望得见那座谷仓的两扇门——两扇都敞开着。

===Original===
For some minutes nothing happened, and Martin Watt was just beginning to assure himself that his first impression of Kennedy’s ideas in general was going to be justified when a terrific backfire sounded from the garage.

“Good heavens!” said Abbershaw. “He’s going to do it.”

Someone began to laugh.

“What a pack of fools they’ll look,” said Prenderby.

Another small explosion sounded from the garage, and the next moment the little car appeared in a cloud of blue smoke, with Mr. Kennedy at the wheel. It was moving slowly but triumphantly, and emitting a stream of backfires like a machine-gun.

===Chinese===
好几分钟里毫无动静。马丁·沃特正开始确信，自己对肯尼迪种种主意的一贯看法眼看就要得到印证，车库那边骤然爆出一声惊天动地的回火。

“天哪！”阿伯肖说。“他真要干成了。”

有人笑出了声。

“他们那帮家伙可要出大洋相了。”普伦德比说。

车库那边又响起一声小小的爆响，紧接着，那辆小车裹在一团蓝烟里现了身，肯尼迪先生坐在方向盘后。它开得缓慢却神气十足，一路喷出一串机关枪似的连珠回火。

===Original===
“Isn’t he marvellous?”

Anne Edgeware clasped her hands as she spoke, and even Martin Watt admitted grudgingly that “the lad had initiative.” Kennedy waved to them, and they saw his face flushed and excited as a child’s. As he changed gear the car jerked forward and set off down the drive at an uneven but ever-increasing pace.

“That’ll show ’em,” said Prenderby with a chuckle.

“They haven’t even tried to stop him,” said little Jeanne Dacre.

At that moment Mr. Kennedy changed into top gear with a roar, and immediately there was a sharp report, followed by a second, which seemed to come from a window above their heads. Instantly, even as they watched it, the Salmson swerved violently, skidded drunkenly across the drive and turned over, pitching its occupant out upon the grass beside the path.

===Chinese===
“他是不是妙极了？”

安妮·埃奇韦尔说话时双手紧紧交握，连马丁·沃特也老大不情愿地承认“这小子有股闯劲”。肯尼迪朝他们挥手，众人看见他的脸兴奋得通红，活像个孩子。他一换挡，车子便猛地向前一蹿，顺着车道驶了出去，速度虽不平稳，却越跑越快。

“这可够他们瞧的了。”普伦德比轻笑道。

“他们甚至都没试着拦他。”小让娜·戴克（Jeanne Dacre）说。

就在这时，肯尼迪先生一声轰鸣挂上最高挡，紧接着便是一声尖锐的爆响，随后又是第二声，似乎来自他们头顶上方的某扇窗子。众目睽睽之下，那辆萨尔姆森霎时猛地一拐，醉汉似的打着滑冲过车道，翻了个身，把车里那个人甩到了小路旁的草地上。

===Original===
“Good God!”

Michael Prenderby’s voice was hoarse in the silence.

Martin Watt spoke quickly.

“Dawlish’s gun. They’ve got him. The Hun was in earnest. Come on, you fellows.”

He thrust open the window and leapt out upon the lawn, the men following him.

===Chinese===
“天哪！”

寂静中，迈克尔·普伦德比的嗓音喑哑。

马丁·沃特急急开了口。

“道利什的枪。他们打中他了。那德国佬是动真格的。走吧，伙计们。”

他推开窗子，纵身跳上草坪，男人们紧随其后。

===Original===
Chris Kennedy was already picking himself up when they reached him. He was very white, and his left hand grasped his other wrist, from which the blood was streaming.

“They got my nearside front wheel and my driving arm,” he gasped, as they came up. “There’s a bloke somewhere about who can shoot like hell.”

He swayed a little on the last word, and smiled valiantly. “Do you mind if we get in?” he murmured. “This thing is turning me sick.”

They got him back to the house and into the room where they had all been standing. As they crossed the lawn, Abbershaw, glancing up at the second-floor windows, fancied he saw a heavy expressionless face peering out at them from behind the dark curtains.

===Chinese===
众人赶到时，克里斯·肯尼迪已经正从地上往起爬。他脸色惨白，左手死死攥着另一只手腕，血正从腕间汩汩涌出。

“他们打中了我左侧的前轮，还有我握方向盘的胳膊，”众人围拢上来时，他喘着气说。“这宅子里不知哪个角落藏着个家伙，枪法邪门得很。”

说到最后几个字，他身子晃了晃，又硬撑着笑了笑。“咱们这就进屋去，不碍事吧？”他低声道。“这玩意儿搞得我直想吐。”

众人把他扶回宅里，送进他们方才一直待着的那间屋子。穿过草坪时，阿伯肖抬眼瞟了瞟二楼的窗子，恍惚看见一张阴沉沉、毫无表情的脸，正从深色窗帘后面窥探他们。

===Original===
The rescue party was considerably subdued. They were beginning to believe in the sincerity of Mr. Benjamin Dawlish’s remarks.

Kennedy collapsed into a chair, and, after saving him from the tender ministrations of Anne Edgeware, Abbershaw was just about to set out in search of warm water and a dress shirt to tear up as a bandage, when there was a discreet tap on the door and a manservant entered bearing a complete surgical outfit together with antiseptic bandages and hot water.

“With Mr. Gideon’s compliments,” he said gravely, and went out.

Kennedy smiled weakly.

“Curse their dirty politeness,” he said, and bowed his head over his injured wrist.

===Chinese===
这支救援的队伍大为蔫颓。他们开始相信，本杰明·道利什先生那番话是字字当真的。

肯尼迪一屁股瘫进椅子里。阿伯肖把他从安妮·埃奇韦尔那双殷勤的巧手底下解救出来之后，正要动身去找温水和一件可以撕作绷带的礼服衬衫，门外响起轻轻一声叩门，一个男仆走了进来，捧着一整套外科器械，外加消毒绷带和热水。

“吉迪恩先生谨致问候。”他一本正经地说完，便退了出去。

肯尼迪虚弱地笑了笑。

“该死的，他们这份殷勤可真够龌龊的。”他说着，垂下头，俯在自己受伤的手腕上。

===Original===
Abbershaw removed his coat and went over to the tray which the man had brought.

“Hullo!” he said. “There’s a note. Read it, Wyatt, will you, while I get on with this. These are Whitby’s things, I suppose. It almost looks as if he was expecting trouble.”

Wyatt took the slip of paper off the tray and read the message aloud in his clear even voice.

> “We are not joking. No one leaves this house until we have what we want.”

“There’s no signature,” he added, and handed the note to Prenderby, who looked at it curiously.

“Looks as if they *have* lost something,” he said. “What the devil is it? We can’t help ’em much till we know what it is.”

No one spoke for a moment.

===Chinese===
阿伯肖脱下外套，走到男仆端来的托盘跟前。

“嚯！”他说。“这儿有张字条。念一念吧，怀亚特，劳驾，我先把手上的事忙完。这些是惠特比的物件，我猜是。瞧这架势，他倒像是一早就防着出乱子。”

怀亚特从托盘上拿起那张纸片，用他那清朗平稳的嗓音，把上面的话大声念了出来。

> “我们不是在开玩笑。在拿到我们想要的东西之前，任何人不得离开这座宅子。”

“没有署名。”他补了一句，把字条递给普伦德比，后者好奇地端详着它。

“看来他们还真是丢了什么东西，”他说，“到底是个什么玩意儿？不知道那是什么，咱们想帮也帮不上多少忙。”

一时无人作声。

===Original===
“Yes, that’s true,” said Martin Watt at last, “and the only thing we know about it is that it isn’t an egg.”

There was a faint titter of laughter at this, but it soon died down; the party was beginning to realize the seriousness of their position.

“It must be something pretty fishy, anyway,” said Chris Kennedy, still white with the pain of his wound which Abbershaw was now bandaging. “Else why don’t they describe it so that we can all have a hunt round? Look here, let’s go to them and tell them that we don’t know what their infernal property is. They can search us if they like, and when they find we haven’t got it they can let us go, and by God, when they do I’ll raise hell!”

“It is precisely for that reason that I’m not inclined to endorse that suggestion, Kennedy,” said Abbershaw without looking up from the bandage he was winding. “Our friends upstairs are very determined, and they’re not likely to risk a possible visit from the police before they have got what they want and have had reasonable time to make a good getaway.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，这话不假，”马丁·沃特终于开了口，“而咱们对那东西唯一摸得清的一点，就是它不是一只鸡蛋。”

这话激起一阵低低的窃笑，但很快便平息了；这伙人开始意识到自身处境的严重。

“不管怎么说，那东西八成见不得人，”克里斯·肯尼迪说——阿伯肖正在给他包扎伤口，他仍旧疼得面无血色。“不然他们为什么不把那东西描画一番，好让大伙儿都帮着搜寻？我说，咱们干脆去找他们，告诉他们，我们不知道他们那件劳什子东西是什么。他们乐意搜身就搜，等发现咱们没拿着，就得放人——老天在上，等他们真放了，我非闹他个天翻地覆不可！”

“恰恰因为这个缘故，我不太赞成这个提议，肯尼迪，”阿伯肖说，眼睛没有离开手里正在缠绕的绷带。“咱们楼上那几位朋友决心极大，不等到手他们要的东西、并留出充裕的时间从容脱身，他们绝不肯冒那种可能招来警察的风险。”

===Original===
Martin Watt raised his hand.

“One moment,” he said, “let us do a spot of neat detective work. What the German gentleman with no manners has lost must be very small. ‘And why, my dear Sherlock?’ you ask. Because, my little Watsons, when our obliging young comrade, Campion, offered them an egg wrapped up in a table napkin they thought they’d holed in one. It isn’t the Black Dudley diamonds, I suppose, Petrie?”

“There aren’t any,” said Wyatt shortly. “Damn it all!” he burst out with a sudden violence. “I never felt so helpless in my life.”

===Chinese===
马丁·沃特举起手来。

“且慢，”他说，“让咱们来干一小段漂亮的侦探活儿。那位没有礼貌的德国绅士丢失的东西，必定非常之小。‘这是为什么呢，我亲爱的夏洛克？’你们要问了。因为啊，我诸位小小的华生，咱们那位殷勤热心的年轻同志坎皮恩奉上一只用餐巾裹好的鸡蛋时，他们还当自己一杆进洞了呢。总不见得是布莱克·达德利的钻石吧，皮特里？”

“这儿压根儿就没有什么钻石。”怀亚特简短地说。“真是见鬼！”他陡然暴烈地发作起来。“我这辈子从没这么束手无策过。”

===Original===
“If only we had a few guns,” mourned Chris Kennedy, whose wound even had not slaked his thirst for a scrap. “Then we might make an attempt to rush ’em. But unarmed against birds who shoot like that we shouldn’t have an earthly.”

“It’s not such a bad thing for you that we’re not armed, my lad,” said Abbershaw, straightening his shoulders and stepping back from the table. “You don’t want too much excitement with an arm like that. You’ve lost enough blood already. If I were you, I’d try and get a spot of sleep. What’s your opinion, Prenderby?”

“Oh, sleep, by all means,” said Michael, grinning, “if he can get it, which doesn’t seem likely.”

They were all standing round the patient on the hearthrug, with their backs to the fireplace, and for the moment Kennedy was the centre of interest.

===Chinese===
“要是咱们有几支枪就好了，”克里斯·肯尼迪哀叹道——就连这处伤也没能浇熄他干一架的瘾。“那咱们还能试着硬冲他们一家伙。可赤手空拳对上枪法那么准的家伙，咱们连半点胜算都没有。”

“咱们没带枪，对你来说倒不算坏事，小伙子，”阿伯肖说着挺直肩膀，从桌边退开一步。“带着那样一条胳膊，你可经不起再折腾了。你的血已经流得够多了。换了我是你，就想法子睡上一小觉。你看呢，普伦德比？”

“哦，睡觉，务必睡觉，”迈克尔咧嘴笑道，“只要他睡得着——瞧这架势，是不大像了。”

众人都背对壁炉，围着炉前地毯上的伤员站成一圈，一时间肯尼迪成了全场瞩目的人物。

===Original===
Hardly were the words out of Prenderby’s mouth when they were suddenly and startlingly confirmed by an hysterical scream from Anne Edgeware.

“He’s gone!” she said wildly, as they turned to her. Her dark eyes were dilated with fear, and every trace of her usual sophisticated and slightly blasé manner had disappeared.

“He was standing here—just beside me. He spoke to me a second ago. He couldn’t have got past me to the door—I was directly in his way. He’s just vanished. Oh, God—I’m going potty! I think—I …” She screamed again.

===Chinese===
话音未落，普伦德比这句话便被安妮·埃奇韦尔一声歇斯底里的尖叫蓦地、骇人地应验了。

“他不见了！”众人转向她时，她狂乱地喊道。她那双黑眼睛吓得睁得溜圆，平素那副世故而略带倦怠的做派荡然无存。

“他方才就站在这儿——就在我身边。一秒钟前还同我说话来着。他不可能绕过我到门口去——我正正好好挡着他的道。他就是凭空没了。哦，上帝——我要疯了！我想——我想……”她又尖叫起来。

===Original===
“My dear girl!”

Abbershaw moved to her side. “What’s the matter? Who’s vanished?”

The girl looked at him in stupid amazement. “He went from my side just as if he had disappeared into the air,” she repeated. “I was just talking to him—I turned away to look at Chris for a moment—I heard a sort of thud, and when I turned round he’d gone.”

She began to cry noisily.

===Chinese===
“亲爱的姑娘！”

阿伯肖走到她身边。“怎么了？谁不见了？”

姑娘呆呆地、一脸惊愕地望着他。“他就从我身边没了，就跟化进空气里去了似的，”她翻来覆去地说，“我正同他说话——我一扭头看了克里斯一小会儿——我听见扑通一声闷响，再回过头来，他人没了。”

她放声大哭起来。

===Original===
“Yes, but who? Who?” said Wyatt impatiently. “Who has vanished?”

Anne peered at him through her tears.

“Why, *Albert*!” she said, and burst into louder sobbing. “Albert Campion. They’ve got him because he made fun of them!”

===Chinese===
“可是，谁？到底是谁？”怀亚特不耐烦地说。“谁不见了？”

安妮泪眼朦胧地打量着他。

“咦，是阿尔伯特呀！”她说着，哭得愈发响了。“阿尔伯特·坎皮恩。他们把他抓走了，谁让他拿他们寻开心来着！”

## X The Impetuous Mr. Abbershaw / 第十章 冲动的阿伯肖先生

===Original===
A hasty search revealed the fact that Mr. Campion had indeed disappeared, and the discovery, coupled with Chris Kennedy’s experience of the morning, reduced the entire company to an unpleasant state of nerves. The terrified Anne Edgeware and the wounded rugby blue comforted each other in a corner by the fire. Prenderby’s little fiancée clung to his hand as a frightened child might have done. The others talked volubly, but every minute the general gloom deepened.

In the midst of this the lunch gong in the outer hall sounded, as if nothing untoward had happened. For some moments nobody moved. Then Wyatt got up. “Well, anyway,” he said, “they seem to intend to feed us﻿—let’s go in, shall we?”

They followed him dubiously into the other room, where a cold luncheon had been prepared at the long table. Two menservants waited on them, silent and surly, and the meal was a quiet one. No one felt in the mood for trivialities, and Mr. Campion was not there to provide his usual harmless entertainment.

===Chinese===
匆匆搜索一番，结果表明坎皮恩先生确实失踪了。这个发现，再加上克里斯·肯尼迪清早的遭遇，把大伙儿全都弄得神经紧张、惶惶不安。吓坏了的安妮·埃奇韦尔和负了伤的橄榄球健将待在壁炉边一个角落里互相安慰。普伦德比的小未婚妻紧紧攥着他的手，活像个受惊的孩子。其余的人滔滔不绝地说着话，可满屋的阴郁一分钟比一分钟浓重。

正当此时，外厅里的午饭锣悠悠响起，仿佛天下太平，什么事都不曾发生。有好一阵子没人动弹。随后怀亚特站起身来。“唉，不管怎么说，”他说，“他们看样子是打算管我们饭的——咱们进去吧，好不好？”

众人将信将疑地跟着他进了另一间屋子，长桌上已备好一顿冷餐。两名男仆在旁侍候，一言不发、面色阴沉，这顿饭吃得悄无声息。谁都没有说笑闲谈的兴致，坎皮恩先生又不在场，没人来表演他那一贯无伤大雅的助兴节目。

===Original===
There was a certain amount of apprehension, also, lest Mr. Dawlish might reappear and the experience of breakfast be repeated. Everyone felt a little relieved, therefore, when the meal ended without a visitation. The explanation of this apparent neglect came ten minutes or so later, when Martin Watt, who had gone up to his room to replenish his cigarette-case, came dashing into the hall where they were all sitting, the lazy expression for once startled out of his grey eyes.

“I say,” he said, “the blighters have searched my room! Had a real old beano up there by the look of it. Clothes all over the place﻿—half the floor boards up. I should say the Hun has done it himself﻿—it looks as if an elephant had run amok there. If I were you people I’d trot up to your rooms and see if they’ve done the thing thoroughly.”

This announcement brought everybody to their feet. Wyatt, who still considered himself the host of the party, fumed impotently. Chris Kennedy swore lurid deeds of revenge under his breath, and Prenderby and Abbershaw exchanged glances. Abbershaw smiled grimly. “I think perhaps we had better take Watt’s suggestion,” he said, and led the way out of the hall.

===Chinese===
此外，大家心里还有几分忐忑，唯恐道利什先生再度露面，把早饭那一场面重演一遍。因此这一餐自始至终没人前来“光顾”，人人都略感宽慰。这番表面怠慢的缘由，约莫十分钟后便见了分晓：马丁·沃特上楼回房去给烟盒添补香烟，一阵风似的冲进众人围坐的门厅，他灰眼睛里那副惯常的懒散神情，破天荒被惊骇一扫而空。

“喂，我说，”他嚷道，“那帮混蛋把我的房间搜了个遍！瞧那样子，他们在上头着实大闹了一场。衣服扔得满地都是——半数地板都撬起来了。要我说，是德国佬亲自动的手——那屋子看上去像头大象在里面撒过野。我要是你们各位，就赶紧上自己房里瞧瞧，看他们搜得彻底不彻底。”

这一声宣告把大家都惊得站了起来。怀亚特——他仍把自己当作这伙人的主人——气得干冒火，却无计可施。克里斯·肯尼迪压着嗓门，恶狠狠地赌咒要报仇雪恨；普伦德比和阿伯肖交换了一个眼色。阿伯肖冷冷一笑。“我看，咱们最好还是采纳沃特的建议。”他说着，领头走出了门厅。

===Original===
Once in his room he found that their fears had been justified. His belongings had been ransacked, his meticulously arranged suitcase lying open on its side, and his clothes strewn in all directions. The door of the big oak press with the carved front, which was built into the wall and took up all one end of the room, stood open, its contents all over the floor.

A wave of uncontrollable anger passed over him, and with that peculiarly precise tidiness which was one of his most marked characteristics he began methodically to put the room straight again.

Prisoners they might be, shots could be fired, and people could disappear apparently into thin air, none of these could shake him, but the sight of his belongings jumbled into this appalling confusion all but unnerved him completely.

He packed up everything he possessed very neatly, and stowed it in the press, then, slamming the heavy oaken door, he turned the key in the lock, and thrust it into his pocket.

===Chinese===
一回到自己房里，他便发现大家的忧虑果然不假。他的行李物件被翻了个底朝天：那只收拾得一丝不苟的手提箱敞着口、侧翻在地，衣服丢得满屋都是。那口正面雕花的大橡木柜嵌在墙里，占了房间整整一头，此刻柜门大开，里面的东西摊了满地。

一阵无法遏制的怒火从他心头涌起，紧接着，他便拿出那种一板一眼的整洁功夫——他最鲜明的特性之一——有条不紊地动手，把房间重新收拾归位。

沦为囚徒也好，枪弹横飞也好，人当着大家凭空消失也好，这些都撼不动他；可眼见自己的衣物杂物被糟蹋成这副骇人的乱象，他差一点儿就乱了方寸。

他把自己全部行装收拾得整整齐齐，塞进柜中，随后砰地关上厚重的橡木柜门，拧转锁孔里的钥匙，把它往口袋里一塞。

===Original===
It was at this precise moment that an extraordinary mental revolution took place in Abbershaw.

It happened as he put the cupboard key in his pocket; during the actual movement he suddenly saw himself from the outside. He was naturally a man of thought, not of action, and now for the first time in his life he was thrust into a position where quick decisions and impulsive actions were forced from him. So far, he realized suddenly, he had always been a little late in grasping the significance of each situation as it had arisen. This discovery horrified him, and in that moment of enlightenment Dr. George Abbershaw, the sober, deliberate man of science, stepped into the background, and George Abbershaw the impulsive, energetic enthusiast came forward to meet the case.

He did not lose his head, however. He realized that at the present juncture infinite caution was vital. The next move must come from Dawlish. Until that came they must wait patiently, ready to grasp at the first chance of freedom. The present state of siege was only tenable for a very short time. For a weekend Black Dudley might be safe from visitors, tradespeople, and the like, but after Monday inquiries must inevitably be made. Dawlish would have to act soon.

There was the affair of Albert Campion. Wyatt had been peculiarly silent about him, and Abbershaw did not know what to make of it all. His impulse was to get the idiot back into their own circle at all costs, but there was no telling if he had been removed or if he had vanished of his own free will. No one knew anything about him.

===Chinese===
恰恰就在这一刻，阿伯肖内心发生了一场异乎寻常的巨变。

巨变就发生在他把柜钥匙揣进口袋的当口；就在那个动作进行之中，他突然从旁观者的角度看清了自己。他生来是个动脑子的人，不是个动手的人，而如今平生头一遭，他被推到了一个逼他快下决断、果敢行动的位置上。他蓦然醒悟：直到此刻，每当新局面出现，他领会其中利害总要慢上半拍。这个发现让他大为骇然；而就在那灵光乍现的一瞬，乔治·阿伯肖医生——那个冷静持重、深思熟虑的科学家——退到了幕后，迎上前去对付眼前局面的，是那个冲动急切、劲头十足的热血汉子乔治·阿伯肖。

不过，他倒没有慌了手脚。他明白，在此刻的关节眼上，万般谨慎至关重要。下一步棋必须由道利什来走。在那一着落下之前，他们只能耐心等待，随时准备好抓住第一个通往自由的机会。眼下的围困态势撑不了多久。单是一个周末，布莱克·达德利兴许还能躲开访客、商贩之流的搅扰，可过了星期一，必然有人前来打听问询。道利什必须尽快行动。

还有阿尔伯特·坎皮恩这桩事。怀亚特对此人蹊跷地缄口不言，阿伯肖全然摸不着头脑。他本能的冲动，是不惜一切代价把那个傻瓜弄回自己人的圈子里来；可谁也说不清，他究竟是被人弄走的，还是自己心甘情愿消失的。此人来历，无人知晓。

===Original===
Abbershaw went slowly out of the room and down the corridor to the staircase, and was just about to descend when he heard the unmistakable sound of a woman crying.

He paused to listen, and discovered that the noise came from behind a door on his left.

He hesitated.

===Chinese===
阿伯肖缓步走出房间，沿走廊来到楼梯口，正要下楼，忽听得一个女人啜泣的声音，错不了。

他驻足倾听，发觉声音出自他左手边一扇门的后面。

他犹豫了。

===Original===
Half an hour before, a fear of being intrusive would have prevented him from doing anything, but a very considerable change had taken place in him in that time, and he listened again.

The sound continued.

The thought dawned upon him that it was Meggie; he fancied that this was her room, and the idea of her alone and in distress banished his last vestige of timidity and caution. He knocked at the door.

===Chinese===
换作半个钟头之前，生怕唐突打扰的顾虑准会让他止步不前；可这半个钟头里，他身上已经起了相当大的变化，于是他又侧耳听了听。

哭声仍在继续。

一个念头在他心里渐渐亮起：那是梅吉。他记得这间似乎正是她的屋子；一想到她孤身一人、伤心垂泪，他最后一点畏怯和谨慎便被驱散得干干净净。他敲响了门。

===Original===
Her voice answered him.

“It’s George,” he said, almost defiantly. “Anything the matter?”

She was some seconds opening the door, and when at last she came he saw that although she had hastily powdered her face the tear-stains were still visible upon it.

For one moment Abbershaw felt that he was going to have a relapse into his old staid self, but he overcame it and there was an expression of fiery determination in his chubby round face which astonished the girl so much that her surprise showed in her eyes. Abbershaw recognized it, and it annoyed him.

===Chinese===
门里传来她的声音。

“是我，乔治。”他说，语气近乎挑衅。“出了什么事？”

她隔了几秒钟才把门打开，及至终于现身，他看得分明：她脸上虽匆匆扑了粉，泪痕却依然可见。

有那么一瞬，阿伯肖觉得自己就要故态复萌、缩回那个老成持重的旧我，但他硬是克制住了；他那张圆乎乎的脸上透出一股火辣辣的决断，惊得姑娘瞪圆了眼睛，讶异之色一望即知。阿伯肖看出来了，心里老大恼火。

===Original===
In a flash he saw himself as she must have seen him all along, a round, self-important little man, old for his years, inclined to be pompous, perhaps﻿—terrible thought﻿—even fussy. A horrible sense of humiliation swept over him and at the same time a growing desire to teach her she was wrong, to show her that she had been mistaken, to prove to her that he was a man to be reckoned with, a personality, a man of action, vigorous, resourceful, a he-man, a﻿ …!

He drew a deep breath.

“I can’t have you crying like this,” he said, and picked her up and kissed her.

Meggie could not have responded more gracefully. Whether it was relief, shock, or simply the last blow to her tortured nerves, he never knew, but she collapsed into his arms; at first he almost thought she had fainted.

===Chinese===
电光石火之间，他看到了自己素来在她眼中的模样：一个圆滚滚、自命不凡的小个子男人，一副与年纪不相称的老成，爱摆架子，说不定——这念头可怕至极——还婆婆妈妈。一阵可怕的羞辱感将他整个淹没，与此同时，一股愈发炽烈的欲望升腾起来：他要让她知道她错了，要让她看到她看错了人，要向她证明他是个不容小觑的男人，一个有分量的人物，一个行动家，精力充沛、足智多谋，一条铁铮铮的汉子，一个……！

他深深吸了一口气。

“我可不能让你就这么哭着。”说罢，他一把将她抱起，吻了上去。

梅吉的回应再温顺体贴不过了。那是如释重负也好，是猝不及防也好，还是仅仅因为她备受折磨的神经挨了最后一击也好，他始终不得而知；总之她整个人瘫倒进他怀里，起初他几乎以为她晕了过去。

===Original===
He led her firmly down the long corridor to the wide window-seat at the far end. It was recessed, and hung with heavy curtains. He sat down and drew her beside him, her head on his shoulder.

“Now,” he said, still bristling with his newly discovered confidence, “you’re going to escape from here tomorrow certainly, if not tonight, and you’re going to marry me because I love you! I love you! I love you!”

He paused breathlessly and waited, his heart thumping against his side like a schoolboy’s.

Her face was hidden from him and she did not speak. For a moment the awful thought occurred to him that she might be angry with him, or even﻿—laughing.

===Chinese===
他毅然挽起她，沿长长的走廊走到尽头那处宽敞的窗座前。窗座凹嵌在墙内，垂挂着厚重的帘子。他坐下来，把她拉到身旁，她的头靠在他肩上。

“好了，”他说，浑身还涨满着他新近发现的自信，“明天你一定能从这里逃出去，就算今晚不成；你还要嫁给我，因为我爱你！我爱你！我爱你！”

他屏着气顿了顿，等着回音，心脏贴着肋骨怦怦直撞，活像个小学生。

她把脸埋着不让他看见，也不作声。一时间，一个可怕的念头掠过他心头：她也许在恼他，甚至——在笑。

===Original===
“You﻿—er﻿—you will marry me?” he said, a momentary anxiety creeping into his tone. “I’m sorry if I startled you,” he went on, with a faint return of his old primness. “I didn’t mean to, but I﻿—I’m an impetuous sort of fellow.”

Meggie stirred at his side, and as she lifted her face to him he saw that she was flushed with laughter, but there was more than mere amusement in her brown eyes. She put her arm round his neck and drew his head down.

“George, you’re adorable,” she said. “I love you ridiculously, my dear.”

A slow, warm glow spread all over Abbershaw. His heart lolloped in his side, and his eyes danced.

===Chinese===
“你——呃——你愿意嫁给我吗？”他说，语气里悄悄渗进一丝焦灼。“要是吓着你了，对不起。”他接着说，旧日那副拘谨劲儿又隐约回来了，“我不是故意的，只是我——我是个容易冲动的人。”

梅吉在他身旁动了动；当她仰起脸来时，他看见她笑得双颊绯红，可她那双棕色眼睛里，又远不止是觉得有趣。她伸出胳膊搂住他的脖子，把他的头拉了下来。

“乔治，你真可爱。”她说，“我爱你爱得不可救药，亲爱的。”

一股暖流缓缓漫过阿伯肖全身。他的心在胸腔里欢蹦乱跳，双眼灼灼生辉。

===Original===
He kissed her again. She lay against his breast very quiet, very happy, but still a little scared.

He felt like a giant refreshed﻿—after all, he reflected, his first essay in his new role had been an unparalleled success.

===Chinese===
他又吻了她。她偎在他胸前，安安静静，满心欢喜，却还有几分害怕。

他只觉得自己像饮足了酒、精神焕发的巨人——毕竟，他思忖道，他在这新角色中的头一回尝试，已是一场无与伦比的成功。

## XI One Explanation / 第十一章 一个解释

===Original===
That evening, after tea had been served in ominous silence by the same two menservants who had waited at lunch, Michael Prenderby crossed the room and spoke confidentially to Abbershaw.

“I say,” he said awkwardly, “poor old Jeanne has got the wind up pretty badly. Do you think we’ve got an earthly chance of making a bolt for it?” He paused, and then went on again quickly, “Can’t we hatch out a scheme of some sort? Between you and me, I’m feeling a bit desperate.”

Abbershaw frowned.

===Chinese===
当天傍晚，还是午间伺候开饭的那两个男仆，在一阵不祥的死寂中把茶点端了上来。茶后，迈克尔·普伦德比穿过房间，走到阿伯肖跟前，附耳说起体己话来。

“我说啊，”他讪讪地开口，“可怜的让娜真是吓破了胆。你说，咱们到底还有没有一星半点的机会，能溜出去逃命？”他顿了顿，又急急地接下去：“咱们就不能合计出一个什么法子来？咱们私下说说，我都觉得有点儿走投无路了。”

阿伯肖皱起了眉头。

===Original===
“We can’t do much at the moment, I’m afraid,” he said slowly; but added, as the boy’s expression grew more and more perturbed, “Look here, come up and smoke a cigarette with me in my room and we’ll talk it over.”

“I’d like to.” Prenderby spoke eagerly, and the two men slipped away from the others and went quietly up to Abbershaw’s room.

===Chinese===
“恐怕眼下一时无能为力。”他缓缓说道；但眼见这小伙子的神色愈来愈惶惶不安，他又补了一句：“我说，上我屋里来陪我抽支烟，咱们把这事儿好好合计合计。”

“求之不得。”普伦德比说得热切。两人便神不知鬼不觉地从众人身边溜开，蹑手蹑脚地上楼，进了阿伯肖的房间。

===Original===
