As far as they could ascertain, Dawlish and the others had their headquarters in the vast old apartment which had been Colonel Coombe’s bedroom and the rooms immediately above and below it, into which there seemed no entrance from any part of the house that they knew.

Even Wyatt could not help them with the geography of Black Dudley. The old house had been first monastery, then farmstead, and finally a dwelling-house, and in each period different alterations had been made.

Besides, before the second marriage of his aunt, the enormous old place had been shut up, and it was not until shortly before her death that Wyatt first stayed at the place. Since then his visits had been infrequent and never of a long enough duration to allow him to become familiar with the numberless rooms, galleries, passages, and staircases of which the place was composed.

Prenderby was getting nerves, his fiancée’s terror was telling on him, and, of course, he knew considerably more of the ugly facts of the situation than anyone of the party save Abbershaw himself.

===Chinese===
据他们所能探明的情况，道利什一伙把老巢设在库姆上校原先那间宽敞的大卧房，以及紧挨着它的上下两层屋子里；而从宅子里他们所熟悉的任何角落，似乎都寻不见通往那些屋子的入口。

就连怀亚特，也帮不上他们弄清布莱克·达德利的地理。这座老宅先做过修道院，后来改作农庄，最后才成了住家宅邸，每个时期都做过不同的翻修改建。

再者，在他姑母再嫁之前，这座硕大无朋的老宅一直封门闭户；直到她去世前不久，怀亚特才头一回在此小住。打那以后他来得分外稀少，逗留也从不长久，不足以叫他摸熟这座宅子数不清的房间、长廊、过道和楼梯。

普伦德比渐渐沉不住气了：未婚妻的恐惧啃噬着他；况且，除了阿伯肖本人，他对这局面里种种丑陋的内情，比聚会的任何人都知道得多得多。

===Original===
“The whole thing seemed almost a joke this morning,” he said petulantly. “That old Hun might have been a music-hall turn then, but I don’t mind confessing that I’ve got the wind up now. Hang it all,” he went on bitterly, “we’re as far away from civilization here as we should be if this was the seventeenth century. The modern ‘Majesty of the Law’ and all that has made us so certain of our own safety that when a trap like this springs we’re fairly caught. Damn it, Abbershaw, brute force is the only real power, anyway.”

“Perhaps,” said Abbershaw guardedly, “but it’s early yet. Some opportunity is bound to crop up within the next twelve hours. I think we shall see our two troublesome friends in gaol before we’re finished.”

Prenderby glanced at him sharply.

“You’re very optimistic, aren’t you?” he said. “You talk as if something distinctly promising had happened. Has it?”

George Abbershaw coughed.

“In a way, yes,” he said, and was silent. Now, he felt, was not the moment to announce his engagement to Meggie.

===Chinese===
“今儿早上，这事儿看着还差不多像个玩笑，”他焦躁地说，“那会儿那个老德国佬活脱是音乐厅里插科打诨的角儿；可我不妨承认，这会儿我自己也怕得够呛。见鬼，”他愤愤地接着说，“咱们在这儿离文明世界之远，就跟回到了十七世纪似的。如今这年头，什么‘法律的威严’之类，把我们自个儿的安全熏陶得笃定无比，以致这么一个陷阱一旦弹起来，我们就结结实实地被扣了个正着。该死，阿伯肖，说到底，蛮力才是唯一真正的力量。”

“也许吧，”阿伯肖出言谨慎，“可时候还早。十二个钟头之内，总归会冒出什么机会来。依我看，不等这出戏收场，咱们准能亲眼瞧见那两位惹是生非的朋友蹲进大牢。”

普伦德比锐利地瞥了他一眼。

“你可真够乐观的，不是吗？”他说，“你这口气，倒像是分明出了什么大有指望的事。是吗？”

乔治·阿伯肖咳了一声。

“从某种意义上说，是的。”他说完便不作声了。他觉得，眼下还不是宣布他和梅吉婚约的时机。

===Original===
They had reached the door of the bedroom by this time, and further inquiries on Prenderby’s part were cut short by a sudden and arresting phenomenon.

From inside the room came a series of extraordinary sounds﻿—long, high-pitched murmurs, intermingled with howls and curses, and accompanied now and then by a sound of scuffling.

“My God!” said Prenderby. “What in the name of good fortune is that?”

Abbershaw did not answer him.

Clearly the move which he had been expecting had been made.

===Chinese===
这时两人已走到卧室门口，普伦德比正待再问，却被一桩突如其来、摄人心魄的怪事截住了话头。

屋里传出一连串异乎寻常的声响——悠长而尖利的呜咽，混着嚎叫与咒骂，还不时夹着一阵扭打扑腾的动静。

“我的天哪！”普伦德比说，“那究竟是个什么名堂？”

阿伯肖没有答话。

显然，他一直等候的那一着棋，已经落子了。

===Original===
With all his new temerity he seized the door-latch and was about to fling it up, when Prenderby caught his arm.

“Go carefully! Go carefully!” he said, with a touch of indignation in his voice. “You don’t want to shove your head in it, whatever it is. They’re armed, remember.”

The other nodded, and raising the latch very cautiously he thrust the door gently open.

Prenderby followed him; both men were alert and tingling with expectation.

The noise continued; it was louder than before, and sounded peculiarly unearthly in that ghostly house.

===Chinese===
他拿出新近炼成的那股子胆气，一把攥住门闩，正要抬手猛地挑开，普伦德比却一把抓住了他的胳膊。

“悠着点儿！悠着点儿！”他说，嗓音里带上一丝恼意，“管那里头是什么，你总犯不上把脑袋往里送。别忘了，他们手里有家伙。”

阿伯肖点点头，极其小心地抬起门闩，轻轻把门推开了一道缝。

普伦德比跟在他身后；两人都全神戒备，浑身的神经因期待而微微发麻。

那声响还在继续；比方才更响了，在这座鬼气森森的宅子里听来，别有一种说不出的阴森诡异。

===Original===
Abbershaw was the first to peer round the door and look in.

“Good Lord!” he said at last, glancing back over his shoulder at Prenderby, “there’s not a soul here.”

The two men burst into the room, and the noise, although muffled, became louder still.

“I say!” said Prenderby, suddenly startled out of his annoyance, “it’s in *there*!”

Abbershaw followed the direction of his hand and gasped.

===Chinese===
阿伯肖头一个从门后探进头去张望。

“老天爷！”他终于开了口，回头瞥了普伦德比一眼，“里面连个人影也没有。”

两人冲进屋去，那声响虽然隔着层什么，反而愈发响亮了。

“我说！”普伦德比道，一时间惊得把满肚子的恼意都抛到了九霄云外，“就在*那儿*！”

阿伯肖顺着他的手势望过去，倒抽了一口冷气。

===Original===
The extraordinary sounds were indubitably proceeding from the great oak press at the far end of the room﻿—the wardrobe which he had locked himself not two hours before and the key of which was still heavy in his pocket. He turned to Michael.

“Shut the door,” he said. “Lock it, and take the key.” Then he advanced towards the cupboard.

Michael Prenderby stood with his back against the door of the room, waiting.

Very gingerly Abbershaw fitted the huge iron key into the cupboard, turned over the lock, and wrenched the door open, starting back instantly.

The noise stopped abruptly.

===Chinese===
那串怪响毫无疑问正是从屋子最里头那座高大的橡木衣橱里传出来的——不到两个钟头之前，他亲手锁上了那口橱子，钥匙此刻还沉甸甸地揣在他口袋里。他转向迈克尔。

“把门关上，”他说，“上了锁，钥匙你拿着。”说罢，他朝那口橱子走去。

迈克尔·普伦德比背抵着房门站在那里，等候着。

阿伯肖小心翼翼地把那把大铁钥匙插进橱门，拧转锁头，猛地拽开橱门，随即立刻闪身后退。

声响戛然而止。

===Original===
There was a smothered exclamation from Prenderby and both men stood back in utter amazement.

There, seated upon a heavy oaken shelf in a square cavity just large enough to contain him, his hair over his eyes, his clothes dishevelled, his inane face barely recognizable, was Mr. Albert Campion.

For several seconds he did not move, but sat blinking at them through the lank strands of yellow hair over his eyes. Then it was that Abbershaw’s memory revived.

In a flash it came to him where he had seen that vacuous, inoffensive face before, and a slow expression of wonderment came into his eyes.

He did not speak, however, for at that moment Campion stirred, and climbed stiffly out into the room.

===Chinese===
普伦德比发出一声压住的低呼，两人一齐退开，惊愕得目瞪口呆。

只见阿尔伯特·坎皮恩先生端坐在一方洞龛里、一道厚实的橡木搁板上，那洞龛四四方方，尺寸刚够容他一人；头发耷拉下来遮住眼睛，衣衫凌乱不堪，那张呆气十足的脸几乎叫人认不出来。

好几秒钟他一动不动，只是坐在那儿，透过垂在眼前的几绺稀疏黄发朝他们眨眼。就在这时，阿伯肖的记忆复苏了。

电光石火间，他想起了先前在哪儿见过那张茫然失神、全然无害的面孔，一层讶异之色缓缓浮上他的眼底。

不过他没有作声，因为就在这时坎皮恩动了动，僵着身子从橱里爬了出来，进了屋。

===Original===
“No deception, ladies and gentlemen,” he said, with a wan attempt at his own facetiousness. “All my own work.”

“How the devil did you get in there?” The words were Prenderby’s; he had come forward, his eyes fixed upon the forlorn figure in childlike astonishment.

“Oh﻿—influence, mostly,” said Campion, and dropped into a chair. But it was evident that a great deal of his spirit had left him. Obviously he had been badly handled, there were crimson marks round his wrists, and his shirt showed ragged beneath his jacket.

===Chinese===
“绝无花假，女士们先生们，”他说，有气无力地想抖一抖自己惯常的机灵劲儿，“全出自本人亲手。”

“你到底是怎么钻进去的？”这话是普伦德比问的；他已走上前来，孩子气地满眼惊愕，直勾勾盯着那副凄惶的人影。

“哦——多半是靠门路。”坎皮恩说着，一屁股跌坐进椅子里。可他显然丢了魂儿似的没了大半精气神。他分明是吃了顿好打：手腕上一圈圈深红的印子，衬衫在外套底下绽成了一条条破布。

===Original===
Prenderby opened his mouth to speak again, but a sign from Abbershaw silenced him.

“Dawlish got you, of course?” he said, with an unwonted touch of severity in his tone.

Mr. Campion nodded.

“Did they search you?” Abbershaw persisted.

“Search me?” said he. A faintly weary expression came into the pale eyes behind the large spectacles. “My dear sir, they almost had my skin off in their investigations. That Hun talks like comic opera but behaves like the Lord High Executioner. He nearly killed me.” He took his coat off as he spoke, and showed them a shirt cut to ribbons and stained with blood from great weals across his back.

===Chinese===
普伦德比张口还想再问，阿伯肖一个眼色止住了他。

“不用说，是道利什把你弄去的？”他说，语气里带着一反常态的严峻。

坎皮恩先生点了点头。

“他们搜你的身了？”阿伯肖追问。

“搜身？”他说。大眼镜背后那双浅色的眼睛里，掠过一丝淡淡的倦意。“亲爱的先生，他们那一番盘查，差一点没把我一层皮给扒下来。那个德国佬，说话像演滑稽歌剧，行事却俨然是位首席大刽子手。他差点要了我的命。”他一边说一边脱下外套，只见那件衬衫已被抽成碎条，背上道道鞭痕的血把它浸染得斑斑点点。

===Original===
“Good God!” said Abbershaw. “Thrashed!” Instantly his magisterial manner vanished and he became the professional man with a case to attend to.

“Michael,” he said, “there’s a white shirt amongst my things in that cupboard, and water and boracic on the washstand. What happened?” he continued briefly, as Prenderby hurried to make all preparations for dressing the man’s injuries.

Mr. Campion stirred painfully.

“As far as I can remember,” he said weakly, “about four hundred years ago I was standing by the fireplace talking to Anne What’s-her-name, when suddenly the panel I was leaning against gave way, and the next moment I was in the dark with a lump of sacking in my mouth.” He paused. “That was the beginning,” he said. “Then I was hauled up before old Boanerges and he put me through it pretty thoroughly; I couldn’t convince him that I hadn’t got his packet of love-letters or whatever it is that he’s making such a stink about. A more thorough old bird in the questioning line I never met.”

===Chinese===
“天哪！”阿伯肖说。“挨鞭子了！”刹那间，他那副俨然法官的做派荡然无存，摇身变回了手头有伤员要处置的专业医生。

“迈克尔，”他说，“那橱子里我的衣物当中有一件白衬衫，洗脸架上有清水和硼酸洗液。”普伦德比匆匆去张罗包扎伤口的一应用品，他便言简意赅地问了下去：“到底出了什么事？”

坎皮恩先生疼得轻轻挪动了一下。

“就我还能记得的来说，”他有气无力地说，“大约四百年前吧，我正站在壁炉跟前，跟安妮——她姓什么来着——说着话，忽然间，我倚着的那块护壁板一松，紧跟着我就到了黑地里，嘴里还塞着一大团麻袋布。”他顿了顿。“那还只是个开头。”他说。“随后我就被拖到老‘雷霆之子’（Boanerges）跟前，他把我结结实实过了一遍堂；我没法叫他相信，他那一包情书——或者管他闹得天翻地覆要找的是个什么劳什子——都不在我手里。审问这道行里如此彻底的老手，我生平还没见过第二个。”

===Original===
“So I should think,” murmured Prenderby, who had now got Campion’s shirt off and was examining his back.

“When they convinced themselves that I was as innocent as a newborn babe,” continued the casualty, some of his old cheerfulness returning, “they gave up jumping on me and put me into a box-room and locked the door.” He sighed. “I sleuthed round for a bit,” he went on, while they listened to him eagerly. “The window was about two thousand feet from the ground with a lot of natty ironwork on it﻿—and finally, looking round for a spot soft enough for me to lie down without yowling, I perceived an ancient chest, under the other cardboard whatnots and fancy basketwork about the place, and I opened it.” He paused, and drank the tooth-glass of water which Prenderby handed to him.

“I thought some grandmotherly garment might be there,” he continued. “Something I could make a bed of. All I found, however, was something that I took to be a portion of an ancient bicycle﻿—most unsuitable for my purpose. I was so peeved that I jumped on it with malicious intent, and immediately the whole show gave way and I made a neat but effective exit through the floor. When I got the old brain working again, I discovered that I was standing on the top of a flight of steps, my head still half out of the chest. The machinery was the ancients’ idea of a blind, I suppose. So I shut the lid of the trunk behind me, and lighting a match toddled down the steps.”

===Chinese===
“这我信。”普伦德比喃喃道；这时他已把坎皮恩的衬衫剥了下来，正在查看他的背。

“等他们自己回过味来，确信我像新生婴儿一样清白无辜，”这位伤患接着说，旧日那点快活劲儿回来了几分，“他们便歇了手，不再拿我练拳脚，把我塞进一间杂物房，还锁上了门。”他叹了口气。“我暗中侦察了一阵子，”他继续说，两人都听得目不转睛。“那扇窗户离地约莫两千英尺，窗上还装着一整套精巧的铁栅——到末了，我想寻个软和些、躺下去不至于嗷嗷叫唤的地方，一眼瞧见一只古旧的大箱子，压在屋里那些纸板零碎和花哨筐篮底下，我就把它打开了。”他停下来，喝掉了普伦德比递给他的那漱口杯清水。

“我寻思着，里头兴许藏了件老祖母辈的衣裳，”他接着说，“好歹能给我铺一张床。可翻来找去，只寻见一样东西，我看那是半辆古董自行车——跟我的用场全不相宜。我这一气非同小可，便起了歹心，跳上去狠狠踩了一通；那整套劳什子登时轰然塌陷，我就这么干净利落、卓有成效地从地板上出走了。等我那颗老脑筋重新开动起来，才发现自己正站在一段阶梯的顶上，脑袋还有半截卡在箱口。我估摸着，那机巧是古人们用来遮掩耳目的障眼法。于是我随手把箱盖掩在身后，划了根火柴，晃晃悠悠地下了台阶。”

===Original===
He stopped again. The two men were listening to him intently.

“I don’t see how you got into the cupboard, all the same,” said Prenderby.

“Nor do I, frankly,” said Mr. Campion. “The steps stopped after a bit and I was in a sort of tunnel﻿—a ratty kind of place; the little animals put the wind up me a bit﻿—but eventually I crawled along and came up against a door which opened inwards, got it open, and sneaked out into your cupboard. That didn’t help me much,” he added dryly. “I didn’t know where I was, so I just sat there reciting ‘The Mistletoe Bough’ to myself, and confessing my past life﻿—such sport!” He grinned at them and stopped. “That’s all,” he said.

===Chinese===
他又停了下来。两人都在全神贯注地听他讲。

“可我还是不明白，你到底是怎么进的这口衣橱。”普伦德比说。

“老实说，我也不明白。”坎皮恩先生说。“那台阶走了一截便到了头，我进了一条隧道模样的东西——一个耗子成群的地方；那些小动物把我吓得够呛——不过我总算连滚带爬到了头，迎面撞上一扇朝里开的门，把它弄开，就溜进了你们这口橱子。可这也没帮我多大忙，”他干巴巴地补了一句，“我不知道自己身在何处，索性坐在那儿，给自己背诵《槲寄生枝》（The Mistletoe Bough），顺便把前半生忏悔了一遍——多有意思！”他朝他们咧嘴一笑，住了口。“完了。”他说。

===Original===
Abbershaw, who had been watching him steadily as he talked, came slowly down the room and stood before him.

“I’m sorry you had such a bad time,” he said, and added very clearly and distinctly, “but there’s really no need to keep up this bright conversation, *Mr. Mornington Dodd*.”

For some seconds Mr. Campion’s pale eyes regarded Abbershaw blankly. Then he started almost imperceptibly, and a slow smile spread over his face.

“So you’ve spotted me,” he said, and, to Abbershaw’s utter amazement, chucked inanely. “But,” went on Mr. Campion cheerfully, “I assure you you’re wrong about my magnetic personality being a disguise. There is *absolutely no fraud*. I’m like this﻿—always like this﻿—my best friends could tell me.”

===Chinese===
他讲述的当口，阿伯肖一直目不转睛地盯着他，这时缓缓穿过房间，在他面前站定。

“你吃了这么大的苦头，我很抱歉，”他说，随即一字一板、清清楚楚地补上一句：“不过，这场妙趣横生的谈话，实在没有必要再继续下去了，*莫宁顿·多德先生*（Mr. Mornington Dodd）。”

有好几秒钟，坎皮恩先生那双浅色的眼睛茫然地望着阿伯肖。随即，他几不可察地一震，一个笑容慢慢在他脸上漾开。

“原来你认出我了。”他说，接着竟痴痴地笑出声来，惊得阿伯肖目瞪口呆。“不过，”坎皮恩先生兴致勃勃地往下说，“我向你保证，你若以为我这副魅力四射的人格是乔装改扮，那可想错了。这儿*绝对没有欺诈*。我生来就是这样——从来都是这样——我最好的朋友们都能作证。”

===Original===
This announcement took the wind out of Abbershaw’s sails; he had certainly not expected it.

Mr. Campion’s personality was a difficult one to take seriously; it was not easy, for instance, to decide when he was lying and when he was not. Abbershaw had reckoned upon his thrust going home, and although it had obviously done so he did not seem to have gained any advantage by it.

Prenderby, however, was entirely in the dark, and now he broke in upon the conversation with curiosity.

“Here, I say, I don’t get this,” he said. “Who and what is Mr. Mornington Dodd?”

Abbershaw threw out his hand, indicating Mr. Albert Campion.

“That gentleman,” he said, “is Mornington Dodd.”

===Chinese===
这一番宣言让阿伯肖顿时泄了气；他万万没有料到这一手。

坎皮恩先生这个人，最难叫人当真看待；比方说，要判断他什么时候在撒谎、什么时候没有，就殊非易事。阿伯肖原本指望这一剑正中要害，虽然显然刺中了，他却似乎没能占到半分便宜。

普伦德比却全然蒙在鼓里，此刻按捺不住好奇，闯进来打断了他们的谈话。

“喂，我说，这儿我可跟不上了，”他说，“这位莫宁顿·多德先生，究竟是何方神圣？”

阿伯肖伸手一指，指向阿尔伯特·坎皮恩先生。

“那位先生，”他说，“就是莫宁顿·多德。”

===Original===
Albert Campion smiled modestly. In spite of his obvious pain he was still lively.

“In a way yes, and in a way no,” he said, fixing his eyes on Abbershaw. “Mornington Dodd is one of my names. I have also been called the ‘Honourable Tootles Ash,’ which I thought was rather neat when it occurred to me. Then there was a girl who used to call me ‘Cuddles’ and a man at the Guards Club called me something quite different﻿—”

“Campion, this is not a joke,” Abbershaw spoke sternly. “However many and varied your aliases have been, now isn’t the time to boast of them. We are up against something pretty serious now.”

“My dear man, don’t I know it?” said Mr. Campion peevishly, indicating the state of his shoulders. “Even better than you do, I should think,” he said dryly.

“Now look here,” said Abbershaw, whose animosity could not but be mollified by this extraordinary naivete, “you know something about this business, Campion﻿—that is your name, I suppose?”

===Chinese===
阿尔伯特·坎皮恩谦虚地微微一笑。尽管明摆着疼痛难当，他依然兴致勃勃。

“也算是，也不是。”他说，眼睛盯住了阿伯肖。“莫宁顿·多德是我的名字之一。我还曾被人称作‘尊敬的图特尔斯·阿什’，这名字冒出来的时候，我自己都觉得相当俏皮。另外有过一位姑娘，惯常管我叫‘小乖乖’；禁卫军俱乐部里又有位先生，对我的称呼则截然不同——”

“坎皮恩，这可不是玩笑。”阿伯肖厉声说道。“不管你用过的化名有多少、多么五花八门，现在都不是夸耀它们的时候。我们眼下对付的，是相当严重的事。”

“亲爱的伙计，这我难道不知道？”坎皮恩先生没好气地说，一边示意自己这副肩膀的惨状。“只怕比你知道得还清楚些。”他干巴巴地说。

“这么着吧，”阿伯肖说——面对这份异乎寻常的天真烂漫，他的敌意没法不被软化，“这桩事你知道些内情，坎皮恩——我猜，这是你的名字吧？”

===Original===
“Well﻿—er﻿—no,” said the irrepressible young man. “But,” he added, dropping his voice a tone, “my own is rather aristocratic, and I never use it in business. Campion will do quite well.”

Abbershaw smiled in spite of himself.

“Very well, then, Mr. Campion,” he said, “as I remarked before, you know something about this business, and you’re going to tell us here and now. But my dear lad, consider,” he went on as the other hesitated, “we’re all in the same boat. You, I presume, are as anxious to get away as anyone. And whereas I am intensely interested in bringing Dawlish and his confederates to justice, there is no other delinquency that I am concerned with. I am not a policeman.”

Mr. Campion beamed. “Is that so?” he inquired.

“Certainly it is,” said Abbershaw. “I am a consultant only as far as the Yard is concerned.”

Mr. Campion looked vastly relieved.

===Chinese===
“呃——这个——不是。”这个按捺不住的年轻人说。“不过，”他压低一度嗓音，补了一句，“我自己的姓氏相当有贵族气派，我从不在办正事的时候用它。坎皮恩就尽够使了。”

阿伯肖不由得笑了。

“那好，坎皮恩先生，”他说，“正如我方才所言，这桩事你知道些内情，而你现在、马上、就在此地，就要讲给我们听。不过，亲爱的老弟，设身处地想想，”他见对方迟疑，便接着说下去，“咱们如今同坐一条船。我料想，你跟任何人一样急着脱身。而我呢，一心一意只想把道利什及其党羽捉拿归案；除此之外，无论什么违法行为，都与我无关。我不是警察。”

坎皮恩先生顿时喜笑颜开。“当真如此？”他问道。

“自然是真。”阿伯肖说。“就伦敦警察厅而言，我不过挂个顾问的名头。”

坎皮恩先生看上去大大松了口气。

===Original===
“That’s rather cheered me up,” he said. “I liked you. When I saw you pottering with your car I thought, ‘There’s a little joss who might be quite good fun if he once got off the lead,’ and when you mentioned Scotland Yard just now all that good impression just faded away.”

He paused, and Abbershaw cut in quickly.

“This doesn’t get us very far,” he said quietly, “does it? You know the explanation of this extraordinary outrage. Let’s have it.”

Mr. Campion regarded him frankly.

“You may not believe me,” he said, “but I don’t know quite what they’re driving at even now. But there’s something pretty serious afoot, I can tell you that.”

It was obvious that he was telling the truth, but Abbershaw was not satisfied.

===Chinese===
“这话让我心里舒坦多了。”他说。“我本来就喜欢你。头一回见你摆弄你那辆车，我心里就想：‘这尊小菩萨，一旦挣脱了链子，说不定会相当好玩。’可方才你一提苏格兰场，那点好印象登时烟消云散。”

他顿了顿，阿伯肖赶紧插了进来。

“这么绕圈子，可绕不出什么名堂，”他不动声色地说，“不是吗？你知道这桩离奇暴行的内情。快讲吧。”

坎皮恩先生坦然地望着他。

“你也许不信，”他说，“可直到这会儿，我也说不准他们究竟意欲何为。但有一件事我可以奉告：眼下正有一桩相当了不得的事儿在酝酿。”

他说的分明是实话，可阿伯肖并不满足。

===Original===
“Well, anyway, you know one thing,” he said. “Why are you here? You just admitted yourself it was on business.”

“Oh, it was,” agreed Campion, “most decidedly. But not my business. Let me explain.”

“I wish to God you would,” said Prenderby, who was utterly out of his depth.

===Chinese===
“不过，反正有一件事你是知道的。”他说。“你为什么在这儿？你方才亲口承认过，你是为一桩正事来的。”

“哦，正是，”坎皮恩附和道，“千真万确。可不是我自己的正事。容我道来。”

“求上帝保佑，你倒是快讲啊。”普伦德比说，他早已听得云里雾里。

===Original===
“Well then, chicks, Uncle Albert speaking.” Campion leant forward, his expression more serious than his words. “Perhaps I ought to give you some little idea of my profession. I live, like all intelligent people, by my wits, and although I have often done things that mother wouldn’t like, I have remembered her parting words and have never been vulgar. To cut it short, in fact, I do almost anything within reason﻿—for a reasonable sum, but nothing sordid or vulgar﻿—quite definitely nothing vulgar.”

He glanced at Abbershaw, who nodded, and then went on.

“In this particular case,” he said, “I was approached in London last week by a man who offered me a very decent sum to get myself included as unobtrusively as possible into the house-party this weekend and then to seize the first opportunity I could get to speaking to my host, the Colonel, alone. I was to make sure that we were alone. Then I was to go up to him, murmur a password in his ear, and receive from him a package which I was to bring to London immediately﻿—unopened. I was warned, of course,” he continued, looking up at Abbershaw. “They told me I was up against men who would have no compunction in killing me to prevent me getting away with the package, but I had no idea who the birds were going to be or I shouldn’t have come for any money. In fact when I saw them at dinner on the first night I nearly cut the whole job right out and bunked back to town.”

===Chinese===
“那么好吧，小家伙们，阿尔伯特叔叔开始播音。”坎皮恩俯身向前，神情比言辞郑重得多。“也许，我该让诸位对我的职业稍稍有一点概念。我同所有聪明人一样，靠脑子过活；虽说我也常干些妈妈不会赞成的事，可我一直记着她老人家的临别赠言，从不流于庸俗。长话短说吧，事实上，只要报酬合理，凡属情理之内的事，我几乎无不为——但不干下三滥的勾当，不沾庸俗——千真万确，绝不庸俗。”

他瞥了阿伯肖一眼，见他点头，便继续说下去。

“具体到眼下这桩案子，”他说，“上礼拜在伦敦，有人找到我，出一笔相当可观的酬金，要我想法儿尽量不惹眼地挤进这个周末的宾客里来，然后抓住头一个得着的机会，跟主人——也就是上校——单独说上话。我还务必确认四下无人。然后我要走到他跟前，对他耳边低声念出一句暗号，从他手里接过一个小包，即刻带回伦敦——原封不动。当然，人家也警告过我，”他抬眼望着阿伯肖，继续说道。“他们告诉我，我要对付的这路人，为了不让我带着那个小包脱身，杀了我也不会有一丝顾忌；可我当时压根儿不知道来的会是哪路角色，要不然，给多少钱我也不来。说实话，头一晚吃晚饭瞧见他们时，我差一点儿当场撂了挑子，一溜烟逃回伦敦去。”

===Original===
“Why? Who are they?” said Abbershaw.

Mr. Campion looked surprised.

“Good Lord, don’t you know?” he demanded. “And little George a Scotland Yard expert, too. Jesse Gideon calls himself a solicitor. As a matter of fact he’s rather a clever fence. And the Hun is no one else but Eberhard von Faber himself.”

Prenderby still looked blank, but Abbershaw started.

“The ‘Trois Pays’ man?” he said quickly.

“And ‘Der Schwarzbund.’ And ‘The Chicago Junker,’ and now our own little ‘0072’ at the Yard,” said Mr. Campion, and there was no facetiousness in his tone.

===Chinese===
“为什么？他们是谁？”阿伯肖说。

坎皮恩先生面露惊讶。

“老天爷，你竟不知道？”他反问。“小个子乔治还是苏格兰场的行家呢。杰西·吉迪恩自称是个事务律师。说实在的，他是个相当精明的销赃贩子。至于那个德国佬，不是别人，正是埃伯哈德·冯·法贝尔（Eberhard von Faber，即道利什）本人。”

普伦德比依旧一脸茫然，阿伯肖却猛然一震。

“那桩‘三国’（Trois Pays）案里的那个人？”他急急问道。

“还有‘黑色同盟’（Der Schwarzbund）。还有‘芝加哥容克’，如今更添上咱们自家伦敦警察厅里那桩小小的‘0072’。”坎皮恩先生说，语气里没有半分插科打诨。

===Original===
“This means nothing to me,” said Prenderby.

Mr. Campion opened his mouth to speak, but Abbershaw was before him.

“It means, Michael,” he said, with an inflection in his voice which betrayed the gravity in which he viewed the situation, “that this man controls organized gangs of crooks all over Europe and America, and he has the reputation of being utterly ruthless and diabolically clever. It means we are up against the most dangerous and notorious criminal of modern times.”

===Chinese===
“这些名号，我是一概不知。”普伦德比说。

坎皮恩先生张口欲言，阿伯肖却抢在了他前头。

“这就意味着，迈克尔，”他说，语调中的一丝起伏，泄露了他对眼前局势的郑重估量，“这个人操控着遍及欧美两洲的盗窃团伙，而且素有名声：狠辣无情到了极点，狡诈聪明赛过魔鬼。这就意味着，我们面对的，是当代最危险、最臭名昭著的罪犯。”

## XII “Furthermore …” Said Mr. Campion / 第十二章 “而且……”坎皮恩先生说

===Original===
After the little silence that followed Abbershaw’s announcement, Prenderby spoke.

“What’s in this mysterious package they’ve lost?” he said.

Abbershaw looked at Mr. Campion inquiringly.

“Perhaps you could tell us that,” he said pointedly.

Albert Campion’s vacuous face became even more blank than usual.

“I don’t know much about it,” he said. “My client didn’t go into all that, naturally. But I can tell you this much, it’s something sewn in the lining of a red leather wallet. It felt to me like paper—might have been a couple of fivers, of course—but I shouldn’t think so.”

===Chinese===
阿伯肖宣布完消息，屋里静了片刻，普伦德比率先开口。

“他们弄丢的那个神秘包裹里究竟是什么东西？”他说。

阿伯肖用探询的目光望向坎皮恩先生。

“这个嘛，你说不定能给我们讲讲。”他话里带着锋芒地说。

阿尔伯特·坎皮恩那张呆滞的脸比平日愈发茫然。

“我也所知不多。”他说，“我的委托人自然不会把底细一一讲给我听。不过有一点我可以奉告：那东西缝在一只红皮夹子的衬里里。我摸上去像纸——当然，也可能只是两张五镑的票子——不过我看不像。”

===Original===
“How do you know?” said Prenderby quietly.

Mr. Campion turned to him cheerfully.

“Oh, I collected the doings all right,” he said, “and I should have got away with them if little George here hadn’t been a car fiend.”

Abbershaw frowned.

“I think you’d better explain,” he said.

“Explain?” said Mr. Campion. “My dear chicks, there was nothing in it. As soon as I saw old Uncle Ben and his friends at the table my idea was to get the package and then beat it, manners or no manners, so when the story of the Ritual came up I thought ‘and very nice too’ and suggested the game. Then while all you people were playing ‘Bats in the Belfry’ with the ancestral skewer, I toddled over to the old boy, whispered ‘Inky-Pinky’ in his ear, got the wallet, and made a beeline for the garage.”

===Chinese===
“你怎么知道的？”普伦德比轻声问。

坎皮恩先生乐呵呵地转向他。

“哦，东西我可是稳稳当当拿到手了。”他说，“要不是这位小乔治是个汽车狂，我早就带着它远走高飞了。”

阿伯肖皱起眉头。

“我看你最好解释解释。”他说。

“解释？”坎皮恩先生说，“我亲爱的雏鸟们，这可没什么好解释的。我一看见本杰明大叔和他那伙朋友坐在桌边，打定的主意就是拿到包裹就开溜，管他礼数不礼数。于是仪式的典故一提出来，我心想‘好得很，正中下怀’，便提议玩那个游戏。随后，趁你们大伙儿拿着那柄祖传烤肉签玩‘钟楼里的蝙蝠’的当口，我摇摇摆摆踱到老头子跟前，在他耳边悄声念了句‘Inky-Pinky’（暗号），接过皮夹，便直奔车库而去。”

===Original===
He paused and sighed.

“It was all very exhilarating,” he went on easily. “My only trouble was that I was afraid that the wretched game would come to an end before I got away. With great presence of mind, therefore, I locked the door leading to the servants’ quarters so that any serenade on the dinner gong would not bring out the torchlight procession immediately. Then I toddled off down the passage, out of the side door, across the garden, and arrived all girlish with triumph at the garage and walked slap-bang into our Georgie looking like an illustration out of ‘How to Drive in Three Parts, Send No Money.’”

He stopped and eyed Abbershaw thoughtfully.

“I got the mental machinery to function with a great effort,” he continued, “and when I had it ticking over nicely I said to myself, ‘Shall I tonk this little cove on the cranium, and stuff him under the seat? Or shall I leap past him, seize the car, and go home on it?’ And neither stunt seemed really promising. If I bunked, I reasoned, George would rouse the house or chase me in one of the other cars. I couldn’t afford to risk either just then. The only other expedient therefore was to tonk him, and the more I looked at him the less I liked the notion. Georgie is a sturdy little fellow, a pugnacious little cove, who might quite easily turn out to be a flyweight champ, somewhere or other. If I was licked I was absolutely sunk, and even if I won we were bound to make a hell of a noise and I was most anxious not to have any attention focused on me while I had that pocketbook.”

===Chinese===
他顿了顿，叹了口气。

“整个过程真是令人心旌摇荡。”他不紧不慢地接着说，“唯一的麻烦是，我生怕那要命的游戏在我脱身之前就收了场。于是我凭着极好的急智，把通向仆人住处的那扇门锁上了，这样一来，哪怕有谁去敲响开饭铜锣奏上一曲，火把游行的队伍也不会立刻倾巢而出。随后我便慢悠悠晃下走廊，出了边门，穿过花园，带着少女般的胜利喜悦一路飘到车库——不偏不倚，一头正撞上咱们的乔治小子，他那副模样，活脱脱是《分三期学开车、无需预付书款》里走出来的一幅插图。”

他停下来，若有所思地端详着阿伯肖。

“我费了九牛二虎之力，才让脑子里的机器重新开动起来。”他继续说道，“等它滴滴答答转得顺当了，我暗自琢磨：‘是照这家伙的脑壳敲上一记，把他塞到座位底下去？还是从他身旁窜过去，夺下汽车，开着它回家去？’可这两招瞧着都没什么胜算。我寻思，倘若我撒腿开溜，乔治要么唤起满宅子的人，要么开上另外哪辆车追我，眼下这两样风险哪样我都担不起。如此一来，剩下的权宜之计便只有敲他一记了，可我越瞧他，越不待见这个主意。乔治小子是个结结实实的小个子，好勇斗狠的小家伙，说不定在哪个旮旯里还真是位蝇量级拳王呢。我要是吃了败仗，那就彻底沉底；就算打赢了，咱们也保准闹出天大的动静，而我揣着那只皮夹的时候，最怕的就是把众人的注意力招到自己身上。”

===Original===
“So you came back to the house with me meaning to slip out later?” said Abbershaw.

“George has made the bell ring—three more shots or a packet of Gold Flake,” said Mr. Campion facetiously. “Of course I did; and I should have got away. All would have been as merry as a wedding bell, in fact,” he went on more sadly, “if that Anne woman had not decided that I was just the sort of harmless mutt to arouse jealousy safely with Mr. Kennedy without giving trouble myself. I couldn’t escape her—she clung. So I had to wait until I thought everyone would be asleep, and then, just as I was sneaking out of my room, that precious mock butler of theirs came for me with a gun. I knocked it out of his hand, and then he started to jump on me. They must have rumbled by that time that the old boy had got rid of the packet, and were on the lookout for anyone trying a moonlight flit.”

===Chinese===
“这么说，你跟我回宅子里来，是打算之后再寻机溜出去了？”阿伯肖说。

“乔治这一枪敲响了铃铛——再送三枪，或是一包金片牌香烟。”坎皮恩先生插科打诨道，“我当然是这么盘算的，而且本来也能走脱。事实上，一切本该欢欢喜喜，像婚礼的钟声一样。”他接着往下说，语气转沉，“可偏偏那位安妮女人打定了主意，认定我正是那种自己不会惹乱子、拿来稳稳当当撩起肯尼迪先生醋意最保险的无害傻小子。我甩不开她——她死缠着不放。所以我只好一直等到心想人人都已睡熟，谁知刚要溜出房门，他们那位宝贝假管家就端着枪找上门来。我把枪从他手里打落，他便扑上来跟我缠斗。想必到那时他们已经察觉老头子把包裹脱了手，正盯紧了任何一个想趁夜潜逃的人。”

===Original===
He paused, a faintly puzzled expression passed over his face. “I could have sworn he got the packet,” he said; “anyway, in the fight I lost it. And that’s the one thing that’s really worrying me at the moment—what has happened to that wallet? For if the man who calls himself Dawlish doesn’t get what he wants, I think we are all of us for a pretty parroty time.”

He stopped and looked at Abbershaw steadily.

“It doesn’t seem to be of any negotiable value,” he said, “and as far as I can see, the only people who are interested in it are my client and Dawlish, but I can tell you one thing. It does interest them very much, and to get hold of it I don’t believe they’d stick at anything.”

===Chinese===
他顿了顿，一丝困惑掠过脸上。“我本来可以发誓，那家伙把包裹拿到手了。”他说，“反正一场混战下来，我把东西弄丢了。而眼下真正让我发愁的正是这一桩——那只皮夹后来怎么样了？因为，倘若那个自称道利什的家伙拿不到他想要的东西，我看咱们大伙儿谁都没有好日子过。”

他停下来，定定地望着阿伯肖。

“那东西似乎没有什么可变现的价值。”他说，“依我看，对它感兴趣的只有我的委托人和道利什两方。不过有一件事我可以告诉你们：他们对它在乎得很，为了弄到手，我相信他们什么事都干得出来。”

===Original===
“But what was it?” persisted Prenderby, who was more puzzled than ever by these explanations.

Campion shook his head.

“I don’t know,” he said, “unless it was the Chart of the Buried Treasure, don’t you know.”

===Chinese===
“可它到底是什么？”普伦德比追问，这番解释反倒让他愈发如坠云雾。

坎皮恩摇了摇头。

“我不知道。”他说，“除非那是一张埋藏珍宝的藏宝图，你懂的。”

===Original===
Abbershaw got up from his chair and paced slowly up and down the room.

“There’s only one weak spot in your story, Campion,” he said suddenly. “It sounds like Gospel apart from that. But there is one thing I don’t understand. It’s this: Why didn’t you have a revolver on you when you came out into the garage?”

“Answered in one,” said Mr. Campion. “Because I hadn’t one: I never carry guns.”

“Do you mean to say that you set out on an infernally dangerous game like this without one?” Abbershaw’s voice was incredulous.

===Chinese===
阿伯肖从椅子上站起身，在屋里缓缓地踱来踱去。

“你这番话里只有一个破绽，坎皮恩。”他突然开口，“除此之外，它听起来简直像福音书一样字字属实。但有一件事我不明白，就是这个：你到车库去的时候，身上为什么不带一支左轮枪？”

“一语中的。”坎皮恩先生说，“因为我压根儿就没有：我从不带枪。”

“你是说，这么一场凶险至极的把戏，你就这样两手空空上阵了？”阿伯肖的语气满是不敢相信。

===Original===
Mr. Campion became momentarily grave.

“It’s a fact,” he said simply. “I’m afraid of them. Horrible things—guns. Always feel they might go off in a fit of temper and I should be left with the body. And no bag to put it in either. Then poor little Albert would be in the soup.” He shuddered slightly.

“Let’s talk about something else,” he said. “I can keep up my pecker in the face of anything else but a corpse.”

===Chinese===
坎皮恩先生一时郑重起来。

“这是实情。”他坦坦荡荡地说，“我怕那玩意儿。枪这东西——可怕得很。我总觉得它们保不齐哪天一时火起就走火，把我撂在原地守着一具尸体，连个装它的袋子都没有。到那时，可怜的小阿尔伯特就要吃不了兜着走了。”他微微打了个寒噤。

“咱们谈点别的吧。”他说，“天塌下来我都能打起精神撑住，唯独尸体不行。”

===Original===
Prenderby and Abbershaw exchanged glances, and Abbershaw turned to where the young man with the tow-coloured hair and the unintelligent smile sat beaming at them through his glasses.

“Campion,” he said, “you know, of course, that Colonel Coombe died last night? Do you know how he died?”

Mr. Campion looked surprised.

“Heart, wasn’t it?” he said. “I thought the old bird had been scratching round the grave for the last year or so.”

===Chinese===
普伦德比和阿伯肖交换了一个眼色，阿伯肖随即转向那位淡黄头发、笑容憨傻的年轻人——他正隔着镜片冲他们俩乐呵呵地笑着。

“坎皮恩，”他说，“库姆上校昨晚死了，这你当然知道吧？你可知道他是怎么死的？”

坎皮恩先生露出讶色。

“心脏，不是吗？”他说，“我还当这老家伙这一年来一直在坟墓边上打转呢。”

===Original===
Abbershaw’s expression did not change.

“Oh,” he said, “if that is all you know it may surprise you to hear that he was murdered—while the Dagger Ritual was going on.”

“Murdered!”

Every trace of frivolity had vanished from Albert Campion’s face. There was no mistaking the fact that the news had appalled him, and he looked at Abbershaw with undisguised horror in his pale eyes.

“Murdered?” he repeated. “How do you know?”

“I saw him,” said Abbershaw simply. “They wanted a signature on the cremation certificate, and got me in for it. They wouldn’t let me examine the body, but I saw the face and neck and I also saw his invalid chair.” His eyes were fixed on Campion the whole time he was speaking. “Then there was the dagger itself,” he said. “There was blood on the dagger, and blood on the cushions of the chair, but even if I had not known of these, the body, though I saw so little of it, would have convinced me that he had been murdered. As perhaps you know,” he went on, “it is my job to explain how men die, and as soon as I saw that dead grey face with the depleted veins I knew that he had died of some wound. Something that would bleed very freely. I should say it was a stab in the back, myself.”

===Chinese===
阿伯肖的神色纹丝不动。

“哦，”他说，“如果你知道的仅止于此，那么听说他是被人谋杀的，你恐怕要吃一惊——就在匕首仪式进行的时候。”

“谋杀！”

阿尔伯特·坎皮恩脸上的每一丝轻浮都消失得无影无踪。这消息令他大为震骇，这一点错认不得；他望着阿伯肖，浅色的眼睛里毫不掩饰地满是惊恐。

“谋杀？”他重复道，“你怎么知道的？”

“我亲眼见到他了。”阿伯肖言简意赅地说，“他们要在火化证书上凑个签名，把我叫了进去。他们不许我查验尸体，但我看到了他的脸和脖颈，也看到了他那把轮椅。”说话的当口，他的目光始终钉在坎皮恩身上。“还有那柄匕首本身。”他说，“匕首上有血，轮椅的坐垫上也有血。不过即便不知道这些，那具尸体——尽管我只见到那么一小部分——也足以让我确信他是被谋杀的。你们大概知道，”他接着说，“解释人是怎么死的，这正是我的本行；一看见那张死灰色的脸和瘪塌的血管，我就知道他死于某种创伤，一种会大量出血的创伤。要我自己说，是背后捅进去的一刀。”

===Original===
The change in Mr. Campion was extraordinary; he pulled himself together with an effort.

“This is horrible,” he said. “I suppose they got him when they discovered that he had parted with the package. Pretty quick work,” he added thoughtfully. “I wonder how they rumbled him so soon.”

There was silence for a moment or two after he had spoken, then Prenderby looked up.

“The store they set by that package must be enormous, on the face of it,” he said. “Clearly they’ll do anything for it. I wonder what their next move will be?”

===Chinese===
坎皮恩先生身上的变化非同小可；他费了一番力气才让自己定下神来。

“这太可怕了。”他说，“我猜是在他们发现他把包裹脱了手之后，对他下的手。手脚真够快的。”他若有所思地补了一句，“我不明白，他们怎么会这么快就察觉。”

他说完，屋里静了片刻，随后普伦德比抬起头来。

“明摆着，他们把那包裹看得比天还大。”他说，“显然，为了它他们什么都干得出来。不知道他们的下一步棋会怎么走？”

===Original===
“He’s searched our rooms,” said Abbershaw, “and I believe he intended to lock us in the dining-room and search us immediately after, but his experiences in the bedrooms taught him the utter impossibility of ever making a thorough search of a house like this. It couldn’t be done in the time he had at his disposal. I think he realizes that his only chance of getting hold of what he wants is to terrorize us until someone hands it over.”

“Then I hope to goodness whoever has got it gets the wind up soon,” said Prenderby.

Campion nodded and sat down gingerly on the edge of the bed. “I expect he’ll have you people up one at a time and bully the truth out of you until he gets what he wants,” he said.

“For a great crook he hasn’t proved very methodical, so far,” said Abbershaw. “He might have known from the first that there’d be no point in churning everybody’s clothes up.”

===Chinese===
“他已经搜过我们的房间了。”阿伯肖说，“而且我相信，他本打算把我们锁进饭厅，紧接着就搜身。但他在那几间卧室里的遭遇让他明白，要把这样一座宅子彻底搜个遍是万万办不到的，在他可以支配的时间里根本来不及。我想他也清楚，要拿到他想要的东西，唯一的法子就是恐吓我们，直到有人把它交出来。”

“那么我只求老天保佑，不管那东西落在谁手里，那人都赶紧吓得魂飞魄散、把它交出去才好。”普伦德比说。

坎皮恩点点头，小心翼翼地在床沿上坐下。“我料想他会把你们这些人一个一个提上去，连吓带逼地榨出实话，直到弄到他想要的东西为止。”他说。

“身为一个大枭雄，到目前为止他行事可算不上多么有条理。”阿伯肖说，“他早该从一开始就明白，把每个人的衣裳翻搅个底朝天，纯属白费力气。”

===Original===
Albert Campion leaned forward. “You know, you fellows don’t understand this bright specimen of German culture,” he said, with more gravity than was usual in his falsetto voice. “He’s not used to little details of this sort. He’s the laddie at the top—the big fellow. He just chooses his men carefully and then says, ‘You do this,’ and they do it. He doesn’t go chasing round the country opening safes or pinching motorcars. I don’t believe he even plans the coups himself. He just buys criminal brains, supplies the finance, and takes the profits. That’s why I can’t understand him being here. There must have been something pretty big afoot, or he’d have had a minion in for it. Gosh! I wish I was well out of it.”

Abbershaw and Prenderby echoed his wish devoutly in their hearts, and Prenderby was the first to speak.

“I wonder whom he’ll start on first,” he said thoughtfully.

===Chinese===
阿尔伯特·坎皮恩把身子向前一倾。“要知道，你们这帮家伙并不了解这位德国文化的光辉样本。”他说，那做作的假高音里透出少见的郑重，“这种琐碎细节，他不惯于亲自过问。他是坐在顶上的那位——大人物。他只消精心挑好人手，然后说一句‘这件事你去办’，事情就办成了。他才不会满世界乱跑去撬保险柜、偷汽车。我看连那些大买卖的谋划本身，都不是他亲手做的。他只管出钱收买犯罪的头脑，供给资金，坐收利润。正因如此，我才想不通他怎么会亲自到这儿来。必定是有天大的事情在酝酿，否则他随便派个小喽啰来就打发了。天哪！真恨不得我已脱身事外。”

阿伯肖和普伦德比在心底虔诚地应和着这个愿望，普伦德比先开了口。

“不知道他会先拿谁开刀。”他若有所思地说。

===Original===
Campion’s pale eyes flickered.

“I fancy I could tell you that,” he said. “You see, when they couldn’t get anything out of me, except banalities, they decided that I was about the fool I looked, and just before a couple of thugs, armed to the teeth, bundled me off to the box-room, I heard a certain amount of what they said. Jesse Gideon had apparently gone carefully over the crowd, and prepared a dossier about each one of us. I came first on the list of people about which nothing was known, and the next was a girl. She wasn’t a friend of Petrie’s apparently, and the enemy couldn’t place her at all.”

===Chinese===
坎皮恩浅色的眼睛忽闪了一下。

“这个，我大概能告诉你。”他说，“你们想，当他们发现从我嘴里除了陈词滥调什么也掏不出来时，便认定我差不多就是我看上去的那个傻子。就在两个武装到牙齿的打手把我推推搡搡押去杂物间之前，我听到了他们谈话的一鳞半爪。显然，杰西·吉迪恩（Jesse Gideon）已把在场众人细细摸了一遍底，给我们每个人都备好了一份档案。在‘来历不明’的人员名单上，我名列榜首，而下一位是个姑娘。她看来不是皮特里的朋友，敌人压根儿摸不清她的来路。”

===Original===
“Who—who was that?”

Abbershaw was staring at the speaker, his eyes grown suddenly hard. A terrible apprehension had sent the colour to his face. Campion glanced at him curiously.

“That red-haired girl who met us in the passage when we came back from the garage. What’s her name—Oliphant, isn’t it? Meggie Oliphant. She’s the next to be for it, I believe.”

===Chinese===
“是——是谁？”

阿伯肖直勾勾盯着说话的人，双眼霎时变得冷硬。一阵可怕的预感把血色逼上了他的脸。坎皮恩好奇地瞥了他一眼。

“就是咱们从车库回来时，在走廊里撞见我们的那个红头发姑娘。她叫什么来着——奥利芬特，对吧？梅吉·奥利芬特。我相信，下一个就轮到她倒霉了。”

## XIII Abbershaw Sees Red / 第十三章 阿伯肖怒火中烧

===Original===
“My God, Abbershaw, he was right! They’ve got her!”

Ten minutes after Mr. Campion had first suggested that Meggie might be the next victim, Prenderby ran into Abbershaw in the corridor outside the girl’s room. “I’ve been all over the house,” he said. “The girls say that she went up to her room an hour ago to lie down. Now there’s not a sign of her about.”

===Chinese===
“天哪，阿伯肖，他说对了！他们把她抓走了！”

坎皮恩先生头一回提出梅吉可能成为下一个受害者，才过了十分钟，普伦德比就在姑娘房间外的走廊里撞上了阿伯肖。“我把整座宅子都找遍了，”他说，“姑娘们说，她一个钟头前上楼回房去躺一躺。现在四处都寻不见她的人影。”

===Original===
Abbershaw did not speak.

In the last few minutes his face had lost much of its cherubic calm. An entirely new emotion had taken possession of him. He was wildly, unimaginably angry.

Never, in all his life before, had he experienced anything that could compare with it, and even as Prenderby watched him he saw the last traces of the cautious methodical expert vanish and the new, impulsive, pugnacious fighter come into being.

===Chinese===
阿伯肖没有作声。

这几分钟里，他脸上那副孩童天使般的恬静已消失大半。一种全然陌生的情绪攫住了他。他狂怒如焚，怒得超乎想象。

平生从未有什么能与此相比；就在普伦德比的注视下，他眼看着那谨慎而井然有序的专家气派荡然无存，一个崭新的、莽撞好斗的斗士就此现形。

===Original===
“Michael,” he said suddenly, “keep an eye on Campion. His story may be absolutely true—it sounds like it—but we can’t afford to risk anything. Keep him up in my room so that he can hide in the passage if need be. You’ll have to smuggle food up to him somehow. Cheer the others up if you can.” Prenderby looked at him anxiously.

“What are you going to do?” he said.

Abbershaw set his teeth.

===Chinese===
“迈克尔，”他突然开口，“盯紧坎皮恩。他的说法也许千真万确——听上去像是真的——可我们担不起半点风险。让他待在我房间里，必要时他可以躲进暗道。你得想个法子偷偷给他送吃的上去。办得到的话，给大家打打气。”普伦德比忧心忡忡地望着他。

“你打算怎么办？”他问。

阿伯肖咬紧了牙。

===Original===
“I’m going to see them,” he said. “There’s been enough of this mucking about. There is going to be some sort of understanding, anyway. Damn it all! They’ve got my girl!” Turning on his heel he strode off down the passage.

A green-baize door cut off that portion of the house where Dawlish had established his headquarters. He passed through it without any interruption, and reached the door of the room that had once been Colonel Coombe’s bedchamber.

===Chinese===
“我要去见他们。”他说，“这种鬼混拖延已经够多了。无论如何，总得有个说法。见鬼！他们抓走了我的姑娘！”他脚跟一转，大步流星地顺着走廊去了。

一扇包着绿呢的门，把宅子里道利什设立总部的那一片隔了开来。他一路畅行无阻地穿了过去，来到一扇房门前——那间屋子，从前正是库姆上校的卧室。

===Original===
He tapped on it loudly, and it was opened immediately by a man he had never seen before, a heavy bull of a fellow whom he guessed to be one of the servants.

“What do you want?” he demanded suspiciously.

===Chinese===
他重重地叩门，门应声而开，开门的是个他从未见过的男人，一条公牛般的壮汉，他猜是那伙仆役中的一个。

“你要干什么？”他满腹狐疑地喝问。

===Original===
“Mr. Dawlish,” said Abbershaw, and attempted to push past him.

A single blow, violent as a mule kick, sent him flying back against the opposite wall of the corridor, and the giant glowered at him.

===Chinese===
“找道利什先生。”阿伯肖说着，便想从他身边硬挤过去。

只一拳，狠得像骡子踢的，把他打得倒飞出去，撞在走廊对面的墙上；那大个子对他怒目而视。

===Original===
“Nobody comes in ’ere,” he said. “Mr. Dawlish isn’t seeing anybody for another hour at least,” he added with a laugh that sent Abbershaw cold as he grasped its inference.

“Look here,” he said, “this is very important. I must get in to Mr. Dawlish. Does this interest you?”

He drew a notecase from his pocket as he spoke. The man advanced towards him and stood glaring down at him, his heavy red face darker than ever with anger.

===Chinese===
“谁也不许进来。”他说，“至少还有一个钟头，道利什先生谁也不见。”他补上这么一句，还笑了笑；阿伯肖咂摸出这话的弦外之音，登时遍体生寒。

“听着，”他说，“这事非常要紧。我必须进去见道利什先生。这个你可有兴趣？”

说话间，他从口袋里摸出一只皮夹子。那人朝他逼上前来，站在那儿居高临下地瞪着他，那张厚重的红脸膛气得比先前更加发黑。

===Original===
Suddenly his hand shot out and Abbershaw’s throat was encased in a band of steel.

===Chinese===
突然，那人手一探，阿伯肖的喉咙便被一道铁箍死死箍住。

===Original===
“You just ’aven’t realized, you and your lot downstairs, what you’re playing about wiv,” he said. “This ’ere isn’t no Sunday School hunt-the-thimble-set-out. There’s nine of us, we’re armed, and *he* isn’t jokin’.” The hand round Abbershaw’s throat tightened as the thug thrust his face close against his victim’s.

“ ’E ain’t ordered about by nobody. Makes ’is own laws, ’e does. *As* you’ll soon find out. At the moment ’e’s busy—talking to a lady. And when ’e’s done wiv ’er I’ll take your message in to ’im and not before. Now get out—if I ’aven’t killed yer.”

===Chinese===
“你还没闹明白，还有你楼下那伙人，你们招惹的是什么东西。”他说，“这儿可不是主日学校里玩‘找顶针’的那套把戏。我们这边有九个人，人人带着家伙，而他——可不是在闹着玩。”那歹徒把脸直凑到他这猎物的脸上，箍着阿伯肖喉咙的手越收越紧。

“他可不受任何人的差遣。他自己立他的法，他自成一套。这一点你很快就会领教。这会儿他正忙着呢——在跟一位女士谈话。等他把她打发完了，我再把你的话捎进去给他，早一步也不行。滚吧——要是老子还没宰了你的话。”

===Original===
On the last words he flung the half-strangled Abbershaw away from him as if he had been a terrier, and, reentering the room, slammed the door behind him, shooting home the bolts.

===Chinese===
说到最后几个字，他把扼得半死的阿伯肖像扔一条小猎犬似的随手抛开，自己转身回房，砰地摔上门，把门闩狠狠推到头。

===Original===
Abbershaw scrambled to his feet, flung himself against the door, beating it with his hands, in a paroxysm of fury.

At last he paused in despair: the heavy oak would have withstood a battering ram. He stood back, helpless and half-maddened with apprehension for Meggie’s safety.

Then from somewhere far away he fancied he heard a muffled cry.

===Chinese===
阿伯肖挣扎着爬起来，扑到门上，双手乱擂，狂怒得几乎发疯。

他终于绝望地住了手：这样厚重的橡木门，只怕攻城槌也奈何它不得。他退开几步站着，一筹莫展，为梅吉的安危揪心得几欲癫狂。

就在这时，他仿佛听见远处什么地方传来一声闷闷的哭喊。

===Original===
The effect upon him was instantaneous. His impotent fury vanished and he became once more cold and reasoning. His one chance of saving her was to get round the other way: to break in upon Dawlish’s inquisition from an unguarded point, and, once there, declare all he knew about the red wallet and the fate of its contents, regardless of the revenge the German would inevitably take.

Campion had been imprisoned conceivably somewhere near the room where Dawlish had dealt with him. It was just possible, therefore, that the passage through the cupboard would lead him to Meggie.

===Chinese===
这一声在他身上立竿见影。他那无能为力的狂怒霎时消散，他重新变得冷静而清明。要救她，他只有一条路：从另一边绕过去，趁一处无人把守的空当闯进道利什的审讯现场，一到那里，便把他所知的关于那只红皮夹及其内容物下落的一切统统说出，全然不顾那德国人必然要行的报复。

坎皮恩被囚禁的地方，多半就在道利什当初处置他的那间屋子左近。因此，穿过橱柜的那条暗道，说不定正好通到梅吉那里。

===Original===
He turned quickly: there was no time to be lost; even now Dawlish might be trying some of the same methods of urging a confession as he had employed upon Campion earlier in the day. The thought sickened him and he dashed down the passage into his own room.

Brushing the astonished Campion aside, he threw open the cupboard door and pressed against the back of the shelf steadily.

It gave before his weight and swung open, revealing a dark cavity behind.

===Chinese===
他霍然转身：片刻也耽搁不得了；说不定此时此刻，道利什正在对她试用白天用来对付坎皮恩的那些逼供手段。这念头让他一阵恶心，他飞奔着穿过走廊，冲进自己的房间。

他一把拨开满脸惊愕的坎皮恩，猛地拉开橱柜门，稳稳地朝搁板背面压去。

搁板被他的力道压开，旋了开来，露出后面一个黑黢黢的洞穴。

===Original===
He took out his pocket torch and flashed it in front of him. The passage was wood-lined and very dusty. Doubtless it had not been used for years before Campion stumbled upon it by chance that afternoon.

It was narrow also, admitting only just enough space for a man to pass along it, crawling on his hands and knees. But Abbershaw set off down it eagerly.

===Chinese===
他掏出手电筒，朝前面照了照。暗道四壁镶着木板，积尘深厚。毫无疑问，在那天下午坎皮恩偶然发现它之前，这里已多年无人通行。

暗道也窄，只容得下一个人手脚并用地爬行通过。但阿伯肖还是急切地爬了进去。

===Original===
The air was almost unbearably musty, and there was a scuttling of rats in front of him as he crawled on, shining the torch ahead of him as he went. At length he reached the steps of which Campion had spoken. They were steep and solid, leading straight up into the darkness which had opened above his head.

He mounted them cautiously, and a moment later found himself cut off by an apparently solid floor over him.

===Chinese===
空气中的霉味浓得几乎令人无法忍受；他一路往前爬，手里的电筒照向前方，身前不时响起老鼠奔窜的窸窣声。他终于到了坎皮恩说起过的那段台阶跟前。台阶陡而结实，笔直向上，通进头顶上方敞开的黑暗里。

他小心翼翼地拾级而上，片刻之后，头顶一方看似严丝合缝的地板把他拦住了。

===Original===
A closer examination, however, showed a catch, which, upon being released, allowed the trap to drop slowly open, so that he had to retreat some steps in order to avoid its catching him.

The machinery which Campion had referred to as a “piece of old bicycle” was in fact an ancient iron device, worked with a pedal, for opening the trap. As soon as he had lifted this hatch, Abbershaw hauled himself into the open space above it which he knew must be the chest itself. The lid was down, and he waited for some moments, breathless, listening. He could hear nothing, however, save the scuffling of the rats behind him, and at length, very cautiously, he put his hands above his head, pressed the lid up an inch or two, and peered out.

===Chinese===
不过细细一查，上面有个搭扣；搭扣一松，那块活板便缓缓落下张开，他只得倒退好几级台阶，才不致被它压着。

坎皮恩口中那段“老古董自行车零件”，其实是一台古老的铁机关，靠脚踏板操纵，专为开启这道活板。把舱门顶起之后，阿伯肖奋力攀进上方敞开的空间——他知道，那必定就是那口大箱子本身。箱盖合着，他屏息等了片刻，侧耳细听。然而，除了身后老鼠的窸窣声，什么也听不见；最后，他极其小心地双手举过头顶，把箱盖顶起一两英寸，向外窥探。

===Original===
No one appeared to be about, and he climbed silently out of the box. He was in a longish vaulted room, one of the relics of the days when Black Dudley had been a monastery. Its stone walls were unpanelled, and a small window high up was closely barred. It was, as Campion had said, used as a box-room, and filled with lumber of every description.

===Chinese===
四下似乎无人，他便悄然无声地从箱子里爬了出来。他置身于一间狭长的拱顶屋子，那是布莱克·达德利还是修道院的年代留下的遗迹之一。石墙不加护板，高处一扇小窗钉着密密的铁栏。正如坎皮恩所说，这屋子被用作杂物间，堆满了各式各样的破旧家什。

===Original===
Abbershaw looked round eagerly for a door, and saw it built almost next door to the fireplace in the wall opposite him.

It was small, iron, hinged, and very heavy.

He tried it cautiously, and found to his relief that it was unlocked. So Campion’s escape had been discovered, he reflected, and went warily. He let himself out cautiously; he had no desire to be apprehended before he reached Dawlish himself.

===Chinese===
阿伯肖急切地环顾寻找房门，只见它嵌在对面墙上，几乎紧挨着壁炉。

那门小小的，铁制，带铰链，非常沉重。

他小心地试着推了推，欣慰地发现门没有上锁。如此说来，坎皮恩的逃走已经被发觉了，他一面这样思忖，一面愈加警觉起来。他轻轻闪身出门；他可不想还没见到道利什本人就先被逮住。

===Original===
The door opened out on to a small stone landing in which were two similar doors. A steep spiral staircase descended almost at his feet.

He listened attentively, but there was no sound, and he decided that Dawlish’s inquisition could not be taking place on that floor. He turned down the steps, therefore, treading softly and hugging the wall. Once round the first bend, he heard a sound which made him stiffen and catch his breath—the muffled murmur of voices somewhere quite close. He went on eagerly, his ears strained to catch the first recognizable word.

===Chinese===
门开向外，通到一方小小的石头平台上，平台上另有两扇同样的门。一道陡峭的旋梯几乎就在他脚边向下延伸。

他凝神细听，四下无声，于是断定道利什的审讯不会在这一层进行。他便顺梯而下，脚下放轻，身子贴墙。刚转过第一个弯，他便听见一个声音，让他浑身一僵、屏住了呼吸——很近的地方，有人在含混地低语。他急急往前赶，耳朵绷得紧紧的，想捕捉第一个能听清的字眼。

===Original===
The stairs ended abruptly in a small oak door to the right of which a narrow passage led off into the darkness.

Through the door he could hear clearly Dawlish’s deep German voice raised menacingly.

===Chinese===
楼梯在一扇小橡木门前戛然而止，门的右边，一条窄窄的过道岔进黑暗深处。

隔着门，他能清清楚楚听见道利什那深沉的德国嗓音正汹汹扬起，句句带着威吓。

===Original===
Abbershaw took a deep breath, and pressing up the latch, carefully pushed the door open. It swung silently on well-greased hinges, and he passed through it expecting to find himself in the Colonel’s bedroom.

To his surprise he came out into what appeared to be a large cupboard. The air in it was insufferably hot, and it dawned upon him that he was in one of those hiding-places that are so often to be found in the sides of ancient fireplaces. Doubtless it was just such another cache that had swallowed up Campion when he disappeared off the hearthrug in the hall. Perhaps the mysterious passage behind him led directly down to that great sombre room.

From where he stood, every sound in the room without was distinctly audible.

===Chinese===
阿伯肖深吸一口气，托起门闩，小心翼翼地把门推开。门在上足了油的铰链上悄然转动，他跨进门去，一心以为自己将走进上校的卧室。

出乎意料，他走出来，进的似乎是一只大橱柜。里头的空气热得叫人无法忍受，他这才恍然：自己是钻进了古式壁炉侧壁里常见的那种藏身暗龛。毫无疑问，当初坎皮恩从门厅的炉前地毯上凭空消失，正是被这样一处暗龛吞没的。也许他身后那条神秘的暗道，就一直通到下面那间阴沉的大屋。

从他站立之处，外间屋里的每一个声响都听得清清楚楚。

===Original===
Dawlish’s voice, bellowing with anger, sounded suddenly quite near to where he stood.

“Speak!” it said. “What do you know? All of it—all of it. Keep nothing back.” And then, explosively, as if he had turned back to someone else in the room—“Stop her crying—make her speak.”

There was a soft, short, unmistakable sound, and Meggie screamed. A blinding flash of red passed before Abbershaw’s eyes, and he hurled himself against the wooden panel nearest him. It gave way before him, and he shot out into the midst of Dawlish’s inquiry like a hand grenade.

===Chinese===
道利什那怒吼着的嗓音，突然在他近旁炸响。

“说！”那声音喝道，“你知道些什么？统统说出来——统统。不许隐瞒半分。”接着又是一声爆喝，仿佛他已转回屋中另外什么人——“别让她哭了——叫她开口。”

随即响起一声轻而短促、绝不会听错的声响，梅吉尖叫起来。一道炫目的红光从阿伯肖眼前闪过，他朝离自己最近的那面木嵌板猛撞过去。嵌板应声而破，他像一颗手榴弹般直射出去，落进道利什审讯现场的正中央。

## XIV Abbershaw Gets His Interview / 第十四章 阿伯肖得到面谈

===Original===
When Abbershaw picked himself up he discovered that he was not in Colonel Coombe’s bedroom as he had supposed, but in a smaller and more luxurious apartment presumably leading off it.

It was lined with books, and had been used apparently as a study or library.

At a heavy oak table-desk set across one end sat Dawlish, his face mask-like as ever, and his ponderous hands resting among the papers in front of him.

Before him stood Jesse Gideon, looking down at Meggie, who sat on a chair; a man Abbershaw had never seen before leaning over her.

She had been crying, but in spite of her evident terror there was a vestige of spirit in her narrow brown eyes, and she held herself superbly.

===Chinese===
阿伯肖从地上爬起来时才发现，自己并不像先前以为的那样在库姆上校的卧室里，而是在一间更小、更奢华的屋子里——想必是与卧室相通的套间。

四壁排满书籍，显然是被用作书房或图书室。

一张沉重的橡木写字台横置在屋子一头，道利什坐在台后，那张脸依旧像面具一般毫无表情，一双厚重的大手搁在面前的文件堆里。

他面前站着杰西·吉迪恩（Jesse Gideon），正低头俯视坐在椅子上的梅吉；一个阿伯肖从未见过的男人逼俯在她身旁。

她哭过，但尽管惊惧分明可见，那双细长的棕色眼睛里仍残存着一丝倔强，她的仪态依然傲然挺拔。

===Original===
Abbershaw’s somewhat precipitate entrance startled everybody, and he was on his feet again before Dawlish spoke.

The German’s dull, expressionless eyes rested on his face.

“You,” he said, in his peculiarly stilted English. “How foolish you are. Since you have come out of your turn you may stay. Sit down.”

As the young man stared at him he repeated the last words violently, but without any movement or gesture.

The man was almost unbelievably immobile.

===Chinese===
阿伯肖这番急如星火的登场把屋里所有人都吓了一跳，还不等道利什开口，他已经重新站直了身子。

德国人那双死气沉沉、毫无表情的眼睛落在他脸上。

“你，”他用他那口生硬别扭得出奇的英语说道，“你多么愚蠢。既然你没轮到就自己出来了，那就留下吧。坐下。”

年轻人瞪着他，他便凶狠地重复了一遍最后几个字，却没有任何动作或手势。

这人简直不可思议地纹丝不动。

===Original===
Abbershaw remained where he was.

His anger was slowly getting the better of him, and he stood there stiffly, his flaming red hair on end and his round face white and set.

“I insist that you listen to me,” he said. “This terrorizing of women has got to stop. What are you gaining by it, anyway? Have you learnt anything of value to you from this girl?” His voice rose contemptuously. “Of course you haven’t. You’re making fools of yourselves.”

The German looked at him steadily, unblinkingly, not a muscle of his face moved.

===Chinese===
阿伯肖站在原地没动。

怒火渐渐压倒了他的自制，他僵立在那里，一头火红的头发根根倒竖，圆脸煞白绷紧。

“我要求你听我说，”他说，“这种拿女人来威吓的把戏必须停止。你们到底图到了什么？从这姑娘嘴里，你们问出什么对你们有价值的东西了吗？”他的声音带着轻蔑扬了起来。“当然没有。你们这是在自己出洋相。”

德国人定定地望着他，眼皮也不眨一下，脸上没有一块肌肉动弹。

===Original===
“Gideon,” he said, “tell me, who is this foolish redheaded young man who so loves to hear his own voice?”

Gideon glided forward obsequiously and stood beside the desk, his grey face and glittering eyes hideous beneath his white hair. He used his hands as he talked, emphasizing his words with graceful fluttering gestures.

“His name is George Abbershaw,” he said. “He is a doctor of medicine, a pathologist, an expert upon external wounds and abrasions with especial regard to their causes. In this capacity he has been often consulted by Scotland Yard. As a university friend of Wyatt Petrie’s, there is no reason to suppose that he came here with any ulterior motive.”

===Chinese===
“吉迪恩，”他说，“告诉我，这个如此爱听自己说话的蠢货红毛小子是谁？”

吉迪恩谄媚地趋步上前，站到书桌旁边，灰色的脸膛和白发下亮闪闪的眼睛显得阴森可憎。他说话时双手比画个不停，用优雅翻飞的手势为自己的字句添着重音。

“他名叫乔治·阿伯肖，”他说，“医学博士，病理学家，对外伤与擦伤——尤其着眼于其成因——是位专家。苏格兰场常以这一身份向他咨询。作为怀亚特·皮特里的大学同窗，没有理由设想他来此地怀有任何不可告人的动机。”

===Original===
The German continued to regard Abbershaw steadily.

“He is not a detective, *ja*?”

“No.” Gideon spoke emphatically. “That is obvious. English detectives are a race apart. They are evident at the first glance. No one who knew anything about the English Police Force could possibly suspect Dr. Abbershaw of holding any rank in it.”

The German grunted.

===Chinese===
德国人继续目不转睛地打量着阿伯肖。

“他不是侦探，*ja*（对吧）？”

“不是。”吉迪恩说得斩钉截铁，“这一点显而易见。英国侦探自成一族，第一眼便能认出来。凡是稍稍了解英国警察体制的人，绝不可能怀疑阿伯肖医生在警队中担任过任何职务。”

德国人咕哝了一声。

===Original===
“So,” he said, and returned to Abbershaw, “you are just an ordinary headstrong young man who, like the others downstairs, is under the impression that this affair is a melodrama which has been especially devised in order that they may have the opportunity of posing heroically before the young ladies of your party. This is an old house, suitable for such gaming, but I, one of the chief actors in your theatre, I am not playing.”

He paused, and Abbershaw was conscious of a faint change in his face, although he did not appear to have moved a muscle.

“What does it matter to me,” he continued, “if you hide yourselves in priestholes or spring upon me out of cupboards? Climb from one room to another, my friend, make yourself dusty in disused passages, attempt to run your motorcars upon alcohol: it does me no harm. My only interest is in a package I have lost—a thing that can be of no use to anyone but myself and possibly one other man in the world. It is because I believe that there is in this house someone who is in the employ of that other man that I am keeping you all here until I recover my property.”

===Chinese===
“如此说来，”他转向阿伯肖，“你不过是个寻常的莽撞小子，跟楼下那些人一样，以为这整桩事是一出特意编排的情节剧，好让你们有机会在同行的小姐们面前摆出英雄气概。这是一座老宅子，倒适合玩这类游戏。可是我——你们这座剧场里的主要演员之一——我可没有在演戏。”

他顿了顿，阿伯肖察觉到他脸上有一种隐微的变化，尽管他看上去连一块肌肉都没有动过。

“与我何干，”他继续说道，“你们是躲进神父藏身的暗龛，还是从橱柜里朝我扑出来？从一间屋子爬到另一间屋子，我的朋友，在废弃的通道里弄得一身灰，企图拿酒精当燃料去开你们的汽车：这些都伤不着我分毫。我唯一在意的，是我丢失的一件包裹——那东西除了我自己，至多再算上世上另外一个人，对谁都毫无用处。正因为我相信这座宅子里有人受雇于那另一个人，我才把你们全体扣在此地，直到我收回我的财物。”

===Original===
The dull, rasping voice stopped for a moment, and Abbershaw was about to speak when Dawlish again silenced him.

“To recover that property,” he repeated, “at *whatever cost*. I am not playing a game. I am not jumping out of cupboards in an attempt to be heroic. I am not pretending. I think the boy who attempted to drive off in his motorcar and the madman who escaped from the room upstairs where I had locked him understood me. The girl here, too, should begin to understand by now. And the rest of you shall be convinced even as they have been.”

Abbershaw’s anger had by no means died down under this harangue, and when he spoke his voice was frigid and very formal.

“If you carry out those threats, Herr Eberhard von Faber,” he said, “you will be wasting your time.”

Gideon started violently at the name, but the German did not appear even to have heard.

===Chinese===
那单调刺耳的嗓音停了片刻，阿伯肖正要开口，道利什又把他镇得噤了声。

“收回我的财物，”他重复道，“不惜*任何*代价。我不是在玩游戏。我不是为了逞英雄才从橱柜里跳出来。我也不是在装模作样。我想，那个企图开汽车逃走的毛头小子，还有那个从我锁他的楼上房间里逃出去的疯子，都已经明白我的意思了。这里的这位姑娘，到这会儿也该开始明白了。其余各位，也必将像他们一样被说服。”

这一通训斥下来，阿伯肖的怒气丝毫未消，开口时嗓音冷若冰霜、一板一眼。

“你要是把这些威胁付诸实施，埃伯哈德·冯·法贝尔（Herr Eberhard von Faber）先生，”他说，“那你就是在浪费自己的时间。”

听到这个名字，吉迪恩猛地一震，德国人却仿佛压根儿没有听见。

===Original===
“I had your packet,” Abbershaw continued bitingly.

They were listening intently, and he fancied he discerned a change in Dawlish’s dull eyes.

“And in the morning before you had the audacity to place us under this restraint I destroyed it in the grate in my bedroom.” He paused, breathless; the truth was out now, they could do what they liked with him.

The German’s reply came, very cold and as contemptuous as his own.

“In the present situation you cannot expect to be believed,” he said. “Do not they tell me after every crime in which great public interest is taken at least four or five imbeciles approach the police, confessing to it? Forgive me if I say that you remind me of one of those imbeciles, Dr. Abbershaw.”

He laughed on the last word, and the effect of the deep-throated chuckle emerging from that still expressionless face was curiously inhuman.

===Chinese===
“我确实拿到过你的包裹。”阿伯肖语带讥刺地继续说。

他们都在凝神细听，他仿佛辨出道利什那双死板的眼睛里起了一丝变化。

“就在今天早上，趁你们还没放肆到把我们软禁起来，我已经把它销毁在我卧室的壁炉里了。”他停下来，气喘吁吁；真相如今已经摊开，他们要拿他怎样都随他们的便。

德国人的回答来了，冷冰冰的，跟他自己的口气一样轻蔑。

“在眼下的处境里，你休想人家相信你的话，”他说，“每一桩万众瞩目的罪案过后，不是都有至少四五个蠢货跑到警方那里去自认凶手吗？请恕我直言，阿伯肖医生，你让我想起了那类蠢货中的一个。”

说到最后一个词时他笑了一声；那嗓音深沉的低笑从依旧毫无表情的脸上发出来，效果诡异得不似活人。

===Original===
Abbershaw thrust his hand into his pocket and drew out the red wallet. To his astonishment neither Dawlish nor his two subordinates betrayed any sign of recognition, and with a feeling approaching dismay he realized that this was not what they had visualized as the container of the thing they sought. He opened it, drew out his own papers, and laid the case upon the desk in front of the German.

“The papers you were looking for were sewn inside the lining of this wallet,” he said. “I ripped them out and destroyed them.”

There was silence for a moment after he had spoken, and Gideon leant forward and picked up the case in his pale, exquisitely tapering fingers.

“It is too small,” he pronounced at last, turning to the German.

Dawlish spoke without taking his eyes off Abbershaw. It was impossible to tell what he was thinking.

===Chinese===
阿伯肖把手插进衣袋，掏出那只红皮夹。令他错愕的是，道利什和他的两个手下都没有流露一丝认出来的神色；他带着一种近乎沮丧的心情意识到，他们心目中所寻之物的容器，并不是这个样子。他打开皮夹，取出自己的文件，把夹子放在德国人面前的书桌上。

“你们要找的那些文件，就缝在这只皮夹的衬里里，”他说，“我把它们扯出来销毁了。”

他话音落下，屋里静了片刻，吉迪恩俯身向前，用他那苍白、尖细得近乎精致的手指把皮夹拈了起来。

“太小了。”他终于断言道，转向德国人。

道利什说话时眼睛始终没有离开阿伯肖，谁也猜不透他在想什么。

===Original===
“If you are not lying, young man with red hair,” he said, “will you explain to me why you saw fit to destroy the papers that were concealed in that pocket-case? Did you read them?”

“They were in code,” said Abbershaw sullenly.

Gideon shot a swift glance at him under his bushy eyebrows, and then turned to Dawlish.

“Code?” he said. Still the German did not look at him, but remained staring at Abbershaw unblinkingly.

“There may have been a code message in the wallet,” he said, “and you may have destroyed it. But I do not think it is likely that it had anything to do with my business down here; unless …”

For the first time during that conversation he turned to Gideon. “Coombe,” he said, and there was sullen ferocity in his tone, “he may have succeeded at last.”

===Chinese===
“你要是没有撒谎，红头发的年轻人，”他说，“那你可否向我解释，你为什么认为有必要销毁藏在那只皮夹里的文件？你读过它们吗？”

“它们是用密码写的。”阿伯肖沉着脸说。

吉迪恩从浓眉底下飞快地瞟了他一眼，随即转向道利什。

“密码？”他说。德国人依旧不看他，只是目不转睛地盯着阿伯肖。

“皮夹里也许曾经有过一份密文，”他说，“你也可能把它毁掉了。但我认为它多半与我在此地办理的事务无关；除非……”

整场谈话中，他头一回转向吉迪恩。“库姆，”他说，语气里透着阴沉的凶狠，“他到头来也许终于得手了。”

===Original===
Gideon started.

“Double-crossed?” he said, and his voice died away in a question.

“We don’t know.”

The German spoke fiercely. “I have no faith in this young fool’s story—he’s only concerned with the girl. Is Whitby back yet?”

===Chinese===
吉迪恩一震。

“被出卖了？”他说，声音低下去，化作一个疑问。

“我们不得而知。”

德国人声色俱厉地说：“这个蠢货小子讲的话我一个字也不信——他满心只有那姑娘。惠特比回来了没有？”

===Original===
“No,” said Gideon. “We can’t expect him yet.”

“So.” Dawlish nodded. “We must keep them till he comes. He may be able to recognize this case. Whose initials are these?”

“Mine,” said Abbershaw. “You’ll find that they are clipped on at the back. I put them on myself.”

Gideon smiled.

“A very singular thing to do, Dr. Abbershaw,” he said. “And may I ask where you got this wallet?”

Abbershaw hesitated. For the moment he was in a quandary. If he told the truth he could hardly help incriminating Campion, and in view of that young man’s present condition it was inhuman to betray him.

===Chinese===
“还没有。”吉迪恩说，“眼下还指望不上他回来。”

“这样啊。”道利什点头道，“我们必须把他们扣到他回来为止。他也许认得出这只皮夹。这花押字母是谁的？”

“我的。”阿伯肖说，“你们会发现它们是从背后别扣上去的。是我自己装上去的。”

吉迪恩微微一笑。

“这可是件稀罕得出奇的事，阿伯肖医生，”他说，“我可否请问一句，您从哪儿得到这只皮夹的？”

阿伯肖迟疑了。他一时进退两难。若是说出实情，便免不了要牵连坎皮恩，而念及那位年轻人眼下的处境，出卖他未免太不近人情。

===Original===
“I found it,” he said at last, realizing at once how lame the explanation must sound. Gideon shrugged his shoulders. “This man is wasting our time,” he said. “No, it is Petrie you should examine, as I have told you all along. He’s just the type *they* would choose. What shall we do with these two?”

“Put them in the other room—not the one the young lunatic got out of,” said Dawlish. “You came through the passage from the fireplace in the hall, I suppose,” he added, turning heavily to Abbershaw, who nodded. “We must wait for Whitby to see this case,” he continued, “then we will consider what is to be done.”

The stranger who had been standing at Meggie’s side laid a hand on her shoulder.

“Come,” he said, jerking her to her feet.

===Chinese===
“我捡到的。”他终于说出口，随即就意识到这解释听起来必定多么站不住脚。吉迪恩耸了耸肩。“这个人在浪费我们的工夫，”他说，“不对，该审的是皮特里，这话我从一开始就在说。*他们*要挑的，正是他这种类型。这两个人怎么处置？”

“把他们关进另一间屋子——别用那个小疯子逃出去过的那间。”道利什说。“你是从门厅壁炉后面的暗道过来的，我料想。”他补充道，笨重地转过身来朝着阿伯肖，阿伯肖点了点头。“我们必须等惠特比来看看这只皮夹，”他接着说，“到那时再考虑如何处置。”

那个一直站在梅吉身旁的陌生人把一只手搭上她的肩头。

“走。”他说，猛一下把她拽了起来。

===Original===
Abbershaw turned on him furiously, only to find a revolver pressed against his ribs. They were heading towards the staircase behind the fireplace by which he had come, but when they reached the threshold Dawlish spoke again.

“Dr. Abbershaw,” he said, “come here.”

Unwillingly, the young man turned and stood before the desk, looking down at the florid Teutonic face with the dull corpse-like eyes.

“So you are an expert often referred to by Scotland Yard.”

The German spoke with curious deliberation.

===Chinese===
阿伯肖勃然大怒，转身就要扑向他，却发现一支左轮手枪顶住了自己的肋骨。他们朝壁炉后面的旋梯走去——他来时正是打那儿下来的——可刚到门槛，道利什又开了口。

“阿伯肖医生，”他说，“到这儿来。”

年轻人心不甘情不愿地转过身，站到书桌跟前，低头端详那张红润的日耳曼脸膛，和那双死气沉沉、宛如尸骸的眼睛。

“原来你就是苏格兰场时常咨询的那位专家。”

德国人说话时带着一种异样的斟酌。

===Original===
“I have heard of you. Your name has been mentioned in several cases which have interested me deeply. You gave evidence in the Waterside-Birbeck murder, didn’t you?”

Abbershaw nodded.

“And in the Sturges affair?”

“Yes.”

“Had it not been for you, Newman would never have been hanged?”

“Very probably not.”

===Chinese===
“我听说过你。好几桩令我深感兴趣的案子里，都提到过你的名字。沃特塞德–伯贝克谋杀案里出庭作证的，是不是你？”

阿伯肖点了点头。

“斯特吉斯那桩案子呢？”

“是我。”

“要不是因为你，纽曼就绝不会被绞死，对不对？”

“多半不会。”

===Original===
A slightly deeper colour seemed to flood the expressionless face.

“Three of my best men,” he said. “I am very glad to have met you, Dr. Abbershaw. Put them in the small room, Wendon, and lock the door very carefully. When I have a little more time to speak I have promised myself another interview with you, Dr. Abbershaw.”

===Chinese===
一层似乎更深浓的血色涌上那张毫无表情的脸。

“我手下三员最得力的人。”他说，“见到你我非常高兴，阿伯肖医生。把他们关进那间小屋，温登（Wendon），把门仔仔细细锁好。等我腾出一点说话的工夫——我已经给自己许下诺言，还要跟你再谈一次，阿伯肖医生。”

## XV Doctor Abbershaw’s Deductions / 第十五章 阿伯肖医生的推断

===Original===
The room into which Meggie and Abbershaw were thrust so unceremoniously in the middle of the night was one of the three which opened out on to the small winding staircase leading down to Colonel Coombe’s study.

It was comparatively empty, containing only a pile of disused tapestries and old curtains, two or three travelling trunks and a chair.

Here, as in the other room, the window was high up in the wall and iron-barred. There was a second door in the room but it appeared to be heavily bolted on the other side. Abbershaw made a thorough investigation of the room with his torch, and then decided that escape was impossible, and they sat down on the tapestry in silence.

===Chinese===
深更半夜，梅吉和阿伯肖就这样被不容分说地推进屋去；那间屋子是三间朝着同一道小旋梯开门的屋子之一，旋梯盘旋而下，直通库姆上校的书房。

屋里相当空旷，只有一堆弃置不用的挂毯和旧窗帘、两三只旅行箱，外加一把椅子。

这里的窗户同另一间屋里的一样，开在墙壁高处，装着铁栏。屋里另有一扇门，但看样子在那一边被人重重地闩上了。阿伯肖打着手电把屋子彻底查勘了一遍，断定逃跑无望，两人便一声不响地在挂毯上坐了下来。

===Original===
Until now they had not spoken very much, save for a brief account from Abbershaw of his interview with Campion and his journey through the passage from his cupboard. Meggie’s story was simpler. She had been seized on her way up to her room and dragged off through the green-baize door to be questioned.

Neither felt that much was to be gained from talking. The German had convinced them of the seriousness of their position, and Abbershaw was overcome with self-reproach for what he could only feel was his own fault. Meggie was terrified but much too plucky to show it.

As the utter silence of the darkness descended upon them, however, the girl laid her hand on Abbershaw’s arm. “We’ll be all right,” she murmured. “It was wonderful of you to come and get me out like that.”

===Chinese===
直到这时，他们都没说上多少话——阿伯肖只简单讲了他同坎皮恩的那次会面，以及从他那口柜子出发、一路穿行暗道的经过。梅吉的经历要简单些：她是在上楼回自己房间的半路被抓住，拖过绿呢门受审去的。

两人都觉得多说无益。那个德国人已经让他们认清了自身处境的严重，而阿伯肖满心自责——他只觉得这一切都是他一个人的过错。梅吉其实吓坏了，偏偏胆气又太足，绝不肯露出一丝怯意。

然而，当黑暗中那全然的沉寂沉沉罩下来时，姑娘把手搭上了阿伯肖的手臂。“我们会没事的，”她低声说，“你能那样闯进来救我，真是太了不起了。”

===Original===
Abbershaw laughed bitterly. “I didn’t get you very far,” he said.

The girl peered at him through the shadow.

“Oh, I don’t know,” she said. “It’s better up here than it was down there.”

===Chinese===
阿伯肖苦笑了一声。“我也没能把你救出多远，”他说。

姑娘隔着暗影端详着他。

“哦，那可说不准，”她说，“待在这儿，总比待在下面强。”

===Original===
Abbershaw took her hand and spoke with unusual violence. “My God, they didn’t hurt you?” he said.

“Oh no, nothing much.”

It was evident from her voice that she was trying to make light of a terrible experience. “I was frightened more than hurt,” she said, “but it was good to see you. Who are they, George? What are they doing here? What’s it all about?”

===Chinese===
阿伯肖握住她的手，语气激烈得异乎寻常。“天哪，他们没伤着你吧？”他说。

“哦，没有，没怎么伤着。”

听她的声音就知道，她是在竭力把一段可怕的经历说得轻描淡写。“我是吓得多、伤得少，”她说，“可是见到你真好。他们究竟是谁，乔治？他们在这儿干什么？这到底是怎么回事？”

===Original===
Abbershaw covered his face with his hands and groaned in the darkness.

“I could kick myself,” he said. “It’s all my fault. I did an absurd, a foolhardy, lunatic thing when I destroyed those papers. I didn’t realize whom we were up against.”

The girl caught her breath.

===Chinese===
阿伯肖双手捂住脸，在黑暗中呻吟了一声。

“我真想踹自己两脚，”他说，“全都怪我。烧掉那些文件的时候，我干了一件荒唐透顶、莽撞无谋、丧心病狂的事。我压根儿没弄明白，我们要对抗的对手是什么人。”

姑娘倒抽了一口气。

===Original===
“Then what you said was true?” she said. “You did destroy what they are looking for?”

“Yes.” Abbershaw spoke savagely. “I’ve behaved like an idiot all the way through,” he said. “I’ve been too clever by half, and now I’ve got you, of all people﻿—the person I’d rather die than see any harm come to﻿—into this appalling situation. I hit on the truth,” he went on, “but only half of it, and like a fool I acted upon my belief without being sure. Oh, my God, what a fool I’ve been!”

The girl stirred beside him and laid her head on his shoulders, her weight resting in the hollow of his arm. “Tell me,” she said.

===Chinese===
“那么，你说的都是真的？”她说，“他们要找的东西，你真的毁了？”

“是的。”阿伯肖狠声说道。“我从头到尾表现得像个白痴，”他说，“我自作聪明、聪明过了头，如今却把你——天下这么多人，偏偏是你，这个我宁可自己死、也不愿见你受半分伤害的人——拖进了这般可怕的境地。我撞见了真相，”他接着说，“却只撞见一半，又像个傻子似的，还没确证就照着自己的猜想行事了。噢，我的天哪，我干的这些蠢事啊！”

姑娘在他身旁动了动，把头靠上他的肩，身子的分量落进他臂弯里。“讲给我听，”她说。

===Original===
Abbershaw was only too glad to straighten out his own thoughts in speech, and he began softly, keeping his voice down lest there should be listeners on the landing behind the bolted door.

“It was Colonel Coombe’s murder that woke me up,” he said. “And then, when I saw the body and realized that the plate across his face was unneeded and served as a disguise, I realized then that it was crooks we had to deal with, and casting about in my mind I arrived at something﻿—not quite the truth﻿—but very near it.”

He paused and drew the girl closer to him.

“It occurred to me that Dawlish and Gideon might very well be part of the famous Simister gang﻿—the notorious bank thieves of the States. The descriptions of two of the leaders seemed to tally very well, and like a fool I jumped to the conclusion that they were the Simister gangsters. So that when the documents came into my hands I guessed what they were.”

===Chinese===
阿伯肖正求之不得，巴望着借说话把脑子里的头绪理理清楚，于是低声开了口，压着嗓音，唯恐那扇闩死的门外的平台上有人偷听。

“点醒我的是库姆上校的命案，”他说，“后来见到尸体，我明白了贴在他脸上的那块假面片纯属多余、实则是个伪装，那时我才意识到，我们打交道的乃是一伙匪徒。我在心里反复盘算，得出了一个结论——不完全是真相，但已非常接近。”

他顿了顿，把姑娘往身边揽了揽。

“我想到，道利什和吉迪恩很可能就是大名鼎鼎的西米斯特（Simister）团伙的人——美利坚那伙声名狼藉的银行大盗。其中两名头目的外貌描述都对得上号，我便像个傻子似的，一口断定他们就是西米斯特的匪徒。于是那几份文件落到我手里时，我猜出了它们是什么。”

===Original===
The girl looked at him.

“What were they?” she said.

Abbershaw hesitated.

“I don’t want to lay down the law this time,” he said, “but I don’t see how I can be wrong. In these big gangs of crooks the science of thieving has been brought to such perfection that their internal management resembles a gigantic business concern more than anything else. Modern criminal gangs are not composed of amateurs﻿—each man has his own particular type of work at which he is an expert. That is why the police experience such difficulty in bringing to justice the man actually responsible for a crime, and not merely capturing the comparatively innocent catspaw who performs the actual thieving.”

===Chinese===
姑娘望着他。

“是什么？”她说。

阿伯肖迟疑了一下。

“这一回我不想妄下断语，”他说，“可我看不出自己怎么会错。在这些大股的匪帮里，盗窃这门学问已经锤炼得炉火纯青，他们的内部管理不像别的，倒像一家庞大的商行企业。现代犯罪团伙绝非乌合之众——人人都有自己专精的一门行当。正因如此，警方才那样难于把一桩罪行背后真正的主谋绳之以法，往往只能逮住那个动手行窃、相对无辜、代人火中取栗的爪牙。”

===Original===
He paused, and the girl nodded in the darkness. “I see,” she said.

Abbershaw went on, his voice sunk to a whisper.

“Very big gangs, like Simister’s, carry this cooperative spirit to an extreme,” he continued, “and in more cases than one a really big robbery is planned and worked out to the last detail by a man who may be hundreds of miles away from the scene of the crime when it is committed. A man with an ingenious criminal brain, therefore, can always sell his wares without being involved in any danger whatsoever. The thing I found was, I feel perfectly sure, a complete crime, worked out to the last detail by the hand of a master. It may have been a bank robbery, but of that I’m not sure. It was written in code, of course, and it was only from the few plans included in the mass of written matter﻿—and my suspicions﻿—that I got a hint of what it was.”

Meggie lifted her head.

===Chinese===
他停了停，姑娘在黑暗中点了点头。“我明白了，”她说。

阿伯肖继续说下去，声音低成了耳语。

“像西米斯特这样特大的匪帮，把这种协作精神推到了极端，”他接着说道，“不止一桩真正的大劫案，其谋划与设计都推敲到了最末一个细节，而出谋划策的人在案发之时，可能远在离现场数百英里的地方。因此，一个长着机巧犯罪头脑的人，永远能把自己的‘货色’兜售出去，而自己丝毫不涉险。我找到的那件东西，我完全肯定，就是一桩由高手之手推敲到最末一个细节的完整罪案。它或许是一桩银行劫案，不过这一点我不敢肯定。它当然是用密码写成的，我之所以能约略窥见它的底细，靠的只是那一大堆文字材料里夹着的几张小图样，再加上我自己的疑心。”

梅吉抬起头来。

===Original===
“But would they write it down?” she said. “Would they risk that?”

Abbershaw hesitated.

“I admit that worried me at first,” he said, “but consider the circumstances. Here is an organization, enormous in its resources, but every movement of which is bound to be carried out in absolute secrecy. A lot of people sneer at the efficiency of Scotland Yard, but not those who have ever had cause to come up against it. Imagine an organization like this, captained by a mind simple, forceful, and eminently sensible. A mind that only grasps one thing at a time, but which deals with that one thing down to the last detail, with the thoroughness of a Hun.”

“Dawlish?” said Meggie.

===Chinese===
“可他们当真会把它写下来？”她说，“他们肯冒这个险？”

阿伯肖又迟疑了。

“我承认，起初这一点也让我犯愁，”他说，“可你想想这情形。有这么一个组织，财力人力浩大至极，可它的一举一动都必须在绝对保密中进行。许多人讥笑苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）效率低下，但凡有过缘由同它正面撞上的，绝不在此列。想象一下这样一个组织，由一个简单、强悍、极端务实的头脑来统领。这种头脑一次只抓一件事，却带着德国佬式的彻底，把那一件事办到最后一细节。”

“道利什？”梅吉说。

===Original===
Abbershaw nodded in the darkness.

“Yes,” he said. “Mr. Benjamin Dawlish is one of his names.” He paused, and then went on again with new enthusiasm, “Then imagine the brains of his gang,” he continued, “the man with the mind of a genius plus just that one crooked kink which makes him a criminal instead of a diplomat. It is most important that this one man of all others shall evade the police.”

Meggie nestled closer to him.

“Go on,” she said.

===Chinese===
阿伯肖在黑暗中点了点头。

“不错，”他说，“本杰明·道利什先生是他所用的名字之一。”他顿了顿，随即又来了新的兴致，接着往下讲。“再想象一下他手下匪帮的那个智囊，”他继续道，“这个人有着天才的头脑，偏偏又多了那么一道邪僻的弯子，正是这道弯子使他成了罪犯，而不是外交家。别人都在其次，唯独这个人万万不能落入警方之手，此点至关重要。”

梅吉朝他偎得更近了些。

“接着讲，”她说。

===Original===
Abbershaw continued, his voice hardly raised above a whisper, but intense and vehement in the quietness.

“He must be kept away from the gang then, at all costs,” he said. “So why not let him live at some out-of-the-way spot in the guise of an innocent old gentleman, an invalid, going out for long drives in his ramshackle old car for his health’s sake; but in reality changing his personality on the road and becoming for a few hours an entirely different person? Not always the same man, you understand,” he explained, “but adopting whatever guise seemed most suitable for the actual detail in hand. A respectable suburban householder eager to open a small account when it was necessary to inspect a certain bank manager’s office; an insurance man when a watchman was to be interviewed; a jovial, openhanded man-about-town when clerks were to be pumped. And all these different personalities vanishing into thin air as soon as their work was done, each one of them merging into the quiet inoffensive old invalid driving about in his joke of a car.”

His voice died away in the darkness, and Meggie stiffened.

===Chinese===
阿伯肖继续说，嗓音几乎压得比耳语还低，在寂静中却炽烈而激切。

“所以说，无论如何也得让他同匪帮隔开，”他说，“那么，何不让他扮成一个无辜的老绅士、一个离群索居的病老头，住在某个偏僻角落，为了养生的缘故，驾着那辆摇摇欲散的老爷车出门长途兜风；实则在路上改头换面，几个钟头里变成一个截然不同的人？你要明白，还不总是同一个人，”他解释道，“而是看手头要办的具体环节需要什么，就换上什么最合宜的伪装。需要察看某位银行经理的办公室时，他是个巴望开个小户头的体面郊区住户；要去找看门人问话时，他是个保险推销员；要从办事员嘴里套取情报时，他是个乐呵呵、出手大方的城中阔少。而这一重又一重的身份，差事一完便烟消云散，个个重新融回那个驾着那辆笑话一般的老爷车四处游荡、安安静静与人无争的老病号身上。”

他的声音在黑暗中渐渐消逝，梅吉浑身一僵。

===Original===
“The Colonel,” she whispered.

“Yes,” murmured Abbershaw. “I’m sure of it. He was the designer of the crimes. Dawlish organized them, and a carefully trained gang carried them out. The arrangements had to be written out,” he went on, “because otherwise it would entail the Colonel spending some considerable time with the gang explaining his schemes, whereas it was much better that they should not know him, or he them. You see,” he went on suddenly, “that’s what Dawlish had to guard against﻿—double-crossing. Old Coombe’s plans had a definite market value. They were worth money to any criminal gang who could get hold of them, and, as I have said, to minimize any danger of this, Coombe was kept here, practically as a prisoner, by Dawlish. I dare say the only time he saw any member of the gang was when Gideon and some other member as witness came down here to collect the finished scheme for one robbery, or to discuss the next. On such occasions it was Coombe’s practice to invite Wyatt to bring down a house-party as a blind to distract attention from any of his other visitors, who may in some cases have been characters ‘known to the police.’ ” He stopped and sighed. “So far,” he said, “I was practically right, but I had made one tremendous error.”

“And that?” The girl’s voice quivered with excitement.

===Chinese===
“上校，”她喃喃道。

“是啊，”阿伯肖喃喃道，“这一点我确信不疑。他就是那些罪案的设计者。道利什负责组织，一支精心训练的匪帮负责执行。那些方案所以必须写出来，”他接着说，“是因为不这样的话，上校就得花上相当多的时间同匪帮厮守在一处讲解他的计划，而他们不认识他、他也不认识他们，才要好得多。你要明白，”他忽然又说道，“道利什不得不防的正是这个——吃里扒外。老库姆的计划是有实实在在的市面价值的，哪个犯罪团伙到得了手，它们就值钱。也正如我方才所说，为了把这种危险压到最小，库姆才被道利什扣在这里，实际上形同囚徒。我敢说，他见匪帮成员的唯一时机，就是吉迪恩带着另一名成员充当见证人下来收取某一次劫案的成品方案、或是商量下一桩的时候。每逢这种场合，库姆照例邀怀亚特带一伙客人下宅子里来作幌子，好把旁人的注意力从他的其他访客身上引开——那些访客，有些恐怕正是警方‘挂了号’的人物。”他停下来，叹了口气。“到这里为止，”他说，“我基本都说对了，只是犯了一个天大的错误。”

“什么错误？”姑娘的声音因兴奋而发颤。

===Original===
“That,” said Abbershaw gravely, “was the fatal one of taking Dawlish for Simister. Simister is a rogue about whom there are as many pleasant stories as unpleasant ones, but about Eberhard von Faber no one ever laughs. He is, without exception, the most notorious, unsavoury villain this era has produced. And I have pitched us all﻿—you too﻿—into his hands.”

The girl repressed a shudder, but she clung to Abbershaw confidently.

“But why,” she said suddenly, “why didn’t they succeed? Why didn’t the Colonel give Dawlish the papers and the whole thing work out according to plan?”

===Chinese===
“那个错误，”阿伯肖神色凝重地说，“是致命的：我把道利什错当成了西米斯特。西米斯特这个恶棍，关于他的传闻，好听的和难听的正好一半对一半；可提起埃伯哈德·冯·法贝尔（Eberhard von Faber），从来没有人笑得出来。他是这个时代所造就的最臭名昭著、最不堪的恶棍，无一例外。而我，竟把我们大伙儿——连你也在内——一齐抛进了他的手心。”

姑娘强自压下一阵战栗，却依旧信赖地紧偎着阿伯肖。

“可是为什么，”她突然问道，“他们为什么没能得手？库姆上校为什么不把文件交给道利什，好让整件事照着计划顺顺当当地了结呢？”

===Original===
Abbershaw stirred.

“It would have done,” he said, “but there *was* double-crossing going on. The Colonel, in spite of his bodyguard﻿—Whitby and the butler﻿—must have got into communication with Simister’s gang and made some arrangement with them. I’m only guessing here, of course, but I should say that the Colonel’s plans were never allowed outside the house and that his attitude towards Simister must have been, ‘I will sell them if you can get them without implicating me.’ So Simister employed our friend, Mr. Campion, to smuggle himself into Wyatt’s party without being recognized by Dawlish.”

Meggie sat up. “I see,” she said, “but then, George, who murdered the Colonel?”

===Chinese===
阿伯肖动了动身子。

“本来是会那样的，”他说，“可这当中偏偏*就*有人在吃里扒外。上校尽管有他的护卫——惠特比和那个管家——必定还是同西米斯特一伙搭上了线，跟他们做成了某种安排。当然，这里头我只是猜测，可依我看，上校的方案从来就不许踏出这座宅子一步，而他对西米斯特的态度必定是：‘只要你们能拿到手、又不把我牵连进去，我就卖。’于是西米斯特雇了我们那位朋友坎皮恩先生，让他悄悄混进怀亚特的宾客中间，还不叫道利什认出来。”

梅吉坐起身来。“我明白了，”她说，“可那样的话，乔治，到底是谁杀了上校？”

===Original===
“Oh, one of the gang, of course﻿—evidently. When they discovered that he had double-crossed them.”

The girl was silent for a moment, then;

“They were very quick,” she said thoughtfully.

Abbershaw jerked his chin up. This was a point which it had never occurred to him to question.

===Chinese===
“哦，当然是匪帮里的某一个人——这是明摆着的。等他们发觉他吃里扒外，就下了手。”

姑娘沉默了片刻，随后——

“他们动作真快，”她若有所思地说。

阿伯肖猛地扬起下巴。这一点，他还从来没有想到要去质疑。

===Original===
“What do you mean?” he demanded.

Meggie repeated her former observation.

“They were very quick,” she said. “If the Colonel didn’t have a heart attack he was murdered when we were playing with the dagger. Before I had the thing in my hand, in fact. Did they see the old man part with the papers? And if so, why did they kill him and not Albert Campion?”

===Chinese===
“你这话是什么意思？”他追问。

梅吉把方才的话又说了一遍。

“他们动作真快，”她说，“倘若上校不是心脏病突发，那他就是死在我们传匕首玩的时候。事实上，是在那东西还没传到我手上之前。他们瞧见老头子交出文件了吗？若是瞧见了，为什么杀他，而不杀阿尔伯特·坎皮恩？”

===Original===
Abbershaw was silent. This point of view had not occurred to him. As far as he knew, apart from the single affair on the landing, they had not spotted Albert Campion at all.

“Besides,” said Meggie, “if you remember, Dawlish seemed to be surprised when something you said suggested that Coombe had double-crossed them.”

Abbershaw nodded: the incident returned to his mind. Meggie went on speaking, her voice very low.

“So Albert Campion was the murderer,” she said.

===Chinese===
阿伯肖沉默了。这个角度他未曾想到过。就他所知，除了楼梯平台上那唯一的一次遭遇，他们根本就没有识破过阿尔伯特·坎皮恩。

“再说，”梅吉道，“你要是还记得，你的话里透出库姆对他们吃里扒外的意思时，道利什分明显出的是吃惊。”

阿伯肖点了点头：那一幕又回到了他脑海里。梅吉接着往下说，声音压得很低。

“这么说，凶手就是阿尔伯特·坎皮恩了，”她说。

===Original===
Abbershaw started.

“Oh, no,” he said. “I don’t think that for a moment. In fact I’m sure of it,” he went on, as he remembered the scene﻿—it seemed incredible that it was only that afternoon﻿—when Mr. Campion had heard of the Colonel’s murder.
