“I’m sure of it,” he repeated, “and besides,” he added, as the extenuating circumstances occurred to him, “why should von Faber have taken all those precautions to conceal someone else’s crime?”

Meggie was silent at this, and Abbershaw continued. “There’s no doubt that the Colonel intended to cheat the gang,” he said. “The documents were exquisite pieces of work, written on the finest paper in a hand so small that it would have taken a reading glass to follow the words. It was in code﻿—not one I know, either﻿—and it was only the tiny plans that gave the clue to what it was. All sewn into the lining of a pocketbook which Dawlish didn’t recognize when I showed it to him. Oh, what a fool I was to destroy it!”

===Chinese===
阿伯肖吓了一跳。

“哦，不，”他说，“我片刻也没有这么想过。事实上，我确信不是他，”他接着说，一面回想起坎皮恩先生得知上校被杀时的那一场面——想来简直难以置信，那竟还是当天下午的事。

“我确信不是他，”他又说了一遍，“况且，”他补充道，这时那些替人开脱的情节浮上了他的心头，“冯·法贝尔又何必费尽那许多周折，去遮掩别人的罪行呢？”

梅吉听了这话沉默不语，阿伯肖继续说道：“毫无疑问，上校本来就打算坑这伙人，”他说，“那几份文件是精工细作的产物，写在上好的细纸上，字迹小得不拿放大镜就别想跟着认下去。通篇是密码——还不是我认得的那一种——全凭那几张小小的图样，才透出了它的底细。全都缝在一本皮夹子的夹层里，我拿给道利什看的时候，他竟没认出来。唉，我把它给毁了，我是个多大的傻瓜啊！”

===Original===
The regret in his tone was very poignant, and for some seconds the girl did not speak. Then she moved a little nearer to him as if to compensate him for any embarrassment her question might cause him.

“Why did you?” she said at last.

Abbershaw was silent for some time before he spoke. Then he sighed deeply.

===Chinese===
他话音里的悔恨痛切之极，姑娘有好几秒钟没有作声。随后她朝他身边又挪近了些，仿佛要弥补她那个问题可能带给他的难堪。

“你当时为什么要那样做？”她终于开口道。

阿伯肖沉默了好一阵子才说话。然后他深深叹了口气。

===Original===
“I was a crazy, interfering, well-meaning fool,” he said, “and there’s no more dangerous creature on the face of the earth. I acted partly on impulse and partly because it really seemed to me to be the best thing to do at the moment. I had no idea whom we were up against. In the first place I knew that if I destroyed it I should probably be preventing a crime at least; you see, I had no means, and no time, to decipher it and thereby obtain enough information to warn Scotland Yard. I didn’t even know where the bank to be robbed was situated, or if indeed it was a bank. I knew we were up against pretty stiff customers, for one man had already been murdered, presumably on account of the papers, but I had no idea that they would dream of attempting anything so wholesale as this.”

He paused and shook his head.

“I didn’t realize then,” he continued, “that there had been any double-crossing going on, and I took it for granted that the pocketbook would be recognized instantly. Situated as we were then, too, it was reasonable to suppose that I could not hold out against the whole gang, and it was ten chances to one that they would succeed in getting back their plans and the scheme would go forward with me powerless to do anything. Acting entirely upon the impulse of the moment, therefore, I stuffed the plans into the grate and set fire to them. That was just before I went down to speak to you in the garden. Now, of course, Dawlish won’t believe me, and if he did, I’m inclined to believe he would take his revenge upon all of us. In fact, we’re in a very nasty mess. If we get out of here we can’t get out of the house, and that Hun is capable of anything. Oh, my dear, I wish you weren’t here.”

===Chinese===
“我是个疯疯癫癫、爱管闲事、一片好心的傻瓜，”他说，“天底下再没有比这更危险的生物了。我一半出于冲动，一半是因为当时在我看来，那实在就是上策。我压根儿不知道我们对抗的是何等人物。首先，我知道毁了它，我至少多半能阻止一桩罪案；你要明白，我既没有法子、也没有工夫去破译它，从而得到足以警示苏格兰场的情报。我甚至不知道要遭劫的那家银行坐落在哪儿，甚至不知道它究竟是不是一家银行。我知道我们碰上的是相当难缠的主儿，因为已经搭上了一条人命，多半就是为了那几份文件；可我万万没有想到，他们竟会异想天开，干出这般大规模下手的勾当。”

他顿了顿，摇了摇头。

“我当时并不明白，”他接着说，“这当中有什么吃里扒外的勾当，我还想当然地以为那本皮夹子立刻就会被人认出来。再说，以我们当时的处境，合理推断是我一个人抵不过整整一伙人，十有八九他们会把计划夺回去，阴谋照旧推进，而我毫无办法。于是，全凭一时冲动，我把那些图样塞进炉栅，点了火。那正是在我下花园去找你说话之前。现在呢，道利什当然不会信我；就算他信了，我看他也多半会拿我们大伙儿泄愤。说实话，我们陷进了一个又糟又险的烂摊子。就算逃得出这里，也逃不出这座宅子，而那个德国佬什么手段都使得出来。噢，亲爱的，我真希望你人不在这里。”

===Original===
The last words broke from him in an agony of self reproach. Meggie nestled closed to his shoulder.

“I’m very glad I am,” she said. “If we’re in for trouble let’s go through it together. Look, we’ve been talking for hours﻿—the dawn’s breaking. Something may turn up today. Don’t these people ever have postmen or milkmen or telegram-boys or anything?”

Abbershaw nodded.

“I’ve thought of that,” he said, “but I think everyone like that is stopped at the lodge, and anyhow today’s Sunday. Of course,” he added brightly, “in a couple of days there’ll be inquiries after some of us, but it’s what von Faber may do before then that’s worrying me.”

===Chinese===
最后这几句话，是从满腔自我谴责的痛楚里迸出来的。梅吉往他肩头偎紧了些。

“我很高兴自己在这儿，”她说，“既然难逃此劫，咱们就一起熬过去。你看，咱们说了好几个钟头话了——天都快亮了。今天说不定会有什么转机。这户人家难道就从没有邮差、送奶工、送电报的孩子之类的上门吗？”

阿伯肖点了点头。

“这个我也想到过，”他说，“可我看，这类人一律都在门房那儿被截住了，再说今天毕竟是星期天。当然，”他语气一爽，补充道，“过不了两三天，就会有人来查问我们当中某几位的情况，可让我发愁的是，在那之前冯·法贝尔会干出什么来。”

===Original===
Meggie sighed.

“I don’t want to think,” she said. “Oh, George,” she added pitifully, “I’m so terribly tired.”

On the last word her head lolled heavily against his breast, and he realized with sudden surprise that she was still a child who could sleep in spite of the horror of the situation. He sat there with his back against the wall supporting her in his arms, staring out across the fast-brightening room, his eyes fixed and full of apprehension.

Gradually the room grew lighter and lighter, and the sun, pale at first, and then brilliant, poured in through the high window with that warm serenity that is somehow peculiar to a Sunday morning. Outside he heard the faraway lowing of the cattle and the lively bickering of the birds.

===Chinese===
梅吉叹了口气。

“我不想去想这些，”她说，“噢，乔治，”她可怜巴巴地加了一句，“我实在是累得要命。”

话到末一个字，她的头已经沉沉歪倒在他胸前。他蓦然一惊，意识到她终究还是个孩子——任凭处境多么骇人，也照样睡得着。他背靠着墙坐在那里，把她搂在怀中支撑着，凝望着越来越亮的屋子，目光呆定，满含忧惧。

屋子渐渐越来越亮，阳光起初是淡淡的，继而灿然夺目，从高窗里倾泻进来，带着一种不知怎的唯独星期天早晨才有的温暖安谧。屋外，他听见远处牛群的哞鸣，还有鸟雀活泼的斗嘴啁啾。

===Original===
He must have dozed a little in spite of his disturbing thoughts, for he suddenly came to himself with a start and sat up listening intently, his ears strained, and an expression of utter bewilderment on his face.

From somewhere close at hand, apparently in the room with the bolted door, there proceeded a curious collection of sounds. It was a hymn, sung with a malicious intensity, unequalled by anything Abbershaw had ever heard in his life before. The voice was a feminine one, high and shrill; it sounded like some avenging fury. He could make out the words, uttered with a species of ferocious glee underlying the religious fervour.

===Chinese===
尽管心绪纷扰，他想必还是打了一会儿盹，因为他猛地一个激灵回过神来，坐直了身子凝神细听，耳朵绷得紧紧的，脸上是一副全然茫然的神情。

就在近旁某处——分明是从那间门被闩死的屋子里——传出一串古里古怪的声音。那是一首圣歌，唱得怨毒而卖力，那股狠劲，阿伯肖有生以来闻所未闻。那是个女人的嗓音，又高又尖，听上去活像个前来索仇的复仇女煞。他听得清那歌词，字字句句都透着宗教狂热底下一种凶狠的快意。

===Original===
> “Oh vain all outward sign of grief,
 

 And vain the form of prayer,
 

 Unless the heart implore relief
 

 And Penitence be there.”

===Chinese===
> “哀恸徒然形之于外，
 

 祈祷徒然止于虚文，
 

 除非心底恳求解脱


 且有忏悔驻于其中。”

===Original===
And then with still greater emphasis:

> “We smite the breast, we weep in vain,
 

 In vain in ashes mourn,
 

 Unless with penitential pain﻿—”

===Chinese===
唱到这里，语气愈发加重：

> “我们捶胸，我们空自哭泣，
 

 披灰蒙尘也是枉然，


 除非以悔罪的疼痛——”

===Original===
The quavering crescendo reached a pinnacle of self-righteous satisfaction that can never be known to more forgiving spirits.

> “Unless with penitential pain
 

 The smitten soul be torn.”

===Chinese===
那颤抖着步步拔高的歌声，攀上了自义自满的极乐顶峰——这样一种顶峰，心地宽厚些的人是永远无从体味的。

> “除非以悔罪的疼痛
 

 受击的灵魂被撕扯。”

===Original===
The last note died away into silence, and a long drawn-out “Ah‑ha‑Ha‑men” followed it.

Then all was still.

===Chinese===
最后一个音符渐渐消逝，归于沉寂，随后又传来一声拖得老长的“阿——哈——哈——门”。

然后，万籁俱寂。

## XVI The Militant Mrs. Meade / 第十六章 好斗的米德太太

===Original===
“Good heavens, what was that?”

It was Meggie who spoke. The noise had awakened her and she sat up, her hair a little wilder than usual and her eyes wide with astonishment.

Abbershaw started to his feet.

“We’ll darned soon find out,” he said, and went over to the second door and knocked upon it softly.

“Who’s there?” he whispered.

“The wicked shall perish,” said a loud, shrill, feminine voice, in which the broad Suffolk accent was very apparent. “The earth shall open and they shall be swallowed up. And you won’t come into this room,” it continued brightly. “No, not if you spend a hundred years a-tapping. And why won’t you come in? ’Cause I’ve bolted the door.”

===Chinese===
“天哪，那是什么？”

说话的正是梅吉。那声响把她惊醒了，她坐起身来，头发比平时还要蓬乱几分，一双眼睛瞪得溜圆，满是惊诧。

阿伯肖一跃而起。

“咱们很快就能见个分晓。”他说着，走到第二扇门前，轻轻叩了叩。

“谁在那儿？”他压着嗓子问。

“恶人必灭亡。”一个响亮尖利的女人嗓音开了腔，那浓重的萨福克乡下口音一听便知。“大地必裂开，把他们吞下去。你们休想进这间屋子，”那声音接着说，口气倒还欢快，“想都别想，任你们敲上一百年也白搭。为啥进不来？因为门闩叫我闩上啦。”

===Original===
There was demoniacal satisfaction in the last words, and Abbershaw and Meggie exchanged glances.

“It’s a lunatic,” whispered Abbershaw.

Meggie shuddered.

“What a horrible house this is,” she said. “But talk to her George. She may know how to get us out.”

“Her chief concern seems to be not to let us in,” said Abbershaw, but he returned to the door and spoke again.

“Who’s there?” he said, and waited, hardly hoping for an answer, but the voice replied with unexpected directness:

“That’s a thing I won’t hide from anybody,” it said vigorously. “Daisy May Meade’s my name. A married woman and respectable. A churchgoing woman too, and there’s some that’s going to suffer for what’s been going on in this house. Both here, *and* in the next world. The pit shall open and swallow them up. Fire and brimstone shall be their portion. The Lord shall smite them.”

===Chinese===
最后那几个字里透着一股魔鬼般的快意，阿伯肖和梅吉对视了一眼。

“是个疯子。”阿伯肖悄声道。

梅吉打了个寒噤。

“这宅子真够吓人的，”她说，“不过乔治，你跟她搭搭话。她兴许知道怎么弄咱们出去。”

“她顶要紧的心思，瞧样子是不放咱们进去。”阿伯肖说。但他还是回到门前，又开了口。

“谁在那儿？”他问了一声，便等着，不指望能得到什么回答，谁知那声音竟干脆得出人意料：

“这事我不瞒人，谁也不瞒。”那声音中气十足。“戴西·梅·米德（Daisy May Meade）就是我的名字。结了婚的体面女人，还是个上教堂的女人。这宅子里干下的那些事，有些人是要遭报应的——阳世里遭一回，*来世*里再遭一回。深坑必要裂开，把他们吞进去。硫磺与烈火必作他们的份。主必击打他们。”

===Original===
“Very likely,” said Abbershaw dryly. “But who are you? How did you get here? Is it possible for you to get us out?”

Apparently his calm, matter-of-fact voice had a soothing effect upon the vengeful lady in the next room, for there was silence for some moments, followed by an inquisitive murmur in a less oracular tone.

“What be you doing of?”

“We’re prisoners,” said Abbershaw feelingly. “We’ve been shut up here by Mr. Dawlish, and are most anxious to get out. Can you help us?”

===Chinese===
“多半是这样。”阿伯肖淡淡地说。“可您是什么人？怎么到这儿来的？您有没有法子放我们出去？”

大概是他这平静务实的声音，对隔壁那位满怀复仇念头的妇人起了安抚之效——先是静了片刻，接着传来一声打探的嘟哝，那腔调少了些先知做派。

“你们这是干啥呢？”

“我们是囚徒。”阿伯肖说得恳切。“道利什先生把我们关在这儿，我们急着出去。您能帮帮我们吗？”

===Original===
Again there was silence for some moments after he had spoken, then the voice said considerately, “I’ve a good mind to have the door open and have a look at ye.”

“Good heavens!” said Abbershaw, startled out of his calm. “Do you mean to say that you can open this door?”

“That I can,” said the voice complacently. “Didn’t I bolt it myself? I’m not having a lot of foreigners running round me. I told the German gentleman so. Oh, they shall be punished. ‘To the devil you’ll go,’ I told them. ‘Fire and brimstone and hot irons,’ I said.”

“Yes, I know,” said Abbershaw soothingly, “but have you any idea how we can get out?”

===Chinese===
他话音落下，那头又静了片刻，然后那声音体贴地说道：“我倒真想开了门，瞧瞧你们俩是啥模样。”

“天哪！”阿伯肖失了镇定，脱口喊道。“您是说，您能把这扇门打开？”

“那可不。”那声音得意洋洋。“门闩不是我自个儿闩上的吗？我才不能让一帮子外国人围着我转来转去。我跟那位德国绅士就是这么说的。哼，他们必受惩罚。‘你们要下地狱去，’我跟他们说了。‘硫磺、烈火，还有烧红的烙铁。’”

“是，是，我知道。”阿伯肖安抚道，“可您知不知道，我们怎么才能出去？”

===Original===
A grunt of consideration was clearly audible through the door. “I will have a look at ye,” said the voice with sudden decision, and thereupon there began a fearsome noise of chains, bolts, and the scraping of heavy furniture, which suggested that Mrs. Meade had barricaded herself in with a vengeance. Soon after there was a creaking and the door swung open an inch or two, a bright black eye appearing in the crack. After a moment or so, apparently satisfied, Mrs. Meade pushed the door open wide and stood upon the threshold looking in on them.

She was a striking old woman, tall and incredibly gaunt, with a great bony frame on which her clothes hung skimpily. She had a brown puckered face in which her small eyes, black and quick as a bird’s, glowed out at the world with a religious satisfaction at the coming punishment of the wicked. She was clothed in a black dress, green with age, and a stiff white apron starched like a board, which gave her a rotundity of appearance wholly false. She stood there for some seconds, her bright eyes taking in every nook and corner of the room. Apparently satisfied, she came forward.

===Chinese===
门那边清清楚楚传来一声沉吟。“我要瞧瞧你们。”那声音突然拿定了主意。紧接着便响起一阵可怕的动静——铁链、门闩，还有沉重的家具在地上刮蹭，可见米德太太把自己堵在屋里，堵得那叫一个严实。不多时，门吱呀一响，开出一两寸宽的缝，一只乌亮乌亮的黑眼睛从门缝里露了出来。约莫过了一会儿，她像是看满意了，米德太太便将门整个推开，站在门槛上朝他们屋里张望。

她是个惹眼的老妇人，个子高高，瘦得叫人难以置信，一副嶙峋的大骨架上，衣裳稀稀拉拉地挂着。一张棕色打皱的脸，那双小眼睛又黑又灵活，像鸟儿似的，对着世人灼灼放光，为恶人即将临头的刑罚透出一股宗教式的快意。她身穿一条黑裙子，旧得年深月久都泛了绿，系一条浆得像木板一样硬挺的白围裙，倒叫她整个人显出一种全然不实的圆胖相。她在那儿站了几秒钟，亮晶晶的眼睛把屋里的旮旮旯旯扫了个遍。看样子是满意了，她这才走上前来。

===Original===
“That’ll be your sister, I suppose,” she said, indicating Meggie with a bony hand, “seeing you’ve both red hair.”

Neither of the two answered, and taking their silence for assent, she went on.

“You’re visitors, I suppose?” she demanded. “It’s my belief the devil’s own work is going on in this house. Haven’t I seen it with me own eyes? Wasn’t I permitted—praise be the Lord!—to witness some of it? It’s four shall swing from the gallows, their lives in the paper, before there’s an end of this business.”

The satisfaction in her voice was apparent, and she beamed upon them, the maliciousness in her old face truly terrible to see. She was evidently bursting with her story, and they found it was not difficult to get her to talk.

===Chinese===
“那位该是你妹妹吧，我寻思。”她用一只瘦骨嶙峋的手指着梅吉说，“瞧你们俩都是红头发。”

两人都没有作声，她把沉默当成了默认，便接着说了下去。

“你们是来作客的，我猜？”她质问道。“要我说，这宅子里干的，是魔鬼亲手做的勾当。我不都亲眼瞧见了吗？我不是蒙了恩准——赞美主！——亲眼见证了其中几桩吗？这档子事不到头，总有四个人要吊上绞架，他们那些个生平还要登到报纸上去呢。”

她声音里那股得意明明白白，她喜滋滋地望着他们，老脸上那副刻毒相，实在怕人得很。她显然满肚子的话憋得要炸开，他们发觉，要引她开口一点也不难。

===Original===
“Who are you?” demanded Abbershaw. “I know your name, of course, but that doesn’t make me much wiser. Where do you live?”

“Down in the village, three mile away,” said the redoubtable Mrs. Meade, beaming at him. “I’m not a regular servant here, and I wouldn’t be, for I’ve no need, but when they has company up here I sometimes come in for the week to help. My time’s up next Wednesday, and when I don’t come home my son’ll come down for me. That’s the time I’m waiting for. Then there’ll be trouble!”

There was grim pleasure in her tone, and she wagged her head solemnly.

“He’ll have someone to reckon with then, the German gentleman will. My son don’t hold with foreigners nohow. What with this on top of it, and him being a murderer too, there’ll be a fight, I can tell you. My son’s a rare fighter.”

“I shouldn’t think the Hun would be bad at a scrap,” murmured Abbershaw, but at the same time he marvelled at the complacency of the old woman who could time her rescue for four days ahead and settle down peacefully to wait for it.

===Chinese===
“您是什么人？”阿伯肖问。“您的大名我当然知道了，可这也不让我开窍多少。您住哪儿？”

“就在下头村子里，三英里地。”这位威风凛凛的米德太太答道，喜盈盈地望着他。“我不是这宅子里的正经佣人，我也不乐意当——我用不着。可他们上头一来了客，我有时就进宅帮上一礼拜的工。我这份工下礼拜三就到期了，到时候我没回家，我儿子就会来接我。我等的就是那个时辰。到那时候，就有热闹瞧了！”

她语气里带着一股狠津津的快意，庄严地晃着脑袋。

“到那时候，德国绅士就有硬对手要对付了。我儿子压根儿看不惯外国人，咋着都看不惯。再添上这桩事——他还是个杀人凶手——那可得干一仗，我告诉你吧。我儿子打架可是把好手。”

“我看那德国佬动起手来也差不到哪儿去。”阿伯肖咕哝道。可与此同时他又暗自称奇：这老太太把救援定在四天开外，居然能安安稳稳定下心来，慢慢等着。

===Original===
There was one phrase, however, that stuck in his mind.

“Murderer?” he said.

The old woman eyed him suspiciously and came farther into the room.

“What do you know about it?” she demanded.

“We’ve told you who we are,” said Meggie, suddenly sitting up, her clever pale face flushing a little and her narrow eyes fixed upon her face.

“We’re visitors. And we’ve been shut up here by Mr. Dawlish, who seems to have taken over charge of the house ever since Colonel Coombe had his seizure.”

===Chinese===
不过，有一个字眼儿钉在了他心里。

“杀人凶手？”他说。

老太太狐疑地上下打量他，往屋里又走进来几步。

“你知道些啥？”她质问道。

“我们告诉过您我们是谁了。”梅吉说着，蓦地坐直了身子，她那聪慧的苍白脸庞微微涨红，细长的眼睛紧盯着老太太的脸。

“我们是来作客的。我们叫道利什先生关在这儿——打库姆上校那场发作起，这宅子瞧样子就归他掌管了。”

===Original===
The old woman pricked up her ears.

“Seizure?” she said. “That’s what they said it was, did they? The fiery furnace is made ready for them, and they shall be consumed utterly. I know it wasn’t no seizure. That was murder, that was. A life for a life, and an eye for an eye, a tooth for a tooth, that’s the law, *and* they shall come to it.”

“Murder? How do you know it was murder?” said Abbershaw hastily. The fanatical forebodings were getting on his nerves.

Once again the crafty look came into the little black eyes and she considered him dubiously, but she was much too eager to tell her story to be dissuaded by any suspicions.

===Chinese===
老太太竖起了耳朵。

“发作？”她说。“他们是这么说的，是吗？烈火的窑已经为他们备下了，他们要被烧个寸草不留。我知道那压根儿不是啥发作。那是谋杀，就是那么回事。以命偿命，以眼还眼，以牙还牙，律法就是这个，他们*断乎*逃不掉。”

“谋杀？您怎么知道是谋杀？”阿伯肖急忙问道。那套狂热的凶兆预言，听得他神经直发紧。

那双小黑眼睛里又浮起狡黠的神色，她将信将疑地掂量着他，可她急着要讲她的事，什么疑心也拦她不住。

===Original===
“It was on the Friday night,” she said, dropping her voice to a confidential monotone. “After dinner had been brought out, Mrs. Browning, that’s the housekeeper, sent me upstairs to see to the fires. I hadn’t been up there more than ten minutes when I come over faint.” She paused and eyed the two defiantly.

“I never touch liquor,” she said, and hesitated again. Abbershaw was completely in the dark, but Meggie had a flash of intuition, born of long experience of Mrs. Meade’s prototypes.

“But as you weren’t well you looked about for something to revive you?” she said. “Of course. Why not?”

Mrs. Meade’s dubious expression faded.

“Of course,” she said. “What else was I to do?”

“What else indeed?” said Meggie encouragingly.

===Chinese===
“那是礼拜五夜里的事。”她说着压低了嗓音，变成一种咬耳朵式的单调。“晚饭撤下去之后，布朗宁太太（Mrs. Browning）——就是管家太太——打发我上楼照看炉火。我上楼统共没待上十分钟，就一阵发晕。”她顿了顿，挑衅地瞅着他们俩。

“我可是滴酒不沾的。”她说着，又迟疑起来。阿伯肖全然摸不着头脑，梅吉却霎时闪过一丝直觉，恍然明白了——那是多年结识米德太太这号人物换来的直觉。

“可您身子不爽利，总得四下里找点东西提提神吧？”她说。“那是自然的。有啥不对？”

米德太太脸上那副迟疑的神色消退了。

“可不，”她说，“不然我还能咋办？”

“可不是嘛。”梅吉鼓励道。

===Original===
“What did I do?” said the old woman, lapsing once more into the rhetorical form she favoured. “I remembered that in the Colonel’s study—that’s through his bedroom, you know—there was a little cupboard behind the screen by the window, where he kept a drop of Scotch whisky. That’s soothing and settling to the stomach as much as anything is. So, coming over faint, and being in the Colonel’s bedroom, I went into the study, and had just poured myself out a little drop when I heard voices, and the German gentleman with his friend Mr. Gideon and Dr. Whitby come in.” She stopped again and looked at Meggie.

“I didn’t holler out,” she said, “because it would have looked so bad—me being there in the dark.”

Meggie nodded understandingly, and Mrs. Meade continued.

===Chinese===
“我咋办的？”老太太说着，又落回她偏爱的那套自问自答的腔调。“我想起来，上校的书房里——就从他卧房穿过去，你们知道——窗户后头屏风背后有个小橱，他存着一口苏格兰威士忌。论压惊养胃，天底下啥都比不过它。就这么着，我一发晕，人又在上校卧房里，就进了书房，刚给自己斟上那么一小口，就听见有人说话，德国绅士带着他那位朋友吉迪恩先生，还有惠特比医生，一块儿进来了。”她又停下来，看了看梅吉。

“我没有嚷嚷，”她说，“因为那样子太难看了——我一个黑灯瞎火地待在那儿。”

梅吉会意地点点头，米德太太接着往下讲。

===Original===
“So I just stayed where I was behind the screen,” she said. “Mr. Gideon was carrying a lamp and he set it down on the desk. They was all very excited, and as soon as Dr. Whitby spoke I knew something was up. ‘What an opportunity,’ he said, ‘while they’re playing around with that dagger he’ll just sit where he is. We’re safe for fifteen minutes at least.’ Then the German gentleman spoke. Very brusque he is. ‘Get on with it,’ says he. ‘Where does he keep the stuff?’”

Mrs. Meade paused, and her little black eyes were eloquent. “Imagine the state I was in, me standing there with the bottle in me ’and,” she said. “But the next moment Dr. Whitby set me at peace again. ‘In the secret drawer at the back of the desk,’ he said. I peeked round the edge of the screen and saw ’im fiddling about with the master’s desk.” She fixed Meggie with a bright black eye. “I *was* upset,” she said. “If it hadn’t been for the whisky and the way it would have looked I’d ’ave gone out, but as it was I couldn’t very well, and so I stayed where I was, but I listened. For I said to myself, ‘The humblest of us are sometimes the ministers of the Lord,’ and I realized someone would have to be brought to justice.”

===Chinese===
“我就待在屏风后头没挪窝，”她说。“吉迪恩先生提着一盏灯，把它搁在书桌上。他们几个全都激动得很，惠特比医生一开口我就知道要出事。‘多好的机会，’他说，‘他们玩那柄匕首的当儿，他就稳稳当当坐在老地方。起码一刻钟里咱们没事。’随后德国绅士开了口。他那人说话冲得很。‘快办事，’他说。‘东西搁在哪儿？’”

米德太太停了停，一双小黑眼睛意味深长。“你们想想我那时光景——我一个人站在那儿，酒瓶子还攥在手里头，”她说。“可下一句话，惠特比医生就让我放了心。‘在书桌背后的暗屉里，’他说。我从屏风边儿上偷偷探头，瞧见他在老爷的桌子上一通乱摸。”她用一只亮晶晶的黑眼睛盯住梅吉。“我*真是*心慌啊，”她说。“要不是顾着那口威士忌，顾着那样子难看，我早出去了，可那会儿实在不便，只好原地待着，好歹我竖起了耳朵。因为我跟自己说：‘咱们这些最卑微的人，有时候也是主的执事。’我就明白了，总得有人把他们送上公堂。”

===Original===
Her self-righteousness was so sublime that it all but carried her hearers away with it, and she went on, whilst they listened to her, fascinated.

“I saw them open the drawer and then there was such a swearing set-out that I was ashamed. ‘It’s gone,’ said Mr. Gideon, and Dr. Whitby he started moaning like an idiot. ‘He always kept them here,’ he kept saying over and over again. Then the German, him that’s for Hell Fire as sure as I’ll be with the Lambs, he got very angry. ‘You’ve played the fool enough,’ he said, in such a loud voice that I nearly cried out and gave myself away. ‘Go and fetch him,’ he said. ‘Bring him up here. I’ve had enough of this playing.’”

Mrs. Meade paused for breath.

“Dr. Whitby’s rather a sullen gentleman,” she continued, “but he went off like a child. I stood there, my knees knocking together, wishing me breathing wasn’t so heavy, and praying to the Lord to smite them for their wickedness, while the German gentleman and Mr. Gideon were talking together in a foreign language. I couldn’t understand it, of course,” she added regretfully, “but I’m not an old fool, like you might imagine. Though I’m sixty-two I’m pretty spry, and I could tell by the way they was waving their hands about and the look on their faces and the sound of their voices that the German gentleman was angry about something or other, and that Mr. Gideon was trying to soothe ’im. ‘Wait,’ he said at last, in a Christian tongue, ‘he’ll have it on him, I tell you.’ Well! …” She paused and looked from one to the other of her listeners, her voice becoming more dramatic and her little black eyes sparkling. Clearly she was coming to the cream of her narrative.

===Chinese===
她那股自义正直崇高得无以复加，差点儿把听的人也一并卷了去。她接着往下讲，他们听得入了迷。

“我瞧着他们开了抽屉，跟着就是那一通咒天骂地，听得我都臊得慌。‘没了，’吉迪恩先生说，惠特比医生像个傻子似的哼哼起来。‘他一贯都搁在这儿的，’他翻来覆去就这一句。跟着那德国人——他那份人注定要下地狱的硫磺火，正如我笃定要归到羔羊群里去——他动了大气。‘你们胡闹够了，’他说，嗓门大得我差点叫出声来、露了馅。‘去把他带来，’他说。‘带到这儿来。这种把戏我受够了。’”

米德太太停下来喘口气。

“惠特比医生是个闷声闷气的绅士，”她接着说，“可他倒像个孩子似的撒腿去了。我站在那儿，两条膝盖直打磕绊，直嫌自己喘气儿太粗，一面求主击打他们、惩治他们的恶行——那当儿，德国绅士和吉迪恩先生正用外国话交谈。那话我自然听不懂，”她不无遗憾地补了一句，“可我也不是个老糊涂，别当我是。我今年六十二，腿脚还挺利索，他们怎么挥胳膊、脸上什么神色、嗓门是个啥动静，我一看便知：德国绅士为着什么事动了怒，吉迪恩先生在一旁劝他。‘等着，’他末了总算说了句基督教的正经话，‘我告诉你们，东西就在他身上。’好家伙！……”她顿了顿，目光从这一位扫到那一位，嗓门越说越带戏文味儿，一双小黑眼睛灼灼放光。显然，她这就讲到故事的关子上了。

===Original===
“Well,” she repeated, when she was satisfied that they were both properly on edge, “at that moment the door was flung open and Dr. Whitby came back, white as a sheet, and trembling. ‘Chief! Gideon!’ he said. ‘He’s been murdered! Stabbed in the back.’”

Mrs. Meade stopped to enjoy the full effect of her announcement.

“Were they surprised?”

Abbershaw spoke involuntarily.

“You be quiet and I’ll tell ’ee,” said Mrs. Meade, with sudden sternness. “They was struck silly, I can tell you. The German gentleman was the first to come to his senses. ‘Who?’ he said. Mr. Gideon turned on him then. ‘Sinisters?’ he says, as if asking a question.”

Meggie and Abbershaw exchanged significant glances, while Mrs. Meade hurried on with her narrative, speaking with great gusto, acting the parts of the different speakers, and investing the whole gruesome story with an air of self-righteous satisfaction that made it even more terrible.

===Chinese===
“于是，”她见两人都吊足了胃口，才满意地又吐出这个字眼，“就在那当口，门一下子给人推开，惠特比医生回来了，脸白得像张纸，浑身直哆嗦。‘首领！吉迪恩！’他说。‘他叫人杀了！背上挨了一刀。’”

米德太太住了口，好生享受她这条宣布的十足效果。

“他们吓着了没有？”

阿伯肖不假思索，脱口问道。

“你给我闭嘴，我才讲给你听。”米德太太突然厉声道。“他们全吓傻了，我告诉你吧。德国绅士头一个回过神来。‘谁？’他说。吉迪恩先生这才猛地转向他。‘辛尼斯特那伙人？’他说道，像是在发问。”

梅吉和阿伯肖意味深长地对视了一眼。米德太太只管滔滔不绝往下讲，讲得津津有味，把说话人一个个学得活灵活现，给这整个阴森的故事敷上一层自义得意的色彩，反倒叫它愈发可怕。

===Original===
“The German gentleman wasn’t pleased at that,” she continued, “but it was he who kept his head, as they say. ‘And the papers,’ said he. ‘Were they on him?’ ‘No,’ says the doctor. ‘Then,’ said the German gentleman, ‘get him upstairs. No one must leave the house till we get back the papers.’ ‘Don’t let anyone know he’s dead, then,’ said Mr. Gideon. ‘Say it’s heart attack—anything you like.’ ‘There’s blood about,’ said Dr. Whitby—‘bound to be.’ ‘Then clear it up,’ says Mr. Gideon. ‘I’ll help you. We must hurry before the lights go up.’”

On the last word her voice sank to a whisper, but the stagey horror with which she was trying to invest the story did not detract from the real gruesomeness of the tale. Rather it added to it, making the scene down in the lamplit panelled room seem suddenly clear and very near to them.

===Chinese===
“德国绅士听了那个，可不痛快，”她接着说，“可临危不乱、稳得住阵脚的，到底还是他。‘那几份文件呢，’他说。‘在他身上吗？’‘不在，’医生说。‘那么，’德国绅士说，‘把他弄上楼去。文件没弄回来，谁也不许出这宅子。’‘那就别叫任何人知道他死了，’吉迪恩先生说。‘就说是心脏病——随他们说啥都成。’‘四处都是血，’惠特比医生说——‘免不了的。’‘那就收拾干净，’吉迪恩先生说。‘我帮你一块儿干。得抓紧，赶在灯亮起来之前。’”

说到最后一个字，她的声音低成了气音。可她那副竭力往故事上敷的做戏似的恐怖，非但没损这段故事真正的骇人，反倒添了几分——楼下那间灯照之下的护壁板屋子里的一幕，霎时间显得清清楚楚、近在眼前。

===Original===
Meggie shuddered and her voice was subdued and oddly breathless when she spoke.

“What happened then?”

Mrs. Meade drew herself up, and her little black eyes burned with the fire of righteousness.

“Then I could hold my tongue no longer,” she said, “and I spoke out. ‘Whoso killeth any person, the murderer shall be put to death by the mouth of witnesses,’ I said, and stepped out from behind the screen.”

Abbershaw’s eyes widened as the scene rose up in his mind—the fanatical old woman, her harsh voice breaking in upon the three crooks in that first moment of their bewilderment.

“They were terrified, I suppose,” he said.

Mrs. Meade nodded, and an expression of grim satisfaction spread over her wrinkled old face.

“They *was*,” she said. “Mr. Gideon went pale as a sheet, and shrank away from me like an actor on the stage—Dr. Whitby stood there stupid like, his eyes gone all fishy and his mouth hanging open …” She shook her head. “You could see there was guilt there,” she said, “if not in deed, in the *heart*—the German gentleman was the only one to stay his natural colour.”

===Chinese===
梅吉打了个寒噤。她开口时，嗓音低沉，透着一股奇异的气短。

“后来呢？”

米德太太挺直了身子，一双小黑眼睛燃起正义的火。

“到那时我再也按捺不住，”她说，“就开了腔。‘凡杀了人的，那凶手必要凭见证人的口被治死，’我这么说着，就从屏风后头迈了出来。”

那情景在阿伯肖脑海里升了起来，他眼睛睁大了——狂热的老妇人，用她那粗厉的嗓音，劈头盖脸闯进三个歹徒惊惶失措的头一刻。

“他们吓坏了吧，我猜。”他说。

米德太太点了点头，一层冷峻的快意在她打皱的老脸上漾了开来。

“可不是，”她说。“吉迪恩先生脸白得像张纸，直往后缩，活像台上的戏子——惠特比医生傻站在那儿，眼神发直泛着死鱼眼似的呆光，嘴巴张着合不拢……”她摇了摇头。“一看就知道心里有鬼，”她说，“就算手上没干，*心里*也有鬼——德国绅士是独一个稳住本色的。”

===Original===
“And then?” Meggie hardly recognized her own voice, so toneless was it.

“Then he come up to me,” the old woman continued, with a return of indignation in her voice. “Slowly he come and put his great heavy face close to mine. ‘You be off,’ said I, but that didn’t stop him. ‘How much have you heard?’ said he. ‘All of it,’ says I, ‘and what’s more I’m going to bear witness.’”

Mrs. Meade took a deep breath.

“That did it,” she said. “He put his hand over my mouth and the next moment Dr. Whitby had jumped forward and opened the cupboard by the fireplace. ‘Put her in here,’ said he: ‘we can see to her after we’ve got *him* upstairs.’”

===Chinese===
“后来呢？”梅吉几乎认不出自己的声音——那声音平淡得了无生气。

“后来他就冲我过来了，”老太太接着说，语气里又涌上一股怒意。“他慢吞吞地走过来，把他那张又大又沉的脸凑到我脸跟前。‘你走开，’我说，可这拦不住他。‘你听见多少？’他说。‘全听见了，’我说，‘不但全听见了，我还要去作证呢。’”

米德太太深深吸了一口气。

“就是这句捅了娄子，”她说。“他一把捂住我的嘴，紧跟着惠特比医生蹿上前去，打开了壁炉边上的小橱。‘把她塞进这儿，’他说，‘等咱们把*他*弄上楼，再来发落她。’”

===Original===
“You struggled, of course,” said Abbershaw. “It’s extraordinary someone in the house didn’t hear you.”

Mrs. Meade regarded him with concentrated scorn.

“Me struggle, young man?” she said. “Not me. If there’s going to be any scrabbling about, I said to myself, better leave it to my son who knows something about fighting, so as soon as I knew where I was I hurried up the stairs and shut myself in here. ‘You can do what you like,’ I said to the German gentleman through the door, ‘but I’m staying here until Wednesday if needs be, when my son’ll come for me—then there’ll be summat to pay, I can tell you!’”

She paused, her pale checks flushing with the fire of battle, as she remembered the incident. “He soon went away after that,” she went on, wagging her head. “He turned the key on me, but that didn’t worry me—I had the bolts on my side.”

===Chinese===
“那您肯定挣扎喊叫了，”阿伯肖说，“怪的是，宅里居然没有一个人听见。”

米德太太用十足的轻蔑瞅着他。

“我？挣扎？小伙子你说的。”她说。“我才不呢。我心里寻思，真要动起手脚来，不如留给我那懂些拳脚的儿子来干，所以等我回过神来、弄清了自己在哪儿，就一溜烟跑上楼，把自己关进了这间屋。‘你们爱咋咋地，’我隔着门跟德国绅士说，‘可我就待在这儿，必要时等到礼拜三，等我儿子来接我——到那时候有他们好受的，我告诉你！’”

她停了停，想起这一幕，苍白的脸颊泛起临阵厮杀的火色。“打那以后他很快就走了，”她接着说，晃着脑袋。“他在外头把门锁了，可我不怕——我这头还有门闩呢。”

===Original===
“But you couldn’t get out?” interrupted Meggie, whose brain failed before this somewhat peculiar reasoning.

“O’ course I couldn’t get out,” said Mrs. Meade vigorously. “No more’n could he come in. As long as my tongue’s in my head someone’ll swing for murder, and I’m quite willing to wait for my son on Wednesday. They won’t get in to me to kill me, I reckon,” she continued, with a flicker of pleasure in her eyes, “and so when my son comes along there’ll be someone to help cast out the wicked. I ain’t a-holding my tongue, not for nobody.”

“And that’s all you know, then?” said Meggie.

“All?” Mrs. Meade’s tone was eloquent. “Some people’ll find it’s quite enough. Those three didn’t actually do the murder, but there’s someone in the house who did, and—” She broke off sharply and glanced from one to the other. “Why’re you two lookin’ at one ’nother so?” she demanded.

But she got no reply to her question. Meggie and Abbershaw were regarding each other fixedly, the same phrase in the old woman’s remark had struck both of them, and to each it bore the same terrible significance. “Those three didn’t actually do the murder, but there’s someone in the house who did.” Dawlish, Gideon, Whitby were cleared of the actual crime in one word; the servants were all confined in their own quarters—Albert Campion insisted that he locked the door upon them. Who then could be responsible? Albert Campion himself—or one of their own party? Neither spoke—the question was too terrifying to put into words.

===Chinese===
“可您自己出不去了呀。”梅吉插嘴道——这套别具一格的道理，让她的脑子一时转不过弯来。

“我当然出不去，”米德太太嗓门洪亮地说。“他也同样进不来，半斤对八两。只要我这条舌头还在嘴里，就总有人得为这桩凶案上绞架，我心甘情愿等到礼拜三，等我儿子来。他们进不来杀我，我料着呢，”她接着说，眼里闪过一丝快意，“等我儿子一到，就有人帮着把恶人赶尽除绝了。我这张嘴是封不住的，谁来说情也封不住。”

“那么，您知道的就这些了？”梅吉说。

“就这些？”米德太太这一声腔调，意味深长。“有些人会发现，这些就够他们受的了。那三个不是亲手行的凶，可宅子里有人干了，而且——”她猛地收住话头，目光从这一位扫到那一位。“你们俩咋这么瞪着眼对瞧呢？”她质问道。

可她的问题没有得到回答。梅吉和阿伯肖彼此直勾勾地对望着——老太太话里那同一句话，击中了他们两个，而在这两人心里，它有着同样可怕的分量。“那三个不是亲手行的凶，可宅子里有人干了。”道利什、吉迪恩、惠特比，一句话便洗清了动手行凶的嫌疑；仆人们全被关在他们自己的下处——阿尔伯特·坎皮恩一口咬定，那扇门是他亲手锁上的。那么，还能是谁呢？阿尔伯特·坎皮恩本人——还是他们自己一伙人中的某一个？两人都没有作声——这个问题太可怕了，可怕得没法说出口。

## XVII In the Evening / 第十七章 入夜时分

===Original===
The disturbing discovery which Meggie and Abbershaw had made in Mrs. Meade’s story silenced them for some time. Until the old woman’s extraordinary announcement ten minutes before, the division between the sheep and the goats had been very sharply defined. But now the horrible charge of murder was brought into their own camp. On the face of it, either Albert Campion or one of the young people in the house-party must be the guilty person.

Of course there was always the saving hope that in his haste Campion had locked one of the servants out instead of confining them all to their quarters as he had intended. But even so, neither Abbershaw nor the girl could blind themselves to the fact that in the light of present circumstances the odds were against the murderer lying in that quarter.

===Chinese===
梅吉和阿伯肖从米德太太的讲述里察觉的那个令人不安的发现，让两人沉默了好一阵子。直到十分钟前老太太抛出那番惊人之语以前，善人与恶人、绵羊与山羊的分野始终一清二楚。可如今，那可怕的谋杀罪名，竟落进了他们自己的阵营。从表面看，不是阿尔伯特·坎皮恩，就是这群来度周末的年轻人当中的某一个，必定是罪魁祸首。

当然，始终还有一线侥幸：坎皮恩情急之下，或许把哪个仆人锁在了外头，而没有像他本意的那样，把仆役们统统关进下房。可即便如此，无论阿伯肖还是姑娘，都无法对这样一个事实视而不见——照眼下的情形看，凶手出在那一边的可能性微乎其微。

===Original===
The entire staff of the house was employed by von Faber or his agents, that is to say that they were actually of the gang themselves. Coombe was an asset to them—it was not in their interests to kill him.

And yet, on the other hand, if the gang had not committed the murder they certainly covered up all traces of it. Mrs. Meade’s story had deepened the mystery instead of destroying it.

===Chinese===
宅子里的全体仆役都是冯·法贝尔或他的手下雇用的，也就是说，他们本身就是那伙匪帮的人。库姆上校在他们眼里是笔有用的资产——杀了他，对他们没有任何好处。

然而换一个角度看，倘若匪帮没有行凶，他们却分明掩盖了罪案的一切痕迹。米德太太的讲述非但没有解开谜团，反倒让它愈发扑朔迷离。

===Original===
Meggie looked at Abbershaw.

“If we could only get out,” she murmured. Abbershaw nodded briskly. Conjectures and theories could wait until afterwards; the main business in hand at the moment was escape, if not out of the house at least back to the others.

===Chinese===
梅吉望向阿伯肖。

“要是能出去就好了。”她低声说。阿伯肖利落地点了点头。推测和理论尽可以留待日后；眼下的头等大事是脱身——就算逃不出宅子，至少也要回到大伙儿中间去。

===Original===
He turned to the old woman.

“I don’t suppose there’s any chance of getting out through there?” he suggested, indicating the inner room in the doorway of which she still stood.

She shook her head.

===Chinese===
他转向老太太。

“从那儿出去，怕是没有一点指望吧？”他试探着说，一面朝里间示意——她还站在那间屋的门口。

她摇了摇头。

===Original===
“There’s nobbut a fireplace and a door,” she said, “and you’ll not get through the door because I’ve bolted it and he’s locked it. You can have a look at the fireplace if you like, but the chimney’ll only land you up on the roof even if you could get up it; best wait till Wednesday till my son comes.”

Abbershaw was inclined to enlighten her on the chances her son was likely to have against the armed Herr von Faber, but he desisted, and contented himself by shaking his head. Meggie, ever practical, came forward with a new question.

===Chinese===
“那屋里就一个壁炉、一扇门，”她说，“门你是过不去的，闩是我上的，锁是他们锁的。壁炉你只管瞧去，可烟囱就算你爬得上，顶多把你送上房顶；还是等星期三我儿子来吧。”

阿伯肖本想给她点明一下：她儿子对上带枪的冯·法贝尔先生，能有几成胜算。但他忍住了，只摇了摇头作罢。梅吉素来务实，上前提出了一个新问题。

===Original===
“But do you eat? Have you been starved all this time?” she said.

Mrs. Meade looked properly aggrieved.

“Oh, they bring me my victuals,” she said; “naturally.”

Apparently the event of her being starved out of her stronghold had not occurred to her. “Lizzie Tiddy brings me up a tray night and morning.”

“Lizzie Tiddy?” Abbershaw looked up inquiringly. “Who’s that?”

===Chinese===
“可您吃什么呢？这么些天一直饿着肚子吗？”她说。

米德太太显出一副当真受了委屈的样子。

“哦，他们给我送饭来着，”她说，“这不消说。”

看样子，自己会被活活饿出这处堡垒的事，她压根儿没想到过。“莉齐·蒂迪（Lizzie Tiddy）每天早晚各给我送一回托盘。”

“莉齐·蒂迪？”阿伯肖抬眼询问。“那是谁？”

===Original===
A smile, derisive and unpleasant, spread over the wrinkled face. “She’s a natural,” she said, and laughed.

“A natural?”

“She’s not right in her head. All them Tiddys are a bit crazed. Lizzie is the wust.”

===Chinese===
一道讥诮而不快的笑纹在那张布满皱纹的脸上漾开。“她是个天生的痴子。”说着她笑了起来。

“天生的痴子？”

“她脑袋不管用。蒂迪家的人个个都有些疯疯癫癫。莉齐要数最不济的一个。”

===Original===
“Does she work here?” Meggie’s face expressed her disapproval.

Mrs. Meade’s smile broadened into a grin, and her quick eyes rested on the girl.

“That’s right. No one else wouldn’t ha’ had her. She helps Mrs. Browning, the housekeeper, washes up and suchlike.”

===Chinese===
“她在这儿当差？”梅吉的脸色流露出不以为然。

米德太太的笑容咧得更开，一双尖利的小眼睛落在姑娘身上。

“可不是。换别家谁也不要她。她给管家布朗宁太太（Mrs. Browning）打下手，洗洗碗碟什么的。”

===Original===
“And brings up the food?” There was an eagerness in Abbershaw’s tone. An idiot country girl was not likely to offer much resistance if they made an attempt to escape as soon as she opened the door.

Mrs. Meade nodded.

“Ah, Lizzie brings up the tray,” she said. “She sets it on the floor while she unlocks my door, then I pull the bolts back and open it ever such a little, and then I pull the tray in.”

===Chinese===
“还管把饭送上楼？”阿伯肖的口气里透着一股急切。等那痴傻的乡下姑娘一开门就动手逃，她多半挡不住什么。

米德太太点了点头。

“是啊，托盘都是莉齐送上来，”她说，“她开我这扇门的时候，先把托盘搁在地上，我再拉开门闩，把门开开一条小缝，然后把托盘拽进来。”

===Original===
It was such a simple procedure that Abbershaw’s spirits rose.

“When does this happen?” he said. “What time of day?”

“Half after eight in the morning and half after eight at night.”

===Chinese===
这法子简单得很，阿伯肖的精神为之一振。

“这送饭都在什么时候？”他问。“一天里哪个钟点？”

“早上八点半，晚上八点半。”

===Original===
He glanced at his watch.

“She’s due now, then, practically?”

Mrs. Meade glanced up at the window. “Shouldn’t be at all surprised,” she agreed. “Light looks about right. I’ll go back to my own room, then, if you don’t mind. Best not to let anybody know that I’ve been havin’ any truck wi’ you.”

===Chinese===
他看了看表。

“这么说，眼下差不多就该她来了？”

米德太太抬眼望了望窗。“那可不，一点也不奇怪，”她应道，“天色瞧着差不离。那我就回自个儿屋里去了，你们别见怪。顶好别叫人知道我跟你们打过什么交道。”

===Original===
On the last word she turned her back on him, and after closing the door, connecting the two rooms, silently, they heard her softly pressing the bolts home.

“What an extraordinary old woman,” whispered Meggie. “Is she mad, do you think?”

Abbershaw shook his head.

===Chinese===
话音未落，她已转身背对着他；她无声地掩上连通两间屋的那扇门之后，两人听见她轻轻把门闩推上了。

“这老太太可真是个人物，”梅吉悄声说，“你说她是不是疯了？”

阿伯肖摇了摇头。

===Original===
“No,” he said. “I almost wish she were. But she’s certainly not crazy, and I believe every word of her story is absolutely true. My dear girl, consider—she certainly hasn’t the imagination to invent it.”

The girl nodded slowly.

“That’s true,” she said, and added suddenly, “but, George you don’t really believe that those dreadful men didn’t kill Colonel Coombe?”

===Chinese===
“没有，”他说，“我倒巴不得她是疯了。可她绝对没有疯，而且我相信她讲的每一个字都千真万确。亲爱的姑娘，你想想——她压根儿没有那份想象力，编不出这些来。”

姑娘缓缓点了点头。

“这倒是。”她说，随即又突然补上一句，“可是乔治，你该不会真相信，那些可怕的人没有杀库姆上校吧？”

===Original===
Abbershaw looked at her seriously.

“I don’t see why they should, do you?” he asked. “Think of it in the light of what we know.”

“Then that means that either Albert Campion or—oh, George, it’s horrible!”

===Chinese===
阿伯肖神情严肃地望着她。

“我想不出他们有什么理由要杀他，你想得出吗？”他反问，“照我们已经知道的这些情形掂量掂量。”

“那就是说，不是阿尔伯特·坎皮恩，就是——哦，乔治，太可怕了！”

===Original===
Abbershaw’s face grew even more serious.

“I know,” he said, and was silent for a minute or so. “But that is not what is worrying me at the moment,” he went on suddenly, as though banishing the thought from his mind. “I’ve got you into this appalling mess, and I’ve got to get you out of it—and that, unless I’m mistaken, is Lizzie Tiddy coming up the stairs now.”

===Chinese===
阿伯肖的脸色愈发凝重。

“我知道。”他说，随后沉默了一分钟左右。“可眼下让我发愁的还不是这个。”他陡然接着说，仿佛把那念头从脑子里赶了出去，“是我把你拖进了这个可怕的乱摊子，我就得把你弄出去——而且，要是我没弄错，这会儿正上楼来的，就是莉齐·蒂迪。”

===Original===
The girl held her breath, and for a moment or two they stood silent, listening. There was certainly the sound of footsteps on the stone landing outside, and the uneasy rattle of crockery on an unsteady tray. Abbershaw’s hand closed round the girl’s arm.

“Now,” he whispered, “keep behind me, and at your first opportunity nip out of here into the room immediately on your left and go straight for the chest I told you of. You can’t miss it. It’s in the corner and enormous. I’ll follow you.”

===Chinese===
姑娘屏住呼吸，两人静静站了片刻，侧耳倾听。外头石平台上分明响着脚步声，还有托盘端不稳时碗碟那阵不安分的磕碰。阿伯肖的手握住了姑娘的手臂。

“听好，”他压低嗓子说，“躲在我身后，一有机会就溜出这儿，进左手边紧挨着的那间屋，直奔我跟你说过的那口箱子。错不了，就在墙角，大得很。我随后就来。”

===Original===
The girl nodded, and at the same moment the key turned in the lock, and whatever hopes Abbershaw had entertained vanished immediately. The door opened some two inches, and there appeared in the aperture the muzzle of a revolver.

===Chinese===
姑娘点了点头。就在同一瞬间，钥匙在锁孔里一转，阿伯肖曾抱有的一切指望顿时化为乌有。门开了约莫两英寸，门缝里探进来一支左轮手枪的枪口。

===Original===
Abbershaw groaned. He might have known, he told himself bitterly, that her captors were not absolute fools. The girl clung to him and he could feel her heart beating against his arm. Gradually the door opened wider, and a face appeared above the gun. It was the stranger whom Dawlish had addressed as Wendon on the day before. He stood grinning in at them, the gun levelled directly at Meggie.

===Chinese===
阿伯肖暗暗叫苦。他苦涩地对自己说，早该想到，抓走她的人并非十足的蠢货。姑娘紧紧偎着他，他能感觉到她的心正抵着他的手臂怦怦直跳。门渐渐开大了些，枪上方露出一张脸——正是前一天道利什称作温登的那个陌生人。他站在门口冲他们咧嘴狞笑，枪口直指着梅吉。

===Original===
“Any monkey-tricks and the girl goes first,” he said. “It’s the Guvnor’s orders. He’s reserving you, mate, for ’is own personal attention. That’s one of the reasons why he’s feeding you. Now then, my girl, push the tray under and hurry about it.”

===Chinese===
“敢耍半点花招，先送这丫头上路。”他说，“这是头儿的命令。老兄，他把你留着，要亲自招呼。这也是他还管你饭的原因之一。行了，姑娘，把托盘从门底下塞进去，麻利点儿。”

===Original===
The last remark was addressed to someone behind him, although he never for a moment took his eyes off Abbershaw and the girl. There was a scuffling in the passage outside, and then a narrow tray appeared upon the floor. It came sliding towards them through the crack in the door, and Abbershaw was suddenly conscious of a pair of idiot eyes, set in a pale, vacant face, watching him from behind it.

===Chinese===
最后那句话是对他身后的什么人说的，可他的眼睛一刻也没离开过阿伯肖和姑娘。外面走廊里一阵窸窸窣窣，随后地板上出现了一只窄窄的托盘。托盘从门缝里朝他们滑进来，阿伯肖猛然察觉，托盘后头有一双痴傻的眼睛，嵌在一张苍白呆滞的脸上，正望着他。

===Original===
His impulse was to leap forward and risk the revolver, but the man had him helpless since it was Meggie whom he covered. Slowly the door closed, and on the moment that the gun disappeared Abbershaw sprang forward fiercely, but it was a forlorn hope. The heavy door slammed to, and they heard the lock shoot home.

===Chinese===
他一时冲动，想扑上去搏一搏那把左轮枪，可对方枪口罩着的是梅吉，他便全然无计可施。门缓缓关上，枪口刚一消失，阿伯肖便猛扑过去，然而这只是徒劳的指望。沉重的门砰地关死，他们听见锁舌咔嗒落进了锁槽。

===Original===
There was food on the tray: a pile of sandwiches, and a jug of water. Meggie stood listening for a moment, then she whispered sharply:

“George, they don’t take the same precautions with her. Perhaps if we got in there we could get past them.”

===Chinese===
托盘上有吃食：一堆三明治，一壶水。梅吉站着听了片刻，随即急促地低声道：

“乔治，他们对她可没这么防。咱们要是能进到她那间屋，兴许就能躲开他们。”

===Original===
Abbershaw darted across the room to the other door, then his face changed.

“She’s bolted us out, of course,” he said, “and besides, we’re too late now. We must wait till they come this evening. Oh, my dear, I’m so sorry I got you into all this.”

===Chinese===
阿伯肖疾步穿过房间奔向另一扇门，随即脸色一变。

“她当然把咱们闩在外头了，”他说，“再说，现在也迟了。只好等今晚他们再来。哦，亲爱的，把你拖进这一切，我实在过意不去。”

===Original===
The girl smiled at him, but she did not reply, and presently, since in spite of their precarious position they were very hungry, they sat down and began to eat.

===Chinese===
姑娘对他微微一笑，却没有作答。过了一会儿，两人顾不得处境凶险，肚子却实在饿了，便坐下来吃了起来。

===Original===
And then the long weary day dragged on. Mrs. Meade did not seem to be inclined for further conversation, and they knew that sooner or later Dr. Whitby and the man who had driven him must return, and the red-leather wallet be identified. What would happen then they could only conjecture, but since Dawlish was already prejudiced against Abbershaw he was not likely to be unmoved when he discovered the story of the burning of the papers to be true.

===Chinese===
随后，漫长难熬的一天缓缓挨了过去。米德太太似乎无意再多谈，而他们心里明白，惠特比医生和开车送他的那个人迟早要回来，那只红皮夹子迟早会被认出来。到那时会出什么事，他们只能瞎猜；不过道利什本来就对阿伯肖怀有成见，等他发现烧掉文件的说法确有其事时，断不会无动于衷。

===Original===
But it was Meggie’s position that chiefly disturbed Abbershaw. Whatever they did to him, they were not likely to let her return to civilization knowing what she did about them. The others, after all, so far as Dawlish knew, realized little or nothing of the true position. Campion had succeeded in convincing them that he was no more than the fool he looked, and they knew nothing of his disclosures to Abbershaw and Prenderby.

===Chinese===
然而最让阿伯肖心神不宁的，还是梅吉的处境。随他们怎么处置他本人，可她知道了他们这么多底细，他们多半不会放她重回文明世界。毕竟在道利什看来，其余的人对真实处境几乎一无所知。坎皮恩已成功让他们相信，他不过是他看上去的那个傻子，而他对阿伯肖和普伦德比吐露的那些话，他们更是毫不知情。

===Original===
The chances, therefore, were against them releasing the girl, and Abbershaw’s brain sickened at the thought of her possible fate. Escape was impossible, however, and there was nothing in the room that could in any way be manufactured into a weapon. The window, even had it been large enough to permit a man’s climbing through it, looked out on to a sheer drop of seventy feet on to the flags below.

There seemed nothing for it but to settle down and wait for Dawlish to make the next move.

===Chinese===
因此，他们放人的可能性微乎其微，一想到她可能落的下场，阿伯肖便五内俱焚。可逃跑全无指望，屋里也没有一样东西能改造成武器。那扇窗，就算大得容一个人爬出去，望出去也是七十英尺（约二十一米）直上直下的陡落，底下便是石板地。

看来别无他法，只能安下心来，等道利什走出下一步。

===Original===
As the morning passed and then afternoon without any change, save for a few martial and prophetic hymns from Mrs. Meade, their spirits sank deeper than ever; and it grew dark.

===Chinese===
上午过去了，下午也过去了，一切毫无变化——除了米德太太唱的那几段杀气腾腾、预言审判的圣歌——两人的情绪一落千丈；天也黑了下来。

===Original===
Clearly Whitby had not yet returned, and Abbershaw reflected that he might quite possibly have experienced some trouble with the cremation authorities, in which case there were distinct chances of the police coming to their rescue. He wondered, if that occasion should arise, what Dawlish would do—if he would remove Meggie and himself, or simply make a dash for it with his own gang, risking detection afterwards.

===Chinese===
惠特比分明还没有回来。阿伯肖暗自琢磨，他在火葬场那边多半遇上了些麻烦，若真如此，警方赶来搭救他们的机会倒着实不小。他纳闷，真到那个时候，道利什会怎么做——是把梅吉和他自己一并弄走，还是索性带着自己那伙人夺路而逃，豁出去事后被追查？

===Original===
On the face of it, he reflected, as he considered what he knew of the man, both from what he had heard and his own experience, the chances were against Meggie and himself being left to tell their story. The prospects looked very black.

===Chinese===
照表面情形看——他一面琢磨着自己对此人的种种了解，既有耳闻，也有亲历——他和梅吉被留下来讲述这段经历的指望，实在渺茫。前景一片漆黑。

===Original===
And then, quite suddenly, something happened that set his heart beating wildly with new hope, and made him spring to his feet with Meggie at his side, their eyes fixed upon the door, their ears strained to catch every sound.

From inside the room where Mrs. Meade had fortified herself, there came an extraordinary sound.

===Chinese===
就在这时，毫无征兆地，出了一桩事，令他心口狂跳，燃起新的希望；他霍地跳起身来，梅吉在他身侧，两人的眼睛死死盯住房门，耳朵绷紧了捕捉每一丝声响。

从米德太太严防死守的那间屋子里，传来一阵异样的动静。

===Original===
A gentle scraping followed by a burst of shrill indignation from the old woman herself, and the next moment, clear and distinct, a slightly nervous falsetto voice said briskly, “It’s all right, my dear madam, I’m not from the assurance company.”

===Chinese===
先是一阵轻轻的刮擦声，接着老太太自己爆出一串尖利的怒骂；下一刻，一个略微发虚的假高音清清楚楚、利利索索地说道：“没事的，亲爱的太太，我不是人寿保险公司派来的。”

===Original===
Meggie grasped Abbershaw’s arm.

“Albert Campion!” she said.

Abbershaw nodded: the voice was unmistakable, and he moved over to the inner door and tapped upon it gently.

===Chinese===
梅吉一把抓住阿伯肖的手臂。

“阿尔伯特·坎皮恩！”她说。

阿伯肖点了点头：这嗓音错不了。他走到里门跟前，轻轻叩了叩。

===Original===
“Campion,” he called softly, “we’re in here.”

“That’s all right, old bird, I’m coming. You couldn’t call the old lady off, could you?”

===Chinese===
“坎皮恩，”他轻声唤道，“我们在这儿。”

“这就好，老伙计，我这就来。你们能不能把这位老太太劝开？”

===Original===
Campion’s voice sounded a little strained.

“She seems to think I’m not the sort of person you ought to know. Can’t you tell her that many a true heart beats beneath a ready-made suit?”

===Chinese===
坎皮恩的嗓音听着有点吃力。

“她似乎觉得，我不是你们该结交的那种人。你就不能告诉她，现成的成衣底下，也照样跳动着一颗真心？”

===Original===
“Mrs. Meade.”

Abbershaw raised his voice a little.

“Mr. Campion is a friend of ours. Could you let him in to us?”

===Chinese===
“米德太太。”

阿伯肖稍稍提高了嗓音。

“坎皮恩先生是我们的朋友。您能放他到我们这边来吗？”

===Original===
“You keep strange company,” came the woman’s strident voice from the other side of the door. “A man that creeps down a chimney upon a body isn’t one that I’d put up with.”

Abbershaw and Meggie exchanged glances. Apparently Mr. Campion had descended from the skies.

===Chinese===
“你们结交的可真是些怪伴儿，”门那头传来女人尖利的嗓音，“打烟囱里爬到人家身上的男人，我可爱不了。”

阿伯肖和梅吉对视了一眼。看样子，坎皮恩先生是从天而降。

===Original===
Then the absurd voice came out to them again, raised a little in indignation.

“But even if your son is coming, my dear old bird,” he was saying, “there’s really no reason why my friends and I should not meet before that happy moment. After all, I too have a mother.” The exact significance of his last remark was not apparent, but it seemed to work like a charm upon the old woman, and with a few mumbled words she opened the door, and Albert Campion stood upon the threshold, beaming at them.

===Chinese===
接着，那荒腔走板的嗓音又传了出来，带上了几分愤愤然。

“可是，就算令郎当真要来，我亲爱的老太太，”他正说着，“也不见得我的朋友们和我，非得等到那个幸福时刻才能见面吧。毕竟，我也是有娘的人。”他最后这句话究竟是什么意思，谁也说不清，可在老太太身上却灵验得出奇；她嘟囔了几句，便开了门，阿尔伯特·坎皮恩站在门槛上，笑容满面地望着他们。

===Original===
“I don’t think I’ll come in,” he said cheerfully. “This lady seems crazy for me to meet her son and I’m afraid that she may compel me to do so by locking me in with you if I get far enough out of the room for her to shut this door. And as the laddie is not expected to call till Wednesday, I don’t want him to get his diploma from me in person. I think if you’re both ready, we’ll all go back the way I came.”

===Chinese===
“我想我就不进来了。”他乐呵呵地说，“这位太太一心巴望我跟她令郎见面，我怕的是，只要我离门口稍远，她就能关上这扇门，把我和你们锁作一处，逼得我非见不可。再说那位小哥要到星期三才登门，我可不想让他从我本人手里领受文凭。我看，你们两位要是都预备好了，咱们就照我来时的路回去吧。”

===Original===
“Down the chimney?” said Meggie, in some trepidation.

“Through the chimney,” corrected Campion, with pride. “I’ve been fooling about all day trying to find the ‘money-back’ handle—and now I’ve got the two coppers,” he added brightly, grinning at the two redheaded young people before him. “You can’t possibly dislike puns more than I do,” he went on hastily. “Let’s get back, shall we? This is an unhealthy spot.”

===Chinese===
“从烟囱下去？”梅吉说，颇有几分心惊。

“是穿过烟囱。”坎皮恩纠正道，颇有些得意。“我瞎折腾了一整天，就为找那个‘包退’的把手——如今总算把两枚铜子儿都到手了。”他喜洋洋地补了一句，冲着面前两个红铜色头发的年轻人咧嘴一笑。“你们再怎么讨厌双关语，也讨厌不过我。”他赶忙接着说，“咱们回去吧，好不好？这地方可不利于健康。”

===Original===
They followed him into the old woman’s room. She stood glaring at them suspiciously with her little bright eyes.

“Where are you going?” she demanded. “I don’t know as ’ow I ought to let ye go.”

===Chinese===
他们跟着他进了老太太的屋子。她站在那里，一双亮晶晶的小眼睛狐疑地瞪着他们。

“你们这是上哪儿去？”她盘问道，“依我说，真不知道该不该放你们走。”

===Original===
“Aren’t you coming with us?” said Meggie quickly. “Surely you want to get away from those dreadful men at once? You’ll be much safer with us.”

“What? And miss seeing my son beat ’em up?” said Mrs. Meade contemptuously. “Not me, miss. Besides,” she added sharply, “I don’t know as I’m not safer with the German gentleman than I am with a natural.” She pointed to Campion suggestively. “Lizzie Tiddy’s not the only half-wit in this house. Chimney-climbing—!” Her remark reminded them, as they turned to where an old stone fireplace, wide and primitive, stood on one side of the small room. It seemed at first utterly impracticable as a means of exit, but Campion led them over to it with a certain pride.

===Chinese===
“您不跟我们一起走吗？”梅吉急忙说，“您一定想马上离开那些可怕的人吧？跟我们在一起，您会安全得多。”

“什么？还错过看我儿子把他们揍扁？”米德太太不屑地说，“我不去，小姐。再说，”她尖刻地补了一句，“我说不好，跟那位德国绅士在一处，是不是倒比跟个痴子在一处还安稳呢。”她意有所指地点了点坎皮恩。“这宅子里的半个傻子，可不止莉齐·蒂迪一个。爬烟囱——！”她这话提醒了他们，两人转过身，只见小屋一侧立着一座老石砌壁炉，宽大而古朴。乍看之下，作为出路它全然不像样，坎皮恩却带着几分得意，把他们领到跟前。

===Original===
“Look,” he said. “It’s so simple when you think of it. The same chimney serves for both this room and the room behind it, which is no other, ladies and gentlemen, than the one which Mr. Campion performed his now famous disappearing trick in. Admission fourpence. Roll up in your hundreds. In fact,” he went on more seriously, “virtually speaking, both rooms have the same fireplace separated only by this little wall arrangement—quite low, you see—to divide the two grates, and topped by a thin sheet of iron to separate the flames.”

===Chinese===
“瞧，”他说，“仔细一想，简单得很。同一根烟囱，既伺候这间屋，也伺候后面那间——而后面那间，女士们先生们，不是别的，正是坎皮恩先生表演他那如今已名声大噪的遁术的那一间。门票四便士，欢迎各位成百成百地涌来。说实在的，”他接着说，口气正经了些，“确切讲，两间屋共用的是同一座壁炉，中间只有这么一截小隔墙——你们瞧，相当低——隔开两边的炉膛，上头再覆一张薄铁板，把两边的火分开。”

===Original===
He paused, and surveyed them owlishly through his horn-rimmed spectacles. “I discovered, all by myself and with no grown-up aid, that this natty device was removable. I lifted it out, and stepped deftly into the presence of this lady on my right, whose opening remark rather cooled my ardour.”

===Chinese===
他顿了顿，隔着角质框眼镜，煞有介事、猫头鹰似的打量他们。“我全凭自个儿一个人、没靠任何大人帮忙，发现这个精巧的机关是可以挪走的。我把它掀了出来，便轻巧地一步迈到了右边这位太太的跟前——她的开场白，可着实给我的热情泼了一瓢冷水。”

===Original===
“I said ‘The wicked shall be cast into hell,’” put in Mrs. Meade, “and so they shall. Into a burning fiery furnace, same as if that grate there was piled up with logs and you atop of them.”

This remark was addressed to Abbershaw, but she turned with tremendous agility upon Campion. “*And* the fools,” she said, “the Lord ’isself couldn’t abide fools.”

===Chinese===
“我说的是‘恶人必被扔进地狱’，”米德太太插嘴道，“他们也必定如此。扔进熊熊燃烧的火炉里，就跟那炉膛里堆满木柴、把你搁在上头一样。”

这话是说给阿伯肖听的，可她随即以惊人的敏捷转向坎皮恩。“*还有*傻子，”她说，“傻子连主他老人家自己都容不下。”

===Original===
Campion looked a little hurt.

“Something tells me,” he said in a slightly aggrieved tone, “that I am not, as it were, a popular hero. Perhaps it might be as well if we went. You’ll bolt your door again, won’t you?” he added, turning to the old woman.

“You may lay I will,” said she meaningly.

===Chinese===
坎皮恩显得有点受伤害。

“不知怎么的，”他微带委屈的口气说，“我好像算不上，怎么说呢，一位广受欢迎的英雄。咱们还是走吧。您会把门重新闩好的，对吧？”他又转身向老太太补了一句。

“你尽管打包票，我准闩。”她意味深长地说。

===Original===
“Are you sure you won’t come with us?”

It was Meggie who spoke, and the old woman eyed her less fiercely than she had done the others.

“Thank you, I’ll bide where I am,” she said. “I know what I’m up to, which is more than you do, I reckon, trapezing round with a pair of gorbies.”

===Chinese===
“您当真不和我们一起走？”

说话的是梅吉，老太太看她的眼神，比方才看其他人时温和了些。

“多谢了，我就守在我这儿，”她说，“我自个儿心里有数——依我看，比你们强：跟着一对乌鸦佬荡来荡去的。”

===Original===
Campion touched the girl’s arm.

“Come,” he said softly. “I thought I heard someone. I’ll go first, then you follow me.”

===Chinese===
坎皮恩碰了碰姑娘的手臂。

“来吧。”他轻声说，“我好像听见有人来了。我先走，你跟着我。”

===Original===
He stepped up on the stone hob as he spoke, and then swung his leg over the brick back of the grate which they now saw was little over three feet high, and disappeared out of sight. Meggie followed him, and Abbershaw sprang after her. Within three minutes they had emerged into the box-room and Campion raised the lid of the chest in the far corner.

Meggie suffered herself to be led down the dusty passage, Campion in front of her, and Abbershaw behind.

===Chinese===
说话间，他已蹬上石砌的炉台，一条腿挥过炉膛后面的砖背墙——他们这才看清，那墙不过三英尺来高——随即没了踪影。梅吉跟着过去，阿伯肖也一跃而上。不出三分钟，他们已钻出杂物间，坎皮恩掀开了远处墙角那口大箱子的盖子。

梅吉由他引着，走下那条积尘的暗道，坎皮恩在前，阿伯肖殿后。

===Original===
As they went, they heard the cracked voice of Mrs. Meade chanting vigorously to herself:

> “While the wicked are confounded
 

 Doomed to flames of woe unbounded,
 

 Call me with Thy saints surrounded.
 

 Ah‑ha‑Ha‑ha‑men.”

===Chinese===
一路走着，他们听见米德太太那沙哑的嗓音，正起劲地自顾自吟唱：

> “恶人尽皆仓皇失措，
 

 注定堕入无边火狱，
 

 求祢召我，列于圣徒。
 

 啊——哈——哈——阿——门。”

## XVIII Mr. Kennedy’s Council / 第十八章 肯尼迪先生的作战会议

===Original===
When Albert Campion and his two refugees crawled out at the far end of the passage, they found the cupboard door open and the entire crowd assembled in the bedroom without, waiting for them. Anne Edgeware threw herself across the room towards Meggie with a little squeaky cry that was part sympathy, part relief. Prenderby’s little Jeanne had not been a reassuring companion.

The strain of the last twenty-four hours had told upon them all. The atmosphere in the wide, old-fashioned room was electric, and Campion’s somewhat foolish voice and fatuous expression struck an incongruous note.

===Chinese===
阿尔伯特·坎皮恩带着他护送的两名落难者从暗道尽头爬出来时，发现橱柜门敞开着，全体同伴已聚在外间的卧室里等候他们。安妮·埃奇韦尔发出一声尖细的叫喊——半是同情，半是如释重负——横穿过房间扑向梅吉。普伦德比那位小小的让娜，实在算不上个能安人心的伴儿。

过去二十四小时的紧张煎熬，在每个人身上都留下了痕迹。这间宽大的老式房间里气氛一触即发，坎皮恩那略带傻气的嗓音和呆头呆脑的神情，便显得格格不入。

===Original===
“Goods as per instructions,” he said brightly, as he scrambled out of the cupboard. “Sign along the dotted line please.”

As soon as they were all in the room, however, he shut the cupboard door carefully, betraying that he was especially anxious that no sound should percolate through into the little box-room they had just left.

===Chinese===
“货品已按订单送达，”他从橱柜里爬出来时，喜洋洋地说，“请在虚线上签字。”

不过，等大家都进了屋，他便小心翼翼地关上橱柜门——这一手露了底：他格外担心声响会渗进他们刚刚离开的那间小杂物间去。

===Original===
Chris Kennedy was the first to speak. He was a little flushed, and there was an air of suppressed excitement about him that showed that his wounded arm no longer damped his spirits.

“Now we’re all here,” he said, “we can get right down to this thing and work out a scheme to get us out of here and those customers what they deserve. I’m for a fight.”

“Here, I say, hold on a minute, my son,” drawled Martin Watt, “let’s all start fair. What have you two lost souls been up to, first of all?” he went on, turning to Meggie and Abbershaw. “How did our little Albert get hold of you? No bickering, I hope?”

===Chinese===
克里斯·肯尼迪第一个开口。他脸上微微泛红，周身透着一股按捺不住的兴奋劲儿，可见臂上的伤已不再压他的锐气。

“现在人齐了，”他说，“咱们可以踏实下来好好琢磨这件事，订个脱身的法子，顺便叫那帮家伙得到应得的报应。我主张干一仗。”

“喂，我说，先慢着点儿，老弟，”马丁·沃特拖长了调子说，“咱们先公公平平从头来。头一件，你们两个落难的家伙干什么去了？”他转向梅吉和阿伯肖，接着问道，“咱们的小阿尔伯特是怎么寻着你们的？没拌嘴吧，我希望？”

===Original===
“No, all done by kindness,” said Mr. Campion cheerfully; “there was only one dragon in my path, a female of the species, and full of good words. Most of them new to me,” he added thoughtfully. The portion of Abbershaw’s story which the little doctor felt inclined to tell did not take very long. The others also had had their adventures; Martin Watt seemed to have instituted himself spokesman, and as soon as the other had finished he began.

“We’ve had sport, too, in our own way. Old Dachshund Dawlish has had us up one at a time, you know, heard our catechism and our family history, searched our pockets and let us go again. He has also locked us all up in the central big hall and had another go at our rooms. Old Prenderby tried to square a servant and got the business end of a gun in his tummy by way of retort. The girls have been overhauled by a ghastly old housekeeper woman and a loony maid. And last but not least, we had a confidential lecture from Gideon, who gave us the jolliest little character-sketch of his pal that one can imagine.”

===Chinese===
“没有，全凭好说好商量，”坎皮恩先生乐呵呵地说，“路上只遇着一条龙，还是这一族里的雌龙，满口良言。大半我都是头一回领教。”他又若有所思地补了一句。阿伯肖医生愿意讲的那一段经历，没花多长时间。其他人也各有各的历险；马丁·沃特俨然自封了发言人，前者话音一落，他便开了腔。

“咱们也自有一番消遣，各有各的玩法。老腊肠犬道利什把咱们一个个提了去，听了咱们的教义问答和家谱渊源，搜了咱们的口袋，又把人放了回来。他还把咱们统统锁进正中的大厅，把咱们的房间又搜了一遍。普伦德比老兄想收买一个仆人，得到的回话是枪口顶上了肚皮。姑娘们则被一个面目可憎的老管家婆和一个疯疯癫癫的女仆从头到脚搜检了一遍。最后压轴的一桩：吉迪恩给咱们上了一堂机密课，把他那位老伙计的为人速写了一通，那份生动劲儿，任谁想都想不出来。”

===Original===
He paused, and a faint smile at the recollection passed over his indolent face.

“According to him, the old boy is a cross between Mr. Hyde, Gilles de Rais, and Napoleon, but without the finesse of any of the three. On the whole I’m inclined to agree with him,” he continued, “but a fat lot of good it does him or us, for that matter, because he can’t find his package and we can’t get home to our mommas. I told him that, but he didn’t seem to see the argument. I’m afraid he’s rather a stupid man.”

Abbershaw nodded.

“Perhaps he is,” he said, “but at the same time he’s a very dangerous one. I may as well tell you fellows,” he went on, with sudden determination in his grey eyes, “there’s something that’s on my conscience. I had those papers—they were papers, as a matter of fact—the first morning we were down here, and I burnt them. I told him what I’d done when I went in to see him yesterday, but he wouldn’t believe me.”

He paused and looked round him. Campion’s pale eyes were goggling behind his enormous spectacles, and Wyatt met Abbershaw’s appealing glance sympathetically. The rest were more surprised than anything else, and, on the whole, approving.

===Chinese===
他顿了顿，回想起那一幕，懒洋洋的脸上掠过一丝浅笑。

“照他的说法，那位老兄是海德先生、吉尔·德·雷（Gilles de Rais）和拿破仑三者的杂糅，只是他们三位中任何一位的手腕都不曾承袭分毫。总的说来，我倒倾向于同意他，”他接着说，“可这话对他也好、对咱们也好，都没有半点用处：他的包裹寻不着，咱们也回不了家见妈妈。这话我跟他说了，他好像瞧不出这个道理。恐怕他是个相当愚蠢的人。”

阿伯肖点了点头。

“也许吧，”他说，“可与此同时，他也是个非常危险的人物。有件事不妨跟你们各位直说了，”他接着往下讲，灰色的眼睛里陡然现出决断，“我心里有桩事一直放不下。咱们下来的头一天早上，我拿到了那些文件——其实正是文件，不假——我把它们烧了。昨天我进去见他，把自己干的事告诉他了，可他不肯信我。”

他停住口，环顾四周。坎皮恩的一双浅色眼睛在硕大的眼镜后面瞪得溜圆；怀亚特迎着阿伯肖恳求的目光，眼色里满含同情。其余的人与其说是别的，不如说是吃惊，而且总的说来，是赞许的。

===Original===
Campion voiced the general thought.

“Do you know what they were—the papers, I mean?” he said, and there was something very like wonderment in his tone. Abbershaw nodded.

“They were all written in code, but I had a pretty shrewd idea,” he said, and he explained to them the outline of his ideas on the subject.

Campion listened to him in silence, and when he had finished glanced across and spoke softly.

“You burnt them?” he said dreamily, and then remarked, as if he had switched on to an entirely new subject, “I wonder if the smoke from five hundred thousand pounds in notes looks any different from any other sort of firing.”

===Chinese===
坎皮恩道出了众人共同的心事。

“你可知道它们是什么——我是说那些文件？”他说，语气里有种近乎叹服的惊奇。阿伯肖点了点头。

“它们全是用密码写的，不过我心里早有相当清晰的眉目。”他说着，把自己在这件事上的推断向众人概述了一遍。

坎皮恩默然听着，等他讲完，朝他瞥了一眼，轻声开了口。

“你把它们烧了？”他梦呓似的说，随即又冒出一句，仿佛拨到了一个全然无关的话题上，“我倒纳闷，五十万英镑的钞票烧起来的烟，跟别的什么东西烧起来的烟，瞧着可有什么不同。”

===Original===
Abbershaw glanced at him sharply.

“Five hundred thousand pounds?” he said.

“Why not?” said Campion lightly. “Half a crown here, half a crown there, you know. It soon tells up.”

The others turned to him, attributing the remark to his usual fatuity, but Abbershaw met the pale eyes behind the big spectacles steadily and his apprehension increased. It was not likely that Mr. Campion would be far out in his estimation since he knew so much about the affair.

Five hundred thousand pounds. The colossal sum brought home to him the extent of the German’s loss, and he understood the crook’s grim determination to recover the lost plans. He had not thought that the men were playing for such great stakes. In a flash he saw the situation as it really was, and his next words were sharp and imperative.

===Chinese===
阿伯肖锐利地瞥了他一眼。

“五十万英镑？”他说。

“怎么不可能？”坎皮恩轻飘飘地说，“这儿半个克朗，那儿半个克朗，你知道的，积少成多，很快就攒出数目来了。”

其余的人都朝他转过脸来，只当这话又是他惯常的蠢话，阿伯肖却稳稳迎住大眼镜后面那双浅色的眼睛，心里的忧虑愈加深重。坎皮恩先生既已知晓这桩事的这么多内情，他估出来的数目便断不至于离谱。

五十万英镑。这笔庞然巨款让他真切掂出了那位德国人损失的分量，他也明白了这个匪徒为何那样阴沉决绝，非把丢失的图纸找回来不可。他原没有想到，这伙人押上的是这么大的赌注。刹那间他看清了处境的真相，接下来出口的话斩钉截铁、不容分说。

===Original===
“It’s more important than I can say that we should get out of here,” he said. “In fact we’ve *got* to get out of here at once. Of course I know it’s been the idea all along, but now it’s imperative. At any moment now Whitby may return, and Dawlish will be convinced that I told him the truth yesterday. And then heaven only knows what he will do. Our one hope is to get out before Whitby comes back.”

“There’s only one way, I’ve been saying it all along.” It was Chris Kennedy who spoke. He was seated on the end of the bed, his knees crossed, and his young face alert and eager. “We shall have to make a straight fight for it,” he said. “It’s our only hope. No one trying to sneak out on his own to inform the local Bobby would have an earthly. I’ve thought of that. They’d spot us and we know they don’t mind shooting.”

===Chinese===
“咱们必须离开这里，这件事的重要，怎么说都不为过，”他说，“事实上，咱们*非得*立刻离开不可。我当然知道，脱身这主意大家从头就在打，可现在已经十万火急。惠特比随时可能回来，到那时道利什就会相信，我昨天对他说的全是实话。接下来天知道他会干出什么。咱们唯一的指望，就是在惠特比回来之前逃出去。”

“办法只有一个，这话我从头就说到了今天。”说话的是克里斯·肯尼迪。他坐在床沿上，两腿交叠，年轻的脸机警而热切。“咱们只好堂堂正正打出去，”他说，“这是唯一的指望。谁想独自个儿溜出去给当地警察报信，都是半点指望也没有。这一层我想过了。他们一眼就能盯上咱们，而咱们知道，他们动起枪来是毫不迟疑的。”

===Original===
“There’s a suit of armour in the hall,” suggested Campion suddenly. “I’ll put it on and toddle forth into the night, if you like. They could pot at me as much as they pleased. How about that?”

Abbershaw glanced at him sharply, but there was no trace of a sneer on the pleasant vacuous face, and he looked abashed when Kennedy spoke a little brutally.

“Sorry,” he said, without looking round, “we haven’t got time for that sort of stuff now. We’re in a devilish unpleasant situation and we’ve got to get the girls and ourselves out of it. I tell you, a straight fight is the only thing for it. Look here, I’ve got it all taped. We’ve got our first chance coming in a moment. We’ve had dinner every night so far, so I expect we can reasonably suppose that we’ll get it again tonight. Two fellows wait on us then. They’re both armed, we know, and judging from the way they treated Michael they know how to use their guns all right.”

“Why, they’re not very tricky, are they?” said Mr. Campion, a faint expression of surprise appearing in his face. “I understood you just pressed the trigger and—pop!—off it went.”

===Chinese===
“门厅里有一副盔甲，”坎皮恩冷不丁提议道，“你们要是乐意，我就把它穿上，迈着小步走进夜色里去。他们爱怎么朝我放枪就怎么放枪。这个主意如何？”

阿伯肖锐利地看了他一眼，可那张讨人喜欢的空茫脸上没有半点讥诮的痕迹；等肯尼迪说得有点粗暴地开口时，他反倒露出讪讪之色。

“抱歉，”他头也不回地说，“现在可没工夫扯这些。咱们的处境糟透顶了，得把姑娘们和咱们自己都弄出去。我跟你们说，堂堂正正打一仗是唯一的办法。听着，我已全盘筹划停当。咱们的第一个机会转眼就到。这几天每晚都开晚饭，所以咱们满可以合情合理地指望，今晚也会照开不误。到时候有两个家伙伺候咱们开饭。咱们知道他们都带着枪，而且从他们对付迈克尔的手段看，他们的枪使得可不含糊。”

“咦，那玩意儿不太难使吧？”坎皮恩先生说，脸上隐隐现出惊讶之色，“我还以为只要一扣扳机——砰！——它就响了。”

===Original===
Chris Kennedy granted him one withering look and went on with his scheme.

“There’s only one way to handle these customers, therefore,” he said. “The first thing is to overpower those two and get their guns. Six of us ought to be able to do that. Then the two best shots had better take those revolvers and scout round for the others. The important thing is, of course, that the first bit of work is done in absolute silence. I believe that once we get those two guns we can lay ’em all by the heels. We shall be prepared, we shall be organized—they won’t. What do you say?”

There was a moment or two of silence. Martin Watt was the first to speak.

“Well, I’m for it,” he said.

“So am I,” said Wyatt quietly.

===Chinese===
克里斯·肯尼迪赏了他一记能杀人的白眼，继续往下讲他的方案。

“所以，对付这帮家伙只有一条路，”他说，“头一件事，制服那两个，缴他们的枪。咱们六个人收拾他们俩，应当绰绰有余。然后让枪法最好的两位拿上左轮枪，去搜捕其余的人。要紧的当然是，头一拨活儿得干得悄没声息。我相信只要缴下那两杆枪，就能把他们一个个全都拿下。咱们是有备而来、有组织的——他们不是。你们说呢？”

沉默了片刻。马丁·沃特第一个开口。

“行，我赞成。”他说。

“我也赞成。”怀亚特轻声说。

===Original===
Abbershaw hesitated, and Prenderby too was silent, whilst Albert Campion remained mild and foolish-looking as if he were looking in on the scene from outside.

Abbershaw was thinking of Meggie. Prenderby too had his fiancée clinging to his arm. Mr. Campion appeared to be thinking of nothing at all.

“After all, it does seem to be our only chance.”

It was Prenderby who spoke, and the words stirred Abbershaw.

What the boy said was perfectly true. He turned to Kennedy.

“All right,” he said, “I’m with you.”

Kennedy looked pointedly at Albert.

“And you?” he said.

Albert shook his head. “Oh, I’m not standing out,” he said. “I don’t like these rough games, but I don’t shirk them when they’re thrust on me. What do we all do?”

===Chinese===
阿伯肖犹豫着，普伦德比也没作声，而阿尔伯特·坎皮恩依旧一副温吞傻相，仿佛只是隔在外头张望眼前这一幕。

阿伯肖心里想着梅吉。普伦德比也有未婚妻紧紧挽着他的手臂。坎皮恩先生呢，看上去什么都没想。

“说到底，这看来真是咱们唯一的机会了。”

说话的是普伦德比，这句话打动了阿伯肖。

这小伙子说的完全是实情。他转向肯尼迪。

“好吧，”他说，“算我一个。”

肯尼迪意味深长地盯着阿尔伯特。

“那你呢？”他说。

阿尔伯特摇了摇头。“哦，我可不是要置身事外，”他说，“这种粗野的游戏我不喜欢，可要是硬栽到我头上，我也不躲。咱们都分派些什么？”

===Original===
Mr. Kennedy appeared to have the whole plan clear in his mind.

“It’s quite simple,” he said, leaning his chin in his unwounded hand and bending forward, an intent expression in his eyes.

“Let *me* shape your career for you!” quoted Mr. Campion brightly. Kennedy reddened angrily and dropped the pose, but he went on doggedly.

“My idea,” he said, “is that three go down to dinner with the girls. I’m afraid they’ll have to come or the men will smell a rat. They start food, and the other three fellows wait outside the door until one of their laddies is at work on the side table and the other serving the dishes at the big table. At that moment someone knocks a glass on to the flags. That’s the signal. Then the blokes outside the door charge in and seize the carver. One of ’em gets his arm. Another stuffs a hanky in his mouth, and the third stands by to slog him over the head if necessary. Hang it, we can’t go wrong like that. The only thing is they mustn’t suspect us. We’ve got to take them by surprise. It’s the simplest thing going as long as we don’t make a row.”

“Yes,” said Mr. Campion, standing up with sudden solemnity. “A very clever idea, but what we have to ask ourselves is: Is it quite fair? Three men on to one. Come, come, we must remember that we are British, and all that. Perhaps we could each tie a hand behind our backs—or shall I offer them single combat instead?”

===Chinese===
肯尼迪先生显然早已把整套计划装在心里。

“简单得很，”他说，下巴支在没受伤的那只手上，身子前倾，眼里一副全神贯注的神情。

“让*我*来为你们规划前程吧！”坎皮恩先生亮着嗓子引用道。肯尼迪气得涨红了脸，撤掉了那副架势，可他仍旧执拗地讲了下去。

“我的想法是，”他说，“三位陪姑娘们下楼用饭。恐怕她们非到场不可，不然那些人要起疑。他们开始上菜，另外三位便候在门外，等到他们的小伙计一个在边桌上忙活、另一个在大餐桌上布菜。就在那一刻，有人把一只杯子碰翻到石板地上。这就是信号。随后门外的大汉们冲进去，按住那个切肉的。一个抱住他的胳膊，一个往他嘴里塞手帕，第三个在旁伺候，必要时照他脑袋来一家伙。见鬼，这么干错不了。唯一要紧的是不能叫他们起疑。必须打他们个措手不及。只要不出一点声响，这法子是再简单不过的了。”

“是啊，”坎皮恩先生说，神情陡然一派庄重地站起身，“主意非常高明，可咱们得问问自己：这公平吗？三个人收拾一个。得了得了，咱们可不能忘了自己是英国人，诸如此类。也许咱们该各自把一只手绑到背后去——要不，干脆由我向他们提出单打独斗？”

===Original===
Chris Kennedy rose to his feet, and walking across to Mr. Campion spoke quietly but vigorously.

Mr. Campion blushed.

“I didn’t think you’d take it like that. You will have it your own way, of course. I shan’t say anything.”

“You’d better not,” said Kennedy, and walked back to his seat. “Abbershaw, you, Michael, and Mr. Campion had better go down with the girls, and Wyatt, Martin, and I will wait for the signal of the broken glass. Who’s going to do that? It had better be a girl. Miss Oliphant, will you do it?”

Meggie nodded.

“As soon as one man is at the carving-table and the other serving us,” she said.

Kennedy smiled at her. “That’s it,” he said. “Now is that clear?” he went on, glancing around him, his eyes dancing with excitement. “Abbershaw, you get the bloke’s arms, Prenderby, you’re responsible for gagging the sportsman!—”

===Chinese===
克里斯·肯尼迪站起身，走到坎皮恩先生跟前，话说得平静，分量却十足。

坎皮恩先生红了脸。

“没想到你会往那处想。当然，一切照你们的办。我什么也不说了。”

“你最好别说。”肯尼迪说罢走回自己的座位，“阿伯肖，你、迈克尔和坎皮恩先生最好陪姑娘们下楼；怀亚特、马丁和我候着碎杯的信号。谁来摔这一杯？最好是个姑娘。奥利芬特小姐，你来行吗？”

梅吉点了点头。

“等他们一个到了切肉桌边、另一个正给我们上菜，我就动手。”她说。

肯尼迪朝她一笑。“这就对了，”他说，“都清楚了吗？”他环视众人，接着分派，眼睛兴奋得闪闪发亮，“阿伯肖，你抱住那家伙的双臂；普伦德比，塞住这位运动健将的嘴，归你负责！——”

===Original===
“Yes?” said Campion, who was apparently gibbering with excitement. “And what can I do?”

“You stand by,” said Kennedy, with something suspiciously like a sneer on his handsome young face.

“Oh, very well,” said Mr. Campion, looking considerably disappointed. “I’ll stand ready to dot the fellow with a bottle if necessary.”

“That’s the idea,” agreed Chris Kennedy somewhat grudgingly, and returned to the others. “Of course,” he said, “it’ll be a bit of a shock for the two lackey-thugs to see you all turning up bright and happy after your adventures; still, I think the idea is to walk in as if nothing had ever happened. You can indulge in a certain amount of bright conversation if you like, to put them off the scent. That’s where you’ll come in useful,” he added, turning to Campion. “Talk as much as you like. That’s the time to be funny.”

“Righto,” said Mr. Campion, brightening visibly. “I’ll show them my two-headed penny. I’ll be awfully witty. ‘They laughed when I sat down at the piano, but when I began to play they knew at once that I had taken Kennedy’s Patent Course. How they cheered me on—’”

===Chinese===
“哎？哎？”坎皮恩说，看那样子兴奋得几乎语无伦次，“那我能干点什么？”

“你随时待命。”肯尼迪说，英俊的年轻脸上浮起一丝迹近讥诮的东西。

“哦，那也好。”坎皮恩先生说，神情大失所望，“我就候着，必要时拿瓶子敲那家伙一下。”

“就是这个意思。”克里斯·肯尼迪勉勉强强地认可了，转回到众人身上。“当然，”他说，“那两个听差打手见你们历险归来还一个个精神抖擞、欢天喜地，多少要吃一惊；不过依我看，要的就是装作什么事都不曾有过，照样走进去。你们乐意的话，尽可以来些聪明快活的谈笑，好把他们的耳目引开。这就用得上你了，”他转向坎皮恩，补了一句，“想说多少说多少。该你逗乐的时候到了。”

“好嘞。”坎皮恩先生说，明显来了精神，“我给他们看我的双头便士。我要妙语连珠，风趣得要命。‘我在钢琴前坐下时，他们都笑；可我一弹起来，他们立刻明白，我上过肯尼迪专利函授课程。他们那个喝彩啊——’”

===Original===
“Oh, shut up,” said Martin Watt, grinning good-naturedly. “The fun starts at dinner, then. Oh, and by the way, when we’ve pinched these fellows’ guns, what do we do with the laddies? Leave them lying about?”

“I’ve thought of that,” said the indefatigable Kennedy; “we tie ’em up. I’ve been collecting portmanteau straps. That’ll do it, you’ll find. We’ll lash ’em both into chairs and leave ’em there.”

“Yes,” said Martin, “and next? When we’ve fixed up all that, what happens next?”

“Then somebody takes charge of the girls,” said Kennedy. “They lock themselves in some safe room—Miss Oliphant’s bedroom just at the head of the stairs, for instance. Then the rest of us form into two parties with a revolver each and storm the servants’ quarters, where, with a certain amount of luck, we shall get another gun or two. Then we can let out at some of these lads who amble round keeping an eye on us after dinner. We’ll tie ’em up and raid old Dawlish’s quarters.”

===Chinese===
“哦，闭嘴吧。”马丁·沃特说，好脾气地咧嘴直笑，“这么说，好戏开锣是在晚饭桌上。哦，对了，还有一件事：咱们缴了那两个家伙的枪之后，拿他们这两个小伙计怎么办？撂在一边不管？”

“这个我想过了。”那位不知疲倦的肯尼迪说，“把他们捆起来。我一直在攒旅行皮箱上的皮带。到时候你们瞧吧，保管够用。把他们俩连人带椅子捆个结实，撂在那儿。”

“好，”马丁说，“再往后呢？这些都料理完了，下一步呢？”

“然后就有人接手照管姑娘们，”肯尼迪说，“她们把自己锁进哪间稳妥的屋子——比如楼梯口正对着的奥利芬特小姐的卧室。其余的人分成两队，一队一杆左轮，突袭仆役住的下房；运气不太坏的话，还能再缴上一两杆枪。然后就可以朝晚饭后游游荡荡、盯着咱们的那几个家伙动手了。把他们捆起来，再去抄老道利什的房间。”

===Original===
He paused and looked round him, smiling.

“As soon as we’ve got everyone accounted for, we get the girls and sheer out of the house in a body. How’s that?”

“Sounds lovely,” said Mr. Campion, adding after a pause, “so simple. It’ll be rather awkward if someone makes a noise, though, won’t it? I mean you might have the entire gang down on you at the one-gun-per-three-men stage.”

Kennedy snapped at him. He was thoroughly tired of Mr. Campion’s helpful suggestions.

“There just hasn’t got to be any noise,” he said, “that’s the point. And by the way, I think you’re the man to stay with the girls.”

There was no mistaking his inference, but to Abbershaw’s surprise Mr. Campion seemed to jump at the idea.

“Righto,” he said, “I shall be delighted.”

Chris Kennedy’s answering remark was cut short, rather fortunately, Abbershaw felt, by a single and, in the circumstances, highly dramatic sound—the deep booming of the dinner gong.

===Chinese===
他停下来，笑吟吟地环视众人。

“等把每个人都料理妥当，咱们就接上姑娘们，成群结队地一齐出宅。怎么样？”

“听着真美。”坎皮恩先生说，顿了顿又补上一句，“简单得很。不过，要是有人出了声响，可就相当尴尬了，是不是？我是说，在三人一杆枪的阶段，说不定整个匪帮都会压到你们头上。”

肯尼迪厉声呵斥了他一句。坎皮恩先生这些“有益的”建议，他早已彻底听腻了。

“就是不能出半点声响，”他说，“关键全在这里。顺带说一句，我看留守陪姑娘们的这份差事，正该派给你。”

他这话里的弦外之音谁也听不错，可令阿伯肖吃惊的是，坎皮恩先生竟像是求之不得。

“好嘞，”他说，“我乐意之至。”

克里斯·肯尼迪正要回敬的那句话被截断了——阿伯肖觉得，这倒是相当凑巧——截断它的，是一声孤零零的、在此情此景中极富戏剧性的声响：开饭铜锣深沉的嗡鸣。

## XIX Mr. Campion’s Conjuring Trick / 第十九章 坎皮恩先生的戏法

===Original===
The six young people went down to the big dining-hall with a certain amount of trepidation. Jeanne clung to Prenderby, the other two girls stuck together, and Abbershaw was able to have a word or two with Mr. Campion.

“You don’t like the idea?” he murmured.

The other shrugged his shoulders.

“It’s the risk, my old bird,” he said softly. “Our pugilistic friend doesn’t realize that we’re not up against a gang of racecourse thugs. I tried to point it out to him but I’m afraid he just thought I was trying to be funny. People without humour always have curious ideas on that subject. However, it may come off. It’ll be the last thing he’ll expect us to do, anyway, and if you really have burnt that paper it’s the best thing we could do.”

===Chinese===
六个年轻人怀着几分忐忑下楼，朝大饭厅走去。让娜紧紧挽着普伦德比，另外两个姑娘彼此偎作一处，阿伯肖这才得了空，同坎皮恩先生搭上一两句话。

“你不赞成这个主意？”他低声问。

对方耸了耸肩。

“险就险在这儿，我的老伙计，”他轻声说，“咱们那位拳击手朋友还没有意识到，咱们对付的可不是赛马场上的那帮小流氓。我试着向他点明，只怕他还只当我是存心逗乐。没有幽默感的人，在这个问题上总有些古怪的成见。不过，事情也许能成。无论如何，这是他最料想不到我们会干的一手；况且你要是真把那张纸烧了，这便是咱们所能走的最好一步棋。”

===Original===
“I suppose you think I’m a fool,” said Abbershaw, a little defiantly. Campion grinned.

“On the contrary, young sir, I think you’re a humorist. A trifle unconscious, perhaps, but none the worse for that.”

Their conversation ended abruptly, for they had reached the foot of the staircase and were approaching the dining-room.

===Chinese===
“想必你是觉得我是个傻瓜。”阿伯肖说，口气里带几分挑衅。坎皮恩咧嘴一笑。

“恰恰相反，年轻先生，我认为你是个幽默家。或许有点浑然不觉，却也无伤大雅。”

他们的谈话戛然而止——楼梯脚已到，饭厅就在眼前。

===Original===
The door stood open, and they went in to find the table set for all nine of them, and the two men who had acted as footmen during the weekend awaiting their coming. They sat down at the table. “The others won’t be a moment, but we’ll start, please,” said Campion, and the meal began.

For some minutes it seemed as if the funereal atmosphere which surrounded the whole house was going to damp any attempt at bright conversation that anyone might feel disposed to make, but Mr. Campion sailed nobly into the breach.

Abbershaw was inclined to wonder at him until he realized with a little shock that considering the man’s profession the art of talking rubbish in any circumstances might be one of his chief stock-in-trades.

===Chinese===
房门敞着，他们走进去，只见桌上已给九个人摆好了餐具，周末里充作男仆的那两个男人正候着他们光临。众人在桌边落了座。“其余几位马上就到，不过咱们这就开席，劳驾。”坎皮恩说，于是这顿饭开始了。

起初几分钟，眼看笼罩全宅的那股送葬似的气氛，就要浇灭任何人兴之所至想说几句轻快话的念头，坎皮恩先生却慨然挺身，填补了这道缺口。

阿伯肖正自啧啧称奇，随即略感一惊地醒悟过来：想想此人的营生，无论身处何种境地都能滔滔不绝地扯淡，恐怕本就是他头号的看家本领之一。

===Original===
At the moment he was speaking of food. His high voice worked up to a pitch of enthusiasm, and his pale eyes widened behind his horn-rimmed spectacles.

“It all depends what you mean by eating,” he was saying. “I don’t believe in stuffing myself, you know, but I’m not one of those people who are against food altogether. I knew a woman once who didn’t believe in food—thought it was bad for the figure—so she gave it up altogether. Horrible results, of course; she got so thin that no one noticed her around—husband got used to being alone—estrangement, divorce—oh, I believe in food. I say, have you seen my new trick with a napkin and a saltcellar—rather natty, don’t you think?”

===Chinese===
此刻他谈的正是吃食。他那高亢的嗓音一路升到热烈的地步，角质框眼镜后面那双浅色的眼睛也瞪得溜圆。

“这全看你把‘吃’字作何解了，”他正说着，“我不主张把肚子塞满，这你是知道的，可我也不是那种对食物一概排斥的人。我从前认识一位女士，她就不信食物这一套——认为食物有损身材——干脆把它整个戒掉了。后果当然惨不忍睹：她瘦得走到哪儿都没人留意她——丈夫习惯了独处——感情疏远，离婚——哦，我是信食物这一套的。我说，你们见过我拿餐巾和盐罐变的新戏法没有——挺别致的，你说是不是？”

===Original===
He covered a saltcellar with his napkin as he spoke, made several passes over it, a solemn expression on his face, and then, whisking the napery away, disclosed nothing but shining oak beneath.

His mind still on Mr. Campion’s profession, Abbershaw was conscious of a certain feeling of apprehension. The saltcellar was antique, probably worth a considerable sum.

Mr. Campion’s trick was not yet over, however. A few more passes and the saltcellar was discovered issuing from the waistcoat of the manservant who happened to be attending to him at the time.

===Chinese===
说话间，他用餐巾罩住一只盐罐，板着一张煞有介事的面孔，在它上头比划了几下，随即猛地掀开餐巾——底下空空如也，只剩光亮的橡木桌面。

阿伯肖心里还惦着坎皮恩先生的营生，不觉生出几分忧惧。那盐罐是件古董，多半值好一笔钱。

不过坎皮恩先生的戏法尚未收场。又是几下手势，那只盐罐竟从当时正侍立他身旁伺候的男仆的马甲里钻了出来。

===Original===
“There!” he said. “A pretty little piece of work, isn’t it? All done by astrology. For my next I shall require two assistants, any live fish, four aspidistras, and one small packet of Gold Flake.” As he uttered the last words he turned sharply to beam around the table, and his elbow caught Meggie’s glass and sent it crashing to the floor.

A little breathless silence would have followed the smash had not he bounded up from his chair immediately and bent down ostensibly to gather up the fragments, jabbering the whole time. “What an idiot! What an idiot! Have I splashed your dress, Miss Oliphant? All over the floor! What a mess, what a mess! Come here, my man, here: bring a dustpan and broom with you.” He was making such a fuss and such a noise that no one had noticed the door open, and the somewhat self-conscious entry of Chris Kennedy’s little band. No one, that is, save Campion, who from his place of vantage halfway under the table had an excellent view of the feet.

===Chinese===
“瞧！”他说，“漂亮的小手艺，是不是？全凭占星术办成的。下一个戏法，我需要两名助手、随便一条活鱼、四盆蜘蛛抱蛋，外加一小包金片牌香烟。”末几个字一出口，他猛地转过身来，朝满桌的人绽开笑脸，胳膊肘扫中梅吉的酒杯，把它撞得哗啦一声摔在地上。

若不是他当即从椅子里蹦起来，俯下身去装作收拾碎片、嘴里絮絮叨叨说个不停，这声脆响之后本该跟着一阵屏息的沉默。“真是个蠢货！真是个蠢货！溅着您裙子了吗，奥利芬特小姐？洒了一地！糟糕，一塌糊涂，一塌糊涂！过来，伙计，这儿呢：把簸箕和扫帚拿来。”他闹出这么大的动静、这么大的声浪，竟没有一个人留意到房门开了，克里斯·肯尼迪的一小队人马带着几分扭捏走了进来。没有一个人——唯独坎皮恩除外，他从桌子底下半截身的绝佳位置，把那几双脚看得一清二楚。

===Original===
At the moment when Martin Watt leapt forward at the man by the carving table, Campion threw his arms round the other manservant’s legs just below the knees, and jerked him back on to the flags with an almost professional neatness. Within two seconds he was seated astride the man’s chest, his knees driven into the fleshy part of his arms, whilst he stuffed a handkerchief into his mouth. Abbershaw and Prenderby hurried to his assistance and between them they strapped the man into a chair, where he sat glaring at them, speechless and impotent.

===Chinese===
就在马丁·沃特朝切肉桌旁那个男人扑上去的当口，坎皮恩双臂一抱，搂住另一个男仆膝盖以下的腿弯，以近乎行家的干净利落，把他仰面掼倒在石板地上。不出两秒，他已骑坐在那人胸口，双膝死死抵进他手臂的肉厚处，同时往他嘴里塞了一块手帕。阿伯肖和普伦德比急忙赶来支援，两人合力把这家伙捆在一张椅子上；他坐在那里怒目而视，说不出话，也使不上劲。

===Original===
Kennedy’s party, though less neat, had been quite as successful, and Chris himself, flushed with excitement, now stood with his man’s loaded revolver in his hand.
