“Have you got his gun?” he said, in a voice which sounded hoarse even to himself, as he indicated Campion’s captive.

“No,” said Abbershaw, and began his search. Two minutes later he looked up, disappointed.

“He hasn’t one,” he said at last, and even the man himself seemed surprised.

Kennedy swore softly and handed the gun which he held to Martin.

===Chinese===
肯尼迪那一路人马虽说干得不及这边的利落，战果却同样可观；克里斯本人兴奋得满脸通红，手里正攥着从他那对手上缴来的、上了膛的左轮手枪。

“搜到他的枪了吗？”他指了指坎皮恩手下的俘虏，问道；那嗓音连他自己听来都觉得发哑。

“没有。”阿伯肖说着，便动手搜身。两分钟后，他抬起头来，一脸失望。

“他身上没有。”他终于说道，连那家伙本人似乎也吃了一惊。

肯尼迪低声咒骂了一句，把手里的枪递给马丁。

===Original===
“You’d better have it,” he said. “I’m hopeless with my right arm gone. Now, then, Campion, will you go upstairs with the girls? Abbershaw, you’d better go with them. As soon as you’ve seen them safely locked in the room, come back to us. We’re making for the servants’ quarters.”

They obeyed in silence, and Abbershaw led Campion and the three girls quietly out of the room, across the hall, and up the wide staircase. On the first landing they paused abruptly. Two figures were looming towards them through the dimness ahead. It was Jesse Gideon and the heavy, red-faced man whom Abbershaw had encountered outside Dawlish’s door in his search for Meggie. They would have passed in silence had not Gideon spoken suspiciously in his smooth silken voice.

“Dinner is over early?” he said, fixing his narrow glittering eyes on Meggie.

She replied coldly that it was, and made as if to pass on up the stairs, but Gideon evidently intended to prolong the conversation, for he glided in front of her so that he and the surly ruffian beside him barred her progress up the stairs from the step above the one on which she was standing.

===Chinese===
“你最好带着它，”他说，“我这右臂一废，什么也干不成。那么，坎皮恩，你送姑娘们上楼去，好吗？阿伯肖，你跟他们一道。一见到她们平安锁进房里，就回来同我们会合。我们这就奔仆人的住处去。”

众人默默从命。阿伯肖领着坎皮恩和三位姑娘悄然出了房门，穿过门厅，登上宽阔的楼梯。行至第一个楼梯平台，他们蓦地收住脚。前方的昏暗里，两个人影正朝他们逼近。那是杰西·吉迪恩，还有那个红脸膛的大块头——阿伯肖先前寻找梅吉时，曾在道利什的房门外撞见过此人。若非吉迪恩开了口，用他那圆滑柔腻的嗓音透着狐疑说话，他们本可以默默错身而过。

“晚饭结束得挺早啊？”他说，一双眯细的、精光闪烁的眼睛盯住了梅吉。

她冷冷地答了声是，便作势要继续上楼，可吉迪恩分明有意把这场谈话拖长——他悄没声儿地滑到她跟前，同身边那个横眉立目的恶汉一道，站在她所站台阶的上一级，把她上楼的路拦了个严严实实。

===Original===
“You are all so eager,” Gideon continued softly, “that it almost looks like an expedition to me. Or perhaps it is one of your charming games of hide-and-seek which you play so adroitly,” he added, and the sneer on his unpleasant face was very obvious. “You will forgive me saying so I am sure,” he went on, still in the same soothing obsequious voice, “but don’t you think you are trying Mr. Dawlish’s patience a little too much by being so foolish in your escapades? If you are wise you will take my advice and keep very quiet until it pleases him to release you.”

===Chinese===
“你们个个都这么心急，”吉迪恩柔声续道，“在我眼里，这简直快像是一支远征队了。要不然，就是你们那迷人的捉迷藏游戏又玩起了新花样——你们玩得可真熟练。”他补了一句，那张惹人嫌的脸上讥诮之意再明白不过。“相信你们一定会原谅我这么说的，”他接着讲下去，仍是那副温言软语、曲意逢迎的腔调，“可你们不觉得，这样胡闹下去，未免把道利什先生的耐心考验得太过分了吗？你们若是识相，就听我一句劝，安安静静待着，等他高兴放你们走。”

===Original===
He spoke banteringly, but there was no mistaking the warning behind his words, and it was with some eagerness that Abbershaw took Meggie’s arm and piloted her between the two men. His one aim at the moment was to get the girl safely to her room.

“We understand you perfectly, Mr. Gideon,” he murmured. Gideon’s sneer deepened into a contemptuous smile and he moved aside a little to let them pass. Abbershaw deliberately ignored his attitude. He wanted no arguments till the girls were safe. They were passing silently therefore, when suddenly from somewhere beneath them there sounded, ugly and unmistakable, a revolver shot.

===Chinese===
他口气带着打趣，话里藏着的警告却错认不了。阿伯肖急切地揽住梅吉的手臂，引着她从那两人中间穿行过去。他此刻唯一的心愿，就是平平安安把姑娘送到她房里。

“我们完全明白您的意思，吉迪恩先生。”他低声说。吉迪恩脸上的讥诮加深，化作一抹轻蔑的微笑，身子侧了侧，放他们过去。阿伯肖故意对他的做派视而不见——姑娘们安全之前，他不想同任何人起争执。于是众人正默默走过，忽然，从脚下不知哪一处，传来一声左轮手枪的枪响，狰狞刺耳，绝无错认。

===Original===
Instantly Gideon’s smiling contempt turned to a snarl of anger as all his suspicions returned—verified.

“So it is an expedition, is it?” he said softly. “A little explanation, if you please.”

Abbershaw realized that once again they were caught, and a feeling of utter dejection passed over him.

===Chinese===
刹那间，吉迪恩那含笑的轻蔑化作一声狂怒的咆哮——他所有的疑心尽数归来，而且坐实了。

“这么说，果然是一支远征队喽？”他轻声说，“那么，请稍稍解释一下吧。”

阿伯肖明白，他们又一次落网了，一阵彻头彻尾的沮丧漫过全身。

===Original===
Suddenly from the darkness behind him a high, rather foolish voice that yet had a certain quality of sternness in it said quickly, “Don’t talk so much. Put ’em up!”

While Abbershaw stood looking at them, Gideon and his burly companion, with mingled expressions of rage and amazement on their faces, raised their hands slowly above their heads.

“Quick, man, get their guns!”

The words were uttered in Abbershaw’s ear by a voice that was still vaguely foolish. He obeyed it instantly, removing a small, wicked little weapon from Gideon’s hip pocket and a heavy service revolver from the thug’s.

===Chinese===
忽然，从他背后的黑暗里，一个高亢、颇带几分傻气的嗓音急促地响起，那嗓音里却又含着某种森然的严厉：“少废话。举起手来！”

阿伯肖立在那里望着他们，只见吉迪恩和他那魁梧的同伴脸上怒容与惊愕交织，缓缓把双手举过了头顶。

“快，伙计，把他们的枪下了！”

这句话是凑在阿伯肖耳边说的，那嗓音依旧隐隐透着傻气。他当即从命，从吉迪恩的裤后袋里摸出一支小巧而凶险的武器，又从那恶汉身上缴下一支沉重的军用左轮。

===Original===
“Now then, turn round. Quick march. Keep ’em right up. I’m a dangerous man and I shoot like hell.”

Abbershaw glanced round involuntarily, and saw what Gideon and his companion must have done some minutes before—Albert Campion’s pleasant, vacuous face, pale and curiously in earnest in the faint light, as he peered at them from behind the gleaming barrel of a heavy Webley.

“Shove the girls in their room. Give Miss Oliphant the little pistol, and then come with me,” he murmured to Abbershaw, as the strange procession set off up the stairs.

“Steady,” he went on in a louder voice to the two men in front of him. “No fancy work. Any noise either of you makes will be voluntary suicide for the good of the cause. It’ll mean one man less to tie up, anyway. I’m taking them up to my room,” he murmured to Abbershaw. “Follow me there. They’re slippery beggars and two guns are better than one.”

===Chinese===
“好了，转身。开步——走。手举得高高的。我是个危险人物，开枪毒得很。”

阿伯肖不由自主地回头一瞥，这才看见了吉迪恩二人几分钟前必定已看见的东西——阿尔伯特·坎皮恩那张和气而茫然的脸，在微光里显得苍白，又出奇地一本正经，正躲在一支沉重的韦伯利左轮手枪闪亮的枪管后面，窥伺着他们。

“把姑娘们塞进房里。把那支小手枪交给奥利芬特小姐，然后跟我来。”当这支古怪的队伍拾级而上时，他低声对阿伯肖吩咐道。

“放规矩点，”他又提高嗓门，对前面那两人说，“不许耍花招。你们俩无论谁弄出声响，都算是为崇高事业自愿捐躯，反正还省得咱们多捆一个人。我把他们带上我的房间去。”他低声对阿伯肖说，“随我到那儿会合。这俩家伙滑得像泥鳅，两支枪总比一支强。”

===Original===
Abbershaw handed Gideon’s little revolver to Meggie, which she took eagerly.

“We’ll be all right,” she whispered. “Go on after him. They’re terrible people.”

“For God’s sake wait here till we come, then,” he whispered back. She nodded, and for a moment her steady brown eyes met his.

“We will, old dear. Don’t worry about us. We’re all right.”

===Chinese===
阿伯肖把吉迪恩那支小左轮递给梅吉，她一把接了过去，接得急切。

“我们会没事的，”她悄声说，“快跟他去吧。这帮人凶得很。”

“看在上帝份上，那就在这儿等我们回来。”他也悄声回她。她点点头，那双沉静的棕色眼睛与他的目光相遇，停了一瞬。

“我们等着，亲爱的。别为我们操心。我们没事。”

===Original===
She disappeared into the room with Jeanne and Anne Edgeware, and Abbershaw hurried after Campion considerably reassured. Meggie was a wonderful girl.

He reached Campion just in time to get the bedroom door open and to assist him to get the two into the room. “Now,” said Campion, “it’s getting infernally dark, so we’ll have to work fast. Abbershaw, will you keep watch over these two gentlemen. I’m afraid you may have to fire at the one on the right, he’s swearing so horribly—while I attend to Mr. Gideon’s immediate needs. That worthy enthusiast, Chris Kennedy, has pinched all my straps, and though I hate to behave as no guest should, I’m afraid there’s no help for it. The Black Dudley linen will have to go.”

As he spoke he stripped the clothes from the great four-poster bed, and began to tear the heavy linen sheets into wide strips. “If you could persuade Mr. Gideon to stand with his back against the post of this bed,” he remarked at length, “I think something might be done for him. Hands still up, please.”

===Chinese===
她同让娜、安妮·埃奇韦尔一道消失在房间里，阿伯肖赶忙追上坎皮恩，心里踏实了许多。梅吉真是个了不起的姑娘。

他赶上坎皮恩时，恰好来得及推开卧室的房门，帮着把那两人押进屋去。“好了，”坎皮恩说，“天黑得邪门，咱们动作得快。阿伯肖，你来看守住这两位先生。恐怕右边那位要劳你开一枪——他骂得实在太难听了——我来料理吉迪恩先生的切身需要。咱们那位可敬的热心人克里斯·肯尼迪，把我备下的皮带搜刮一空，因此尽管我痛恨做出这等有失宾客体统的事，恐怕也别无他法了。布莱克·达德利的床单只好牺牲。”

说话间，他把那张宽大的四柱床上的铺垫尽数扯了下来，动手把厚实的亚麻床单撕成一条条宽布带。“要是您能劝吉迪恩先生背抵着这张床的床柱站好，”他末了说道，“我想总还能为他想点办法。手继续举着，劳驾。”

===Original===
Ten minutes later, a silent mummy-like figure, stretched against the bedpost, arms bandaged to the wood high above his head, an improvised gag in his mouth, was all that remained of the cynical little foreigner.

Mr. Campion seemed to have a touch of the professional in all he did. He stood back to survey his handiwork with some pride, then he glanced at their other captive.

“Heavy, unpleasant-looking bird,” he remarked. “I’m afraid he’s too heavy for the bed. Isn’t there something we can shove him into?”

===Chinese===
十分钟后，倚着床柱立起一具木乃伊似的、无声无息的人形：双臂高高举过头顶，用布带缠在床柱的木头上，嘴里塞着一块临时凑合的堵嘴布——那个玩世不恭的小个子外国人，如今就只剩下这么一副光景。

坎皮恩先生做起事来，似乎样样都带着三分行家气派。他退后一步，颇为得意地端详自己的手笔，随后朝另一个俘虏瞥了一眼。

“块头沉、面目可憎的家伙，”他评说道，“恐怕床经不住他这个分量。有没有什么玩意儿，能把他塞进去的？”

===Original===
He glanced round the room as he spoke, and their captive fancied that Abbershaw’s eyes followed his, for he suddenly lunged forward and caught the doctor, who was unused to such situations, round the ankles, sending him sprawling. The heavy gun was thrown out of Abbershaw’s hand and the thug reached out a great hairy fist for it.

He was quick, but Campion was before him. With a sudden catlike movement he snatched up the weapon, and as the other came for him, lunging forward, all his ponderous weight behind his fist, Campion stepped back lightly and then, raising his arm above his head, brought down the butt of the pistol with all his strength upon the close-shaven skull.

The man went down like a log as Abbershaw scrambled to his feet, breathless and apologetic.

===Chinese===
说话间，他朝屋里四下打量，那俘虏只当阿伯肖的目光也随着他的视线望了过去，便突然发难，一把搂住这位不惯应付此类场面的医生的脚踝，把他掀了个四脚朝天。那支沉重的手枪从阿伯肖手中甩脱出去，恶汉伸出一只毛茸茸的大拳，便朝枪抓去。

他动作够快，坎皮恩却抢先一步。只见他身形倏地一闪，猫一般抄起了武器；那家伙挺拳直冲过来，全身沉甸甸的分量都压在拳头上，坎皮恩轻盈地退后半步，随即手臂高高扬过头顶，使出全身力气，把枪柄猛砸在那颗剃得铁青的头颅上。

那人像根木头似的轰然倒地，阿伯肖这才手忙脚乱地爬起身来，气喘吁吁，连声致歉。

===Original===
“My dear old bird, don’t lose your Organizing Power, Directive Ability, Self-Confidence, Driving Force, Salesmanship, and Business Acumen,” chattered Mr. Campion cheerfully. “In other words, look on the bright side of things. This fruity affair down here, for instance, has solved his own problem. All we have to do now is to stuff him in a cupboard and lock the door. He won’t wake up for a bit yet.”

Abbershaw, still apologetic, assisted him to lift the heavy figure into a hanging cupboard, where they deposited him, shutting the door and turning the key.

===Chinese===
“亲爱的老伙计，千万保住您的组织力、指挥才干、自信心、驱动力、推销术和商业眼光。”坎皮恩先生快快活活地絮叨着，“换句话说，凡事要往好的一面看。比方说吧，脚下这位红光满面的老兄，就自己把自己的难题解决了。如今咱们只需把他塞进柜子，锁上柜门，就完事大吉。这一时半会儿，他还醒不过来呢。”

阿伯肖兀自歉疚着，帮着他把那沉重的身躯抬进一口挂衣柜，把他安放妥当，关上柜门，转上了钥匙。

===Original===
“Well, now I suppose we’d better lend a hand with the devilry downstairs,” said Mr. Campion, stretching himself. “I haven’t heard any more shots, have you?”

“I don’t know,” said Abbershaw. “I fancied I heard something while you were dealing with—er—that last customer. And I say, Campion, I haven’t liked to ask you before now, but where the devil did you get that gun from?”

Mr. Campion grinned from behind his enormous spectacles. “Oh, that,” he said, “that was rather fortunate as it happened. I had a notion things might be awkward, so I was naturally anxious that the guns, or at least one of them, should fall into the hands of someone who knew something about bluff at any rate.”

===Chinese===
“那么，眼下我想咱们最好下楼去，助那场大闹一臂之力。”坎皮恩先生说着，伸展了一下四肢，“再没听见枪响了吧？你听见了吗？”

“说不准，”阿伯肖说，“你对付——呃——对付上一位主顾的时候，我恍惚听见了一声响。我说，坎皮恩，这话我一直不好意思问——你那支枪究竟是从哪儿弄来的？”

坎皮恩先生在那副硕大眼镜后面咧嘴一笑。“哦，那个呀，”他说，“说来也算走运。我早料到事情可能不妙，自然巴不得那些枪——至少其中一支——能落进一个多少懂得唬人之道的人手里。”

===Original===
“Where did you get it from?” demanded Abbershaw. “I thought only one of those men in the dining-room had a gun?”

“Nor had they when we tackled ’em,” agreed Mr. Campion. “I relieved our laddie of this one earlier on in the meal, while I was performing my incredible act with the saltcellar, in fact. It was the first opportunity I’d had, and I couldn’t resist it.”

===Chinese===
“你到底从哪儿弄来的？”阿伯肖追问，“我还以为，饭厅里那些人只有一个带枪呢？”

“咱们动手拿下他们的时候，他们确实一支都没有。”坎皮恩先生表示赞同，“这一支嘛，是席间早些时候，我从咱们那位小哥身上卸下来的——事实上，正是我表演盐罐绝活那会儿。那是我头一回逮着机会，实在忍不住。”

===Original===
Abbershaw stared at him.

“By Jove,” he said, with some admiration, “while you were doing your conjuring trick you picked his pocket.”

Mr. Campion hesitated, and Abbershaw had the uncomfortable impression that he reddened slightly.

“Well,” he said at last, “in a way, yes, but if you don’t mind—let’s call it legerdemain, shall we?”

===Chinese===
阿伯肖直勾勾地盯着他。

“老天爷，”他说，语带几分钦佩，“你变着戏法，就顺手扒了他的口袋。”

坎皮恩先生迟疑了一下，阿伯肖得到一个不自在的印象：他脸上微微泛红。

“这个嘛，”他终于开口，“从某种意义上说，是的。不过你要是不介意——咱们不妨称它为‘空空妙手’，好不好？”

## XX The Roundup / 第二十章 一网打尽

===Original===
As Abbershaw and Campion made their way slowly down the staircase to the first floor, the house seemed to be unnaturally silent. The candles in the iron sconces had not been lighted, and the corridors were quite dark save for a faint greyness here and there when the open doors of a room permitted the faint light of the stars to penetrate into the gloom.

===Chinese===
阿伯肖和坎皮恩缓缓走下楼梯、来到二楼时，整座宅子静得出奇。铁制烛台上的蜡烛没有点，走廊里一片漆黑，只有哪间屋子敞着门、容许星光的微芒渗入昏暗的地方，才偶尔透出些许朦朦的灰白。

===Original===
Abbershaw touched his companion’s arm.

“How about going through the cupboard passage to the box-room and then down the staircase into Dawlish’s room through the fireplace door?” he whispered. “We might take him by surprise.” Mr. Campion appeared to hesitate. Then his voice, high and foolish as ever, came softly through the thick darkness.

“Not a bad notion, doctor,” he said, “but we’re too late for that, I’m afraid. Hang it all, our friends’ target practice downstairs must have given the old boy a hint that something was up. It’s only natural. I think we’d better toddle downstairs to see how the little ones progress. Walk softly, keep your gun ready, and for heaven’s sake don’t shoot unless it’s a case of life or sleep perfect sleep.”

===Chinese===
阿伯肖碰了碰同伴的手臂。

“从碗柜暗道穿到杂物间，再顺着楼梯下去，从壁炉门进道利什的房间，你看怎么样？”他悄声道。“咱们可以打他个措手不及。”坎皮恩先生似乎迟疑了一下。随后，他那个一如既往又高又傻的嗓音，穿过浓重的黑暗轻轻传来。

“主意不坏，医生，”他说，“只怕咱们动手已经晚啦。见鬼，楼下那几位朋友的打靶练习，必定已经给这位老先生提了醒，知道要出事。这也再自然不过。我看咱们还是溜下楼去，瞧瞧小家伙们进展如何。脚步放轻，枪握在手，千万看在老天爷的份上别开枪——除非事关生死，或者说，一觉长眠不醒。”

===Original===
On the last word he moved forward so that he was a pace or two ahead of Abbershaw, and they set off down the long corridor in single file.

They reached the head of the staircase without hindrance and paused for a moment to listen.

All beneath them was silent, the husky, creaky quiet of an old house at night, and Abbershaw was conscious of an uneasy sensation in the soles of his feet and a tightening of his collar band.

After what seemed an interminable time Campion moved on again, hugging the extra shadow of the wall, and treading so softly that the ancient wood did not creak beneath him. Abbershaw followed him carefully, the gun clenched in his hand. This sort of thing was manifestly not in his line, but he was determined to see it through as creditably as he was able. He might lack experience, but not courage.

===Chinese===
话音未落，他已抢上前去，比阿伯肖领先一两步，两人一前一后，沿着长长的走廊出发了。

他们一路无阻地走到楼梯口，停下听了片刻。

脚下的一切都悄然无声，那是老宅深夜里沙哑而带着吱呀声的寂静。阿伯肖只觉得脚底板泛起一阵莫名的不安，领口也仿佛紧了一紧。

仿佛熬过了无穷无尽的一段时间之后，坎皮恩才又继续前行，紧贴着墙上格外浓重的阴影，脚步轻得连古老的木地板都不曾在他脚下呻吟一声。阿伯肖握紧手枪，小心翼翼地跟在后面。这种事明摆着不是他的本行，可他打定了主意，要尽自己所能体面地撑到底。他缺的或许是经验，却绝不缺勇气。

===Original===
A sudden stifled exclamation from Mr. Campion a pace or so ahead of him made him start violently, however; he had not realized how much the experience of the past forty-eight hours had told on his nerves.

“Look out!” Campion’s voice was barely audible. “Here’s a casualty.”

He dropped silently as he spoke, and the next moment a little pinprick of light from a minute electric torch fell upon the upturned face of the body upon the stairs.

===Chinese===
然而，走在前面一步开外的坎皮恩先生突然憋出一声低呼，惊得他猛然一颤；他这才发现，过去四十八小时的种种经历，已经把自己的神经折磨成了什么样子。

“当心！”坎皮恩的声音低得几乎听不见。“这儿有一位伤员。”

说话间他已悄无声息地矮身蹲下，下一刻，一支微型电筒射出针尖大的一点光，落在楼梯上那具躯体仰起的脸上。

===Original===
Abbershaw felt the blood rise and surge in his ears as he looked down and recognized Chris Kennedy, very pale from a gash over his right temple.

“Dotted over the beam with the familiar blunt instrument,” murmured Campion sadly. “He was so impetuous. Boys will be boys, of course, but—well, well, well.”

“Is he dead?” Abbershaw could not see the extent of the damage, and he hardly recognized his own voice, it was so strained and horror-stricken.

“Dead?” Mr. Campion seemed to be surprised. “Oh, dear me, no—he’s only out of action for a bit. Our friends here are artists in this sort of thing, and I rather fancy that so far Daddy Dawlish has decided against killing off his chicks. Of course,” he went on softly, “what his attitude will be now that we’ve taken up the offensive deliberately I don’t like to suggest. On the whole I think our present policy of complete caution is to be maintained. Hop over this—he’s as safe here as anywhere—and come on.”

===Chinese===
阿伯肖低头一看，认出是克里斯·肯尼迪，右太阳穴上一道口子让他脸色惨白；阿伯肖只觉得血一下子涌上耳畔，嗡嗡作响。

“被那件熟门熟路的钝器敲了天灵盖。”坎皮恩悲哀地低声道。“他就是那么个急性子。男孩子终归是男孩子，当然，不过——唉，唉，唉。”

“他死了吗？”阿伯肖看不清伤势有多重，连自己的声音都几乎认不出来了——又紧绷，又惊恐。

“死了？”坎皮恩先生似乎吃了一惊。“哎呀老天，没有——他只是暂时退出战斗罢了。咱们这儿这几位朋友干这种活计可是艺术家，我倒倾向于认为，道利什老爹到目前为止还不曾决定宰掉他的小鸡崽们。当然，”他放轻声音接着说，“如今咱们蓄意转守为攻，他老人家会是什么态度，我不敢妄加揣测。总的说来，我认为咱们眼下这套全面谨慎的方针，还得继续贯彻。跨过他走吧——他躺在这儿和躺在哪儿一样安全——来吧。”

===Original===
Abbershaw stepped carefully over the recumbent figure, and advanced softly after the indefatigable Mr. Campion.

They had hardly reached the foot of the staircase, and Abbershaw was speculating upon Campion’s plan of campaign, when their direction was suddenly decided for them. From the vicinity of the servants’ quarters far below them on their left there came a sudden crash which echoed dully over the entire house, followed by a volley of shots and a hoarse scream as of a man in pain or terror.

===Chinese===
阿伯肖小心地跨过那具横卧的身躯，跟在不知疲倦的坎皮恩先生身后轻轻前行。

他们刚到楼梯脚下，阿伯肖正琢磨着坎皮恩的作战方略，前进的方向便猛地被人替他们定了下来。从左下方远处的仆人住处一带，传来一声突如其来的巨响，闷闷地回荡过整座宅子，紧接着是一阵连环枪响，还有一声嘶哑的惨叫，仿佛出自一个正在受痛或受惊的男人之口。

===Original===
Albert Campion paused abruptly.

“That’s done it!” he said. “Now we’ve *got* to lick ’em! Come on, Doc.” On the last word he darted forward, Abbershaw at his heels. The door in the recess under the stairs was shut but unlocked, and on opening it they found themselves in a narrow stone corridor with a second door at the far end.

The noise was increasing; it sounded to Abbershaw as if a pitched battle were taking place somewhere near at hand.

===Chinese===
阿尔伯特·坎皮恩猛地停住脚步。

“这一来是躲不掉了！”他说。“如今咱们*非*把他们收拾了不可！来吧，大夫。”话音未落他已飞身抢出，阿伯肖紧紧跟上。楼梯底下凹处的那扇门关着却没上锁，推开门，他们便置身于一条狭窄的石砌走廊，尽头还有第二道门。

喧闹声愈演愈烈；在阿伯肖听来，仿佛一场短兵相接的激战正在近旁某处打响。

===Original===
The second door disclosed a great stone kitchen lit by two swinging oil lamps. At first Abbershaw thought it was deserted, but a smothered sound from the far end of the room arrested him, and he turned to see a heavy, dark-eyed woman and an hysterical weak-faced girl gagged and bound to wooden kitchen chairs in the darkest corner of the room.

These must be Mrs. Browning and Lizzie Tiddy; the thought flitted through his mind and was forgotten, for Mr. Campion was already at the second door, a heavy iron-studded structure behind which pandemonium seemed to have broken loose.

===Chinese===
第二道门后是一间宽大的石砌厨房，两盏摇曳的吊油灯照着亮。起初阿伯肖以为里面空无一人，可屋子尽头一声被堵住嘴的闷响让他蓦地止步；他转过身，看见一个身量厚重、黑眼睛的女人和一个面容怯弱、歇斯底里的姑娘，被堵着嘴捆在木厨椅上，丢在屋里最暗的角落。

这想必就是布朗宁太太和莉齐·蒂迪了；这个念头在他脑中一闪而过，随即被抛在一边，因为坎皮恩先生已经到了第二道门前——那是一扇钉满铁钉的厚重门扇，门后仿佛已经闹翻了天。

===Original===
Mr. Campion lifted the iron latch, and then sprang aside as the door shot open to meet him, precipitating the man who had been cowering against it headlong into the room. It was Wendon, the man who had visited Meggie and Abbershaw in their prison room early that morning.

He struggled to his feet and sprang at the first person he caught sight of, which unfortunately for him was Campion himself. His object was a gun, but Mr. Campion, who seemed to have a peculiar aversion to putting a revolver to its right use, extricated himself from the man’s hold with an agility and strength altogether surprising in one of such a languid appearance, and, to use his own words, “dotted the fellow.”

It was a scientific tap, well placed and of just adequate force; Wendon’s eyes rolled up, he swayed forward and crashed. Abbershaw and Campion darted over him into the doorway.

===Chinese===
坎皮恩先生掀起铁门闩，随即闪身跃开——那扇门朝他迎面弹开，把原本缩在门后抵着门的男人一头栽进了屋里。是温登，就是今天一早到囚室里来看过梅吉和阿伯肖的那个人。

他挣扎着爬起来，扑向撞进眼帘的第一人——不幸的是，那人偏偏是坎皮恩本人。他图的是那支枪，可坎皮恩先生似乎对把左轮枪派上正当用场怀有一种特殊的嫌恶；他以一种在那副懒散外表的人身上全然出人意料的敏捷和膂力，从那人的钳制中脱出身来，并且——用他自己的话说——“敲了那家伙的闷棍”。

那是一记讲科学的拍击，落点精准，力道恰到好处；温登两眼上翻，身子前晃，轰然倒地。阿伯肖和坎皮恩从他身上一跃而过，冲到门口。

===Original===
The scene that confronted them was an extraordinary one.

They were on the threshold of a great vaulted scullery or brewhouse, in which the only light came from a single wall lamp and a blazing fire in the sunken hearth. What furniture there had been in the room, a rickety table and some benches, was smashed to firewood, and lay in splinters all over the stone floor.

===Chinese===
眼前的一幕不同寻常。

他们站在一间高大的拱顶洗涤室的门槛上——也可以叫它麦芽坊（brewhouse，旧酿酒房）；屋里唯一的光亮，来自一盏壁灯和凹灶里熊熊的炉火。屋里原有的家什——一张摇摇晃晃的桌子和几条长凳——已被砸成柴火，碎木片撒了一地石板。

===Original===
There were seven men in the room. Abbershaw recognized the two he had last seen bound and gagged in the dining-hall, two others were strangers to him, and the remaining three were of his own party.

Even in the first moment of amazement he wondered what had happened to their guns.

The two prisoners of the dining-room had been relieved of theirs, he knew, but then Martin Watt should be armed. Wendon, too, had had a revolver that morning, and the other two, quick-footed Cockneys with narrow suspicious eyes, should both have had weapons, surely.

Besides, there were the shots he had just heard. There was evidence of gunfire also. Michael Prenderby lay doubled up on a long, flat stone sink which ran the whole length of the place some three feet from the floor. Martin Watt, every trace of his former languidness vanished, was fighting like a maniac with one of the erstwhile prisoners in the shadow at the extreme end of the room; but it was Wyatt who was the central figure in the drama.

===Chinese===
屋里有七个男人。阿伯肖认出两个是他上回见到时正被捆着堵着嘴扔在饭厅里的，另两个是生面孔，余下三个则是他们一伙的。

即便在这目瞪口呆的头一瞬，他也在纳闷：他们的枪都到哪儿去了。

他知道，饭厅里那两个俘虏的枪早被缴了去；可马丁·沃特总该是带了武器的。再说温登今天早上也有一支左轮，另外那两个腿脚麻利、眯着猜疑窄眼的伦敦佬，按说也该人手一件家伙才对。

况且，方才还有枪声传进他耳朵，屋子里也看得出开过枪的痕迹。迈克尔·普伦德比蜷在一方长而扁的石水槽上——那水槽纵贯整间屋子，离地约三英尺高。马丁·沃特往日的懒散荡然无存，正在屋子尽头的暗影里跟先前那两个俘虏之一疯了一样缠斗；然而这出戏的中心人物，却是怀亚特。

===Original===
He stood balanced on the edge of the sink in front of Michael. The flickering firelight played on the lines of his lank figure, making him seem unnaturally tall. His longish hair was shaken back from his forehead, and his clothes were bloodstained and wildly dishevelled; but it was his face that most commanded attention. The intellectual, clever, and slightly cynical scholar had vanished utterly, and in its place there had appeared a warrior of the Middle Ages, a man who had thrown his whole soul into a fight with fanatical fury.

In his two hands he wielded a wooden pole tipped at the end with a heavy iron scoop, such as are still used in many places to draw water up out of wells. It was clearly the first thing that had come to his hand, but in his present mood it made him the most formidable of weapons. He was lashing out with it with an extraordinary fury, keeping the three men at bay as if they had been yelping dogs, and as an extra flicker from the fire lit up his face afresh it seemed to Abbershaw that it was transformed; he looked more like the Avenging Angel than a scholar with a well scoop.

===Chinese===
他立在迈克尔身前那道水槽的边沿上，站得稳稳当当。跳动的炉火勾出他瘦长身形的轮廓，让他显得高得出奇。略长的头发从额前甩了回去，衣服上血迹斑斑、凌乱不堪；可最攫住人目光的还是他的脸。那位有学识、聪颖、略带讥诮的学者消失得无影无踪，取而代之的是一位中世纪的武士，一个把全副灵魂都掷进战斗、狂热得如痴如狂的人。

他双手挥着一根木杆，杆头装着一柄沉重的铁斗——那种许多地方至今仍在用来从井里提水的家什。这明摆着是他顺手抓到的头一件东西，可在他此刻的心境里，它使他成了最令人生畏的武器。他带着一股异乎寻常的狂怒抡将出去，逼得那三个人不敢近身，仿佛他们是一群汪汪乱叫的狗；炉火又一阵闪动，重新照亮他的脸，在阿伯肖看来，那张脸已经变了样；他看上去与其说是个拎着井斗的学者，不如说是复仇天使。

===Original===
Campion whipped out his gun, and his quiet high voice sounded clearly through the noise.

“Now then, now then! Put ’em up!” he said distinctly. “There’s been enough fun here for this evening. Put ’em up! I’m firing,” he added quietly, and at the same moment a bullet flashed past the head of the man nearest Wyatt and struck the stone wall behind him. The effect was instantaneous. The noise ceased, and slowly the four members of Dawlish’s gang raised their hands above their heads.

Gradually Wyatt’s uplifted weapon sank to the ground and he dropped down off the sink and collapsed, his head between his knees, his arms hanging limply by his sides.

Martin Watt came reeling into the circle of light by the fire, somewhat battered and dishevelled but otherwise unhurt.

“Thank God you’ve come,” he said breathlessly, and grinned. “I thought our number was up.”

===Chinese===
坎皮恩唰地拔出枪，他那个安静的尖嗓门穿透喧闹，清清楚楚地响了起来。

“喂，喂！举起手来！”他一字一句地说。“今晚这儿的乐子够多的了。举起手来！我要开枪了。”他不动声色地补了一句，与此同时，一颗子弹擦着离怀亚特最近那人的脑袋飞过，打在他身后的石墙上。效果立竿见影。喧闹声戛然而止，道利什那伙人的四名成员缓缓把手举过了头顶。

怀亚特高举的武器慢慢垂落到地上，他也从水槽上溜下来，瘫坐下去，头埋在两膝之间，两条手臂软软地垂在身侧。

马丁·沃特摇摇晃晃走进炉边的光圈，有些鼻青脸肿、衣衫不整，除此之外倒没受伤。

“谢天谢地你们来了。”他上气不接下气地说，咧嘴一笑。“我还当咱们的气数尽了。”

===Original===
Mr. Campion herded his captives into a straight line along one wall.

“Now if you fellows will hold them up,” he said pleasantly, “I will repeat my celebrated rope trick. For this performance I shall employ nothing less than actual rope, which I see is all ready waiting for me.”

As he spoke he was unfastening the hank of clothes line which hung ready for use near the fire. He handed Martin his gun, while Abbershaw, more alert this time, held up their captives. As he corded up the four, Martin Watt, still breathless, recounted briefly the events which had led up to the scene they had just witnessed.

===Chinese===
坎皮恩先生把俘虏们驱赶到一边墙前排成一条直线。

“现在，劳驾你们几位把他们看住了，”他和和气气地说，“我来重演本人那套著名的绳技。本场演出，我使用的道具不是别的，正是如假包换的绳子——我瞧它已经备好，正等着我呢。”

说话间，他已解开那卷挂在炉边待用的晾衣绳。他把枪递给马丁，而阿伯肖——这一回警醒多了——用枪看住俘虏。他捆那四个人的时候，仍旧气喘吁吁的马丁·沃特简短讲述了事情的原委，也就是他们刚才撞见的那一幕的前因。

===Original===
“We got into the kitchen first,” he said. “There didn’t seem to be a soul about except the women. They started to scream the place down though, so we tied ’em up. It wasn’t till we’d done that that we realized that Chris wasn’t with us. We guessed he’d met trouble, so we started to go back. We hadn’t got halfway across the room, though, before the door burst open and a man came in.”

He paused and took a deep breath.

“I told him to put up his hands or I’d fire at him,” he went on jerkily, “but he didn’t. He just came for me, so I did fire. I didn’t hit him, of course—I didn’t mean to—but the noise seemed to start things up generally. There seemed to be footsteps all round us. We didn’t know where to shove the cove. The door into here seemed handy and we’d just got him inside when these four charged in on us from the kitchen passage. Michael had got the first fellow’s gun by that time. He lost his head a bit, I guess, and blazed at them—shooting wildly over their heads most of the time. Then one of the fellows got him and he curled up on the sink over there with his gun underneath him. By this time, however, I’d got ’em fairly well under control, God knows how.”

===Chinese===
“我们先摸进了厨房。”他说。“除了那两个女人，四下里瞧不见一个人影。可她们扯开嗓子叫得天翻地覆，我们只好把她们捆了起来。捆完了才发现克里斯没跟上来。我们猜他八成遇上了麻烦，便动身往回走。可还没走到房间一半，门就砰地撞开，闯进来一个男人。”

他顿了顿，深深吸了口气。

“我喝令他举起手来，不然我就开枪，”他断断续续地往下说，“可他不听，径直朝我扑过来，我就真的开了枪。我当然没打中他——我也没想打中——可那声枪响似乎把各处都惊动了。我们四面八方好像全响起了脚步声。我们不知道该把这家伙塞到哪儿去。通这儿的那扇门看着顺手，我们刚把他弄进来，这四位就从厨房过道朝我们冲杀了进来。那时候迈克尔已经拿到了头一个家伙的枪。他有点沉不住气，我估摸着，就朝他们开了火——大半时候都冲着他们头顶乱放。后来其中一个家伙得了手，他就蜷到那边水槽上，枪压在了身子底下。不过到这个时候，我已经把他们制得服服帖帖了——天知道是怎么制住的。”

===Original===
The boy spoke modestly, but there were indications of “how” upon the faces of their captives.

“I got them to stick up their hands,” he continued, “and then I yelled to Wyatt to get their guns.”

He paused, and glanced at the silent figure hunched up on the flags.

“Poor old chap,” he said. “I think he went barmy—almost ran amok. He got the guns all right—there were only two of them—and before I could stop him or yell at him even, he had chucked them into that bricked-up place over there. See what it is? A darn great well—I heard them splash ages after they went in. I bawled at him, but he yelled out what sounded like ‘Sweet Seventeen’ or something equally potty, grabbed that scoop, and began to lay about with it like a loony.” He shook his head and paused for breath. “Then a foul thing happened,” he went on suddenly. “One of them came for me—and I warned him I’d shoot, and finally I tried to, but the thing only clicked in my hand. The shot I had already fired must have been the last. Then we closed. When you came in the other three were trying to get at Prenderby for his gun—he was knocked out, you know—and old Wyatt was lashing round like the flail of the Lord. Then, of course, you just finished things off for us.”

===Chinese===
小伙子说得谦逊，可“怎么制住的”，俘虏们的脸上自有明证。

“我逼得他们举起了手，”他接着说，“然后我冲怀亚特大喊，叫他把他们的枪收了。”

他停了停，瞥了一眼蜷在石板地上那沉默的身影。

“可怜的老伙计。”他说。“我看他是疯魔了——差点儿发作起来见人就打。枪他是收走了没错——统共就两支——可我还没来得及拦他、连喊都来不及喊一声，他就把它们扔进那边那个砖封的口子里了。知道那是什么吗？一口了不起的大井——枪扔进去老半天之后，我还听见它们扑通落水的声音。我朝他吼，他却喊出一句听着像《甜蜜十七岁》还是什么一样不着调的玩意儿，抓起那柄井斗，就像个疯子似的抡开了。”他摇摇头，停下来喘气。“接着出了一档子糟糕事，”他猛地又接着说。“他们里头有一个朝我扑过来——我警告他我要开枪，最后也真扣了扳机，可那玩意儿在我手里只咔哒响了一声。我先前打的那一枪，必定已是最后一发。于是我们扭作一团。你们进来的时候，另外三个正想法子抢普伦德比的枪——他已经昏死过去了，你们知道——老怀亚特正抡得像主的连枷一样。然后，当然，你们一来就把事情替我们收拾利索了。”

===Original===
“A very pretty tale of love and war,” murmured Mr. Campion, some of his old inanity returning. “ ‘Featuring Our Boys. Positively for One Night Only.’ I’ve finished with the lads now, Doc—you might have a look at the casualties.”

Abbershaw lowered his revolver, and approached Prenderby with some trepidation. The boy lay on the stone sink dangerously doubled up, his face hidden. A hasty examination, however, disclosed only a long superficial scalp wound. Abbershaw heaved a sigh of relief.

“He’s stunned,” he said briefly. “The bullet grazed along his temple and put him out. We ought to get him upstairs, though, I think.”

===Chinese===
“好一出爱与战争的妙戏。”坎皮恩先生喃喃道，几分往日的痴气又回来了。“‘主演：我方众小子。确确实实，仅此一夜。’小伙子们我这头算是收拾完了，大夫——劳驾你瞧一瞧伤号。”

阿伯肖垂下左轮枪，提心吊胆地走向普伦德比。小伙子蜷在石水槽上，蜷得吓人，脸埋着看不见。可匆匆一查，只发现一道长长的头皮浅伤。阿伯肖如释重负地长出了一口气。

“他是昏过去了。”他简短地说。“子弹擦过他的太阳穴，把他打晕了。不过我看，咱们还是该把他弄上楼去。”

===Original===
“Well, I don’t see why we shouldn’t,” said Martin cheerfully. “Hang it, our way is fairly clear now. Gideon and a thug are upstairs, you say, safely out of the way; we have four sportsmen here and one outside; that’s seven altogether. Then the doctor lad and his shover are still away presumably, so there’s only old Dawlish himself left. The house is ours.”

“Not so eager, not so eager!” Albert Campion strolled over to them as he spoke. “Old Daddy Dawlish is an energetic bit of work, believe me. Besides, he has only to get going with his Boy Scout’s ever-ready, self-expanding, patent pocketknife and the fun will begin all over again. No, I think that the doc had better stay here with his gun, his patient and the prisoners, while you come along with me. I’ll take Prenderby’s gun.”

“Righto,” said Martin. “What’s the idea, a tour of the works?”

“More or less,” Campion conceded. “I want you to do a spot of ambulance work. The White Hope of our side is draped tastefully along the front stairs. While you’re gathering up the wreckage I’ll toddle round to find Poppa von Faber, and on my way back after the argument I’ll call in for the girls, and we’ll all make our final exit en masse. Dignity, Gentlemen, and British Boyhood’s Well-known Bravery, Coolness, and Distinction are the passwords of the hour.”

Martin looked at him wonderingly. “Do you always talk bilge?” he said.

“No,” said Mr. Campion lightly, “but I learnt the language reading advertisements. Come on.”

===Chinese===
“呃，我看没什么不成的。”马丁兴冲冲地说。“见鬼，咱们的路这会儿可算清净了。照你说，吉迪恩和一个打手在楼上，已经妥妥地解决掉了；这儿有四位运动健将，外头还有一位；一共七个。再说那位大夫小哥和他的司机想必还没回来，剩下的就只有道利什老头儿自己了。这宅子是咱们的了。”

“别急，别急！”阿尔伯特·坎皮恩说着，踱到他们跟前。“道利什老爹可是个精力旺盛的厉害角色，信我没错。再说，他只消掏出他那把童子军式的随取随用、自行展开的专利折刀折腾起来，好戏就得从头再唱一遍。不行，我看大夫还是留在这儿，守着他的枪、他的病人和这些俘虏的好；你呢，跟我来。普伦德比的枪归我用。”

“成。”马丁说。“什么章程？参观全厂吗？”

“差不多吧。”坎皮恩让步道。“我要你干点儿救护队的活儿。咱们这边的白人希望正风雅地横陈在前楼梯上。你收拾残骸的工夫，我去溜达一圈，找一找冯·法贝尔老爹；等我把这场辩论吵完，回来路上再顺道接上姑娘们，大家一起来一场集体大退场。诸位，‘体面’，还有‘英伦少年素负盛名的英勇、冷静与出众’，就是此时此刻的口令。”

马丁愕然地看着他。“你张口永远是这股废话味儿吗？”他说。

“不。”坎皮恩先生轻飘飘地说，“可我这门语言，是读广告学会的。走吧。”

===Original===
He led the way out of the brewhouse into the kitchen, Martin following. On the threshold he paused suddenly, and an exclamation escaped him.

“What’s happened?” Abbershaw darted after them, and the next moment he, too, caught his breath.

Wendon, the man Campion had laid out not ten minutes before, and left lying an inert mass on the fibre matting, had vanished utterly. Campion spoke softly, and his voice was unusually grave.

“He didn’t walk out of here on his own,” he said. “There’s not a skull on earth that would withstand that tap I gave him. No, my sons, he was fetched.” And while they looked at him he grinned.

“To be continued—evidently,” he said, and added lightly, “Coming, Martin?”

===Chinese===
他领路走出麦芽坊进了厨房，马丁跟在后面。一到门槛他突然顿住，失声低呼了一下。

“出什么事了？”阿伯肖一个箭步追上去，下一刻他也倒抽了一口凉气。

温登——坎皮恩不到十分钟前把他打翻、像个死物撂在粗纤维地垫上的那个人——消失得无影无踪。坎皮恩放低了声音说话，语气罕见地凝重。

“他绝不是自己走出这儿的。”他说，“天底下没有哪颗脑壳，挨了我那一记还能站得住。不，我的小子们，是有人把他抬走的。”他们望着他，他却咧嘴一笑。

“且听下回分解——明摆着的。”他说，又轻快地补了一句，“来吗，马丁？”

===Original===
Abbershaw returned to his post in the brewhouse, and, after doing all he could for the still unconscious Prenderby, settled down to await further developments.

He had given up reflecting upon the strangeness of the circumstances which had brought him, a sober, respectable London man, into such an extraordinary position, and now sat staring ahead, his eyes fixed on the grey stone wall in front of him.

Wyatt remained where he had collapsed; the others had not addressed him, realizing in some vague subconscious way that he would rather that they left him alone.

Abbershaw had forgotten him entirely, so that when he raised himself suddenly and staggered to his feet the little red-haired doctor was considerably startled. Wyatt’s face was unnaturally pale, and his dark eyes had become lacklustre and without expression.

===Chinese===
阿伯肖回到麦芽坊的岗位上，对仍昏迷不醒的普伦德比尽了人事，便安顿下来，等待事态的下一步发展。

他早已不再琢磨，是何等离奇的境遇把他这么一位持重体面的伦敦人抛进了如此匪夷所思的处境；此刻他只是坐在那儿朝前瞪视，目光钉在面前那堵灰石墙上。

怀亚特仍瘫在原先倒下的地方；旁人谁也没有去跟他搭话，凭着某种说不清的潜意识，都觉得他宁可独自待着。

阿伯肖把他忘了个干净，因此当他猛然撑起身子、摇摇晃晃站起来时，红头发的小个子医生着实吓了一跳。怀亚特的脸白得反常，深色的眼睛黯淡无光，毫无表情。

===Original===
“I’m sorry,” he said quietly. “I had a brain storm, I think—I must get old Harcourt Gieves to overhaul me if we ever get back to London again.”

“If we ever get back?” The words started out of Abbershaw’s mouth. “My dear fellow, don’t be absurd! We’re bound to get back some time or other.” He heard his own voice speaking testily in the silence of the room, and then with a species of forced cheerfulness foreign to him. “But now I think we shall be out of the house in an hour or so, and I shall be delighted to inform the county police of this amazing outrage.”

Even while he spoke he wondered at himself. The words and the voice were those of a small man speaking of a small thing—he was up against something much bigger than that.

===Chinese===
“对不住。”他平静地说。“我大概是犯了一阵失心疯——要是咱们还能回伦敦，我得去找老哈考特·吉夫斯（Harcourt Gieves）给我大修一回。”

“要是还能回伦敦？”这话不由分说地冲出了阿伯肖的口。“亲爱的伙计，别荒唐了！咱们迟早总能回去的。”他听见自己的声音在寂静的屋子里先是烦躁地说着，随即又换上一种他素来没有的、强打出来的欢快。“不过眼下我看，不出一个钟头咱们就能出宅子了，到时候能把这桩骇人听闻的暴行报告郡警察局，我可要高兴坏了。”

话虽这么说，他心里却在暗自诧异。这番话、这个声口，都是一个小人物在谈一桩小事——而他此刻较量的，是比这大得多的东西。

===Original===
Further conversation was cut short by the arrival of Martin with the now conscious but still dazed Kennedy. The four prisoners remained quiet, and after the first jerky word of greeting and explanation there was no sound in the brewhouse, save the crackling of the fire in the great hearth.

It was Abbershaw himself who first broke the silence. It seemed that they had waited an age, and there was still no audible movement in the house above them.

“I hope he’s all right,” he said nervily.

Martin Watt looked up.

“An extraordinary chap,” he said slowly. “What is he?”

===Chinese===
进一步的交谈被马丁的回来打断了——他带来了已经苏醒却仍晕头晕脑的肯尼迪。四个俘虏安安静静，寒暄和说明的头几句断断续续说过之后，麦芽坊里便再无声息，只有大壁炉里的炉火噼啪作响。

率先打破沉默的正是阿伯肖本人。他们仿佛已经等了一个世纪，头顶的宅子里依然听不见一丝动静。

“但愿他平安无事。”他声音发紧地说。

马丁·沃特抬起头来。

“一个非同寻常的家伙。”他慢吞吞地说。“他到底是干什么的？”

===Original===
Abbershaw hesitated. The more he thought about Mr. Albert Campion’s profession the more confused in mind he became. It was not easy to reconcile what he knew of the man with his ideas on con-men and that type of shady character in general. There was even a possibility, of course, that Campion was a murderer, but the farther away his interview with Mrs. Meade became, the more ridiculous and absurd that supposition seemed. He did not answer Martin’s question, and the boy went on lazily, almost as if he were speaking to himself.

“The fellow strikes one as a congenital idiot,” he said. “Even now I’m not sure that he’s not one; yet if it hadn’t been for him we’d all be in a nasty mess at the present moment. It isn’t that he suddenly stops fooling and becomes serious, though,” he went on, “he’s fooling the whole time all right—he *is* a fool, in fact.”

“He’s an amazing man,” said Abbershaw, adding as though in duty bound, “and a good fellow.” But he would not commit himself further, and the silence began again.

===Chinese===
阿伯肖迟疑了。他对阿尔伯特·坎皮恩先生的职业想得越多，心里就越糊涂。他所了解的这个人，与他心目中的骗子和那一路阴暗角色，实在难以捏合到一处。当然，坎皮恩也未必就没有杀人的可能；可他与米德太太的那次会面每远去一分，这个猜测就显得更可笑、更荒唐一分。他没有回答马丁的问题，小伙子便懒懒地接着说下去，几乎像在自言自语。

“这家伙给人的印象是个天生的傻子。”他说，“直到现在我也不敢断定他不是；可要不是有他，咱们这会儿早陷进大麻烦里了。不过，倒也不是说他忽然收起胡闹、一本正经起来，”他接着说，“他从头到尾都在胡闹，没错——实际上，他*就是*个傻子。”

“他是个了不起的人。”阿伯肖说，又仿佛出于义务地补了一句，“也是个好人。”可他不肯再多说什么，沉默再度开始。

===Original===
Yet no one heard the kitchen door open, or noticed any approach, until a shadow fell over the bright doorway, and Mr. Campion, inoffensive and slightly absurd as ever, appeared on the threshold.

“I’ve scoured the house,” he murmured, “not a soul about. Old Daddy Hun and his pal are not the birds I took them for. They appear to have vamoosed—I fancy I heard a car. Ready?”

“Did you get the women?” It was Abbershaw who spoke. Campion nodded. “They’re here behind me, game as hell. Bring Prenderby over your shoulder, Watt. We’ll all hang together, women in the centre, and the guns on the outside; I don’t think there’s anyone around, but we may as well be careful. Now for the wide open spaces!”

Martin hoisted the unconscious boy over his shoulder and Abbershaw and Wyatt supported Kennedy, who was now rapidly coming to himself, between them. The girls were waiting for them in the kitchen. Jeanne was crying quietly on Meggie’s shoulder, and there was no trace of colour in Anne Edgeware’s round cheeks, but they showed no signs of panic. Campion marshalled the little force into advancing order, placing himself at the head, Meggie and Jeanne behind him, with Abbershaw on one side and Martin and Anne on the other, while Wyatt and Kennedy were behind.

===Chinese===
然而谁也没听见厨房的门开，谁也没察觉有人走近，直到一道影子落在明亮的门洞上——坎皮恩先生，一如既往地人畜无害、略带荒诞，出现在门槛上。

“我把宅子梳了个遍。”他低声道，“一个人影也没有。德国佬老爹和他的伙计，不是我原先想的那种鸟。他们像是溜之大吉了——我恍惚听见有汽车响。准备好了吗？”

“两个女人你接到了吗？”说话的是阿伯肖。坎皮恩点点头。“就在我身后，一个赛一个硬气。把普伦德比扛上肩，沃特。咱们全体抱成团走，女人居中，枪在外围；我看周围是没人了，不过小心无大错。这就向广阔天地进发！”

马丁把昏迷的小伙子扛上肩，阿伯肖和怀亚特一左一右架着肯尼迪——他这会儿正迅速缓过神来。姑娘们已在厨房里等着他们。让娜伏在梅吉肩头无声地啜泣，安妮·埃奇韦尔的圆脸蛋上血色全无，但她们都没有惊慌的迹象。坎皮恩把这支小小队伍排成行进队形：他自己打头，梅吉和让娜跟在身后，阿伯肖在一侧，马丁和安妮在另一侧，怀亚特和肯尼迪殿后。

===Original===
“The side door,” said Campion. “It takes us nearest the garage—there may be some juice about now. If not, we must toddle of course. The tour will now proceed, visiting the Albert Memorial, Ciro’s, and the Royal Ophthalmic Hospital …”

As he spoke he led them down the stone passageway, out of the door under the stairs, and down the corridor to the side door, through which Abbershaw had gone to visit the garage on the fateful night of the Dagger Ritual.

===Chinese===
“走边门。”坎皮恩说。“离车库最近——这会儿那儿说不定还剩点儿油。要是没有，咱们自然只好靠两条腿溜达了。观光团现在继续行程，途经阿尔伯特纪念塔、西罗餐厅（Ciro’s）和皇家眼科医院……”

说话间，他领着他们沿石砌过道而下，出了楼梯底下的那扇门，顺着走廊走到边门——就在匕首仪式那定夺命运的凶夜里，阿伯肖正是从这道门出去查看车库的。

===Original===
“Now,” he said, as with extraordinarily silent fingers he manoeuvred the ponderous bars and locks on the great door, “this is where the orchestra begins to play soft music and the circle shuffles for its hats as we fall into one another’s arms—that’s done it!”

On the last word the hinges creaked faintly as the heavy door swung inwards. The night was pitch dark but warm and pleasant, and they went out eagerly on the gravel, each conscious of an unspeakable relief as the realization of freedom came to them.

===Chinese===
“好了。”他说，一面用轻得出奇的手指摆弄着大门上沉重的门闩和铁锁，“这一刻，乐队奏起柔美的音乐，楼座观众摸索着取帽，我们相互跌进彼此的怀抱——成了！”

话音落下，沉重的门朝里荡开，铰链发出轻微的吱呀声。夜黑得像泼了墨，却温暖宜人；他们急切地踏上砂砾地，自由已成现实，人人心里都涌起一股难以言说的宽慰。

===Original===
“My God!” The words were uttered in a sob as Campion started forward.

At the same moment the others caught a shadowy glimpse of the radiator of a great car not two yards ahead of them. Then they were enveloped in the glare of enormous headlights, which completely blinded them.

They stood dazed and helpless for an instant, caught mercilessly and held by the glare.

A quiet German voice spoke out of the brightness, cold, and inexplicably horrible in its tonelessness.

“I have covered the girl with red hair with my revolver; my assistant has the woman on the left as his aim. If there is any movement from anybody other than those I shall command, we shall both fire. Put your hands over your heads. Everybody! … So.”

===Chinese===
“我的上帝！”坎皮恩抢步向前，这四个字几乎是呜咽着吐出来的。

同一瞬间，其余人也影影绰绰瞥见一辆大轿车的散热器，就在他们前方不到两码处。紧接着，巨大的车头灯光柱将他们整个罩住，晃得他们什么也看不见。

他们茫然无措地站了一瞬，被那光毫不留情地攫住，钉在原地。

一个平静的德语嗓音从光亮里传出来，冰冷，不带一丝抑扬，透着说不出的可怖。

“我的左轮枪已罩住红头发姑娘；我的助手瞄准左边的女人。除我下令者外，任何人若敢动一动，我们两人便同时开枪。把手举到头顶上。所有人！……好。”

## XXI The Point of View of Benjamin Dawlish / 第二十一章 道利什的观点

===Original===
It was all over very quickly.

There was no way of telling if the cold merciless voice behind the blinding lights was speaking truth or no, but in the circumstances it was impossible not to regard it.

The little party stood there, hands raised above their heads; then hurrying footsteps echoed down the stone corridor behind them and their erstwhile prisoners surrounded them.

The German had lied when he spoke of his assistant, then. The man must have slipped into the house by the other door and released the men in the brewhouse.

===Chinese===
这一切结束得非常之快。

强光灯影后头那冰冷无情的嗓音，究竟是真是假，无从判断，但在此情此景之下，谁也不可能不俯首听命。

这一小队人站在原地，双手高举过头；随后匆匆的脚步声从他们身后的石砌走廊里震荡传来，方才还是他们阶下囚的那伙人，此刻将他们团团围住。

这么说，那德国人说起他助手时是撒了谎的。那人必定是从另一道门溜进了宅子，放出了押在麦芽坊里的人。

===Original===
“You will now go up to a room on the top floor to which my men will lead you. Anyone who makes the least attempt to escape will be shot instantly. By ‘shot’ I mean shot dead.”

The voice of Benjamin Dawlish came clearly to them from behind the wall of light. The icy tonelessness which had made the voice so terrible on the first hearing was still there and Abbershaw had a vision of the expressionless face behind it, heavy and without life, like a mask.

The spirit of the little group was momentarily broken. They had made their attempt and failed in the very moment when their success seemed assured.

===Chinese===
“你们现在就上顶层的一个房间去，我的人会带路。谁有半分逃跑的企图，立刻枪毙。我所说的‘枪毙’，就是当场打死。”

本杰明·道利什的声音从那道光墙后清晰地传来。初次听闻时令这嗓音显得那般可怖的冰冷平板，此刻依旧未改，阿伯肖脑中随之浮现出嗓音背后那张毫无表情的脸——滞重、死气沉沉，宛如一副面具。

这一小队人的士气一时被击垮了。他们放手一搏，却偏偏在胜券在握的那一刻功败垂成。

===Original===
Again unarmed, they were forced back into the house and placed in a room on the top floor at the far end of the long gallery where Albert Campion had had his fight with the butler. It was a long narrow room, oak-panelled, but without a fireplace, and lighted only from a single narrow iron-barred window.

Even as Abbershaw entered it, a feeling of misgiving overcame him. Other rooms had possibilities of escape; this held none.

It was completely empty, and the door was of treble oak, iron-studded. It had doubtless been used at one time as a private chapel, possibly in those times when it was wisest to hold certain religious ceremonies behind barred doors.

The only light came from a hurricane lantern which one of the men had brought up with him. He set it on the floor now so that the room was striped with grotesque shadows. The prisoners were herded down to the end of the room, two men keeping them covered the whole time.

===Chinese===
再度被缴了械，他们被押回宅内，安置在顶层的一个房间里——就在长廊顶头，正是阿尔伯特·坎皮恩当初与管家交手的地方。这是一间狭长的屋子，四壁镶着橡木护板，却没有壁炉，唯一的光线来自一扇窄窄的装着铁栏的窗。

阿伯肖一踏进屋，一股不祥的预感便压上心头。别的房间多少还有脱身的可能；这一间却半点也无。

屋里空空如也，房门是三层橡木所制，钉满铁钉。它无疑曾一度被用作私家礼拜堂，或许是在那个年代——彼时把某些宗教仪式关在门闩后面举行，才是最稳妥的。

唯一的光亮来自一个歹徒随身带上来的风暴雨提灯。他此刻把它搁在地上，屋里顿时被怪诞的条条阴影割得七零八落。囚徒们被驱赶到屋子的尽头，两人持枪监视，寸步不离。

===Original===
Martin Watt set Prenderby down in a corner, and Jeanne, still crying quietly, squatted down beside him and took his head in her lap.

Abbershaw darted forward towards their captors.

“This is absurd,” he said bitterly. “Either let us interview Mr. Dawlish downstairs or let him come up to us. It’s most important that we should come to a proper understanding at last.”

One of the men laughed.

“I’m afraid you don’t know what you’re talking about,” he said in a curiously cultured voice. “As a matter of fact I believe Mr. Dawlish is coming up to talk to you in a moment or so. But I’m afraid you’ve got a rather absurd view of the situation altogether. You don’t seem to realize the peculiar powers of our chief.”

===Chinese===
马丁·沃特把普伦德比安置在一个角落里，让娜仍在低声啜泣，她挨着他蹲下身，把他的头搂到自己膝上。

阿伯肖朝看守他们的人疾步抢上前去。

“这太荒唐了，”他愤愤地说，“要么让我们到楼下去见道利什先生，要么让他上楼来见我们。最要紧的是，我们总得最终达成一个像样的谅解。”

其中一人笑了。

“恐怕你们根本不知道自己在说什么，”他说，嗓音出奇地斯文有教养，“不瞒各位，我想道利什先生过一会儿就会上来跟你们谈谈。不过恕我直言，你们对眼下局面的看法未免太荒唐了。你们似乎没有领教过我们头儿的特殊手段。”

===Original===
Wyatt leaned against the oak panelling, his arms folded and his chin upon his breast. Ever since the incident in the brewhouse he had been peculiarly morose and silent. Mr. Campion also was unusually quiet, and there was an expression on his face that betrayed his anxiety. Meggie and Anne stood together. They were obviously very frightened, but they did not speak or move. Chris Kennedy fumed with impotent rage, and Martin Watt was inclined to be argumentative.

“I don’t know what the damn silly game is,” he said, “but whatever it is it’s time we stopped playing. Your confounded ‘Chief’ may be the great Pooh-Bah himself for all I care, but if he thinks he can imprison nine respectable citizens for an indefinite period on the coast of Suffolk without getting himself into serious trouble he’s barmy, that’s all there is to it. What’s going to happen when inquiries start being made?”

The man who had spoken before did not answer, but he smiled, and there was something very unpleasant and terrifying about that smile.

===Chinese===
怀亚特倚着橡木护壁，双臂交叠，下巴抵在胸前。自麦芽坊那场变故以来，他就格外阴郁沉默。坎皮恩先生也异乎寻常地安静，脸上带着掩饰不住的忧色。梅吉和安妮站在一起，显然吓得不轻，却既不说话也不动弹。克里斯·肯尼迪满腔怒火却无处发泄，马丁·沃特则一副随时要抬杠的架势。

“我不知道这该死的蠢游戏到底是个什么名堂，”他说，“但不管它是什么，也到该收场的时候了。你们那位混账“头儿”就算是大波巴（Pooh-Bah）本人我也懒得管，可他要是以为能把九个体面公民无限期地囚禁在萨福克的海边，还不给自己招来天大的麻烦，那他就是疯子，没什么好说的。等外面开始追查询问的时候，他打算怎么办？”

先前说话的那人没有答腔，只是微微一笑，而那笑容里有一种令人极不痛快、毛骨悚然的东西。

===Original===
Further remarks from Martin were cut short by steps in the corridor outside and the sudden appearance of Mr. Benjamin Dawlish himself, followed by Gideon, pale and stiff from his adventure, but smiling sardonically, his round eyes veiled, and his wicked mouth drawn all over to one side in the “O” which so irritated Abbershaw.

“Now look here, sir.” It was Martin Watt who spoke. “It’s time you had a straight talk with us. You may be a criminal, but you’re behaving like a lunatic, and—”

“Stop that, young man.”

Dawlish’s deep unemotional voice sounded heavily in the big room, and instantly the boy found that he had the muzzle of a revolver pressed against his ribs.

“Shut up,” a voice murmured in his ear, “or you’ll be plugged as sure as hell.”

===Chinese===
马丁还想再说，却被走廊里的脚步声打断——本杰明·道利什先生本人赫然现身，吉迪恩紧随其后，一场历险下来脸色苍白、浑身僵直，嘴角却挂着讥诮的笑，圆眼睛半掩着，那张凶相的嘴整个撇向一边，扭成那个令阿伯肖大为光火的“O”形。

“喂，你听着，先生。”开口的是马丁·沃特，“你该跟我们开诚布公地谈一谈了。你做罪犯归做罪犯，可你这行事做派简直像个疯子，而且——”

“住口，年轻人。”

道利什那深沉而不带感情的嗓音在大房间里沉沉响起，刹那间这小伙子便觉得一杆左轮手枪的枪口顶住了自己的肋骨。

“闭嘴，”一个声音在他耳边低语，“不然保准给你一颗枪子儿，绝无二话。”

===Original===
Martin relapsed into helpless silence, and the German continued. He was still unblinking and expressionless, his heavy red face deeply shadowed in the fantastic light. He looked at them steadily from one to the other as if he had been considering them individually, but there was no indication from his face or his manner to betray anything of his conclusions.

“So,” he said, “when I look at you I see how young you all are, and it does not surprise me any longer that you should be so foolish. You are ignorant, that is why you are so absurd.”

“If you’ve come here to be funny—” Martin burst out, but the gun against his ribs silenced him, and the German went on speaking in his inflexible voice as if there had been no interruption.

“Before I explain to you what exactly I have ordained shall happen,” he said, “I have decided to make everything quite clear to you. I do this because it is my fancy that none of you should consider I have behaved in any way unreasonably. I shall begin at the beginning. On Friday night Colonel Coombe was murdered in this house while you were playing in the dark with that ancient dagger which hangs in the hall. It was with that dagger that he was killed.”

===Chinese===
马丁颓然归于沉默，德国人继续说下去。他依旧目不斜视、面无表情，那张厚重的红脸在光怪陆离的灯影里显得阴影深重。他的目光平稳地从一张脸移到另一张脸，仿佛在逐一掂量每个人，但他的脸色和举止都丝毫透不出他的结论。

“这么说吧，”他开了口，“我看着你们，才看清你们是多么年轻，也就不再奇怪你们何以这般愚蠢。你们无知，所以才会这般荒唐。”

“你要是专程来耍宝的——”马丁脱口喊道，可顶着肋骨的枪口让他闭了嘴，德国人以那不容变通的语调继续说下去，仿佛中间不曾有过任何打断。

“在我向诸位说明我已定下何等处置之前，”他说，“我已决定把一切都向你们交代清楚。我之所以如此，是因为我不愿让你们中间任何一个人觉得我的行事有半分不讲道理。我就从头说起。星期五夜里，库姆上校在这座宅子里被人谋杀了——当时你们正在黑暗中传那柄悬在门厅里的古匕首玩。要了他性命的，正是那柄匕首。”

===Original===
This announcement was news to some of his hearers, and his quick eyes took in the expressions of the little group before him. “I concealed that murder,” he continued deliberately, “because at that time there were several very excellent reasons why I should do so. It would have been of very great inconvenience to me if there had been an inquest upon Coombe, as he was in my employ, and I do not tolerate any interference, private or official, in my affairs. Apart from that, however, the affair had very little interest for me, but I should like to make it clear now that although I do not know his identity, the person who killed Gordon Coombe is in this room facing me. I say this advisedly because I know that no one entered the house from outside that night, nor has any stranger left it since, and even had they not perfect alibis there is no reason why I should credit it to one of my own people.”

His inference was clear, and there was a moment of resentment among the young people, although no one spoke. The German went on with inexorable calm.

“But as I have said,” he repeated, in his awkward pedantic English, “that does not interest me. What is more important to me is this. Either the murderer stole a packet of papers off the body of his victim, or else Colonel Coombe handed them at some time or other in that evening to one of you. Those papers are mine. I think I estimate their value to me at something over half one million pounds. There is one other man in the world to whom they would be worth something approaching the same value. I assume that one of you here is a servant of that man.”

===Chinese===
这话对听者中的某些人而言不啻是新闻，他那双锐眼将面前这一小队人的神色尽收眼底。“我隐瞒了那桩谋杀，”他不慌不忙地继续道，“因为在那当时，有几个非常充分的理由要我这么做。倘若对库姆展开死因研讯，会给我添极大的麻烦，因为他受雇于我，而我的事务不容任何人插手，官方的也好，私下的也罢。不过除此以外，这桩案子本身于我并无多少趣味。只是我现在想说明白：尽管我不知道那人是谁，杀害戈登·库姆的人就在这间屋子里，正对着我。我这话是经过深思的，因为我知道那晚没有任何外人进过这座宅子，此后也没有任何生人离开过；退一万步说，纵然他们没有天衣无缝的不在场证明，我也没有丝毫理由把这桩事安到自己人头上。”

他的言下之意再清楚不过，年轻人中间起了一阵愤懑的骚动，却没有人出声。德国人以一种不容置辩的平静继续说下去。

“但正如我方才所言，”他又用他那生硬迂腐的英语重复道，“那桩事与我无关。对我更要紧的是这个：要么是凶手从死者身上窃走了一包文件，要么是库姆上校在那晚某个时候把它们交给了你们中间的某一位。那些文件是我的。我估量它们对我的价值，总在五十万英镑开外。普天之下，只有另外一个人，它们在他手里的价值能与这数目相仿佛。我据此认定，你们中间就有一人是那人的爪牙。”

===Original===
Again he paused, and again his small round eyes scrutinized the faces before him. Then, apparently satisfied, he continued. “You will admit that I have done everything in my power to obtain possession of these papers without harming anyone. From the first you have behaved abominably. May I suggest that you have played hide-and-seek about the house like schoolchildren? And at last you have annoyed me. There are also one or two among you”—he glanced at Abbershaw—“with whom I have old scores to settle. You have been searched, and you have been watched, yet no trace of my property has come to light. Therefore I give you one last chance. At eleven o’clock tomorrow morning I leave this house with my staff. We shall take the side roads that will lead us on to the main Yarmouth motor way without passing through any villages. If I have my property in my possession when I go, I will see that you can contrive your release for yourselves. If not—”

He paused, and they realized the terrible thing that was coming a full second before the quiet words left his lips.

“I shall first set fire to the house. To shoot you direct would be dangerous—even charred skeletons may show traces of bullet fractures. No, I am afraid I must just leave you to the fire.”

In the breathless silence that followed his announcement Jeanne’s sobs became suddenly very audible, and Abbershaw, his face pale and horror-stricken, leapt forward.

===Chinese===
他又停了下来，那双小圆眼再一次细细审视面前的面孔。随后，似乎是满意了，他才接着说下去。“你们得承认，为了拿到那包文件而不伤人害命，我已尽了全力。而你们从一开头就胡闹得不像话。恕我提醒一句：你们像一群学童似的，满宅子玩了这么一场捉迷藏。到头来，你们把我惹恼了。再者，你们中间还有一两位”——他瞥了阿伯肖一眼——“跟我有些旧账要算。你们被搜过身，也被盯过梢，可我的财物至今不见踪影。因此，我给你们最后一次机会。明天上午十一点整，我率部下离开此宅。我们会走那些岔路，绕上雅茅斯（Yarmouth）的公路干道，沿途不经过一座村庄。我走的时候，那包文件若已在我手中，我自会让你们能有法子自行脱身。若不然——”

他顿住了，而他那些平静的字眼还未出口，众人早在足足一秒之前，便已料到那件可怕的事情是什么了。

“我会先放火烧宅。直接枪杀你们未免危险——烧焦的骸骨上，也验得出枪伤的裂痕。不，恕我只好把你们留给烈火了。”

他话音落下，一片令人窒息的死寂中，让娜的呜咽陡然清晰可闻；阿伯肖面色惨白、惊骇欲绝，猛地抢上前去。

===Original===
“But I told you,” he said passionately. “I told you. I burnt those papers. I described them to you. I burnt them—the ashes are probably in my bedroom grate now.”

A sound that was half a snarl, half a cry, broke from the German, and for the second time they saw the granite composure of his face broken, and had a vision of the livid malevolence behind the mask.

“If I could believe, Dr. Abbershaw,” he said, “that you could ever be so foolish—so incredibly foolish—as to destroy a packet of papers, a portion of whose value must have been evident to you, then I could believe also that you could deserve no better fate than the singularly unpleasant death which most certainly awaits you and your friends unless I am in possession of my property by eleven o’clock tomorrow morning. Good night, ladies and gentlemen. I leave you to think it over.”

He passed out of the room on the last words, the smirking Gideon on his heels. His men backed out after him, their guns levelled. Abbershaw dashed after them just as the great door swung to. He beat upon it savagely with his clenched fists, but the oak was like a rock.

===Chinese===
“可我说过了，”他激愤地喊道，“我说过了！那包文件是我烧掉的。样子我都向你们描述过了。我烧了它们——灰烬这会儿多半还在我卧室的炉格里。”

德国人喉中迸出一声半似咆哮、半似怒吼的声音，众人第二次眼见他那张脸花岗岩般的镇定碎裂开来，窥见了面具背后那青灰色的刻骨凶残。

“阿伯肖医生，倘若我能相信，”他说，“你竟能愚蠢到这地步——愚蠢得匪夷所思——竟会毁掉一包文件，一包其部分价值你必定心中有数的文件，那么我同样可以相信：你也就不配有比这更好的下场了——除非明天上午十一点之前我的财物回到我手中，否则那场极不好受的死法，定然正等着你和你的朋友们。晚安，女士们、先生们。你们好好思量吧。”

话音未落，他已步出房间，嬉皮笑脸的吉迪恩紧随其后；他的人倒退着退出门去，枪口平端。阿伯肖疾追上去，正赶上那扇大门轰然阖拢。他攥紧双拳狠狠捶打，但那橡木坚如磐石。

===Original===
“Burn?” Martin’s voice broke the silence, and it was almost wondering. “But the place is stone—it can’t burn.”

Wyatt raised his eyes slowly.

“The outer walls are stone,” he said, and there was a curious note in his voice which sent a thrill of horror through everyone who heard it. “The outer walls are stone, but the rest of it is oak, old, well-seasoned oak. It will burn like kindling wood in a grate.”

===Chinese===
“烧？”马丁的声音打破了沉默，语气近乎茫然，“可这宅子是石头的——石头烧不起来啊。”

怀亚特缓缓抬起眼睛。

“外墙是石头的，”他说，嗓音里有一种奇异的音调，听得人人不由自主地打了个寒噤，“外墙是石头的，可其余的尽是橡木——老旧的、干透了的橡木。烧起来，就跟炉膛里的引柴一样利索。”

## XXII The Darkest Hour / 第二十二章 至暗时刻

===Original===
“The time,” said Mr. Campion, “is nine o’clock.”

Chris Kennedy stretched himself wearily.

“Six hours since that swine left us,” he said. “Do you think we’ve got an earthly?”

There was a stir in the room after he had spoken, and almost everybody looked at the pale-haired bespectacled young man who sat squatting on his haunches in a corner. Jeanne and Prenderby were alone unconscious of what was going on. The little girl still supported the boy’s head in her lap, with her timid little figure crouched over him, her face hidden.

===Chinese===
“时间嘛，”坎皮恩先生说，“现在是九点整。”

克里斯·肯尼迪疲惫地舒展了一下身子。

“那个猪猡丢下咱们，已经六个钟头了。”他说。“你说咱们还有半点儿指望吗？”

他话音落下，屋里起了一阵骚动，几乎人人都望向角落里那个蹲坐着的淡色头发、戴眼镜的年轻人。唯有让娜和普伦德比两个，对眼前发生的一切浑然不觉。小姑娘仍把小伙子的头托在膝上，怯生生的瘦小身子伏在他上方，脸埋着看不见。

===Original===
Albert Campion shook his head.

“I don’t know,” he said, but there was no hopefulness in his tone, and once again the little group relapsed into the silence that had settled over them after the first outburst which had followed von Faber’s departure.

Whatever their attitude had been before, they were all now very much alive to the real peril of their position.

Von Faber had not been wasting his time when he had spoken to them, and they had each been struck by the stark callousness which had been visible in him throughout the entire interview.

===Chinese===
阿尔伯特·坎皮恩摇了摇头。

“不知道。”他说，可语气里没有半点指望。这一小群人再度沉入静默——冯·法贝尔离去后那阵最初的骚动平息下来，这静默便一直笼罩着他们。

不管先前各人态度如何，此刻他们全都清清楚楚地感到了自身处境的真正凶险。

冯·法贝尔同他们说话时，字字句句都不是白说的；那场谈话自始至终，他浑身上下透出的赤裸裸的冷酷无情，在场每个人都看在眼里，心头凛然。

===Original===
At last Campion rose to his feet and came across to where Meggie and Abbershaw were seated. Gravely he offered Abbershaw his cigarette-case in which there was a single cigarette neatly cut into two pieces.

“I did it with a razor blade,” he said. “Rather neat, don’t you think?”

Abbershaw took the half gratefully and they shared a match.

===Chinese===
终于，坎皮恩站起身，走到梅吉和阿伯肖坐着的地方。他郑重其事地把烟盒递给阿伯肖——盒里孤零零一支香烟，齐齐整整剖成了两半。

“我用剃刀片弄的。”他说。“挺利索的吧，你说？”

阿伯肖感激地接过那半支，两人合用了一根火柴。

===Original===
“I suppose,” said Campion suddenly, speaking in a quiet and confidential tone, “I suppose you did really burn that junk, Doc.”

Abbershaw glanced at him sharply.

“I did,” he said. “God forgive me. When I think what I’m responsible for I feel I shall go mad.”

Mr. Campion shrugged his shoulders.

===Chinese===
“我猜啊，”坎皮恩忽然开了口，语气平静而推心置腹，“我猜你真把那玩意儿烧掉了，大夫。”

阿伯肖锐利地瞥了他一眼。

“烧了。”他说。“上帝饶恕我。一想到我如今担着多大的干系，我就觉得自己快要疯了。”

坎皮恩先生耸了耸肩。

===Original===
“My dear old bird,” he said, “I shouldn’t put too much stress on what our friend von Faber says. He doesn’t seem to me to be a person to be relied upon.”

“Why? Do you think he’s just trying to frighten us?”

Abbershaw spoke eagerly, and the other shook his head.

“I’m afraid not, in the sense you mean,” he said. “I think he’s set his heart on this little conflagration scene. The man is a criminal loony, of course. No, I only meant that probably, had someone handed over his million-dollar book of the words, the Guy Fawkes celebrations would have gone forward all the same. I’m afraid he’s just a nasty vindictive person.”

===Chinese===
“我亲爱的老伙计，”他说，“冯·法贝尔朋友的话，不必太往心里去。依我看，他不是个靠得住的人。”

“为什么？你觉得他不过是想吓唬咱们？”

阿伯肖说得急切，对方摇了摇头。

“恐怕不是，不是你说的那层意思。”他说。“我看他是铁了心要上演这出小小的纵火戏。这人当然是个犯罪疯子。不，我不过是说，多半就算有人把他那本价值百万美元的密码册子交了出来，这场盖伊·福克斯（Guy Fawkes）庆典照样照办不误。我看他就是个歹毒记仇的家伙。”

===Original===
Meggie shuddered but her voice was quite firm.

“Do you mean to say that you really think he’ll burn the house down with us up here?” she said.

Campion looked up at her, and then at Abbershaw.

“Not a nice type is he?” he murmured. “I’m afraid we’re for it, unless by a miracle the villagers see the bonfire before we’re part of it, or the son of our friend in the attic calls earlier than was expected.”

===Chinese===
梅吉打了个寒噤，声音却十分镇定。

“你是说，你真的认为他会把咱们困在这儿，一把火烧了这宅子？”她说。

坎皮恩抬眼望了望她，又望了望阿伯肖。

“不是个善茬儿，对吧？”他喃喃道。“恐怕咱们在劫难逃，除非奇迹出现——村里人赶在咱们变成篝火的一部分之前望见了火光，要不然，就是咱们阁楼上那位朋友的儿子，来得比预想的早。”

===Original===
Meggie stiffened.

“Mrs. Meade,” she said. “I’d forgotten all about her. What will Mr. Dawlish do about her, do you suppose?”

Mr. Campion spoke grimly.

“I could guess,” he said, and there was silence for a while after that.

===Chinese===
梅吉浑身一僵。

“米德太太。”她说。“我把她忘得一干二净了。你猜道利什先生会怎么处置她？”

坎皮恩先生说得冷冷的。

“我猜得出来。”他说。此后有好一阵子，谁都没有作声。

===Original===
“But how terrible!” Meggie burst out suddenly. “I didn’t believe that people like this were allowed to exist. I thought we were civilized. I thought this sort of thing couldn’t happen.”

Mr. Campion sighed. “A lot of people believe things like that,” he said. “They imagine the world is a well-ordered nursery with Scotland Yard and the British Army standing by to whack anybody who quarrels or uses a naughty word. I thought that at one time, I suppose everybody does, but it’s not like that really, you know. Look at me, for example—who would dream of the cunning criminal brain that lurks beneath my inoffensive exterior?”

The other two regarded him curiously. In any other circumstances they would have been embarrassed. Abbershaw was the first to speak.

===Chinese===
“多可怕啊！”梅吉突然脱口喊道。“我从前不信，世上竟会被容许有这样的人存在。我以为我们是文明人。我以为这种事根本不可能发生。”

坎皮恩先生叹了口气。“许多人都是这么信的。”他说，“他们把世界想象成一间秩序井然的育儿室，苏格兰场和大英陆军守在一旁，谁吵架、谁讲顽皮话，就揍谁一顿。我从前有一阵也这么想——我猜人人都这么想过——可实情并不是那样，你知道。就拿我来说吧——谁能梦想得到，我这副人畜无害的外表底下，竟潜藏着一颗狡诈的犯罪头脑？”

另外两人好奇地望着他。换在任何别的场合，他们准会觉得尴尬。阿伯肖先开了口。

===Original===
“I say,” he said, “if you don’t mind my asking such a thing, what on earth made you take up your—er—present profession?”

Mr. Campion regarded him owlishly through his enormous spectacles.

“Profession?” he said indignantly. “It’s my vocation. It seemed to me that I had no talent for anything else, but in this line I can eke out the family pittance with tolerable comfort. Of course,” he went on suddenly, as he caught sight of Meggie’s face, “I don’t exactly ‘crim,’ you know, as I told the doc here. My taste is impeccable. Most of my commissions are more secret than shady. I occasionally do a spot of work for the Government, though, of course, that isn’t as lucrative as honest crime. This little affair, of course, was perfectly simple. I had only to join this house-party, take a packet of letters from the old gentleman, toddle back to the Savoy, and my client would be waiting for me. A hundred guineas, and all clean fun—no brain-work required.” He beamed at them. “Of course I knew what I was in for,” he went on. “I knew that more or less as soon as I got down here. I didn’t expect anything quite like this, though, I admit. I’m afraid the Gay Career and all that is in the soup.”

===Chinese===
“我说，”他开口道，“要是你不嫌我问得冒失，究竟是什么缘故，让你干上了你那个——呃——如今的行当？”

坎皮恩先生从他那副硕大眼镜的后面，猫头鹰似的瞪眼瞧着他。

“行当？”他愤愤然道。“这是我的天职。我自觉旁的什么都无才可用，干上这一行呢，倒还能称得上舒舒服服地贴补些微薄的家用。“当然啦，”他忽然瞥见梅吉的脸色，赶忙接着说，“你也知道，我并不真‘干黑活儿’——我跟这位大夫说过的。我的品味无可挑剔。我接的委托，与其说见不得人，不如说机密得很。我偶尔也替政府做点儿零星活计，当然啦，那可不如老老实实犯罪来钱多。眼下这桩小差事，本来简单至极：我只消混进这场宅邸聚会，从老先生那儿取走一包信件，再溜溜达达回萨伏伊饭店（the Savoy），委托人自会等着我。一百几尼（一几尼合二十一先令），桩桩件件干净爽利——一点脑子都不必费。”他冲他们咧嘴一笑。“当然，这一趟水深不浅，我早知道。”他接着说。“下到这儿没多久，我就大致心里有数了。不过我承认，这般光景确实没料到。恐怕我这‘快乐生涯’连同这一整套，全都要泡汤了。”

===Original===
He spoke lightly, but there was no callousness in his face, and it suddenly occurred to Abbershaw that he was doing his best to cheer them up, for after a moment or two of silence he remarked suddenly:

“After all, I don’t see why the place should burn as he says it will, and I know people do escape from burning houses because I’ve seen it on the pictures.”

His remarks were cut short by a thundering blow upon the door, and in the complete silence that followed, a voice spoke slowly and distinctly so that it was audible throughout the entire room.

“You have another hour,” it said, “in which to restore Mr. Dawlish’s property. If it is not forthcoming by that time there will be another of these old country-mansion fires which have been so frequent of late. It is not insured and so it is not likely that anyone will inquire into the cause too closely.”

===Chinese===
他说得轻描淡写，脸上却没有一丝冷漠。阿伯肖忽然省悟：他这是在竭力给大伙儿打气。因为沉默了片刻之后，他冷不丁说道：

“话说回来，我不明白这宅子凭什么就非得像他说的那样烧起来；再说，我知道人确实能从着火的房子里逃生——我在电影里见过。”

话音未落，门上雷霆般的一击把他打断了。随之而来的是一片死寂，一个声音缓缓响起，字字清晰，整间屋子都听得见。

“你们还有一个钟头，”那声音说，“可以用来归还道利什先生的财物。届时若仍拿不出来，近来频发的乡间古宅火灾，便要再添一桩。这宅子没有上保险，只怕不会有人把起火原因追查得太细。”

===Original===
Martin Watt threw himself against the door with all his strength, and there was a soft amused laugh from outside.

“We heard your attempts to batter down the door last night,” said the voice, “and Mr. Dawlish would like you to know that although he has perfect faith in it holding, he has taken the precaution to reinforce it considerably on this side. As you have probably found out, the walls, too, are not negotiable and the window won’t afford you much satisfaction.”

“You dirty swine!” shouted Chris Kennedy weakly from his corner, and Martin Watt turned slowly upon his heel and came back into the centre of the room, an expression of utter hopelessness on his face.

===Chinese===
马丁·沃特使出全身力气朝门撞去，门外传来一声轻悠悠、饶有兴味的笑。

“昨夜你们砸门的努力，我们都听见了。”那声音说，“道利什先生愿各位知道：他固然对这扇门撑得住信心十足，但还是防了一手，在这一侧大大加固过了。各位多半也试出来了，墙壁同样钻不动，窗户嘛，也供不了各位多少乐趣。”

“你这龌龊的猪猡！”克里斯·肯尼迪在角落里有气无力地喊道。马丁·沃特缓缓转过脚跟，走回屋子中央，一脸彻底的绝望。

===Original===
“I’m afraid we’re sunk,” he said slowly and quietly and moved over towards the window, where he stood peering out between the bars.

Wyatt sat propped up against the wall, his chin supported in his hands, and his eyes fixed steadily upon the floor in front of him. For some time he had neither moved nor spoken. As Abbershaw glanced at him he could not help being reminded once again of the family portraits in the big dining-hall, and he seemed somehow part and parcel of the old house, sitting there morosely waiting for the end.

Meggie suddenly lifted her head.

===Chinese===
“恐怕咱们是完了。”他慢悠悠、轻声地说，随即朝窗边走去，站在那儿，从铁栏杆缝里向外张望。

怀亚特靠着墙坐在地上，双手托着下巴，目光一动不动地盯在面前的地板上。有好一阵子，他既没动过，也没说过话。阿伯肖朝他望去，不由得又一次想起大饭厅里那些家族肖像——他坐在那里，阴郁地静候着终结，不知怎的，仿佛已同这座老宅浑然一体。

梅吉忽然抬起头来。

===Original===
“How extraordinary,” she said softly, “to think that everything is going on just the same only a mile or two away. I heard a dog barking somewhere. It’s incredible that this fearful thing should be happening to us and no one near enough to get us out. Think of it,” she went on quietly. “A man murdered and taken away casually as if it were a light thing, and then a criminal lunatic”—she paused and her brown eyes narrowed—“I hope he’s a lunatic—calmly proposes to massacre us all. It’s unthinkable.”

There was silence for a moment after she had spoken, and then Campion looked at Abbershaw.

“That yarn about Coombe,” he said quietly. “I can’t get over it. Are you sure he was murdered?”

===Chinese===
“多不可思议啊，”她轻声说，“想想看，不过一两英里外，一切都在照旧运转。我还听见过不知哪儿有狗叫。这么可怕的事正落在咱们头上，却没有任何一个人离得够近、能把咱们救出去——简直叫人难以置信。你想想，”她静静地接着说，“一个人遭了谋杀，又被随随便便抬走，仿佛那不过是桩轻巧事；接着一个犯罪疯子”——她顿了顿，棕色的眼睛眯了起来——“但愿他是个疯子——又不慌不忙地提议把咱们斩尽杀绝。这简直无法想象。”

她说完，屋里静了片刻，随后坎皮恩朝阿伯肖望过来。

“关于库姆的那番说辞，”他平静地说，“我怎么也想不通。你确定他是被谋杀的？”

===Original===
Abbershaw glanced at him shrewdly. It seemed unbelievable that this pleasant, inoffensive-looking young man could be a murderer attempting to cast off any suspicion against himself, and yet, on the face of Mrs. Meade’s story, the evidence looked very black against him.

As he did not reply, Campion went on.

“I don’t understand it at all,” he said. “The man was so valuable to them  … he must have been.”

===Chinese===
阿伯肖机敏地瞥了他一眼。这个讨人喜欢、看似无害的年轻人，竟可能是想甩脱自身嫌疑的杀人凶手——这似乎难以置信；然而照米德太太那番讲述来看，证据对他又大大不利。

见他不答话，坎皮恩接着往下说。

“我完全闹不明白。”他说。“那人对他们太有价值了……不可能没有。”

===Original===
Abbershaw hesitated, and then he said quietly:

“Are you sure he was—I mean do you *know* he was?”

Campion’s pale eyes opened to their fullest extent behind his enormous glasses.

===Chinese===
阿伯肖迟疑了一下，随即平静地说：

“你确定他是——我是说，你*知道*他是吗？”

坎皮恩那双浅色的眼睛，在硕大的镜片后面瞪到了最大。

===Original===
“I know he was to be paid a fabulous sum by Simister for his services,” he said, “and I know that on a certain day next month there was to be a man waiting at a big London hotel to meet him. That man is the greatest genius at disguise in Europe, and his instructions were to give the old boy a facelift and one or two other natty gadgets and hand him a ticket for the first transatlantic liner, complete with passport, family history, and pretty niece. Von Faber didn’t know that, of course, but even if he did I don’t see why he should stick the old gentleman in the gizzard, do you? The whole thing beats me. Besides, why does he want to saddle us with the nasty piece of work? It’s the sort of thing he’d never convince us about. I don’t see it myself. It can’t be some bright notion of easing his own conscience.”

Abbershaw remained silent. He could not forget the old woman’s strangely convincing story, the likelihood of which was borne out by Campion’s own argument, but the more he thought about the man at his side, the more absurd did an explanation in that direction seem.

===Chinese===
“我知道西米斯特原打算为他的效劳付他一笔骇人听闻的巨款，”他说，“我还知道，下个月的某一天，本该有个人在伦敦一家大饭店里等着与他会面。那人是欧洲首屈一指的易容天才，领受的任务是给老头子拉一拉脸皮，再置办一两样精巧的小道具，然后递给他一张最早一班跨大西洋邮轮的船票——护照、家世，外加一位漂亮侄女，一应俱全。冯·法贝尔当然不知道这一层；可就算知道，我也不明白他干吗要一刀捅进老先生的心窝，你明白吗？整桩事情我摸不着头脑。再者，他干吗要把这桩脏活栽到咱们头上？这种事他无论如何也休想让咱们相信。反正我看不透。总不见得是什么让自家良心好过些的灵光妙想吧。”

阿伯肖依旧沉默。他忘不掉老太太那番出奇令人信服的讲述——坎皮恩这番话反倒印证了它确实可信；可他对身旁这个人想得越多，往那个方向去解释就越显得荒唐。

===Original===
A smothered cry of horror from Martin at the window brought them all to their feet.

“The swine,” he said bitterly, turning to them, his face pale and his eyes glittering. “Look. I saw Dawlish coming out of the garage towards the house. He was carrying petrol cans. He intends to have a good bonfire.”

“Good God!” said Chris Kennedy, who had taken his place at the window. “Here comes a lad with a faggot. Oh, why can’t I get at ’em!”

===Chinese===
窗边马丁一声压住的惊呼，把众人全都带得站了起来。

“这群猪猡。”他恨恨地说，一边转过身来，脸色煞白，两眼放光。“瞧。我看见道利什从车库那边朝宅子来了。他拎着几桶汽油。他是要痛痛快快烧一场好火。”

“天哪！”接替他守在窗口的克里斯·肯尼迪说。“有个小子抱着一捆柴来了。哦，要是我能冲出去收拾他们就好了！”

===Original===
“They’re going to burn us!”

For the first time the true significance of the situation seemed to dawn upon little Jeanne, and she burst into loud hysterical sobbing which was peculiarly unnerving in the tense atmosphere. Meggie crossed over to her and attempted to soothe her, but her self-control had gone completely and she continued to cry violently.

Anne Edgeware, too, was crying, but less noisily, and the tension became intolerable.

===Chinese===
“他们要烧死我们了！”

小让娜似乎这才头一回恍然明白处境的真正含义，顿时放声嚎啕起来，那歇斯底里的哭喊在紧绷的空气里格外叫人心胆俱裂。梅吉走过去试图安抚她，可她的自持早已荡然无存，只是一个劲儿地拼命哭。

安妮·埃奇韦尔也在哭，只是没那么大声；紧绷的气氛已叫人再难忍受。

===Original===
Abbershaw felt for his watch, and was about to draw it out when Albert Campion laid a hand over his warningly. As he did so his coat sleeve slipped up and Abbershaw saw the dial of the other’s wristwatch. It was five minutes to eleven.

At the same moment, however, there were footsteps outside the door again, and this time the voice of Jesse Gideon spoke from without.

“It is your last chance,” he said. “In three minutes we leave the house. You know the rest. What shall I say to Mr. Dawlish?”

===Chinese===
阿伯肖伸手去摸怀表，正要掏出来，阿尔伯特·坎皮恩一把按住他的手，示意别动。这一按，他的袖口滑了上去，阿伯肖看见了对方腕表的表盘。十一点差五分。

然而就在同一刻，门外再度响起脚步声，这一回，是杰西·吉迪恩的声音在外头开了口。

“这是你们最后的机会。”他说。“三分钟后，我们就离开宅子。往后的事，你们是知道的。叫我如何回复道利什先生？”

===Original===
“Tell him to burn and to be damned to him!” shouted Martin.

“Very appropriate!” murmured Mr. Campion, but his voice had lost its gaiety, and the hysterical sobs of the girl drowned the words.

And then, quite suddenly, from somewhere far across the fields there came a sound which everybody in the room recognized. A sound which brought them to their feet, the blood returning to their cheeks, and sent them crowding to the window, a new hope in their eyes.

===Chinese===
“叫他烧吧，让他下地狱去！”马丁喊道。

“答得妙极！”坎皮恩先生喃喃道，可他嗓音里那份轻快已经没了，这句话也淹进姑娘歇斯底里的呜咽里，听不真切了。

接着，毫无征兆地，远远的田野那头传来一个声音——屋里人人认得的声音。一个让他们霍然起身、血色重回双颊的声音；一个让他们涌向窗前、眼中燃起新的希望的声音。

===Original===
It was the thin far-off call of a hunting horn.

Martin, his head jammed between the bars of the narrow window, let out a whoop of joy.

“The Hunt, by God!” he said. “Yes—Lord! There’s the pack not a quarter of a mile away! Glory be to God, was that a splodge of red behind that hedge? It was! Here he comes!”

===Chinese===
那是猎号的鸣声，细弱而遥远。

马丁把脑袋挤在窄窗的栏杆中间，爆发出一声欢呼。

“是猎队，老天作证！”他说。“没错——主啊！猎犬群就在不到四分之一英里外！荣耀归于上帝，树篱后头那一小抹红是什么？是红衣！他来啦！”

===Original===
His voice was resonant with excitement, and he struggled violently as if he would force himself through the iron bars.

“There he is,” he said again; “and yes, look at him—look at him! Half the county behind him! They’re in the park now. Gosh! They’re coming right for us. Quick! Yell to ’em! God! They mustn’t go past! How can we attract them! Yell at ’em! Shout something! They’ll be on us in a minute.”

===Chinese===
他的声音因激动而洪亮震耳，身子拼命挣扎，仿佛要硬从铁栏杆中间挤出去了。

“他在那儿。”他又喊道。“没错，快看他——快看他！半个郡的人跟在他后头！他们进园地了。天哪！他们径直朝咱们来了。快！朝他们喊！上帝！千万不能让他们走过去！怎么才能引他们注意！朝他们喊！随便喊点什么！一转眼他们就要到跟前了！”

===Original===
“I think,” murmured a quiet, rather foolish voice that yet had a note of tension in its tone, “that in circumstances like this a ‘view-halloo’ would be permissible. Quickly! Now, are you ready, my children? Let her go!”

There was utter silence after the shout died away upon the wind, and then Campion’s voice behind them murmured again:

“Once more. Put your backs into it.”

===Chinese===
“我想，”一个平静而略显傻气的声音咕哝道，语气里却绷着一丝紧张，“在这般情形之下，喊一声‘见狐啦’（view-halloo）想必是合乎礼数的。快！好了，孩子们，都准备好了吗？放开了喊！”

呼喊声随风散尽，四下里一片彻底的寂静，随后坎皮恩的声音在他们身后又喃喃响起：

“再来一次。都使出吃奶的劲儿。”

===Original===
The cry rang out wildly, agonizingly, a shout for help, and then again there was stillness.

Martin suddenly caught his breath.

“They’ve heard,” he said in a voice strangled with excitement. “A chap is coming over here now.”

===Chinese===
呼喊再一次冲天而起，狂乱而凄厉，那是一声呼救；随后又是一片沉寂。

马丁猛地屏住呼吸。

“他们听见了。”他说，声音激动得发噎。“有个人正朝这边过来了。”

## XXIII An Error in Taste / 第二十三章 品味之误

===Original===
“What shall I shout to him?” said Martin nervously, as the solitary horseman came cantering across the turf towards the house. “I can’t blab out the whole story.”
“Yell, ‘We’re prisoners,’ ” suggested Kennedy, “and, ‘Get us out for the love of Mike.’ ”
“It’s a young chap,” murmured Martin. “Sits his horse well. Must be a decent cove. Here goes.”
He thrust his head as far out of the window as the bars would permit, and his clear young voice echoed out across the grass.
“Hello! Hello! Hell-o! Up here—top window! Up here! I say, we’re prisoners. A loony in charge is going to burn the house down. For God’s sake give the alarm and get us out.”

===Chinese===
“我该朝他喊什么呢？”马丁紧张地说。那名独行的骑手正策马小跑着穿过草地，朝宅子而来。“总不能把整件事原原本本嚷出去吧。”
“就喊‘我们是囚犯’，”肯尼迪出主意道，“再添一句‘看在老天的份上，救我们出去’。”
“是个小伙子，”马丁低声道，“马骑得挺像样，准是个正派家伙。我喊了。”
他把脑袋尽量从窗口的栏杆间探出去，清亮的嗓音在草地上远远回荡开去。
“喂！喂！喂——！在这儿——最顶上的窗户！在这儿！我说，我们是囚犯。管着这宅子的疯子要放火烧房子。看在上帝的份上，快去报警，救我们出去。”

===Original===
There was a period of silence, and then Martin spoke over his shoulder to the others:
“He can’t hear. He’s coming closer. He seems to be a bit of an ass.”
“For heaven’s sake get him to understand,” said Wyatt. “Everything depends on him.”
Martin nodded, and strained out of the window again.

===Chinese===
沉寂了片刻，随后马丁回过头对众人说：
“他听不见。他走近了。这人看样子有点儿傻气。”
“千万设法让他明白，”怀亚特说，“一切都指望他了。”
马丁点点头，再次竭力探身出窗。

===Original===
“We’re locked in here. Prisoners, I tell you. We—” he broke off suddenly and they heard him catch his breath.
“Dawlish!” he said. “The brute’s down there talking to him quietly as if nothing were up.”
“We’re imprisoned up here, I tell you,” he shouted again. “That man is a lunatic—a criminal. For heaven’s sake don’t take any notice of him.”
He paused breathless, and they heard the heavy German voice raised a little as though with suppressed anger.
“I tell you I am a doctor. These unfortunate people are under my care. They are poor imbeciles. You are exciting them. You will oblige me by going away immediately. I cannot have you over my grounds.”

===Chinese===
“我们被锁在这儿了。我跟你说，是囚犯。我们——”他猛地收住话头，众人听见他倒抽了一口气。
“道利什！”他说，“那畜生正在底下跟他心平气和地说话呢，好像什么事都没发生似的。”
“我们真被囚在这上头了，我告诉你，”他又喊道，“那人是个疯子——是个罪犯。看在老天的份上，千万别理睬他。”
他停了下来，喘不上气；众人听见那个沉重的德国口音略微扬起，像是强压着怒火。
“我告诉你，我是医生。这些不幸的人都在我的看护之下。他们是一些可怜的低能儿。你把他们激躁了。劳驾您立刻离开。我不能容您在我的地界上逗留。”

===Original===
And then a young voice with an almost unbelievable county accent spoke stiffly:
“I am sorry. I will go away immediately, of course. I had no idea you—er—kept lunatics. But they gave the ‘view-halloo’ and naturally I thought they’d seen.”
Martin groaned.
“The rest of the field’s coming up. The pack will be past in a moment.”

===Chinese===
接着，一个年轻的声音开了腔，带着一股简直令人难以置信的郡绅土腔，话说得一板一眼：
“对不起。我当然立刻就走。我全然不知您——呃——府上养着疯子。可他们发出了‘见狐’呼号（view-halloo），我自然以为他们见着（猎物）了。”
马丁呻吟了一声。
“大队人马跟上来了。犬群一转眼就要过去了。”

===Original===
Mr. Campion’s slightly falsetto voice interrupted him. He was very excited. “*I* know that voice,” he said wildly. “That’s old ‘Guffy’ Randall. Half a moment.”
On the last word he leapt up behind Martin and thrust his head in through the bars above the boy’s.
“Guffy!” he shouted. “Guffy Randall! Your own little Bertie is behind these prison bars in desperate need of succour. The old gentleman on your right is a fly bird—look out for him.”
“That’s done it!”
Martin’s voice was triumphant.

===Chinese===
坎皮恩先生那略带假高音的嗓音打断了他。他兴奋已极。“*我*认得这个声音，”他忘乎所以地嚷道，“那是老‘古菲’·兰德尔。且慢。”
话音未落，他已从马丁身后一跃而起，把脑袋从小伙子头顶的栏杆间挤了出去。
“古菲！”他高喊，“古菲·兰德尔！你的小伯蒂正关在这些监狱铁栏后头，急需搭救。你右手边那位老先生是只老滑头鸟——提防着他。”
“成了！”
马丁的声音透着胜利。

===Original===
“He’s looking up. He’s recognized you, Campion. Great Scott! The Hun is getting out his gun.”
At the same moment the German’s voice, bellowing now in his fury, rose up to them.
“Go away. You are trespassing. I am an angry man, sir. You are more than unwise to remain here.”

===Chinese===
“他抬头了。他认出你了，坎皮恩。天哪！德国佬在掏枪。”
同一瞬间，那德国人的声音——此刻已怒得咆哮起来——直冲到他们头顶。
“滚开。你擅闯私地。先生，我已动怒。你还留在这里，实在远不止是不明智。”

===Original===
And then the other voice, well bred and protesting.
“My dear sir, you have a friend of mine apparently imprisoned in your house. I must have an explanation.”
“Good old Guff—” began Mr. Campion, but the words died on his lips as the German’s voice again sounded from the turf beneath them.
“You fool! Can none of you see when I am in earnest? Will that teach you?”

===Chinese===
接着是另一个声音，教养良好，满是抗议的意味。
“亲爱的先生，您显然把我的一个朋友囚禁在您府上。我必须讨一个解释。”
“好样的老古夫——”坎皮恩先生刚开口，那德国人的声音又从他们脚下的草地上传来，话便僵在了他嘴边。
“你们这些蠢货！难道没有一个人看得出我是认真的？这下总能教训你们了？”

===Original===
A pistol shot followed the last word, and Martin gasped.
“Good God! He hasn’t shot him?” The words broke from Abbershaw in horror.
Martin remained silent, and then a whisper of horror escaped the flippant Mr. Campion.
“Shot him?” he said. “No. The unmitigated arch-idiot has shot one of the hounds. Just caught the tail end of the pack. Hullo! Here comes the huntsman with the field bouncing up behind him like Queen Victoria rampant. Now he’s for it.”

===Chinese===
最后一个字刚落便是一声手枪响，马丁倒抽了一口气。
“天哪！他没把他打死了吧？”这话从阿伯肖嘴里迸出来，满是惊恐。
马丁没作声，随后，一贯轻浮的坎皮恩先生也漏出一声惊骇的低语。
“打死他？”他说，“没有。这个彻头彻尾、不折不扣的顶级大蠢货，打死的是一条猎犬。正好撵上犬群的队尾。喂！猎手来了，身后大队骑手颠颠簸簸地涌上来，活像一尊后足直立的维多利亚女王。这下他有得受了。”

===Original===
The noise below grew to a babel, and Albert Campion turned a pink, excited face towards the anxious group behind him.
“How like the damn fool Guffy,” he said. “So upset about the hound he’s forgotten me.”
He returned to his lookout, and the next moment his voice resounded cheerfully over the tumult.
“I think they’re going to lynch Poppa von Faber. I say, I’m enjoying this.”

===Chinese===
楼下的喧声涨成一片乱哄哄的嘈杂，阿尔伯特·坎皮恩转过一张兴奋得发红的脸，望向身后忧心忡忡的一伙人。
“古菲这个该死的傻瓜就是这个德行，”他说，“为那条猎犬气昏了头，把我忘得一干二净。”
他重新守回瞭望的位置，下一刻，他的声音欢欢喜喜地响彻这片喧嚣。
“我看他们要把冯·法贝尔老爹拖去私刑处死了。我说，这场戏我看得真过瘾。”

===Original===
Now that the danger was less imminent, the spirits of the whole party were reviving rapidly.
There was an excited guffaw from Martin.
“Campion,” he said, “look at this.”
“Coo!” said Mr. Campion idiotically, and was silent.
“The most militant old dear I’ve ever seen in all my life,” murmured Martin aloud. “Probably a Lady Di-something-or-other. Fourteen stone if she weighs an ounce, and a face like her own mount. God, she’s angry. Hullo! She’s dismounting.”

===Chinese===
危险既然不那么迫在眉睫了，众人的精神头便迅速回升。
马丁兴奋地爆出一阵大笑。
“坎皮恩，”他说，“瞧这个。”
“嚯！”坎皮恩先生傻里傻气地应了一声，便不出声了。
“我这辈子见过的最最好斗的老太太，”马丁高声咕哝道，“八成是位‘黛安娜什么’的夫人。往少里说也有十四英石（一英石约合六点三五公斤），一张脸长得活像她自己的坐骑。天哪，她气坏了。喂！她下马了。”

===Original===
