“行。要是引擎抱死或者冲出路外，罪责都记在你头上，”普伦德比咬着牙说，“她已经全开马力了。”

两侧的树篱变得模糊难辨。最终，在马克斯泰（Marks Tey）与莱克斯登（Lexden）之间那条笔直的长路段上，他们又慢慢爬到了那辆大车的尾后。

“就是它没错，”马丁说，“它在爬着走呢，是不是？我是说，相对而言。我看阿伯肖说中了。它在赴约。我说，咱们坠后一点，关掉头灯——侧灯够照路了。”

===Original===
“Hullo! Where’s he off to now?”

It was Michael who spoke. The car ahead had taken a sudden turn to the right, forsaking the main road.

“After her,” said Martin, with suppressed excitement. “Now we’re coming to it, I do believe. Any idea where that leads to?”

“No,” said Michael. “I haven’t the least. There’s only a lane there if I remember. Probably the drive of a house.”

“All the better.” Martin was enthusiastic. “That means we have located them anyway.”

“Wait a bit,” said Michael, as, dimming his lights, he swung round after the other car. “It’s not a drive. I remember it now. There’s a signpost over there somewhere which says, ‘To Birch,’ wherever ‘Birch’ may be. Gosh! No speeding on this road, my children,” he added suddenly, as he steered the Riley round a concealed right-angle bend in the road.

===Chinese===
“喂！他这又要上哪儿去？”

说话的是迈克尔。前车突然右转，撇下大路，拐了进去。

“跟上它，”马丁说，压着满腔的兴奋，“我看，好戏这就来了。你知道那条路通哪儿吗？”

“不知道，”迈克尔说，“一点概念都没有。没记错的话，那儿只有一条小径。多半是哪户人家宅前的私家车道。”

“那更好了。”马丁热情高涨，“不管怎么说，这总算叫咱们摸到了他们的行踪。”

“且慢，”迈克尔说着，调暗车灯，一打方向盘跟了上去，“那不是私家车道。我想起来了。那边什么地方立着一块路牌，写着‘通往伯奇（Birch）’——天知道‘伯奇’是个什么所在。天哪！这条路可开不得快车，孩子们，”他忽然又补了一句，一面驾着赖利拐过一道藏在暗处的直角急弯。

===Original===
The headlights of the car they were following were still just visible several turns ahead. For the next few miles the journey developed into a nightmare. The turns were innumerable.

“God knows how we’re going to get back,” grumbled Michael. “I don’t know which I prefer; your friend with the gun or an attempt to find our way back through these roads before morning.”

“Cheer up,” said Martin consolingly. “You may get both. Any idea where we are? Was that a church we passed just now?”

“I thought I heard a cow,” suggested Abbershaw helpfully.

“Let’s catch ’em up,” said Martin. “It’s time something definite happened.”

===Chinese===
前车的头灯仍隐约可见，隔着好几个弯道在前头亮着。接下来的几英里，这段路程演成了一场噩梦。弯道多得数也数不清。

“天知道咱们怎么回去，”迈克尔嘟囔道，“我都不知道更愿意要哪一样：你那位带枪的朋友，还是赶在天亮前从这些路里摸回去的本事。”

“乐观点，”马丁宽慰道，“没准儿两样你都能得着。咱们眼下到哪儿了，有数吗？方才过去的那个是教堂吗？”

“我倒像是听见了一声牛叫，”阿伯肖好心献策。

“咱们追上去，”马丁说，“也该出点实实在在的事儿了。”

===Original===
Abbershaw shook his head.

“That’s no good, my dear fellow,” he said. “Don’t you see our position? We can’t stop a man in the middle of the night and accuse him of murder without more proof or more authority. We must find out where he is and that’s all.”

Martin was silent. He had no intention of allowing the adventure to end so tamely. They struggled on without speaking.

At length, after what had seemed to be an interminable drive, through narrow miry lanes with surfaces like ploughed fields, through forgotten villages, past ghostly churches dimly outlined against the sky, guided only by the glare ahead, the darkness began to grey and in the uncertain light of the dawn they found themselves on a track of short springy grass amid the most desolate surroundings any one of them had ever seen.

On all sides spread vast stretches of salting covered with clumps of rough, coarse grass with here and there a ragged river or a dyke-head.

===Chinese===
阿伯肖摇了摇头。

“那可不行，亲爱的伙计，”他说，“你没看清咱们的处境吗？总不能深更半夜拦下一个人，就指控他谋杀，咱们既没有更多的证据，也没有更多的职权。咱们只需查明他身在何处，仅此而已。”

马丁不吭声了。他可无意让这场冒险就此温吞收场。三人闷头赶路，谁也不再说话。

终于，在一段仿佛无尽无休的驱车之后——穿过窄小泥泞、路面如犁过的田垄般坎坷的小径，穿过被世人遗忘的村落，掠过天幕下轮廓依稀、鬼影般的教堂，全凭前方那一团亮光引路——黑暗开始泛出灰白。在晨光熹微、明暗未定之中，他们发现自己行驶在一条富有弹性的矮草车道上，置身于他们仨此生谁都不曾见过的最荒凉凄寂的境地。

四面八方铺展着大片大片的盐沼草滩，滩上丛生着一簇簇粗糙的野草，间或有一道道支离的河汊或沟渠的口子。

===Original===
Far ahead of them the old black car lumbered on.

Martin sniffed.

“The sea,” he said. “I wonder if that old miracle ahead swims? A bus like that might do anything. That would just about sink us if we went to follow them.”

“Just about,” said Michael dryly. “What do we do now?”

“I suppose we go on to the bitter end,” said Martin. “They may have a family houseboat out there. Hullo! Look at them now.”

===Chinese===
远远的前方，那辆黑黢黢的老爷车仍在笨重地爬行。

马丁嗅了嗅。

“海水的味儿，”他说，“也不知前头那件老奇迹会不会凫水？那么一辆车，什么事儿干不出来。咱们要是跟下去，多半得一块儿沉底。”

“多半得沉，”迈克尔干巴巴地说，“现在怎么办？”

“我想咱们就一路奉陪到底了，”马丁说，“说不定他们在那外头泊着一条住家船呢。喂！瞧他们现在的动静。”

===Original===
The Rolls had at last come to a full stop, although the headlights were still streaming out over the turf.

Michael brought the Riley up sharply.

“What now?” he said.

“Now the fun begins,” said Martin. “Get out your gun, Abbershaw.”

===Chinese===
那辆劳斯莱斯终于彻底停了下来，只是头灯的光柱仍漫过草皮倾泻而出。

迈克尔麻利地把赖利刹停在近旁。

“接下来呢？”他说。

“这下好戏开场了，”马丁说，“掏枪吧，阿伯肖。”

===Original===
Hardly had he spoken when an exclamation came down the morning to them, followed immediately by a revolver shot which again fell short of them.

Without hesitation Martin fired back. The snap of his automatic was instantly followed by a much larger explosion.

“That’s their back tyre,” he said. “Let’s get behind the car and play soldiers. They’re sure to retaliate. This is going to be fun.”

===Chinese===
话音未落，一声呼喝顺着清晨的空气传了过来，紧接着便是一响左轮枪声，子弹照旧落了空。

马丁毫不迟疑地还了一枪。他的自动手枪脆响甫落，随即便是一声大得多的爆响。

“打的是他们的后胎，”他说，“咱们躲到车后头玩打仗去。他们肯定要还手的。这下有意思了。”

===Original===
But in this he was mistaken. Neither Whitby nor his companion seemed inclined for further shooting. The two figures were plainly discernible through the fast-lightening gloom, Whitby in a long dust coat and a soft hat, and the other man taller and thinner, his cap still well down over his face.

And then, while they were still looking at him, Whitby thrust his hand into his coat pocket and pulled out a large white handkerchief which he shook at them solemnly, waving it up and down. Its significance was unmistakable.

Abbershaw began to laugh. Even Martin grinned.

“That’s matey, anyway,” he said. “What happens next?”

===Chinese===
可这一回他猜错了。无论惠特比还是他的同伴，似乎都无意再开枪。两个人影在渐渐发亮的幽暗中清晰可辨：惠特比穿一件长长的风尘外套，戴一顶软呢帽；另一个身材更高更瘦，帽檐依旧深深压在脸上。

就在他们还盯着他看的时候，惠特比把手伸进外套口袋，掏出一方白色大手帕，郑重其事地朝他们抖开，一上一下地挥着。其中意味，再明白不过。

阿伯肖朗声笑了。连马丁也咧开了嘴。

“不管怎么说，够朋友，”他说，“接下来该演哪一出？”

## XXVIII Should a Doctor Tell? / 第二十八章 医生该不该说？

===Original===
Still holding the handkerchief well in front of him, Whitby came a pace or two nearer, and presently his weak, half-apologetic voice came to them down the wind.

“Since we’ve both got guns, perhaps we’d better talk,” he shouted thinly. “What do you want?”

Martin glanced at Abbershaw.

“Keep him covered,” he murmured. “Prenderby, old boy, you’d better walk behind us. We don’t know what their little game is yet.”

They advanced slowly—absurdly, Abbershaw could not help thinking—on that vast open salting, miles from anywhere.

===Chinese===
惠特比仍旧把那方手帕端端正正举在身前，又向前挪近一两步，不多时，他那细弱、带着几分歉意的嗓音便顺着风传到了他们耳中。

“既然咱们双方都带着枪，恐怕还是谈谈的好，”他尖细着嗓子喊道，“你们想要什么？”

马丁瞥了阿伯肖一眼。

“拿枪看住他，”他低声说，“普伦德比，老伙计，你最好走在我们后面。他们玩的什么把戏，咱们还没弄明白呢。”

他们缓缓向前行进——荒唐，阿伯肖禁不住想——在这片开阔无垠的盐沼草滩上，方圆数英里渺无人迹。

===Original===
Whitby was still the harassed, scared-looking little man who had come to ask Abbershaw for his assistance on that fateful night at Black Dudley. He was, if anything, a little more composed now than then, and he greeted them affably.

“Well, here we are, aren’t we?” he said, and paused. “What do you want?”

Martin Watt opened his mouth to speak; he had a very clear notion of what he wanted and was anxious to explain it.

Abbershaw cut him short, however.

“A word or two of conversation, Doctor,” he said.

===Chinese===
惠特比依旧是那个惶惶不安、面带惧色的小个子男人，同当初在布莱克·达德利那个要命的夜晚来找阿伯肖求助时一模一样。若说有什么不同，他此刻反倒比那时镇定了些许，还客客气气地招呼了他们。

“呃，咱们这不都到齐了么？”他说着，顿了一顿，“你们想要什么？”

马丁·沃特张口正要说话；他对自己想要什么一清二楚，正急着要说个明白。

然而阿伯肖截住了他的话头。

“谈上一两句，医生。”他说。

===Original===
The little man blinked at him dubiously.

“Why, yes, of course,” he said, “of course. I should hate to disappoint you. You’ve come a long way for it, haven’t you?”

He was so patently nervous that in spite of themselves they could not get away from the thought that they were very unfairly matched.

“Where shall we talk?” continued the little doctor, still timidly. “I suppose there must be quite a lot of things you want to ask me?”

===Chinese===
小个子男人将信将疑地朝他眨了眨眼。

“哦，是的，当然，”他说，“当然。我实在不愿叫你们失望。你们大老远赶来，图的就是这个，不是么？”

他的紧张明明白白写在脸上，害得他们不由自主地总摆脱不了一个念头：这场对垒，未免太不公平了。

“咱们在哪儿谈呢？”小个子医生接着说，仍旧怯生生的，“我料想，你们要问我的事一定多得很吧？”

===Original===
Martin pocketed his gun.

“Look here, Whitby,” he said. “That is the point—there are lots of things. That’s why we’ve come. If you’re sensible you’ll give us straight answers. You know what happened at Black Dudley after you left, of course?”

“I—I read in the papers,” faltered the little figure in front of them. “Most regrettable. Who would have thought that such a clever, intelligent man would turn out to be such a dreadful criminal?”

Martin shook his head.

“That’s no good, Doc,” he said. “You see, not everything came out in the papers.”

Whitby sighed. “I see,” he said. “Perhaps if you told me exactly how much you know I should see precisely what to tell you.”

===Chinese===
马丁把枪收回口袋。

“听着，惠特比，”他说，“症结就在这儿——事情多着呢。我们就是为这个来的。你要是识相，就痛痛快快地照直回答。你走了之后布莱克·达德利出了什么事，你总归是知道的吧？”

“我——我在报上看到的，”他们面前那个矮小的人影讷讷地说，“遗憾之至。谁想得到那么一位精明聪慧的先生，到头来竟是个这般可怕的罪犯呢？”

马丁摇了摇头。

“这可糊弄不过去，大夫，”他说，“你要知道，报上并没有把什么都登出来。”

惠特比叹了口气。“明白了，”他说，“倘若你们把自己知道的一五一十告诉我，我也好确切知道该对你们说些什么。”

===Original===
Martin grinned at this somewhat ambiguous remark.

“Suppose we don’t make things quite so simple as that,” he said. “Suppose we both put our cards on the table—all of them.”

He had moved a step nearer as he spoke and the little doctor put up his hand warningly.

“Forgive me, Mr. Watt,” he said. “But my friend behind me is very clever with his pistol, as you may have noticed, and we’re right in his range now, aren’t we? If I were you I really think I’d take my gun out again.”

Martin stared at him and slowly drew his weapon out of his pocket.

“That’s right,” said Whitby. “Now we’ll go a little farther away from him, shall we? You were saying—?”

===Chinese===
听了这句模棱两可的话，马丁咧嘴一笑。

“咱们不妨别把事情弄得那么简单，”他说，“不如双方都把牌摊到桌面上——一张不落。”

说话间他又上前一步，小个子医生赶忙抬手示意。

“恕我冒昧，沃特先生，”他说，“不过我身后那位朋友使起手枪来本事大得很，你们或许也留意到了；这会儿咱们可正在他的射程之内，是不是？我要是你们，实在觉得还是把枪重新掏出来的好。”

马丁怔怔地望着他，慢吞吞地从口袋里拔出了枪。

“这就对了，”惠特比说，“现在咱们离他稍微走远一些，好不好？你方才说到——？”

===Original===
Martin was bewildered. This was the last attitude he had expected a fugitive to take up in the middle of a saltmarsh at four o’clock in the morning.

Abbershaw spoke quietly behind him.

“It’s Colonel Coombe’s death we are interested in, Doctor,” he said. “Your position at Black Dudley has been explained to us.”

He watched the man narrowly as he spoke but there was no trace of surprise or fear on the little man’s face.

He seemed relieved.

“Oh! I see!” he said. “You, Doctor Abbershaw, would naturally be interested in the fate of my patient’s body. As a matter of fact, he was cremated at Eastchester, thirty-six hours after I left Black Dudley. But, of course,” he went on cheerfully, “you will want to know the entire history. After we left the house we went straight over to the registrar’s. He was very sympathetic. Like everybody else in the vicinity he knew of the Colonel’s weak health and was not surprised at my news. In fact, he was most obliging. Your signature and mine were quite enough for him. He signed immediately and we continued our journey. I was on my way back to the house when I received—by the merest chance—the news of the unfortunate incidents which had taken place in my absence. And so,” he added with charming frankness, “we altered our number plates and changed our destination. Are you satisfied?”

===Chinese===
马丁一头雾水。凌晨四点，在盐沼的正中央，一个亡命之徒竟会摆出这么一副做派，这是他万万没有料到的。

阿伯肖在他身后平静地开了口。

“我们关心的是库姆上校的死，医生，”他说，“您在布莱克·达德利的身份，有人已经向我们说明白了。”

说话时他细细打量着对方，可小个子男人的脸上不见一丝惊讶或惧色。

他反倒像是松了口气。

“哦！原来如此！”他说，“阿伯肖医生，您自然关心我那病人遗体的下落。事实上，他已在伊斯特切斯特（Eastchester）火化了，就在我离开布莱克·达德利三十六个小时之后。不过当然，”他兴致勃勃地接着说，“你们是想了解全部经过。我们离开宅子后，便径直去了登记官那里。他非常通情达理。和左近所有人一样，他也知道上校身子骨一向孱弱，听了我的噩耗并不觉得意外。说实在的，他再热心周到不过了。有您和我的两个签名，对他来说就尽够了。他当即签了字，我们便继续赶路。我正在返回宅子的途中，纯属偶然地——得到了我不在期间发生的那些不幸变故的消息。于是呢，”他带着一种迷人的坦率补上一句，“我们换掉了车牌，也改换了目的地。诸位满意了么？”

===Original===
“Not quite,” said Martin grimly.

The nervous little doctor hurried on before they could stop him.

“Why, of course,” he said, “I was forgetting. There must be a great many things that still confuse you. The exact import of the papers that you, Doctor Abbershaw, were so foolhardy as to destroy? Never revealed, was it?”

“We know it was the detailed plan of a big robbery,” said Abbershaw stiffly.

“Indeed it was,” said Whitby warmly. “Quite the largest thing our people had ever thought of undertaking. Have you—er—any idea what place it was? Everything was all taped out so that nothing remained to chance, no detail left unconsidered. It was a complete plan of campaign ready to be put into immediate action. The work of a master, I assure you. Do you know the place?”

He saw by their faces that they were ignorant, and a satisfied smile spread over the little man’s face.

===Chinese===
“还差得远呢。”马丁冷冷地说。

不等他们拦阻，那位神经质的小个子医生便急急说了下去。

“哦，对了，”他说，“我差点儿忘了。一定还有许许多多的事让你们摸不着头脑吧？阿伯肖医生，您那么莽撞地毁掉的那些文件，确切内容究竟是什么？始终没有泄露过，对吧？”

“我们知道，那是一桩大宗劫案的详细计划。”阿伯肖板着脸说。

“正是如此，”惠特比热切地说，“是我们这些人盘算过的买卖里最大的一桩。你们——呃——可知道那是哪个地方？一切都丈量安排得明明白白，不留半分给运气，没有一处细节不曾虑及。那是一整套完整的作战方案，随时可以立即付诸行动。我向各位保证，那是出自一位大师之手。你们知道那地方么？”

他从他们的脸色看出他们一无所知，一丝得意的微笑在小个子男人的脸上漾开。

===Original===
“It wasn’t my secret,” he said. “But naturally I couldn’t help hearing a thing or two. As far as I could gather von Faber’s objective was the Repository of the Bullion for the Repayment of the American Debt.

The three were silent, the stupendousness of the scheme suddenly brought home to them.

“Then,” continued Whitby rapidly, “there was Colonel Coombe’s own part in von Faber’s affairs. Perhaps you don’t know that for the greater part of his life Colonel Coombe had been under von Faber’s influence to an enormous extent, in fact I think I might almost say that he was dominated absolutely by von—”

“It’s not Colonel Coombe’s life, Doctor Whitby, which interests us so particularly,” cut in Martin suddenly. “It’s his death. You know as well as we do that he was murdered.”

===Chinese===
“那不是我的秘密，”他说，“不过自然而然，我也免不了听到一星半点。据我所知，冯·法贝尔（von Faber）的目标，是那座存放偿还美国债务之金银的国库。

三个人都沉默了，这项计划的惊人体量猛然间压上了他们的心头。

“再者，”惠特比飞快地接着说，“还有库姆上校本人在冯·法贝尔那些事务里的角色。你们或许不知道，库姆上校一生的大半岁月，都在极大的程度上受着冯·法贝尔的影响；说实在的，我想我几乎可以说，他被冯——”

“惠特比医生，让我们特别感兴趣的，可不是库姆上校的生平，”马丁突然插嘴道，“是他的死。他被人谋害了，这一点您跟我们一样清楚。”

===Original===
For an instant the nervous garrulousness of the little doctor vanished and he stared at them blankly.

“There are a lot of people interested in that point,” he said at last. “I am myself, for one.”

“So we gathered,” murmured Martin, under his breath, while Abbershaw spoke hastily.

“Doctor Whitby,” he said, “you and I committed a very grave offence by signing those certificates.”

“Yes,” said Whitby, and paused for a moment or so, after which he brightened up visibly and hurried on. “But really, my dear sir, in the circumstances I don’t see that we could have done anything else, do you? We were the victims of a stronger force.”

===Chinese===
刹那间，小个子医生那副神经质的饶舌消失得无影无踪，他茫然地瞪着他们。

“关心这一点的，大有人在，”他终于开口道，“我自己就算一个。”

“这我们看出来了。”马丁压着嗓子咕哝道，阿伯肖急忙接过话头。

“惠特比医生，”他说，“您和我签了那些证明书，可是犯了桩非常严重的罪。”

“是啊，”惠特比说着，沉吟了片刻，随后明显地振作起来，急急往下说道，“可是说真的，亲爱的先生，处在当时那种情形下，我看咱们也别无他法，您说是不是？咱们是身不由己，受了强权的摆布。”

===Original===
Abbershaw disregarded the other’s smile and spoke steadily.

“Doctor Whitby,” he said, “do you know who murdered Colonel Coombe?”

The little doctor’s benign expression did not alter.

“Why, of course,” he said. “I should have thought that, at least, was obvious to everybody—everybody who knew anything at all about the case, that is.”

Abbershaw shook his head.

“I’m afraid we must plead either great stupidity or peculiarly untrusting dispositions,” he said. “That is the point on which we are not at all satisfied.”

===Chinese===
阿伯肖不去理会对方的微笑，不疾不徐地说了下去。

“惠特比医生，”他说，“您知道是谁谋害了库姆上校么？”

小个子医生那副慈和的表情纹丝未变。

“哦，当然知道，”他说，“我原以为，这一点至少对所有人都是明摆着的——也就是说，对凡是对此案略知一二的人而言。”

阿伯肖摇了摇头。

“恐怕我们只得认下一条：要么蠢笨透顶，要么疑心重得出奇，”他说，“恰恰在这一点上，我们怎么也想不通。”

===Original===
“But my dear young people—” for the first time during that interview the little man showed signs of impatience. “That is most obvious. Amongst your party—let us say, Mr. Petrie’s party, as opposed to von Faber’s—there was a member of the famous Simister gang of America. Perhaps you have heard of it, Doctor Abbershaw. Colonel Coombe had been attempting to establish relations with them for some time. In fact, that was the reason why I and my pugnacious friend behind us were placed at Black Dudley—to keep an eye upon him. During the progress of the Dagger Ritual, Simister’s man eluded our vigilance and chose that moment not only to get hold of the papers, but also to murder the unfortunate Colonel. That, by the way, was only a title he adopted, you know.”

===Chinese===
“可是，亲爱的年轻人们——”这番交涉里，小个子男人头一回显出不耐烦的神色。“这不是明摆着的事么。在你们那伙人当中——姑且说是皮特里先生的一伙，以别于冯·法贝尔的一伙——原有一名美国大名鼎鼎的西米斯特团伙的成员。阿伯肖医生，您或许听说过这伙人。库姆上校一直试图同他们搭上关系，为时已久。事实上，这正是我本人和我身后这位好斗的朋友被安插在布莱克·达德利的缘故——盯住他。匕首仪式进行的当口，西米斯特的人避开了我们的监视，专挑那个时机，不但把文件弄到了手，还结果了那位不幸的上校。顺带说一句，那不过是他自封的一个头衔罢了，您是知道的。”

===Original===
The three younger men remained unimpressed.

Martin shook his head.

“Not a bad story, but it won’t wash,” he said. “If one of our party stabbed the old boy, why do you all go to such lengths to keep it so quiet for us?”

“Because, my boy,” said Whitby testily, “we didn’t want a fuss. In fact, the police on the scene was the last thing we desired. Besides, you seem to forget the extraordinary importance of the papers.”

===Chinese===
三个年轻人依旧无动于衷。

马丁摇了摇头。

“故事编得不坏，可惜站不住脚，”他说，“如果是我们一伙里的人捅了那老爷子，你们何苦费这么大周章，替我们把这事瞒得死死的？”

“因为啊，老弟，”惠特比不耐烦地说，“我们不想把事情闹大。说实在的，警察到场正是我们最最不愿见到的事。再者，你们似乎忘了那几份文件非同小可的分量。”

===Original===
Again Martin shook his head.

“We’ve heard all this before,” he said; “and it didn’t sound any better then. To be perfectly frank, we are convinced that one of your people was responsible. We want to know who, and we want to know why.”

The little doctor’s face grew slowly crimson, but it was the flush of a man annoyed rather than a guilty person accused of his crime.

“You tire me with your stupidity,” he said suddenly. “Good God, sir, consider it. Have you any idea how valuable the man was to us? Do you know what he was paid for his services? Twenty thousand pounds for this coup alone. Simister would probably have offered him more. You don’t hear about these things. Government losses rarely get into the papers—certainly not with figures attached. Not the smallest member of our organization stood to gain anything at all by his death. I confess I was surprised at Simister’s man, unless he was double-crossing his own people.”

===Chinese===
马丁又摇了摇头。

“这一套我们早听过了，”他说，“那时听着也不比现在高明。跟您打开天窗说亮话：我们认定这是你们的人干的。我们要知道是谁，还要知道为什么。”

小个子医生的脸慢慢涨成了深红色，但那是一个人被惹恼了的涨红，而非罪案败露、遭人指控者的愧色。

“你们的愚钝真叫人厌烦，”他突然说道，“老天爷，先生，好好想想吧。您可知道那人对我们价值几何？您可知道他这趟差使拿多少酬金？光这一票就是两万英镑。西米斯特多半还会出更高的价。这种事你们是听不到的。政府的损失很少见报——附上数目的就更别想了。我们组织里最微不足道的一员，也休想从他的死里得到半分好处。我承认，西米斯特那人的行径令我诧异，除非他是在吃里扒外、出卖自己人。”

===Original===
For a moment even Martin’s faith in his own theory was shaken.

“In that case,” said Abbershaw unexpectedly, “it will doubtless surprise you to learn that the man employed by Simister to obtain the package had a complete alibi. In fact, it was impossible for him ever to have laid hands upon the dagger.”

“Impossible?” The word broke from Whitby’s lips like a cry, but although they were listening to him critically, to not one of them did it sound like a cry of fear. He stared at them, amazement in his eyes.

“Have you proof of that?” he said at last.

“Complete proof,” said Abbershaw quietly. “I think you must reconsider your theory, Doctor Whitby. Consider how you yourself stand, in the light of what I have just said.”

===Chinese===
有一瞬间，连马丁对自己那套理论的信念都动摇了。

“既然如此，”阿伯肖出其不意地开了口，“那么听说西米斯特雇来夺取那只小包的人有着完完整整的不在场证明，您想必会大吃一惊。事实上，他根本就不可能碰到那把匕首。”

“不可能？”这个词从惠特比唇间迸出来，像一声惊呼；然而尽管他们正竖着耳朵挑剔地听，三个人里却没有一个听出那是恐惧的呼喊。他瞪着他们，眼里满是惊愕。

“您有证据么？”他终于问道。

“铁证。”阿伯肖平静地说，“我想您必须重新掂量您那套理论了，惠特比医生。不妨照着我方才的话，掂量掂量您自己如今站在什么位置。”

===Original===
An expression of mild astonishment spread over the insignificant little face. Then, to everybody’s surprise, he laughed.

“Amateur detectives?” he said. “I’m afraid you’ve had a long ride for nothing, gentlemen. I confess that my position as accessory after the fact is a dangerous one, but then, so is Doctor Abbershaw’s. Consider the likelihood of your suggestion. Have you provided me with a motive?”

“I suggest,” said Martin calmly, “that your position when von Faber discovered that your prisoner had ‘eluded your vigilance,’ as you call it, would not have been too good.”

Whitby paused thoughtfully.

“Not bad,” he said. “Not bad at all. Very pretty. But”—he shook his head—“unfortunately not true. My position with Coombe dead was ‘not good’ as you call it. But had Coombe been alive he would have had to face the music, wouldn’t he? It was von Faber’s own fault that I ever left his side at all.”

===Chinese===
一丝淡淡的讶异在那张不起眼的小脸上漾开。接着，他竟笑出了声，众人都始料未及。

“业余侦探？”他说，“诸位先生，恐怕你们这趟远路是白跑了。我承认，我作为事后从犯，处境堪危；可是话说回来，阿伯肖医生的处境又何尝不是。掂量掂量你们那项指摘有几分可能吧。你们给我备下杀人的动机了么？”

“我这么看，”马丁不动声色地说，“等冯·法贝尔发现您看管的囚徒——照您的说法——‘避开了您的监视’的时候，您的处境只怕也好不到哪儿去。”

惠特比若有所思地顿了一顿。

“不赖，”他说，“当真不赖。相当漂亮。可是”——他摇了摇头——“可惜不是实情。库姆一死，我的处境，照你们的说法，是‘不好’。可库姆若是活着，就得由他自己去承当后果了，不是么？说到底，我当初之所以会离开他的身边，本来就是冯·法贝尔自己的过错。”

===Original===
This was certainly a point which they had not considered. It silenced them for a moment, and in the lull a sound which had been gradually forcing itself upon their attention for the last few moments became suddenly very apparent—the steady droning of an aeroplane engine.

Whitby looked up, mild interest on his face.

It was now quite light, and the others, following his gaze, saw a huge Fokker monoplane flying low against the grey sky.

“He’s out early,” remarked Prenderby.

“Yes,” said Whitby. “There’s an aerodrome a couple of miles across here, you know. Quite near my house, in fact.”

===Chinese===
这确实是他们不曾虑及的一点，一时都语塞了。就在这静默的间隙里，一个方才渐渐强挤进他们注意力的声响蓦地清晰起来——飞机引擎沉稳的嗡鸣。

惠特比抬起头，脸上带着淡淡的兴味。

天已大亮，其余的人顺着他的目光望去，只见一架庞大的福克（Fokker）单翼机正贴着灰色天幕低低飞行。

“起得真早。”普伦德比说。

“是啊，”惠特比说，“要知道，翻过这儿两英里就有一座机场。其实，离我的房子相当近。”

===Original===
Martin pricked up his ears.

“Your house?” he echoed.

The little doctor nodded.

“Yes. I have a small place down here by the sea. Very lonely, you know, but I thought it suited my purpose very well just now. Frankly, I didn’t like the idea of your following me and it made my friend quite angry.”

===Chinese===
马丁竖起了耳朵。

“您的房子？”他跟着问了一句。

小个子医生点了点头。

“是的。我在这海边有一处小宅子。你们知道，冷清得很，不过我想眼下倒正合我的用途。不瞒各位，我不喜欢被人一路跟来的念头，我那位朋友还为这个动了好大的气。”

===Original===
“Hullo! He’s in difficulties or something.”

It was Prenderby who spoke. He had been watching the aeroplane, which was now almost directly above their heads. His excited cry made them all look up again, to see the great plane circling into the wind.

There was now no drone of the engine but they could hear the sough of the air through the wires, and for a moment it seemed as if it were dropping directly on top of them. The next instant it passed so near that they almost felt its draught upon their faces. Then it taxied along the ground, coming to a halt in the glow of the still burning headlights of the big car.

===Chinese===
“喂！它出岔子了，还是怎么的。”

说话的是普伦德比。他一直在注视那架飞机，此刻它几乎就在他们头顶正上方。他这声兴奋的叫喊让众人再度抬头，只见那巨大的机身正迎着风盘旋。

这时引擎已不嗡鸣，他们却听得见气流穿过张线的呜呜声；有一瞬间，它仿佛就要当头直坠到他们身上。下一刹它已贴得极近地掠过，带起的风几乎扑上他们的脸。随后它贴着地面滑行，停在了那辆大轿车依然燃亮的头灯光晕之中。

===Original===
Instinctively, they hurried towards it, and until they were within twenty yards they did not realize that Whitby’s confederate had got there first and was talking excitedly to the pilot.

“Good God!” said Martin suddenly stopping dead in his tracks. The same thought struck the others at precisely the same instant.

Through the waves of mingled anger and amazement which overwhelmed them, Whitby’s precise little voice came clearly.

“I observe that he carries a machine-gun,” he remarked. “That’s what I like about these Germans—so efficient. In view of what my excitable colleague has probably said to the pilot, I really don’t think I should come any nearer. Perhaps you would turn off your headlights when you go back, they have served their purpose. Take the car too if you like.”

He paused and beamed on them.

“Goodbye,” he said. “I suppose it would annoy you if I thanked you for coming to see me off? Don’t do that,” he added sharply, as Martin’s hand shot to his side pocket. “Please don’t do that,” he repeated more earnestly. “For my friends would most certainly kill you without the least compunction, and I don’t want that. Believe me, my dear young people, whatever your theories may be, I am no murderer. I am leaving the country in this melodramatic fashion because it obviates the inconveniences which might arise if I showed my passport here just at present. Don’t come any nearer. Goodbye, gentlemen.”

===Chinese===
他们出于本能，急忙朝它奔去，直到相距二十来码（一码约合零点九米）才发觉，惠特比的同伙早已抢先赶到，正在激动地跟飞行员说着什么。

“天哪！”马丁猛地钉在原地。同一个念头在同一瞬间击中了其余两人。

在吞没他们的怒火与惊愕交叠的波涛中，惠特比那细谨工整的小嗓音清晰地传来。

“我注意到机上载着一挺机关枪，”他说，“这就是我喜欢这些德国人的地方——办起事来真利落。鉴于我那位易激动的同事多半已经对飞行员说了些什么，我实在认为自己不宜再靠近了。诸位回去时，劳驾把车头灯关掉吧，它们的使命已经当完了。愿意的话，把那辆车也一并拿去好了。”

他顿了顿，满面春风地朝他们微笑。

“再见了，”他说，“我若是感谢诸位赶来给我送行，恐怕只会惹你们恼火吧？别那样做，”马丁的手倏地伸向侧面的口袋，他急忙尖声加了一句，“请千万别那样做，”他又更恳切地重复了一遍，“因为我那几位朋友准会毫无怜悯地要了你们的命，而我不愿见到那样。相信我，亲爱的年轻人们，不管你们的理论如何，我绝不是杀人凶手。我之所以用这般戏台上的做派离境出国，是因为它能免去种种不便——此刻我若在这里亮出护照，那些不便难免就要冒头。别再靠近了。再会，诸位先生。”

===Original===
As they watched him go, Martin’s hand again stole to his pocket.

Abbershaw touched his arm.

“Don’t be a fool, old man,” he said. “If he’s done one murder, don’t encourage him to do another, and if he hasn’t, why help him to?”

Martin nodded and made a remark which did nobody any credit.

They stood there watching the machine with the gun trained upon them from its cockpit until it began to move again; then they turned back towards the Riley.

===Chinese===
他们眼看他走远，马丁的手又悄悄探向口袋。

阿伯肖碰了碰他的手臂。

“别犯傻了，老伙计，”他说，“他要是已经害过一条人命，就别再招他害第二条；他要是没害过，又何苦帮他开这个戒？”

马丁点了点头，说了一句对谁都谈不上光彩的话。

他们站在那儿，望着那架飞机——机关枪从座舱里瞄着他们——直到它重新动了起来；随后他们转身向赖利车走去。

===Original===
“Right up the garden,” said Martin bitterly. “Fooled, done brown, put it how you like. There goes Coombe’s murderer and here are we poor mutts who listened trustingly while he told us fairy stories to pass the time away until his pals turned up for him. I wish we’d risked that machine-gun.”

Prenderby nodded gloomily.

“I feel sick,” he said. “We spotted him and then he got away with it.”

Abbershaw shook his head.

“He got away certainly,” he said. “But I don’t think we’ve got much cause to regret it.”

===Chinese===
“彻头彻尾让人给遛了，”马丁恨恨地说，“耍了，涮了，随你怎么说。库姆的凶手就这么走掉了，咱们这几个可怜的呆子还满心信赖地听他讲童话消磨时间，一直磨到他那帮朋友来接他。真希望咱们当时豁出去赌一赌那挺机关枪。”

普伦德比阴沉沉地点了点头。

“我恶心得想吐，”他说，“咱们明明找着了他，到头来还是让他全身而退。”

阿伯肖摇了摇头。

“他自然是跑了，”他说，“可我认为，咱们没有什么值得懊悔的。”

===Original===
“What do you mean? Think he didn’t kill him?”

They looked at him incredulously.

Abbershaw nodded.

“I know he didn’t kill him,” he said quietly.

Martin grunted.

“I’m afraid I can’t agree with you there,” he said. “Gosh! I’ll never forgive myself for being such a fool!”

Prenderby was inclined to agree with him, but Abbershaw stuck to his own opinion, and the expression on his face as they drove silently back to Town was very serious and, somehow, afraid.

===Chinese===
“你这是什么意思？你认为他没杀人？”

他们难以置信地望着他。

阿伯肖点了点头。

“我知道他没有杀人。”他轻声说。

马丁咕哝了一声。

“这一点上，恐怕我没法同意你，”他说，“天哪！我竟这么蠢，一辈子也不能原谅自己！”

普伦德比倾向于赞同他，阿伯肖却坚持己见；他们默默驱车返回伦敦，一路上他脸上的神情极其凝重，而且，不知怎的，透着一丝惧意。

## XXIX The Last Chapter / 第二十九章 最后一章

===Original===
In the six weeks which followed the unsatisfactory trip to the Essex Marshes, Abbershaw and Meggie were fully occupied preparing for their wedding, which they had decided should take place as soon as was possible.

Prenderby seemed inclined to forget the Black Dudley affair altogether: his own marriage to Jeanne was not far distant and provided him with a more interesting topic of thought and conversation, and Martin Watt had gone back to his old haunts in the City and the West End.

Wyatt was in his flat overlooking St. James’s, apparently immersed as ever in the obscurities of his reading.

But Abbershaw had not forgotten Colonel Coombe.

===Chinese===
自那趟不甚如意的埃塞克斯沼泽（Essex Marshes）之行以后，六个星期里，阿伯肖和梅吉一直忙于筹备婚礼——两人已经商定，婚期越早越好。

普伦德比似乎恨不得把布莱克·达德利那桩事整个儿忘个干净：他自己与让娜的婚期已然不远，给了他一个更有意思的念想与谈资；马丁·沃特则回到了他在伦敦城和西区流连惯了的旧地。

怀亚特待在他那俯瞰圣詹姆斯（St. James's）的公寓里，看样子和往常一样，一头沉在他那些艰深晦涩的书卷之中。

可是阿伯肖没有忘记库姆上校。

===Original===
He had not put the whole matter before his friend, Inspector Deadwood of Scotland Yard, for a reason which he was unable to express in definite words, even to himself.

An idea was forming in his mind﻿—an idea which he shrank from and yet could not wholly escape.

In vain he argued with himself that his thought was preposterous and absurd; as the days went on and the whole affair sank more and more into its true perspective, the more the insidious theory grew upon him and began to haunt his nights as well as his days.

At last, very unwillingly, he gave way to his suspicions and set out to test his theory.

===Chinese===
他一直没有把整桩事情原原本本摆到他朋友、苏格兰场的德德伍德探长（Inspector Deadwood）面前，个中缘由，他甚至连对自己都说不分明。

一个念头正在他心里渐渐成形——这念头令他畏缩，却又无法全然摆脱。

他跟自己争辩，说这想法荒唐透顶、无稽之极，却是徒然；随着日子一天天过去，整桩事渐渐沉入它真实的大小轻重之中，那个阴魂不散的推想反倒愈发盘踞不去，白天缠着他，夜里也开始缠着他。

最后，他极不情愿地向疑心让了步，着手去验证自己的推想。

===Original===
His procedure was somewhat erratic. He spent the best part of a week in the reading-room of the British Museum; this was followed by a period of seclusion in his own library, with occasional descents upon the bookshops of Charing Cross Road, and then, as though his capacity for the tedium of a subject in which he was not naturally interested was not satiated, he spent an entire weekend in the Kensington house of his uncle, Sir Dorrington Wynne, onetime Professor of Archaeology in the University of Oxford, a man whose conversation never left the subject of his researches.

Another day or so at the British Museum completed Abbershaw’s investigations, and one evening found him driving down Whitehall in the direction of the Abbey, his face paler than usual, and his eyes troubled.

He went slowly, as if loth to reach his destination, and when a little later he pulled up outside a block of flats, he remained for some time at the wheel, staring moodily before him. Every moment the task he had set himself became more and more nauseous.

Eventually, he left the car, and mounting the carpeted stairs of the old Queen Anne house walked slowly up to the first floor.

===Chinese===
他的路数有点东一榔头西一棒子。他先在大英博物馆的阅览室里泡了将近一个星期；随后又闭门蛰居在自己的书房里，偶尔杀到查令十字街的书店去扫荡一回；再往后，仿佛他天生不爱这门学问、对其枯燥的耐受力却还远没熬够似的，他又足足一个周末泡在舅父多林顿·韦恩爵士（Sir Dorrington Wynne）位于肯辛顿的宅子里——这位爵士曾任牛津大学考古学教授，三句话不离他的研究。

又在大英博物馆待了一两天，阿伯肖的调查便告完成。一天傍晚，他驱车沿白厅朝威斯敏斯特教堂的方向驶去，脸色比平日更加苍白，眼神里透着不安。

他开得很慢，仿佛并不情愿抵达目的地。过了一会儿，他在一片公寓楼前停了车，却还在方向盘后坐了好一阵，郁郁地凝望着前方。他给自己派下的这桩差事，一刻比一刻更令他作呕。

终于，他下了车，登上那座安妮女王时代老宅里铺着地毯的楼梯，慢慢走到二楼。

===Original===
A manservant admitted him, and within three minutes he was seated before a spacious fireplace in Wyatt Petrie’s library.

The room expressed its owner’s personality. Its taste was perfect but a little academic, a little strict. It was an ascetic room. The walls were pale-coloured and hung sparsely with etchings and engravings﻿—a Goya, two or three moderns, and a tiny Rembrandt. There were books everywhere, but tidily, neatly kept, and a single hanging in one corner, a dully burning splash of old Venetian embroidery.

Wyatt seemed quietly pleased to see him. He sat down on the other side of the hearth and produced cigars and Benedictine.

Abbershaw refused both. He was clearly ill at ease, and he sat silent for some moments after the first words of greeting, staring moodily into the fire.

===Chinese===
一名男仆开门迎他进去，不出三分钟，他已在怀亚特·皮特里书房里那座宽敞的壁炉前落了座。

这房间处处透着主人的性情。趣味无可挑剔，却稍带学究气，稍嫌严苛。这是一间苦行僧式的屋子。四壁颜色浅淡，稀稀落落悬着几幅蚀刻画与雕版画——一幅戈雅，两三幅现代作品，还有一幅小小的伦勃朗。书俯拾皆是，却码得整整齐齐、一丝不苟；屋角悬着唯一的一幅挂饰，一抹幽暗得近乎闷燃的古威尼斯绣品。

见到他，怀亚特显得暗自高兴。他在炉床另一侧坐下，端出雪茄和本笃甜酒。

阿伯肖两样都谢绝了。他分明坐立不安，寒暄过后又默然枯坐了好一会儿，郁郁地盯着炉火。

===Original===
“Wyatt,” he said suddenly, “I’ve known you for a good many years. Believe me, I’ve not forgotten that when I ask you this question.”

Wyatt leaned back in his chair and closed his eyes, his liqueur glass lightly held in his long, graceful fingers. Abbershaw turned in his chair until he faced the silent figure.

“Wyatt,” he said slowly and evenly, “why did you stab your uncle?”

No expression appeared upon the still pale face of the man to whom he had spoken. For some moments he did not appear to have heard.

===Chinese===
“怀亚特，”他蓦地开口，“你我相识已有不少年头。请相信，我问出下面这个问题的时候，并没有忘记这一点。”

怀亚特朝椅背上一靠，阖上双眼，那盏甜酒杯轻轻擎在他修长优雅的指间。阿伯肖在椅中转过身子，正对着那沉默的身影。

“怀亚特，”他缓缓地、不动声色地说，“你为什么要刺杀你的叔父？”

对方那张沉静的苍白脸孔上不见一丝波澜。有好一阵子，他像是根本没有听见。

===Original===
At last he sighed and, leaning forward, set his glass down upon the little book-table by his side.

“I’ll show you,” he said.

Abbershaw took a deep breath. He had not been prepared for this; almost anything would have been easier to bear.

Meanwhile Wyatt crossed over to a small writing-desk let into a wall of bookshelves and, unlocking it with a key which he took from his pocket, produced something from a drawer; carrying it back to the fireplace, he handed it to his visitor.

Abbershaw took it and looked at it with some astonishment.

===Chinese===
终于，他叹了口气，俯身把酒杯搁在身畔的小书几上。

“我拿给你看。”他说。

阿伯肖深深吸了一口气。这样的回答他没有料到；换成几乎任何别的情形，都要好受些。

这当口，怀亚特走到嵌在书架墙里的一张小写字台前，从口袋里掏出钥匙开了锁，自抽屉中取出一样东西，拿回炉边，递到客人手里。

阿伯肖接过来，颇为诧异地端详着。

===Original===
It was a photograph of a girl.

The face was round and childlike, and was possessed of that peculiar innocent sweetness which seems to belong only to a particular type of blonde whose beauty almost invariably hardens in maturity.

At the time of the portrait, Abbershaw judged, the girl must have been about seventeen﻿—possibly less. Undeniably lovely, but in the golden-haired unsophisticated fashion of the medieval angel.

The last face in the world that he would have suspected Wyatt of noticing.

He turned the thing over in his hand. It was one of those cheap, glossy reproductions which circulate by the thousand in the theatrical profession.

He sat looking at it helplessly; uncomprehending, and very much at sea.

===Chinese===
那是一个姑娘的照片。

相中那张脸圆圆的，带着孩子气，自有一种天真无邪的甜美——这甜美似乎只属于某一特定类型的金发美人，而这类美人的姿色一到成年，几乎无一例外会变得硬而乏味。

阿伯肖估摸着，拍照时这姑娘约莫十七岁——兴许还不到。不可否认她很可爱，但那是中世纪天使画里那种金发懵懂的可爱。

这是天底下他最想不到会被怀亚特多看一眼的脸。

他把照片在手里翻了个面。那是戏剧界里成千上万流通的廉价光面相片之一。

他捏着它干坐着，无计可施；不明就里，如坠云雾。

===Original===
Wyatt came to the rescue.

“Her stage name was ‘Joy Love,’ ” he said slowly, and there was silence again.

Abbershaw was still utterly perplexed, and opened his mouth to ask the obvious question, but the other man interrupted him, and the depth and bitterness of his tone surprised the doctor.

“Her real name was Dolly Lord,” he said. “She was seventeen in that photograph, and I loved her﻿—I do still love her﻿—most truly and most deeply.” He added simply, “I have never loved any other woman.”

He was silent, and Abbershaw, who felt himself drifting further and further out of his depth at every moment, looked at him blankly. There was no question that the man was sincere. The tone in his voice, every line of his face and body proclaimed his intensity.

“I don’t understand,” said Abbershaw.

===Chinese===
还是怀亚特替他解了围。

“她的艺名叫『乔伊·拉芙』（Joy Love），”他缓缓说道，随后又是一阵沉默。

阿伯肖依旧一头雾水，张口正要问那个明摆着的问题，对方却打断了他，那语调的深沉与怨愤让医生暗暗吃惊。

“她的本名叫多莉·洛德（Dolly Lord），”他说，“拍那张照片时她十七岁，我爱上了她——如今依然爱着她——爱得最真，也爱得最深。”他又淡淡补了一句，“此外我再没有爱过任何别的女人。”

他沉默下来，阿伯肖只觉得自己每一刻都在朝更深的深水里漂去，一时茫然地望着他。毫无疑问，此人说的是真心话。他的语调，他脸上身上的每一道线条，都在宣告他的炽烈。

“我不明白。”阿伯肖说。

===Original===
Wyatt laughed softly and began to speak quickly, earnestly, and all in one key.

“She was appearing in the crowd scene in *The Faith of St. Hubert*, that beautiful little semi-sacred opera that they did at the Victor Gordon Arts Theatre in Knightsbridge,” he said. “That’s where I first saw her. She looked superb in a snood and wimple. I fell in love with her. I found out who she was after considerable trouble. I was crazy about her by that time.”

He paused and looked at Abbershaw with his narrow dark eyes in which there now shone a rebellious, almost fanatical light.

“You can call it absurd with your modern platonic-suitability complexes,” he said, “but I fell in love with a woman as nine-tenths of the men have done since the race began and will continue to do until all resemblance of the original animal is civilized out of us and the race ends﻿—with her face, and with her carriage, and with her body. She seemed to me to fulfil all my ideals of womankind. She became my sole object. I wanted her, I wanted to marry her.”

He hesitated for a moment and looked at Abbershaw defiantly, but as the other did not speak he went on again. “I found out that in the ordinary way she was what they call a ‘dancing instructress’ in one of the nightclubs at the back of Shaftesbury Avenue. I went there to find her. From the manager in charge I discovered that for half a crown a dance and anything else I might choose to pay I might talk as long as I liked with her.”

Again he hesitated, and Abbershaw was able to see in his face something of what the disillusionment had meant to him.

===Chinese===
怀亚特轻笑一声，随即说了起来，语速飞快，言辞恳切，从头到尾一个调子。

“她那时正在《圣休伯特的信仰》的群众场面里客串，那部小巧秀美、半带宗教色彩的小歌剧，当时正在骑士桥的维克多·戈登艺术剧院上演，”他说，“我就是在那儿头一回见到她。她戴着发网，罩着修女式的头巾，模样漂亮极了。我爱上了她。费了好一番周折，我才打听出她是谁。到那时，我已经为她痴狂了。”

他顿了顿，用那双细长的黑眼睛望着阿伯肖，眼里此刻闪动着一种反叛的、近乎狂热的光。

“你们尽管搬出你们现代人那套『柏拉图式门当户对』的讲究，说这荒唐，”他说，“可我爱上一个女人，正如自有人类以来十个男人里有九个干过的那样，而且只要人种延续一天，他们还要一直这样干下去，直到那点原始兽性的影子被文明从我们身上涤荡干净、人类就此告终——我爱她的脸，爱她的仪态，爱她的身体。在我眼里，她满足了我对女性的一切理想。她成了我唯一的目标。我要她，我要娶她。”

他迟疑了片刻，挑衅似的望着阿伯肖，见对方不作声，便又说了下去。“我查明，她平日里的营生，是在沙夫茨伯里大街后身一家夜总会里当所谓的『伴舞女郎』。我上那儿找她。从主管的经理口中我了解到：半克朗（两先令六便士）跳一支舞，此外再随我高兴添付多少，便可同她爱聊多久聊多久。”

他又迟疑起来，阿伯肖从他脸上多少看出了这场幻灭对他说来究竟意味着什么。

===Original===
“As you know,” Wyatt continued, “I know very little of women. As a rule they don’t interest me at all. I think that is why Joy interested me so much. I want you to understand,” he burst out suddenly with something akin to savagery in his tone, “that the fact that she was not of my world, that her accent was horrible, and her fingernails hideously over-manicured would not have made the slightest difference. I was in love with her: I wanted to marry her. The fact that she was stupid did not greatly deter me either. She was incredibly stupid﻿—the awful stupidity of crass ignorance and innocence. Yes,” he went on bitterly as he caught Abbershaw’s involuntary expression, “innocence. I think it was that that broke me up. The girl was innocent with the innocence of a savage. She knew nothing. The elementary civilized code of right and wrong was an abstruse doctrine to her. She was horrible.” He shuddered, and Abbershaw fancied that he began to understand. An incident that would have been ordinary enough to a boy in his teens had proved too much for a studious recluse of twenty-seven. It had unhinged his mind.

Wyatt’s next remark therefore surprised him.

“She interested me,” he said. “I wanted to study her. I thought her extraordinary mental state was due to chance at first﻿—some unfortunate accident of birth and upbringing﻿—but I found I was wrong. That was the thing that turned me into a particularly militant type of social reformer. Do you understand what I mean, Abbershaw?”

He leant forward as he spoke, his eyes fixed on the other man’s face. “Do you understand what I’m saying? The state of that girl’s mentality was not due to chance﻿—it was *deliberate*.”

===Chinese===
“你是知道的，”怀亚特接着说，“我对女人知道得很少。平素她们根本引不起我的兴趣。我想，这正是乔伊让我如此着迷的缘故。我要你明白，”他突然迸出这么一句，语气里带着近乎凶狠的意味，“她不属于我这个世界，她口音难听，指甲修剪得俗不可耐——这些绝不会有一丝一毫的影响。我爱她：我要娶她。她蠢，这也没有怎么吓退我。她蠢得叫人难以置信——那是浑噩的无知加上懵懂的天真所特有的可怕的蠢。是的，”他捕捉到阿伯肖情不自禁的神色，苦涩地说了下去，“天真。我想正是这个把我击垮了。那姑娘天真得像个野蛮人。她什么都不知道。文明社会最初步的是非准则，在她看来都是玄奥难解的学问。她可怕极了。”他打了个寒噤，阿伯肖自觉开始明白了。一桩对十几岁的少年来说再寻常不过的事，落在一个二十七岁埋头书卷的隐士身上，却重得叫他承受不起。这桩事把他的神智搅得脱了榫。

因此，怀亚特接下去那句话反倒叫他吃了一惊。

“她引起了我的兴趣，”他说，“我想研究她。起初我以为她那异乎寻常的心智状态纯属偶然——出身和教养上某种不幸的意外——后来我发现我错了。正是这件事，把我变成了一个格外好斗的社会改革者。你明白我的意思吗，阿伯肖？”

说话间他探身向前，双眼紧盯对方的脸。“你听懂我的话了吗？那姑娘的心智状态并非偶然形成——那是『蓄意』造就的。”

===Original===
Abbershaw started.

“Impossible,” he said involuntarily, and Wyatt seized upon the word.

“Impossible?” he echoed passionately. “That’s what everybody would say, I suppose, but I tell you you’re wrong. I went right into it. I found out. That girl had been trained from a child. She was a perfect product of a diabolical scheme, and she wasn’t the only victim. It was a society, Abbershaw, a highly organized criminal concern. This girl, my girl, and several others of her kind, were little wheels in the machinery. They were the catspaws﻿—specially prepared implements with which to attract certain men or acquire certain information. The thing is horrible when the girl is cognizant of what she is doing﻿—when the choice is her own﻿—but think of it, trained from childhood, minds deliberately warped, deliberately developed along certain lines. It’s driven me insane, Abbershaw.”

He was silent for a moment or so, his head in his hands. Abbershaw rose to his feet, but the other turned to him eagerly.

“Don’t go,” he said. “You must hear it all.”

The little red-haired doctor sat down immediately.

===Chinese===
阿伯肖悚然一动。

“不可能。”他脱口而出，怀亚特立刻抓住了这个词。

“不可能？”他激愤地应声反问。“想必人人都会这么说，可我告诉你，你错了。我把这事从头到尾查了个透。我查清楚了。那姑娘从小便受调教。她是一项恶毒计划精心制成的完美产品，而且她绝非唯一的受害者。那是一个团伙，阿伯肖，一个组织严密得可怕的犯罪集团。这姑娘，我的姑娘，还有好几个和她同类的姑娘，都是这部机器上的小小齿轮。她们是供人驱使的爪牙——专门调教出来的工具，用来勾住某些男人，或猎取某些情报。倘若姑娘对自己干的勾当心知肚明——倘若那是她自己的选择——这事已经够骇人了——可你想想看：自幼受训，心智被蓄意扭曲，被蓄意朝特定的路子栽培。这简直把我逼疯了，阿伯肖。”

他双手抱头，沉默了片刻。阿伯肖起身要走，对方却急切地转了过来。

“别走，”他说，“你必须听我讲完。”

红头发的小个子医生立刻坐了回去。

===Original===
“I found it all out,” Wyatt repeated. “I shook out the whole terrible story and discovered that the brains of this organization were bought, like everything else. That is to say, they had a special brain to plan the crime that other men would commit. That appalled me. There’s something revolting about mass-production anyway, but when applied to crime it’s ghastly. I felt I’d wasted my life fooling around with books and theories, while all around me, on my very doorstep, these appalling things were happening. I worked it all out up here. It seemed to me that the thing to be done was to get at those brains﻿—to destroy them. Lodging information with the police wouldn’t be enough. What’s the good of sending brains like that to prison for a year or two when at the end of the time they can come back and start afresh? It took me a year to trace those brains and I found them in my own family, though not, thank God, in my own kin﻿ ﻿… my aunt’s husband, Gordon Coombe. I saw that there was no point in simply going down there and blowing his brains out. *He* was only the beginning. There were others, men who could organize the thing, men who could conceive such an abominable idea as the one which turned Dolly Lord into Joy Love, a creature not quite human, not quite animal﻿—a machine, in fact. So I had to go warily. My uncle was in the habit of asking me to take house-parties down to Black Dudley, as you probably know, to cover his interviews with his confederates. I planned what I thought was a perfect killing, and the next time I was asked I chose my house-party carefully and went down there with every intention of putting my scheme into action.”

===Chinese===
“我全都查明白了，”怀亚特重复道，“我把这整个可怕的故事抖搂了出来，发现这个组织的脑力也是买来的，一如其他一切那样。也就是说，他们专门供养着一副脑子，专门替旁人谋划那些由旁人动手去干的罪行。这让我胆寒。大规模生产这码事，本身就有点令人作呕，用到了犯罪上更是骇人听闻。我只觉得自己一辈子在书本和理论里瞎打转，全属虚掷，而在我四周，就在我自家门槛外，这些骇人的勾当正在生生不息。我就在这颗脑袋里把一切想了个透。依我看，该做的事就是揪出那些脑子——除掉他们。把情报递到警察手里是不够的。那样的脑子，送进大牢关上一两年，期满之后照样回来重起炉灶，这有什么用处？我花了一年工夫才追到那些脑子的下落，结果发现它们就在我自己家里——不过，感谢上帝，总算不在我的血亲之列……是我叔母的丈夫，戈登·库姆。我明白，单是跑到那儿去一枪崩了他的脑袋，毫无意义。『他』只不过是个开头。还有旁人，有本事组织这整盘营生的男人，想得出那等伤天害理主意的男人——正是那种主意，把多莉·洛德造成了乔伊·拉芙，一个不人不兽的东西——实际上，是一台机器。所以我必须步步谨慎。你们多半也知道，我叔父惯于央我带一拨拨客人下布莱克·达德利去，好遮掩他与同伙的会面。我谋划了一桩我自认天衣无缝的凶杀，待他下一次再开口相邀，我便细细挑定了我的宾客，抱着将计划付诸实行的十足决心下了乡。”

===Original===
“You *chose* your house-party?”

Abbershaw looked at him curiously as he spoke.

“Certainly,” said Wyatt calmly. “I chose each one of you deliberately. You were all people of blameless reputation. There was not one of you who could not clear himself with perfect certainty. The suspicion would therefore necessarily fall on one of my uncle’s own guests, each of whom had done, if not murder, something more than as bad. I thought Campion was of their party until we were all prisoners. Until Prenderby told me, I thought Anne Edgeware had brought him, even then.”

===Chinese===
“那些客人，是你亲手挑的？”

说这话时，阿伯肖好奇地打量着他。

“当然，”怀亚特平静地说，“你们每一位都是我刻意挑定的。你们全都是名声清白无瑕的人。你们当中随便哪一位，都能十拿九稳地把自己洗刷干净。这样一来，嫌疑便必然落到我叔父自家客人中的一人头上——那批人个个都干过不说凶案、也与之不相上下的勾当。直到我们统统沦为阶下囚，我还当坎皮恩是他们一伙的。甚至在那时，直到普伦德比告诉我之前，我一直以为他是安妮·埃奇韦尔带来的。”

===Original===
“You ran an extraordinary risk,” said Abbershaw.

Wyatt shook his head.

“Why?” he said. “I was my uncle’s benefactor, not he mine. I had nothing to gain by his death, and I should have been as free from suspicion as any of you. Of course,” he went on, “I had no idea that things would turn out as they did. No one could have been more surprised than I when they concealed the murder in that extraordinary way. When I realized that they had lost something I understood, and I was desperately anxious that they should not recover what I took to be my uncle’s notes for the gang’s next coup. That is why I asked you to stay.”

“Of course,” said Abbershaw slowly, “you were wrong.”

“In not pitching on von Faber as my first victim?” said Wyatt.

===Chinese===
“你冒的风险可非同小可。”阿伯肖说。

怀亚特摇了摇头。

“何以见得？”他说，“我才是我叔父的恩主，而不是他施恩于我。他的死给不了我半点好处，而我本该和你们当中任何一个一样，干干净净不沾嫌疑。当然，”他接着说，“我压根没想到事情会闹到后来那步田地。他们用那般离奇的手段遮掩这桩命案时，谁也不可能比我更吃惊。待我明白他们丢了东西，我就懂了，于是急得要命，生怕他们寻回那件东西——我认定那是我叔父替那伙人下一票大买卖记下的札记。正因如此，我才请你留下来的。”

“当然啦，”阿伯肖缓缓地说，“你错了。”

“错在没挑冯·法贝尔当头一名牺牲者？”怀亚特说。

===Original===
Abbershaw shook his head.

“No,” he said. “In setting out to fight a social evil single-handed. That is always a mad thing to do.”

Wyatt raised his eyes to meet the other’s.

“I know,” he said simply. “I think I am a little mad. It seemed to me so wicked. I loved her.”

There was silence after he had spoken, and the two men sat for some time, Abbershaw staring into the fire, Wyatt leaning back, his eyes half-closed. The thought that possessed Abbershaw’s mind was the pity of it﻿—such a good brain, such a valuable idealistic soul. And it struck him in a sudden impersonal way that it was odd that evil should beget evil. It was as if it went on spreading in ever-widening circles, like ripples round the first splash of a stone thrown into a pond.

===Chinese===
阿伯肖摇了摇头。

“不，”他说，“错在单枪匹马去讨伐一桩社会罪恶。这种事，从来都是疯子才干的。”

怀亚特抬起眼来，迎上对方的目光。

“我知道，”他淡淡地说，“我想我是有点疯。那桩事在我看来，实在太邪恶了。我爱过她。”

他说完便沉默了。两人坐了好一会儿，阿伯肖盯着炉火，怀亚特仰靠着，双眼半阖。盘踞在阿伯肖心头的念头只有一个：可惜——多好的一副头脑，多可贵的一颗理想主义的灵魂。他又忽然以一种置身事外的方式察觉到：恶竟会孳生出恶，这真是咄咄怪事。那恶仿佛就此一圈圈不断荡漾开去，好比一块石头掷进池塘，最初扑通一响之后，涟漪一圈圈越漾越远。

===Original===
Wyatt recalled him from his reverie.

“It was a perfect murder,” he said, almost wonderingly. “How did you find me out?”

Abbershaw hesitated. Then he sighed. “I couldn’t help it,” he said. “It was too perfect. It left nothing to chance. Do you know where I have spent the last week or so? In the British Museum.”

He looked at the other steadily.

“I now know more about your family history than, I should think, any other man alive. That Ritual story would have been wonderful for your purpose, Wyatt, if it just hadn’t been for one thing. It was not true.”

===Chinese===
怀亚特把他从冥想中唤了回来。

“那是一桩完美无缺的谋杀，”他说，语气近乎惊叹，“你是怎么识破我的？”

阿伯肖迟疑了一下，随即叹了口气。“我实在没法子，”他说，“它太完美了，完美得不留半点侥幸。你知道过去这一个来星期，我都待在哪儿吗？大英博物馆。”

他定定地望着对方。

“如今论起对皮特里家史的了解，我想世上活着的任何人都不如我。那个匕首仪式的故事，本来对你的用途来说再妙不过，怀亚特，可惜就坏在一件事上。它不是真的。”

===Original===
Wyatt rose from his chair abruptly, and walked up and down the room. This flaw in his scheme seemed to upset him more than anything else had done.

“But it might have been true,” he argued. “Who could prove it? A family legend.”

“But it wasn’t true,” Abbershaw persisted. “It wasn’t true because from the year 1100 until the year 1603﻿—long past the latest date to which such a story as yours could have been feasible, Black Dudley was a monastery and not in the possession of your family at all. Your family estate was higher up the coast, in Norfolk, and I shouldn’t think the dagger came into your possession until 1650 at least, when an ancestor of yours is referred to as having returned from the Papal States laden with merchandise.”

===Chinese===
怀亚特霍地从椅中起身，在屋里来回踱步。计划里的这处纰漏，似乎比别的任何事都更叫他心烦意乱。

“可它本可能是真的呀，”他争辩道，“谁能证明不是呢？不过是一则家族传说。”

“可它偏偏不是真的，”阿伯肖寸步不让，“它不是真的，因为从 1100 年直到 1603 年——这远在你那个故事还能成立的最后期限之后——布莱克·达德利都是一座修道院，压根儿就不在你们家族手里。你们家的地产在沿海岸更往上去的地方，在诺福克；而且我料想，那柄匕首最早也要到 1650 年前后才落到你们手上——那一年，有记载说你们家的一位先祖从教皇国满载货殖归来。”

===Original===
Wyatt continued to pace up and down the room.

“I see,” he said. “I see. But otherwise it was a perfect murder. Think of it﻿—Heaven knows how many fingerprints on the dagger handle, no one with any motive﻿—no one who might not have committed the crime, and by the same reasoning no one who might. It had its moments of horror too, though,” he said, pausing suddenly. “The moment when I came upon Miss Oliphant in the dark﻿—I had to follow the dagger round, you see, to be in at the first alarm. I saw her pause under the window and stare at the blade, and I don’t think it was until then that I realized that there was blood on it. So I took it from her. It was an impulsive, idiotic thing to do, and when the alarm did come the thing was in my own hand. I didn’t see what they were getting at at first, and I was afraid I hadn’t quite killed him, although I’d worked out the blow with a medical chart before I went down there. I took the dagger up to my own room. You nearly found me with it, by the way.”

Abbershaw nodded.

“I know,” he said. “I think it was instinct, but as you came in from the balcony I caught a glimpse of something in your hand, and although I didn’t see what it was, I couldn’t get the idea of the dagger out of my mind.”

===Chinese===
怀亚特继续在屋里踱来踱去。

“我明白了，”他说，“我明白了。可除此之外，它是一桩完美无缺的谋杀。想想看吧——天知道匕首柄上留了多少枚指纹，人人都没动机——人人都可能犯下这桩罪，而照同样的道理，人人都也不可能。不过，它也有叫人心惊肉跳的时刻，”他说着，蓦地停住，“我在黑暗中撞见奥利芬特小姐的那一刻——你晓得，我必须跟着匕首一路传下去，好赶上第一声惊叫时在场。我看见她在窗下停住脚，盯着刀刃瞧，我想直到那时我才意识到上面有血。于是我从她手里接过了它。那是一时冲动的蠢行，而警报果真响起时，那东西正攥在我自己手里。起初我没看透他们意在何处，还生怕自己没能把他杀死干净——虽然下乡之前，我早对着医学图谱把那一击的力道演练周详了。我把匕首带上了我自己房里。顺便说一句，你差一点就撞见我拿着它了。”

阿伯肖点了点头。

“我知道，”他说，“我想那是出于本能。你从阳台进来的时候，我瞥见你手里攥着个什么东西，虽然没看清是什么，可那柄匕首的念头，从此就再没从我心里出去过。”

===Original===
“Two flaws,” said Wyatt, and was silent.

The atmosphere in the pleasant room had become curiously cold, and Abbershaw shivered. The sordid glossy photograph lay upon the floor, and the pretty childish face with the expression of innocence which had now become so sinister smiled up at him from the carpet.

“Well, what are you going to do?”

It was Wyatt who spoke, pausing abruptly in his feverish stride.

===Chinese===
“两处破绽。”怀亚特说罢，便不作声了。

这间惬意的屋子里，气氛变得异样地冷，阿伯肖打了个寒噤。那张寒碜的光面相片躺在地板上，那张俊美的、孩子气的脸，带着如今已变得如此凶险的天真神情，从地毯上朝他微笑着。

“那么，你打算怎么办？”

说话的是怀亚特，他在亢奋的踱步中蓦地收住了脚。

===Original===
Abbershaw did not look at him.

“What are *you* going to do?” he murmured.

Wyatt hesitated.

“There is a Dominican Foundation in the rocky valley of El Puerto in the north of Spain,” he said. “I have been in correspondence with them for some time. I have been disposing of all my books this week. I realized when von Faber passed into the hands of the police that my campaign was ended, but﻿—”

He stopped and looked at Abbershaw; then he shrugged his shoulders.

“What now?” he said.

===Chinese===
阿伯肖没有看他。

“你自己打算怎么办？”他喃喃道。

怀亚特迟疑了。

“西班牙北部埃尔普埃尔托的岩谷里，有一座多明我会修院，”他说，“我与他们通信有些时日了。这个星期，我一直在处置我全部的藏书。冯·法贝尔落入警察之手的那一刻，我就明白我的战事结束了，不过——”

他顿住话头，望了望阿伯肖，随后耸了耸肩。

“那如今呢？”他说。

===Original===
Abbershaw rose to his feet and held out his hand.

“I don’t suppose I shall see you again before you go,” he said. “Goodbye.”

Wyatt shook the outstretched hand, but after the first flicker of interest which the last words had occasioned his expression had become preoccupied. He crossed the room and picked up the photograph, and the last glimpse Abbershaw had of him was as he sat in the deep armchair, crouching over it, his eyes fixed on the sweet, foolish little face.

As the little doctor walked slowly down the staircase to the street his mind was in confusion. He was conscious of a strong feeling of relief, even although his worst fears had been realized. At the back of his head, the old problem of Law and Order as opposed to Right and Wrong worried itself into the inextricable tangle which knows no unravelling. Wyatt was both a murderer and a martyr. There was no one who could decide between the two, in his opinion.

And in his thoughts, too, were his own affairs: Meggie, and his love for her, and their marriage.

===Chinese===
阿伯肖站起身，伸出手去。

“想来你动身之前，我是见不着你了，”他说，“再见了。”

怀亚特握住那只伸来的手，只是最后那几句话激起的一星兴味闪过之后，他的神情便又变得心事重重。他穿过房间，拾起那张相片。阿伯肖回头最后一瞥望见的他，是坐在那把深扶手椅里，佝偻着身子伏在相片上，双眼凝驻在那张甜甜的、痴痴的小脸上。

小个子医生缓步走下楼梯，来到街上，心里一团乱麻。他意识到一股强烈的如释重负，尽管他最坏的忧惧已成事实。在他脑海深处，那个老问题——法律与秩序对峙是非与公道——又自行拧成了一个再也解不开的死结。怀亚特既是杀人犯，又是殉道者。依他看，普天之下没有谁能在这两者之间下一道裁决。

他的思绪里，也装着他自己的事：梅吉，他对她的爱，还有他们的婚事。

---

===Original===
As he stepped out into the street, a round moon face, red and hot with righteous indignation loomed down upon him out of the darkness.

“Come at last, ’ave yer?” inquired a thick sarcastic voice. “Your name and address, *if* you please.”

Gradually it dawned upon the still meditative doctor that he was confronted by an excessively large and unfriendly London bobby.

“This is your car, I suppose?” the questioner continued more mildly, as he observed Abbershaw’s blank expression, but upon receiving the assurance that it was, all his indignation returned.

“This car’s been left ’ere over an hour to my certain personal knowledge,” he bellowed. “Unattended and drawn out a foot from the kerb, which aggravates the offence. This’ll mean a summons, you know”﻿—he flourished his notebook﻿—“Name and address.”

===Chinese===
他刚一步出门，来到街上，一张红涨发烫、满腔义愤的圆月脸，便从黑暗里朝他当头逼了下来。

“可算肯露面了，是吧？”一个又浊又刻薄的嗓音发问。“劳您大驾，报上姓名住址。”

这位犹自出神的医生这才渐渐醒悟：眼前拦住他的，是一名块头大得出奇、满脸不善的伦敦巡警。

“这辆车是您的吧，我猜？”发问的人见阿伯肖一脸茫然，语气缓和了几分，可一得到确属其车的保证，满腔义愤又尽数回来了。

“这辆车搁在这儿一个多钟头了，是我亲眼所见、确凿无疑的事，”他吼道，“无人看管，还探出路缘足足一英尺，罪上加罪。您知道，这可是要吃传票的。”——他把手里的记事簿一挥——“姓名，住址。”

===Original===
Abbershaw having furnished him with this information, he replaced the pencil in its sheath and, clicking the book’s elastic band smartly, continued his homily. He was clearly very much aggrieved.

“It’s people like you,” he explained, as Abbershaw climbed into the driving seat, “wot gives us officers all our work. But we’re not goin’ to have these offences, I can tell you. We’re making a clean sweep. Persons offending against the Law are not going to be tolerated.”

He paused suspiciously. The slightly dazed expression upon the face of the little red-haired man in the car had suddenly given place to a smile.

“Splendid!” he said, and there was unmistakable enthusiasm in his tone. “Really, really splendid, Officer! You don’t know how comforting that sounds. My fervent wishes for your success.” And he drove off, leaving the policeman looking after him, wondering a little wistfully if the charge in his notebook should not perhaps have read, “Drunk in charge of a car.”

===Chinese===
阿伯肖报上了这两项，他便把铅笔插回笔套，又把记事簿上的橡皮筋利落地一弹，接着训示下去。他分明一肚皮的委屈。

“就是你们这号人，”趁着阿伯肖爬进驾驶座，他宣讲道，“害得我们当差的有的是活儿干。可我把话放这儿，这种犯法的事咱们不能容。我们正来一场彻头彻尾的大扫除。胆敢触犯法律之徒，一个也容不得。”

他狐疑地顿住了。车里那个红头发的小个子男人脸上那副微微发怔的神情，突然换成了一副笑容。

“好极了！”他说，语气里的热忱货真价实、不容错认，“真的，真的好极了，警官先生！您不知道这话听起来多么叫人安心。我热切祝愿您旗开得胜。”说罢他便驱车而去，撇下那警察望着他的背影，多少有点怅然地犯着嘀咕：自己记事簿里记下的那一条罪名，是不是当初就该写成『醉酒驾车』才对。
